# Queer Guide

LGBTQ+ travel & community platform at queer.guide

## Commands

| Task | Command |
|------|---------|
| Install | `npm install` |
| Dev | `npm run dev` (port 8080) |
| Build | `npm run build` |
| Test | `npm test` |
| Lint | `npm run lint` |
| Typecheck | `npm run typecheck` |
| Format | `npm run format` |

**Typecheck is a baseline ratchet.** `npm run typecheck` runs `scripts/typecheck-ratchet.mjs` → real `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit`, compared against `scripts/typecheck-baseline.json`; it fails only on errors NEW per (file, TS-code). ~1.4k pre-existing errors are recorded there (a large share are Supabase client type-inference `SelectQueryError` unions, not live bugs). Fix errors rather than re-baselining; after fixing, run `npm run typecheck:baseline` to lock the improvement in. `npm run typecheck:functions` is a separate **zero-tolerance** gate (`tsconfig.functions.json`) covering `functions/` — the Cloudflare Pages edge middleware, which `tsconfig.app.json` (`"include": ["src"]`) never saw; it starts and must stay at 0 errors. Until 2026-07-29 `typecheck` was bare `tsc --noEmit` against the solution-style root tsconfig (`"files": []` + `references`) — with no `-b` that compiles **zero** files and always exits 0, so the CI gate was vacuously green for its whole life. Never "fix" errors by loosening `tsconfig.app.json`.

The scraper has its own `package.json` under `scraper/` — `cd scraper && npm install`, then `npm test` etc. there. Workers each from their own directory: `wrangler dev` / `wrangler deploy`.

## Architecture

```
queer-guide-hub/
├── src/                  # React 19 + Vite + TS + Tailwind + shadcn/ui (frontend)
├── scraper/              # Node.js scraping pipeline (Cheerio + Playwright) — own package.json,
│                         # has its own src/, tests/, docs/, scripts/ inside
├── supabase/
│   ├── functions/        # Deno edge functions
│   └── migrations/       # PostgreSQL migrations
├── workers/
│   ├── ingest/           # CF Worker: search-intelligence ingest pipeline
│   ├── search-proxy/     # CF Worker: search proxy (Postgres-backed), Postgres-driven synonyms
│   ├── snapshot-archiver/ # CF Worker: archives admin/editorial snapshots
│   └── submit/           # CF Worker: extension submissions → ingestion_staging
├── docs/                 # Project-wide docs (a11y-audit, architecture, search-intelligence, …)
├── scripts/              # One-shot operator scripts (search-eval/, …)
└── e2e/                  # Playwright e2e specs
```

**Frontend stack:** React 19, Vite 6, TypeScript 5.8, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, TanStack Query/Router/Table, MapLibre GL, Tiptap editor, i18next (11 langs), Recharts, react-force-graph-2d

**Backend:** Supabase (PostgreSQL 17.4, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions), Cloudflare Pages + Workers, GitHub Actions (scraper cron)

**Workflow orchestration:** pgmq v1.4.4 + `workflow-dispatcher` edge function. Tables: `workflow_definitions` (24 workflows), `workflow_runs`. Queues: scheduled_jobs, import_jobs, content_processing, dead_letter. Exponential backoff retry, concurrency limits, idempotency keys.

**Ingestion pipeline:** `source-*` edge functions (data fetchers) feed into `pipeline-*` functions (normalize, validate, deduplicate, quality-score, review-gate). Each source maps to a workflow definition.

**News pipeline (cut over, 2026-04-30):** Canonical path is cron `0 * * * *` (`wf-news-pipeline`) → `pipeline-executor` → `news-ingestion` DAG (10 nodes: `source-rss-news` → `pipeline-normalize` → `pipeline-sanitize-news` → `pipeline-enrich-news` (LLM tags + summary + geo, circuit-broken) → `pipeline-quality-enhance` → `pipeline-validate` → `pipeline-deduplicate` → `pipeline-quality-score` → `pipeline-review-gate` → `pipeline-commit`). Idempotent commit via `news_commit_staging_batch` RPC, UNIQUE on `news_articles.fingerprint` (SHA-256 of normalized_title + published_day + source_id, URL fallback). Source health auto-managed: exp backoff (5min × 2ⁿ, cap 24h), auto-pause at 8 consecutive failures, eligibility via `news_sources_eligible()` RPC. Full audit in `news_dedup_audit`. Visible / editable / observable at `/admin/pipelines?pipeline=news-ingestion` (Builder) and `/admin/pipelines?tab=news` (Sources / Staging / Dedup audit). Manual admin triggers from NewsSourcesManager now also enqueue this canonical pipeline. Migration `20260429310000` disabled the legacy cron + workflow-dispatcher trigger.

**Pipeline overhaul (2026-08-08, P1–P7 live):** the ingest machine is now **two layers** — `admin_automations` (registry of record) → pg_cron is the ONLY scheduler; the `ingestion_staging` state machine + dual-mode `pipeline-*` stage fns + `commit_*_staging_batch` SQL RPCs are the ONLY recurring engine; `pipeline-executor` + Builder stay as the MANUAL/on-demand tool (an unregistered node slug now fails loudly instead of silently no-op'ing — the old ghost-stage trap). **Search sync is decoupled**: entity writes enqueue into `search_reindex_queue` (append-only, deliberately NO unique key — an upsert queue would let writers block on the drain's row locks); `search_reindex_drain(1000)` applies reindexes every minute (observability: single-row `search_reindex_drain_stats`; rollback: one CREATE OR REPLACE from `search_documents_sync_inline_legacy`). Measured: a 300-row events UPDATE fell 14.6s → 0.96s; batch caps that cite the trigger can now be raised gradually. **Canonical stage order** (news v10 / marketplace v9 / events v8 / venue v10): `source(+prefilter) → normalize → extract/sanitize → validate → dedup → [LLM] → quality → review-gate → commit` — cheap deterministic gates before paid stages (`enrichment-driver` `requireGates`; `source-ticketmaster` LGBTQ+ keyword prefilter default-ON killed the 10.6k junk rows/mo that paid five stages to be 99.9% rejected; `source-rss-news` bounded to `max_feeds_per_run` 15 against the HTTP 546s). **LLM spend is centrally budgeted** in `llm_budget` + `llm_budget_consume(caller,n)` (adopters fail open to legacy counting; `AI_DISABLED=1` stays the hard stop; `marketplace-description-enhance` was silently running the 70B on a */5 cron uncapped — now 8B + 500/day). **Dedup sweep runs `mode=full`** with `conditions.mode_overrides` — personality is pinned `queue_only` in the registry AND hard-clamped in `run_dedup_truth_sweep_all` itself; nightly `run_dedup_review_autoapprove` resolves stale pairs (duplicate_of_id chain-following) then approves ≥0.95 non-personality pairs. **Sentinel:** `pipeline_hygiene_stats().stale_pending_by_entity` (>48h pending per target_table) hard-fails CI on NEW starvation. **Entry points consolidated:** 6 `import-*` fns deleted into `source-*` (`sourceType` passthrough keeps source-reliability continuity), dormant scraper machinery deleted (only the weekly gaycities GH Action path remains, already thin-fetcher), keyless fill crons feed the previously-dead fill side (`vn-fill-osm` daily, `city-fill-geonames` + `vn-fill-restrooms` weekly; keyed sources stay admin-triggered). **How to add a source:** write `source-*` fn using `_shared/source-adapter.ts` → register cron + `admin_automations` row in ONE migration. **How to retire a cron:** disable the registry row FIRST, then guarded unschedule (pattern `20260813100000`) — never DELETE the row. **Gated Wave B** (DAG-start retirement per family incl. news drains, search-drain A2 upsert): `docs/plans/2026-08-pipeline-overhaul-wave-b.md`; baseline numbers: `docs/audits/2026-08-pipeline-overhaul-baseline.md`.

**A human "approve" in the triage inbox did nothing, for as long as the inbox has existed (fixed 2026-08-22):** every approve path — `triage_action('staging','approve')`, `triage_bulk_approve_high_conf`, the Import Hub's `ingestion-review-api`, the dedup-resolution RPCs — writes `ingestion_staging.review_status='approved'`, but **every downstream stage gates on a different column**, `ai_validation_status`: `pipeline-deduplicate`, `pipeline-review-gate`, `marketplace-relevance`, `enrichment-driver` and all six `commit_*_staging_batch` require it to read `approved`, and **the only writer of that value is `pipeline-validate`**. So once validate returned `needs_review` — for a venue a single `W_NO_COORDS` is enough — the row was invisible to dedup, to the review gate and to commit forever, and the queue collected human decisions and discarded them. Measured on prod: **14 events stranded since 2026-07-13 (40 days)** plus 25 eventfrog venues. What is NOT wrong is commit, which already accepts `review_status IN ('auto','approved')` — `ai_validation_status` was the only door shut, which is why hand-flipping that one column drained the 25 venues. **The promotion lives in a BEFORE UPDATE trigger (`trg_staging_human_approval_clears_validation`), not in the approve RPC**, because there are at least four writers of `review_status='approved'` and patching one leaves three; it stamps `ai_validation_result.human_override` (from/by/at) so the override is visible on the row without erasing the validator's errors/warnings/quality. It promotes `pending` and `needs_review` **only** — a validator hard rejection is never silently overridden — and the new sentinel `pipeline_hygiene_stats().stranded_human_approved` is deliberately **wider** than the trigger (`ai_validation_status IS DISTINCT FROM 'approved'`), so any shape the trigger refuses to clear (a hard rejection marked approved, or a writer that INSERTs `approved` instead of UPDATEing it) hard-fails `check-pipeline-health.mjs` at **one row, no baseline allowance**, instead of being auto-cleared. It is its own key rather than part of `stale_pending_by_entity` because **those thresholds are why this hid for 40 days**: 14 rows sit far under a 3,500-row warn floor. The one-shot repair **publishes every stranded row, past-dated events included.** A first draft dispositioned them by their own date — a row approved 40 days ago was approved as an *upcoming* event — and that was **rejected as a rule for this corpus**: `events` deliberately holds ~36.5k past events from the Wayback import, so a past date is not a defect here and "already happened" is not grounds to discard a human approval. (The 14 were all `gaycities` pride/festival imports flagged `W_PRIDE_TIME_WINDOW` + `W_PRIDE_NOT_SATURDAY` + `W_NO_GEO` — 3 warnings ≥ `warnReview` — and 5 had already ended.) Undated rows are promoted rather than pre-rejected too: commit RAISEs `event_missing_start_date` and the batch handler writes that on the row, so the commit path dispositions them with a real reason instead of the migration guessing. Migration `20260916120000`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/stagingHumanApproval.test.ts`.

**Auto-pause erases its own evidence, and took the whole ingest engine down for 40 hours (2026-08-25):** `workflow_dispatcher_1min` is the ONLY consumer of the `pipeline_steps` queue, i.e. the sole stepper for every DAG. A burst of `workflow-dispatcher` HTTP 500s on 2026-08-23 13:28 tripped `auto_pause_threshold=3`, setting `enabled=false`; pg_cron kept firing for another ~14h (auto-pause does not unschedule), the job kept SUCCEEDING, and then the nightly `automation_cron_sync` (`10 5 * * *` → `sync_automations_to_cron(true)`) branch (b) unscheduled every disabled row at **2026-08-24 05:10**. Measured effect: `pipeline_runs` went 84 completed/0 failed on 08-23 → **0 completed/70 reaped** on 08-25, all seven ingestion pipelines with every node still `pending`; news_articles/day 141→**0**, events 232→7, venues 1718→2; 181 undrained `pgmq.q_pipeline_steps` messages. **Push notifications died with it** — the same sweep took nine automations. `reap_stuck_pipeline_runs()` was reporting the outage, not causing it. **The trap is that the pause deletes the proof it happened:** the success branch of `admin_automation_runs_after_finish()` resets `consecutive_failures=0` and `last_run_status='success'` but **never re-enables**, so a falsely-paused row ends up reading exactly like a deliberate human retirement. The only durable record is `summary.auto_paused` on the historical run row. **`workflow_runs` says the work COMPLETED and that is not evidence** — `enqueue_workflow()` posts the `action:'start'` leg directly over HTTP and gets a 200; the per-node `'continue'` steps are what the dispatcher drives, and only those stopped (`pipeline-executor` runs 274→6). Always confirm on `pipeline_runs`/`node_states`, never on a start-leg 200. **This was the THIRD occurrence** (`search_reindex_drain` 2026-08-22, `admin_automation_reap` 2026-08-16) and the sentinel written after the second one was hardcoded to `slug=eq.search_reindex_drain` while its own comment described the mechanism verbatim — one slug out of ~240. `scripts/check-pipeline-health.mjs` now checks **every** slug and separates the two cases: auto-paused **and still failing** is legitimate and only warns (`marketplace_variant_backfill` sits at 288 real failures and must stay off), while auto-paused **then recovered** (`consecutive_failures=0 AND last_run_status='success' AND enabled=false`) hard-fails. That rule found an 8th victim the by-hand restore had missed, `marketplace_taxonomy_backfill`, with 21,613 listings still unclassified. **Recovery is not just `enabled=true`:** an `action->>'type'='rpc'` row carries **no `action.command`**, so `sync_automations_to_cron()` branch (d) structurally CANNOT reschedule it (all rpc automations are scheduled by their own migration) — re-enabling leaves it on-but-unscheduled, and the cron must be recreated from that migration. **Always read the `recreated` list the sync returns instead of assuming it worked.**

**Cron automations record their own runs (2026-09-10):** until `20260910163700`, **142 of the 144 enabled `action->>'type'='cron'` rows had `last_run_at = NULL` and zero `admin_automation_runs`** — nothing on the pg_cron → `net.http_post` path wrote back, so `consecutive_failures` never moved and the auto-pause net from `20260523340000` **could not fire for any of them**. Not theoretical: `city_safety_backfill` failed six consecutive nights (2026-08-11…16) on an `ON CONFLICT` with no matching constraint, and nothing anywhere said so. **The 144 are THREE families and each needs a different truth source** — 74 call `net.http_post` in the command text, 20 call `enqueue_workflow()` which posts internally, 50 are pure synchronous SQL. `cron.job_run_details` was populated all along (7-day retention) and is exact for family C, but for A and B it reports `succeeded` the instant the request is **enqueued** — so projecting it wholesale manufactures a false green that *resets* `consecutive_failures` on every failing run, which is worse than the blank column it replaces. Hence two mechanisms: **dispatch truth** `admin_automation_project_cron_runs()` (records a raised exception as an error run for every family — for a tracked job that is the ONLY evidence, since the pg_cron transaction rolls the bookkeeping back with it — and records successes for family C only, sampled hourly, because four registered jobs fire every minute and per-execution rows would add ~10k/day to a disk-constrained DB); **response truth** `admin_automation_reap_runs()` (*/5), which joins `net._http_response` **BY REQUEST ID**. A and B get `admin_automation_run_begin('<slug>')` prefixed onto the cron command and their posts re-pointed at `public.automation_http_post`, a signature-identical shim that files each request id against the open run through the transaction-local GUC `app.automation_run_id`. **The registry stays canonical** — `action.command` keeps the original readable SQL, the wrapped form exists only in pg_cron, is derived by `admin_automation_effective_command()` and applied by `sync_automations_to_cron()`'s new command-drift branch, so one reconciler pass converted all 95 and a later migration re-scheduling the raw command is re-wrapped automatically. **Traps:** (1) **a trigger on `net.http_request_queue` is the design this wants to be and is NOT available** — the table is owned by `supabase_admin` and postgres is not a member (`42501 must be owner of relation`); don't re-attempt it. (2) **Never resolve a pg_net response by recency or URL** — `net._http_response` is shared by every caller on the instance and **has no url column at all**, and urls aren't unique across jobs anyway (`city_factual_backfill` and `city_factual_sparql` post to the same one); a "newest row" reaper already mis-read a `translate-i18n-batch` response as the adult-links one during the #2795 verification, which is why `request_id` is the **PRIMARY KEY** of `admin_automation_run_requests` — the mistake is unspellable, not merely discouraged. (3) The rewrite is a **token substitution, never an argument-list parse**: the 74 commands include a `DO` block, a `WITH … WHERE EXISTS` and two that post twice in one statement, and every expression-level wrap breaks on all three. (4) **A run with zero requests is a no-op SUCCESS, not a failure** — several commands post conditionally, and the run row's own survival proves its transaction committed. (4b) **A pg_net `timed_out` response is `partial`, NOT `error`, and never touches the counter** — it means pg_net gave up client-side while the edge function kept running, so it is absence of evidence, not evidence of failure. Measured on the first live pass: 2 of 41 requests, both jobs whose registered command omits `timeout_milliseconds` and so inherits pg_net's **5s default**; counting them would have auto-paused `workflow_dispatcher_1min` (every minute, core) within three minutes. The cost is that a target which merely *hangs* never auto-pauses, so `admin_automation_tracking_gaps().unverifiable_automations` (≥3 runs in 24h, all unverifiable) is what stops such a job reading as healthy — the fix is per job: raise the registered `timeout_milliseconds` until the response fits, and the hang becomes a countable failure. (5) `net._http_response` retention is **~6h measured**, so the reaper's `*/5` is load-bearing; a stalled reaper loses the evidence permanently rather than catching up. (6) Wrapping a command whose helper still calls `net.http_post` directly yields a false green, so only helpers listed in `admin_automation_tracked_callers` (proof of patching, not a wish list) may be wrapped, and `admin_automation_tracking_gaps().untracked_http_dispatchers` hard-fails CI on a new one. The projector's cursor starts at the current head, **forward-only**: backfilling seven days would auto-pause everything already failing, which is a decision to take deliberately rather than a migration side effect. Auto-pause sets `enabled=false`; the cron itself stops at the next `sync_automations_to_cron(true)` (nightly 05:10), so a paused job can still fire for up to a day. **Its first day in production found two faults in itself, both fixed 2026-08-16.** (7) **There was ALREADY a projector and I did not find it before shipping a second one.** `run_cron_failure_sweep` (hourly, since ~2026-07-31) records the same `cron.job_run_details` failures; the two dedupe against different keys (`summary->>'runid'` vs the `cron_runid` column) so neither can see the other's rows — measured, 9 runids carried two error rows each. Duplicate audit rows would be cosmetic, except that widening the auto-pause trigger to `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` means an already-finished row now increments, so **two writers increment twice per failure and `auto_pause_threshold=3` trips after two real failures** — which is how `admin_automation_reap`, the layer everything else depends on, auto-paused itself. `20260816113024` makes them **disjoint by construction** rather than cross-deduping: the projector owns every job with a registry row, the sweep keeps only crons with none (those land `automation_id IS NULL`, so they can never reach the counter). `consecutive_failures` cannot be repaired by arithmetic after double-counting — recompute it as *terminal error runs newer than the most recent terminal non-error run*, which is the same quantity the trigger accumulates. (8) **Two `*/5` jobs that both write `admin_automations` will deadlock, and these did** — the projector locks rows in automation order, the reaper in open-run order (indirectly, via the auto-pause trigger on the parent row): four deadlocks in the first three hours. `20260816113041` gives both a shared `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('admin_automation_tracking'))` via thin `*_tick()` wrappers (try, not blocking — a skipped tick is free, a queued pg_cron worker turns a stall into a pile-up) and offsets the reaper to `2-59/5` so the lock is a backstop rather than the mechanism. They stay **two jobs**: merging them into one transaction also kills the deadlock but couples the reaper's unrecoverable ~6h evidence window to the projector's recoverable 7-day one. The wrappers exist so the reviewed function bodies are not restated to insert two lines at the top.

**Marketplace pipeline (hardened, 2026-04-15):** Cron `0 4 * * *` → `marketplace-ingestion` DAG (13 nodes, multi-source fan-in): `source-awin` + `source-shopify` + `source-etsy` → `fan-in` → `pipeline-normalize` → `pipeline-validate` (marketplace branch: title/price/URL/image/currency/availability) → `marketplace-relevance` (CF Workers AI llama-3.1-8b LGBTQ+ gate — NOT Claude Haiku, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` unset; rejects < 0.5 confidence) → `pipeline-deduplicate` (marketplace branch: source_entity_id → external_url → domain+title → brand+title → title trigram) → `pipeline-quality-score` → `pipeline-review-gate` → `pipeline-commit` (marketplace branch) → parallel `marketplace-image-mirror` (→ `marketplace-images` R2/Storage bucket, SHA-256 dedup) + `embedding-generator`. Atomic commit via `commit_marketplace_staging_batch` RPC with advisory lock + price-history delta + source-junction upsert. UNIQUE on `(source_type, source_entity_id)`. `price_usd` auto-computed from `fx_rates` (23 currencies, refreshed daily via `marketplace-fx-sync`). Affiliate links resolved to `affiliate_partners` via `merchant_domain`. Link-rot sweeper `marketplace-link-checker` (weekly) updates `link_health`, demotes broken listings to `status='inactive'`. Multi-merchant registry `marketplace_merchants` (provider, shop_domain/shop_id, api_key_env, last_sync_*). Visible at `/admin/pipelines?pipeline=marketplace-ingestion` (Builder).

**Brand logos (marketplace makers, 2026-08-22):** `marketplace_brands.logo_url` had existed since the maker pages shipped and was non-null on **0 of 5,142 rows**, so every plate on `/marketplace/brands`, every maker masthead and the weekly brand spotlight rendered a monogram. Nothing had ever written it: the logo machine (`enrich-logos` → logo.dev probe → R2 mirror, 1,340 venues deep) keys on a `website`, and only **23 of the 885 live brands have one**. **The domain has to come from the listings, and that is the whole difficulty** — a listing's `merchant_domain` is the SHOP that sells the brand, not the brand: Oxballs sells through six retailers and owns none of those domains, and the brand literally named "Custom" (799 listings) sells only on automicgold.com. So `marketplace_brand_logo_candidates(p_limit)` (migration `20260917100000`) requires the brand's own name to corroborate the domain label — `dedup_despace` both sides — in three arms: exact (`cherrykitten` = cherrykitten.com), domain-extends-brand (`d.franklin` ⊂ dfranklincreation.com), brand-extends-domain (`nothosaur toy` ⊃ nothosaur.com), both widened arms requiring ≥4 chars on the shorter side. 110 of 885 brands resolve, covering 33,312 of 48,832 listings; all 38 rows the widened arms add were read by hand and all 38 are the brand's own shop (Mister B's six sub-lines → misterb.com, FETCH → fetchshop.co.uk). The other 775 sell only through third-party shops, have no defensible domain, and are stamped attempted so the batch terminates instead of re-offering the same unresolvable head forever. **logo.dev contributed ZERO, and the reason is NOT the one this file gave until 2026-09-18** — it said logo.dev "knows household brands and this catalogue is independent makers", which was inference from a null, never measured. The token is REJECTED: instrumented on 2026-09-18, a 20-row venue probe came back `unauthorized: 20/20`. `fetchRealLogo` collapsed every non-200 into null, so a dead token, a rate limit and a genuine "not indexed" were the same answer — and because venues and events have NO other source, every row the sweep touched while the token was dead was stamped `logo_fetched_at` and written off permanently (6,498 venues carry that stamp with no logo; an unknown share of them were never really probed). `probeRealLogo` now returns a typed outcome, the run reports a `logodev` tally plus a top-level `logodev_unauthorized`, and the venue/event branch **aborts instead of stamping** — absence of evidence must not be recorded as evidence of absence. **The key was renewed the same day and the corrected measurement inverts the original claim**: re-queueing the 39 `not_found` brands returned **38 logos, all 38 from logo.dev**, and a fresh 20-venue probe went from 0/20 to **10 found / 10 not-indexed**. logo.dev covers this catalogue well; it was never a coverage problem. Note the key must be the **publishable** `pk_` one — the `img.logo.dev` endpoint takes that, not the `sk_` Brand Search key. Brand logos went 80 → **118 of 885 (71.5% of listings)**, and the only remaining gap is the 766 brands with no defensible domain, which is a DOMAIN problem, not a logo-source one. **RESOLVED — and this sentence claimed the opposite until 2026-08-30.** It read *"Still open: 6,498 venues stamped `logo_fetched_at` with no logo … a re-queue is worth roughly 3,200 venue logos … a product decision, not a cleanup."* The re-queue has since happened: venue logos went from the 1,340 quoted earlier in this entry to **6,530**, and the stamped-without-logo cohort is down to **3,467**. **Those 3,467 are NOT the dead-token cohort and must not be re-queued** — every day of the sweep window 2026-08-23 → 08-28 shows a **55-84% hit rate** with successes and failures interleaved, which is the signature of a WORKING token; a dead one gives 0%, which is exactly how the original fault was diagnosed (`unauthorized: 20/20`). They are genuine "not indexed at logo.dev and no usable site icon" answers, and re-probing them spends API calls to re-derive the same null. The real remaining headroom is the **16,866 venues never probed at all**, which the nightly sweep is working through — throughput, not a decision. Re-measure with a `group by date_trunc('day', logo_fetched_at)` hit-rate before believing any future claim here, including this one. The second source, which carried the whole sweep before the key was fixed and still wins for 80 of the 118, is `_shared/site-icon.ts`, which reads the shop's own HTML: JSON-LD `Organization.logo` > the header's logo `<img>` > `apple-touch-icon` > `rel=icon`. **The Shopify arm is what makes it work and it inverts the obvious rule:** Shopify emits `rel=icon` pointing at the store's real logo asset with `?width=32&height=32`, where the 32 is a RESIZE REQUEST, not the asset — cherrykitten.com declares a 32px icon whose file is a 256×256 PNG named "Logo1", so rejecting on declared size threw away the best mark on the site. Those urls are re-requested at `width=512` with the crop dropped and the ANSWER is measured (`imageSize` on the returned bytes), because Shopify never upscales past the original and rodeoh.com's asset really is 32px. **Declared size is a claim; pixel dimensions are the evidence.** Two guards earn their place: `og:image` is never a source (on a shop it is a hero or product photo, which in a 56px plate reads as a corrupted logo), and the host check runs against the url we ASKED for, not where we landed — cellblock13.net 301s to timoteo.net and declares Timoteo's logo, which judged by the final url would publish a sibling brand's wordmark as CellBlock 13's. White/inverted variants are demoted (not dropped) because **the plate is paper in BOTH themes**: `--color-logo-plate` is a literal in `@theme`, not `var(--card)`, since a merchant logo is a transparent PNG drawn for a light ground and on a dark card most of the catalogue would render as nothing. Demotion is not enough on its own: **9 of the 118 mirrored marks contain no dark pixel at all** (7 of the first 80, then 2 more that logo.dev returned — detected with no intervention, which is the point of measuring rather than listing) (Automic Gold ×2, Good Boy Underwear, Jeuf, Nattaup, Provocateur, SUPAWEAR) — the site's only logo IS the white one — and those render as an empty square. **No CSS treatment fixes this**; all three were built and compared side by side, and a blurred edge barely rescues a white mark, a hard 1px outline rescues it but fattens every thin dark wordmark into a smear (Vilain Garçon, gc2b), and an ink plate is perfect for white marks and erases every dark one. So polarity is MEASURED per row: `_shared/png-luminance.ts` decodes the bytes at mirror time and stores `marketplace_brands.logo_on_ink` in the same UPDATE as the url it describes. The metric is the **fraction of visible pixels that are dark**, not mean luminance — a dark mark on its own white field (RUFSKIN) has a high mean and is perfectly legible. The 2% floor sits in a measured gap: the corpus is bimodal with nothing between 0.000 and 0.027. **The file name is not evidence** — SUPAWEAR's asset is `…-logo-white.png` but Automic Gold's is `Automic_GOLD_logo-_left_align` and is equally invisible. PNG-only is not a gap: the failure needs transparency, JPEG cannot be transparent, and an unreadable image stays on paper because a wrong ink plate erases a dark wordmark completely. **The ink plate must be a mode-independent LITERAL, and `bg-foreground` is the trap** — it reads as "ink" and is ink in light mode, so it passes review and every class-name unit test, but in dark mode `--foreground` IS paper: the plate rendered paper-on-paper and the white mark vanished again, in exactly the mode the fix existed to survive. Caught only on prod (`class` said `bg-foreground`, computed `background-color` said `rgb(250,250,245)`). Hence `--color-logo-plate-ink`, declared in `@theme` alongside `--color-logo-plate` and for the same reason both are literals: polarity belongs to the ARTWORK, not the theme — the same rule that makes `--track-ring` ink in both modes. A test asserts neither plate carries a theme token. Result: 82 brands, **9.3% of brands but 60% of listings** — the directory is ordered by listing count, so the logo-dense rows are the ones a reader meets. `BrandMark` renders the monogram ALWAYS and layers the logo over it, and **also needs an `onError` that hides the img** — Chrome paints its torn-page glyph for a failed image even with `alt=""`, so the monogram underneath is not on its own enough (found live). Driver `scripts/data-quality/backfill-brand-logos.mjs`; `enrich-logos` gained `table:'marketplace_brands'`. **Local dev cannot verify this visually**: a Cloudflare rule on the zone answers `img.queer.guide` with `error code: 1011` for any Referer that is not `https://queer.guide/` — localhost AND pages.dev both 403, so a broken logo plate on `npm run dev` is the WAF, not the data.

**Event Truth Loop (continuous quality, 2026-05-30):** Layered on top of the events ingest DAG — turns each event into a living record that re-verifies itself. `events` gains `trust_score` (composite 0-100, distinct from `quality_score`=completeness), `last_verified_at`, `liveness_status` (live/sold_out/cancelled/postponed/moved_online/dead_link/unknown), `field_provenance` jsonb. Append-only `event_quality_signals` ledger (corroboration/liveness/freshness/engagement/admin_feedback/enrichment/relevance/safety) feeds a nightly **pure-SQL** `run_event_trust_recompute()` (decaying composite: completeness·0.25 + corroboration·0.20 + freshness·0.15 + engagement·0.15 + relevance·0.15 + admin_feedback·0.10, hard-cap 10 on dead/cancelled). Weekly pure-SQL `run_event_coverage_radar()` flags thin major cities → `event_coverage_gaps` with suggested source queries. Three edge functions (gated by Vault secret `event_quality_webhook_secret` / `EVENT_QUALITY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` env, hybrid-by-confidence: certain→auto-apply, ambiguous→`needs_attention`): `event-liveness-checker` (daily; HEAD/GET ticket_url+website, JSON-LD `eventStatus`/`availability`), `event-corroboration` (daily; multi-source field fusion across `event_sources.payload.normalized` → `field_provenance`, flags only title conflicts), `event-agentic-enrich` (hourly, circuit-broken `llm.openai.agentic-enrich` + daily cap; grounds Claude/OpenAI extraction in the event's own source page → accessibility/target_groups/age/safety moat fields via `researchEnrichEventFromPage`). Both SQL jobs registered in `admin_automations`; all five crons active. Admin surface: trust + liveness columns and an `EventQualityPanel` (coverage gaps + counts) on `/admin/events`.

**Event field backfill + vocabularies (2026-08-01):** The 40k-row `events` corpus (99% past, 36.5k scraped from the Wayback Machine — `event_sources.payload.metadata.from_wayback`) had almost every optional field empty. **The derivation logic already existed as triggers and had simply never fired on legacy rows**: `trg_events_geo_derive` (→ `derive_entity_geo_address('full')`) resolves country text → `country_id`, `city_id` → `country_id`/`city`/`country`, `cities.region_name` → `state`; `trg_events_set_currency` (→ `set_event_currency_from_country()`) fixes `currency`. The one gap is city *text* → `city_id`, so writing `city_id` is the keystone that cascades into four other columns. Two batched runners (migration `20260801135216`, crons `event_city_link` `5 3 * * *` / `event_geo_fill` `10 3 * * *`, both in `admin_automations`): `run_event_city_link` matches `(country_id, lower(cities.name)=lower(events.city))`; `run_event_geo_fill` adds city-centroid coords (stamped `field_provenance.latitude.source='derived:city_centroid'`) + `cities.timezone`, falling back to `countries.timezone` **only for single-timezone countries** (`countries.timezone` for US is `America/New_York`, so a blanket fallback would mislabel every LA event). Both stamp `enrichment_status` so unresolvable rows are tried once, not nightly. **Batch cap is load-bearing** — 6,000 rows trips the statement timeout inside `search_documents_index_events`, and a timeout is a full rollback. A third runner `run_event_timezone_fill` (migration `20260801180253`, cron `event_timezone_fill` `15 3 * * *`) covers what the city-linked path cannot: **nearest timezone-bearing city in the same country within 250 km**. That beats any state→timezone table because zone boundaries follow geography and it self-corrects for split states (TX, FL, IN, KY, MI, TN, OR, ID…). Validated against the 35,332 events whose timezone was already known: **99.71% agreement within 250 km vs 96.79% beyond**, hence the cap — uncapped it reached 2,200 km from Anchorage to Bellingham WA (no Alaskan city in `cities` carries a timezone) and would have labelled it `America/Los_Angeles`. It only fills NULLs, never overwrites; the validation incidentally showed some STORED values are wrong (Portland, Oregon events stamped `America/New_York`). Result: `city_id` 27,618→634 null, `country_id` 21,682→5, `state` 39,384→2,206, coords 36,408→659, timezone 7,058→570, currency 3,615 wrong→0, tags 29,835→4,727 untagged. **`events.country` mixes ISO2 country codes with US state codes** (`AZ`/Sedona resolved to Azerbaijan, `KY`/Murray to Cayman Islands; `CA` is genuinely split Canada/California) — repaired in `20260801142627`, which pins Whistler/Victoria/Quebec City to Canada by name because `Victoria` exists in `cities` only as Victoria, Seychelles. Vocabularies: `age_restriction` 60 spellings → `<n>+`/`all-ages` via `normalize_age_restriction` (supervision caveats resolve to all-ages **only when no explicit minimum is stated**); `accessibility_attributes` 54 values → `public.amenities` kind=`accessibility` via `normalize_event_accessibility`, **negative assertions (`not-wheelchair-accessible`, `not-step-free`, `no-accessible-restroom`) are first-class vocabulary and are never collapsed into a positive claim**; `target_groups` ~280 free-text values → 30 queer-first slugs via table-driven `normalize_event_target_groups` (extend `target_groups.aliases`, no migration needed). Both `age_restriction` and `target_groups` are exact-match filters (`useEvents.tsx`) and `target_groups` is a live search facet, so the free text was breaking them functionally. `MOAT_SYSTEM_PROMPT` in `_shared/ai-enrichment.ts` is now constrained to the closed lists so the mess cannot regrow.

**Same-name city collisions — `cities` cannot represent them (2026-08-02):** `public.cities` holds at most **one row per (name, country)**, so a corpus containing Charleston SC *and* Charleston IL can only ever match one. An "exactly one match" test therefore proves **nothing** — an unrepresentable twin looks identical to a genuinely unambiguous name. Measuring "0 ambiguous matches" was evidence the table cannot express the distinction, not evidence of safety, and the first `run_event_city_link` pass silently attached **116 events** to the wrong city (Portland ME → Portland OR, Charleston SC → Charleston IL, Springfield MO → Springfield VT, Columbia SC → Columbia MO, Wilmington NC → Wilmington DE) and then stamped each with that city's centroid coords, `state` and timezone. Ground truth for the audit is the gaycities metro slug (`event_sources.payload.metadata.gaycities_subdomain`), which spells out the state for exactly the ambiguous names (`charlestonsc`, `springfieldmo`, `portland-maine`). Migration `20260802090844` adds two guards to `run_event_city_link` — (A) `events.state` vs `cities.region_name`, (B) the metro slug's state suffix — and **blocks rather than guesses**, stamping `enrichment_status.event_city_link.blocked` + `needs_attention`: a null `city_id` is recoverable, a wrong one is not. The 122 affected rows (116 from the backfill + 6 pre-existing) are unlinked and flagged; the correct cities **do not exist in `cities` at all**, which is what made the collision invisible, so they stay unlinked until real rows exist. **`run_event_city_link` is not the only writer of `events.city_id`** — `geo-link-content` (hourly cron) resolves by name and falls back to `candidates[0]` sorted by *population descending*, i.e. it actively prefers the bigger same-name city; it re-linked a quarantined Portland ME event within the hour. The same defect class hit the venue linker: migration `20260802100455` filters events whose `venue_name` is really a city or queer-village name ("San Francisco", "Puerto Vallarta", "East Village"), which `venues` also contains — 15 of 65 `name_exact` matches were place collisions, a 23% error rate on the branch `link_event_venues` auto-applies with no review. **Rule: never resolve an entity by name alone when the reference table cannot represent the ambiguity — require a second, independent signal, and block when it disagrees.** **The news path had the same hole (guard C, 2026-08-02):** 13 of the 132 articles linked to Portland, Oregon named Maine. An article has neither a `state` column nor a metro slug, so the corroborating signal has to come from its own prose. **The writer was not the one it looked like** — 9 of the 13 never mention "Portland" in title or excerpt, so `extractGeoFromText` (which reads only those two fields) could not have produced them; all 13 sat in `news_articles.city_ids`, which only the commit path writes, and the real source was `resolveEntities` in `_shared/news-quality/entity-link.ts`, whose `passesDisambiguationGuard` returns ok immediately for every non-country type — cities had **no** guard at all. Both writers are now guarded via `proseStateContradiction()`. **The obvious rule was measured and rejected:** "any US state in the text that disagrees with `cities.region_name` blocks" fires on **873 of 9,538** US city links and is overwhelmingly correct links that merely mention another state in passing (a Seattle article referencing Indiana, a SCOTUS roundup listing five states) — it would delete ~9% of a working topical index to fix 13 rows. What separates the 13 from the 873 is that **Portland is an ambiguous name and Seattle is not**, so the burden of proof is inverted for a curated ambiguous-name set only, bringing it to **53 of 9,538 (0.55%)**. Two artifacts are load-bearing: a state whose name is inside the city's own name must be ignored (else "Kansas City, Missouri" reads every mention of Kansas as a contradiction — the largest false-positive group), and **`Washington` is excluded from the state vocabulary entirely** (state + city + everyday name for DC at once, so it false-blocked Arlington, Virginia). Bare 2-letter codes are never matched in prose — "ME", "OR", "IN", "OK" are ordinary English words — and demonyms ("Mainers") are deliberately not matched either. Cleanup `20260809163000` cleared the 53 rows from `news_article_cities` **and** `news_articles.city_ids`; clearing only the join table would let them resurrect, because the commit path unions the array (`city_ids || v_city_ids`) on every update.

**The same-name sweep found TWELVE, and the worst-looking row needed the opposite fix (2026-09-20, `99991789886174`):** `99970901120000` (#3791) repaired two of these events and `99991789843667` (#3828) a third, each found by following ONE row. Sweeping mechanically instead — every live event over 250 km from the city it is linked to — returns **twelve**, every one the same `events.city = cities.name` name-only shape. **The brief for this pass named one of them (Salisbury) and called it the last; it ranks NINTH by distance**, and four rows are between two and seven thousand kilometres out. None carries a prior `event_city_link` stamp, so none is a producer re-break — they are originals no pass had reached. **THE REMEDY IS NOT THE SAME FOR ALL TWELVE AND THE DISTANCE DOES NOT DECIDE IT.** Sorting by distance and applying one fix would have damaged two rows, both caught by reading each event's own source payload rather than trusting the sweep's framing. **`Girls in Wonderland` is 8,526 km out, the worst row in the sweep, and its city link is CORRECT**: its own description reads *"in St. Pete Beach, Florida"*, its source is `stpetersburg.gaycities.com`, and the coordinates — 59.94/30.30, **Saint Petersburg, RUSSIA** — came verbatim from the scraped payload's `lat`/`lng`. Unlinking it, which its distance argues for, detaches a correctly-placed Florida event; creating a Russian city row and moving it there, **which is what the brief proposed**, publishes a Florida pride event as Russian. So the three groups are **block** (8 — same country, and `cities` holds at most one row per (name, country), so Portland MAINE, Lakewood OHIO, Arlington TEXAS, Salisbury MASSACHUSETTS, Sanford FLORIDA and Milton DELAWARE are unrepresentable exactly as College Park, Georgia was), **relink** (2 — the collision crosses a border AND the correct row already exists, Birmingham AL `Q79867` at 2.03 km and Cambridge MA `Q49111` at 1.04 km, so nothing is created), and **retract the contradicted derivation, keep the link** (2). The third remedy the pass was scoped for — create a city row — is written by nobody: **its only candidate dissolved on the evidence.** Group A requires BOTH signals (the `state` text AND coordinates within 25 km of the place it names) because `state` alone is near-random on this corpus, the measurement `99991789843667` recorded. **A NEW CLASS, and it is wider than this one row: a merge moves the LINK and leaves the DERIVED GEOGRAPHY pointing at the dead row.** `PIANA PARTY / FOAM PARTY` is linked to the real Łódź, Poland and that link is right; its source payload carries `lat: null, lng: null`, so its coordinates are not scraped at all — they are `50.743110 / 25.318991`, byte-identical to the centroid of a **second Łódź row (slug `od`, region "Volyn Oblast", UKRAINE)** since merged away, and its timezone is `Europe/Kiev` while its city's is `Europe/Warsaw`. Measured corpus-wide, **4,083 events carry coordinates identical to a merged-away city's centroid** — most benignly, because the dead row and its survivor are the same place (the Berlin `tmp-` shell), but the cohort where the dead row was a DIFFERENT place is unexamined and is not sized here. Coordinates are **retracted, never replaced with a centroid** (prefer NULL to a guess) and the retracted value is stamped on the row itself, so a retraction that records nothing is a deletion. **Two guard lessons, both from this file's own mutation run.** (1) **A whole mutation round was invalidated by a flag**: `vitest run --environment node` broke `src/test/setup.ts` (`window is not defined`), so all 16 "caught" results were a module-load error rather than the guard firing — **indistinguishable from a real catch**, and the only thing that surfaced it was the comment-only CONTROL being reported as caught too. A control that must SURVIVE is not a formality; it is the detector for a harness that has stopped testing. (2) **Anchoring a scoped assertion on a `-- Pn MIRROR:` comment label yields an EMPTY slice** when the source is comment-stripped, and every assertion scoped to it then passes against a deleted check — two tests were written that way and failed loudly, a third passed vacuously. Anchor on code (`into v_bad`), and note `lastIndexOf('select ')` finds the innermost SUBQUERY, not the outer one. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/geoNamesakeEventCityLinks.test.ts` (18 assertions, mutation-tested 17/17 with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES) and by `e2e/geo-namesake-city-links.spec.ts`, whose corpus-wide form deliberately stays in the migration's postcondition P4 — **anon cannot read safety-gated events, so a sweep from that role checks a subset and reports it as the whole.**

**The invariant came back in four days, and the producer was not the one I had flagged (2026-09-25, `99991790379818`):** `99991789886174` asserted zero events over 250 km from their city as a postcondition and passed. Four days later the count was **1**. The entry above named `geo-link-content` as the unsealed risk; **that guess was wrong and reading it is what settled it** — `resolveCity` returns a row only when the name is globally unambiguous, and "Fort Lauderdale" resolves to exactly one row, so it could not have produced this. **`content_revisions` named the writer exactly**: a `system` UPDATE on 2026-09-24 17:37 touching `city_id`, `geo_linked_at` and `field_provenance` — the signature of **`tg_event_venue_geography`**, a BEFORE trigger added *that same day* by `20260924043359`, which did `new.city_id := v.city_id` **unconditionally**. **Use the revision trail to name a writer; do not reason from which producer you already suspect.** **THE TRIGGER IS ONLY THE SECOND LINK, and the first one is the older defect class.** "Harte Arbeit Fort Lauderdale" sits at 26.1224/-80.1373 with coordinates byte-identical to the Fort Lauderdale centroid, so it was linked correctly once — and it carries `venue_id` for **"The Eagle" at 40.7517/-74.0043, New York City**. "The Eagle" is a bar name in a dozen cities, so the event↔venue link is itself the name collision `20260802100455` documents for `link_event_venues`; the trigger then faithfully propagated that venue's city 1,718 km. **So the fix is a corroboration test, not a ban** — a venue is the strongest available signal for an event's city and **1,669 of 1,670 live links are correct**. **THE BOUND IS MEASURED AND THE DISTRIBUTION IS WHY IT IS SAFE**: p50 0.0 km, p95 3.4, p99 6.1, **second-largest 23.1 against a largest of 1,718.1** — bimodal with nothing in between, so 250 km sits in the gap with 10.9× headroom over the largest legitimate value. It **fails open** when either side lacks coordinates (1 event corpus-wide), because absence of evidence is not disagreement. **On a refusal the WHOLE venue-derived block is skipped, not just the city** — a venue 1,718 km away is not this event's venue, so its country and country code are equally suspect — and **`geo_linked_at` is deliberately left unset so the row does not read as successfully linked**. **The repair detaches the wrong venue and does NOT attach the likely right one:** "Eagle Wilton Manors" sits 4 km away and shares a word, which is exactly the evidence that produced the defect, so it is NAMED in the stamp for a human and `venue_name` text is kept as the only readable record of what to re-attach; the city is restored from the event's **own coordinates at 0.0 km**, which needs no name matching at all. **Two things worth carrying forward.** (1) **A BEFORE trigger on `UPDATE OF city_id` can silently undo a repair** — measured, 0 of the previous pass's twelve carry a `venue_id`, so they were safe, but that was luck rather than design and P7 now asserts it. (2) The guard was proven **behaviourally on prod in a rolled-back transaction**, not only by asserting its source text: handed the New York venue it stamps `venue_too_far_from_event_coordinates` with `distance_km: 1718` and propagates nothing; handed the Wilton Manors venue 4 km away it links normally. **A source-text postcondition cannot tell a live guard from a dead one.** **THE MERGE ITSELF COST MORE THAN THE FIX, AND THE NUMBERS ARE WORTH KEEPING (2026-09-26).** Shipping this two-file change took five CI cycles, and not once for its own content. **(1) `apply_migration` without the follow-up commit reds EVERY PR in the repo**, because `check-migration-versions.mjs` fails on any applied version with no repo file — measured, orphans appeared at roughly one per 30-60 minutes across the session, from at least five different sessions, and each one turns a TypeScript-only PR red. **(2) The automated recovery LIVELOCKS when it produces partial PRs.** The drift gate is all-or-nothing; #3941, #3942 and #3943 each carried the *identical 3 files* while 9 objects were orphaned, so every one failed its own drift check, stayed `BLOCKED`, and the automation regenerated the same partial PR on its next tick. **One PR carrying all of them is the only shape that can clear an all-or-nothing gate** (#3945). **(3) Check whether an orphan is already on a branch before recovering it** — `20260926001748` was sitting on `fix/event-quality-operations-durable` (PR #3949), so recovering it would have created a duplicate file conflicting with its author's PR; the right move there is to wait, and the two PRs then block each other until one lands. **(4) Merging a recovery RAISES THE CEILING PAST YOUR OWN PENDING MIGRATION.** `99991789843667`-style renumbering is not a one-off: this file's own version had to move after its author merged the orphan recovery, because the recovered objects took higher versions. The version is bound in **three** places — filename, the test's `MIGRATION` constant, and the `migration:<version>` stamp written into `enrichment_status` — and the way to prove all three moved is to re-dry-run and read the **live stamp**, not to trust the rename. **(5) Strict branch protection plus a moving main is a treadmill with measured odds:** main's median gap between merges is **45.8 min** and its minimum is **14.7 min**, against a required-check suite of **~16 min**, so a PR that goes green can be `BEHIND` before it can be merged, and every lift re-runs all 12 checks. `gh pr merge --auto` is the only way to win it reliably — but a lift DISARMS auto-merge, so it must be re-armed after every lift, and a check that has already FAILED does not re-run on its own, so clearing the upstream cause still needs a fresh push. None of this is a code problem; it is the coordination problem CLAUDE.md already names, with the cost now measured rather than asserted. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/eventVenueGeographyCorroboration.test.ts` (16 assertions, mutation-tested **19/19** with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES) and by two new cases in `e2e/geo-namesake-city-links.spec.ts`, the second of which pages PostgREST's 1000-row cap to check the invariant corpus-wide through the ANON role.

**Venue categories — ONE vocabulary, source beats name (2026-08-22, PRs #2927 #2934 #2937):** `venues.category` has **17 values**, governed by exactly two artifacts: the DB CHECK `venues_category_check` and `src/lib/venueCategories.ts` (drift-tested — the test parses the migration carrying the LATEST CHECK definition, currently `20260915140000`; a new ALTER of the constraint must update that test's filename reference or it fails). **`organization` was REMOVED from the vocabulary** — "is this a venue at all" is answered by the `nonvenue_candidate` flow + the `organizations` table, never by a category value; the 3 rows carrying it were misfiled LA lighting companies, soft-archived. The fourth vocabulary table `public.venue_categories` is **DROPPED** (`20260915120100`; contents preserved in that migration's header) — do not recreate it; the CMS/vocab-dedup/crosswalk surfaces were rebuilt without it. **Classification rule: source beats name.** `run_venue_category_reclassify` (nightly `35 3 * * *`, batch-capped 300 — search-trigger discipline) resolves a sole-source `refuge-restrooms` row to `toilet` @ 1.0 BEFORE any name inference; the name-inference tier auto-applies only bar/sauna/community_center (≥85% measured agreement), everything else lands as a review suggestion. That rule exists because inference on refuge-restrooms read the BUILDING name and produced 167 cafés/bars/shops out of public toilets — including a hair salon labelled `sauna`, which on this platform asserts a sexual venue type. The 2026-08 triage of the whole backlog (7,400 unexamined + 2,209 suggestions + 197 nonvenue candidates) emptied both review queues: auto-accept only where an independent signal corroborated (provider tags, hotels-table membership, nude-places subtype); **`club` suggestions were 60% NOT clubs** (parties/festivals/strip clubs/sports teams) and cruising/sauna are never bulk-accepted. `venue_subtype` is now a single slug vocabulary (`nude-beach`/`naturist-resort`/`hot-spring`/`bnb`, NULL for none) — no ingest path writes it; `infer_venue_category` matches both slug and label forms. **`venues.accommodation_type` is an ingest/dedup SIGNAL only, never render it** — `pipeline-deduplicate`'s hotel branch keys on it, so do NOT drop the column; the canonical accommodation vocabulary lives on `hotels.hotel_type` (both column comments say so in the DB). Remaining honest state: ~9.5k venues are `other` (4.5k with measured no-signal — enrichment or the nonvenue split are the only ways forward, not more inference), and the review-queue counts on `/admin` are live again.

**Venue Truth Engine (consensus enrichment, 2026-05-30):** The `venue-ingestion-unified` DAG (daily cron `pipeline-venue-ingestion`, `0 3 * * *`, 7 sources) gained a `pipeline-consensus-merge` node between `dedupe` and `quality`. It groups staging rows that dedup linked to the same existing venue (`dedup_match_id`), votes each field across sources + the venue's current value (`source='existing'`), and writes per-field provenance + confidence. Logic is pure + unit-tested in `_shared/venue-consensus.ts` (source trust weights, noisy-OR confidence, comparator per field kind: identity/url/phone/email/coords/text/number/array). Agreement ≥2 sources → high confidence → auto-commit; conflict on a HIGH-RISK field (name/lat/lng/category) → `review_status='pending_review'` → existing triage. Per-(venue,field,source) candidates in `venue_field_provenance` (`is_winning` flag); merge decisions audited in `venue_consensus_audit`. **`venue_consensus_audit` is a conflict/triage/closure log, not a decision log** (`pipeline-consensus-merge/index.ts` only inserts an audit row when a field has disagreeing sources, is gated to triage, or a closure signal fires — a unanimous field never gets one) — measured 2026-08-20: 6 audit rows ever (3 venues) vs. 1,082 `venue_field_provenance` rows across 1,057 distinct venues, written continuously across 55 days including the same day measured. The engine is running correctly on every cron pass; **`venue_field_provenance` distinct-venue count is the coverage signal, `venue_consensus_audit` row count is not** — a near-empty audit table is expected when dedup match groups are mostly single-member (one re-fetched source vs. the existing row), since a lone new source filling a previously-null field is agreement, not conflict. Root constraint on coverage (4.5% of live venues) is upstream: only rows that dedup links to an existing venue via `dedup_match_id` enter the grouped path at all, and only ~4.5% of venue staging rows ever get one — most ingest volume is net-new venues, not re-corroboration of known ones. Closure is a voted signal (Google `business_status`, `url_status` 404/410, `permanently_closed`): ≥2 signals auto-set `venues.closed_at`, 1 signal sets `needs_attention`. Every consensus pass stamps `venues.last_refreshed_at`; selector RPC `venues_due_for_refresh(limit)` ranks never-refreshed > broken-url > low-quality > stale. Admin sees sources + per-field confidence + closure flags in the triage detail panel (`src/components/admin/triage/TriageDetailPanel.tsx`). Follow-ups (deferred): free `source-wikidata-venue` to add a cheap voter; per-venue targeted detail re-fetch driven by `venues_due_for_refresh`.

**`detect-stale-venues` threshold fix never took effect — fixed 2026-08-20:** `20260806100000_revive_dead_scoring_crons.sql` moved the venue staleness threshold 60d→180d (60d flags 94% of venues, making `needs_attention` meaningless) and issued `cron.schedule('detect-stale-venues', ..., 'run_detect_stale_venues(180, 1500)')` to repoint it. **The reschedule never took** — both `admin_automations.action.command` (slug `detect_stale_venues`) and the live `cron.job` kept calling the old `detect_stale_venues(60, false)`, verified live on 2026-08-20. Since `run_detect_stale_venues` only ever SETS `needs_attention=true` and never clears it, every night since kept adding more of the 60-day-stale-but-not-180-day-stale majority to the flag, driving it to **99.5% of live venues (23,217/23,335)** — indistinguishable from no signal at all. `20260820191944_fix_detect_stale_venues_drift.sql` repoints both the registry and the live cron, and adds `run_needs_attention_recompute(p_batch)` (batched — `trg_search_documents_venue` fires unscoped on every venue UPDATE) to one-time-clear the flag wherever the ONLY reason it was set no longer holds, keeping it wherever a real signal still does: missing coords, `url_status='broken'`, `closed_at`, `enrichment_status->'category_backfill'->>'status'='review'`, `enrichment_status ? 'nonvenue_candidate'`, an open `entity_review_queue` row, or genuine 180-day staleness. Result: 99.5% → **42.7%** (10,046 flagged), cross-tabbed against those same known writers to confirm the remainder is real (missing coords alone accounts for 6,872 of it). **This is the same failure class as the Wolfram cron retirement above** (`sync_automations_to_cron()` only creates a MISSING cron job from the registry command — it does not correct a command drift on an already-existing job) — a `cron.schedule()` call inside a migration is not itself durable against a later reconciler pass, or in this case, apparently didn't even apply cleanly the first time. Anything that "fixes" a threshold/behavior by rescheduling a cron inside a migration should be verified live afterward (`select command from cron.job where jobname=...`), not assumed from the migration file.

**Queer villages (Stadtteile) — three faults, one cleanup (2026-08-24):** `queer_villages` is the ONLY district entity (spine mirror `geo_places.place_type='village'`); 190 rows over 104 of 5,762 cities. (1) **14 rows were the whole city, not a district** — same name as their parent city (West Hollywood, Palm Springs, Provincetown, Wilton Manors, Sitges, Rehoboth Beach, Ogunquit, Saugatuck, Guerneville, Asbury Park, Hudson, New Hope, Northampton, Pine City), gay resort towns imported as a neighbourhood of themselves, each publishing `/villages/x` alongside `/city/x` about the same place and holding 123 venues + 14 hotels. Hard-merged by `merge_village_into_city` (`20260928100000`), which exists because `_queer_village_merge_core` is village→village only and because the FK topology makes order load-bearing: `venues`/`events`/`hotels`.`queer_village_id` reference `geo_village_profiles(place_id)` with **no `ON DELETE`**, so a DELETE before unlinking simply errors. **`village_slug_redirects` cannot carry this redirect** — it cascades away with the spine row and only ever resolves to another `/villages/:slug`; the 301s are 14 STATIC rules in `public/_redirects` (the `/cities/:slug` pattern, verified live) plus `src/lib/mergedVillageRedirects.ts` for the `/:lang/` paths and in-app navigation, kept in step by a test that reads both. `public.redirects` is NOT the place — it is the `/go/:slug` shortlink store (0 rows). **The one real loss is named, not hidden:** `cities` has no `history` column and all 14 villages carried 205–1,375 chars of queer local history, so `village_city_merge_audit.village_row` is its only surviving copy (rendering it needs a `cities.queer_history` column — a separate decision). (2) **60 ghost villages were indexable** — `run_village_trust_recompute` routed `venues=0 AND events=0` to `shell_status='ghost'` behind `seo_indexable AND is_empty` and never WROTE the column, so the gate could only ever be true; exactly the cities bug of `20260821051221`, one entity later, fixed the same three ways in `20260928100100`. `sitemap-villages.xml` already excluded ghosts, but `CityDistricts` links every village from its city page — and `villageDetail` in `functions/_lib/detail.ts` never selected `seo_indexable` at all, so the bot response was unconditionally indexable regardless of the column (same hole `personalityDetail` once had). (3) **The relink engine was never scheduled** — `run_village_relink_batch`/`relink_village_venues` shipped with the Village Truth Engine but sat in NO `cron.job` and NO `admin_automations` row, which is why 58 villages had zero venues and **21 of 47,815 events** carried a village at all. The 60 ghosts were therefore mostly real districts (Shinjuku Ni-chōme, Cherry Grove, Fire Island Pines, Green Point, Jackson Heights) that nothing pointed at, not junk. Cron `village_relink` (`20 3 * * *`, before completeness 03:35 and trust 03:50, so a village that gains venues is rescored and re-indexed the same night) wraps the existing batch in the sibling jobs' run bookkeeping. **Radius 800 m is a measured choice**: `queer_villages.geometry` is NULL on all 190 rows, so a village is a POINT and the radius IS the district; dry run 800 m → 370 venues / 84 villages / 11 of 58 empties lifted, 1200 m → 1,161 / 24, 2000 m → 2,337 / 30, and the wider radii buy reach by pulling neighbouring districts in, which nearest-village cannot correct in a one-village city. ~28 villages find nothing even at 2 km (Heera Mandi/Lahore, Sakurazaka/Naha) and stay correctly ghost + deindexed. Events are NOT relinked — `run_village_relink_batch` handles `venues` only.

**City Truth Engine (content quality, 2026-06-07):** Cities gained the same self-maintaining quality layer as events/venues (they had none; ~90% had no description, ~0% had queer-specific fields). `cities` gains `trust_score`, `completeness_score` (queer-weighted: queer_content 0.30 + description 0.20 + travel 0.15 + geo 0.15 + image 0.10 + basics 0.07 + trivia 0.03), `shell_status` (real/placeholder/ghost/merged), `needs_attention`, `field_provenance`, `enrichment_status`, `safety_notes`, `last_verified_at`. Pure-SQL nightly jobs `run_city_completeness_recompute` + `run_city_trust_recompute` (caps: placeholder ≤5, ghost ≤15; freshness decays over 90d; `IS DISTINCT FROM` guard) and weekly `run_city_coverage_radar` (scans EVERY non-dup city → `city_coverage_gaps` with missing_fields + content_counts + resolution enrich/merge/review) — all registered+enabled in `admin_automations` + pg_cron. `city_quality_signals` ledger (daily-pruned), `cities_due_for_refresh(limit)` work-list selector. Three cron edge functions gated by Vault secret `city_quality_webhook_secret` / env `CITY_QUALITY_WEBHOOK_SECRET`: `city-factual-backfill` (Wikipedia full-text extract + Wikidata claims + OSM, fills EMPTY columns only, records per-source candidates), `city-corroboration` (fuses candidates → `field_provenance`, flags coord conflicts), `city-agentic-enrich` (grounded queer LLM moat via `researchEnrichCityFromSources` in `_shared/ai-enrichment.ts` — note `chatCompletion` defaults to CF Workers AI llama-70b whose `response`/`content` can be an OBJECT, so coerce before parsing; uses the full-text Wikipedia `extracts` API, not the summary). **Safety gate (load-bearing):** narrative fields (description/best_time/local_customs) auto-fill empty columns at confidence ≥0.8; `lgbt_friendly_rating` (integer 1-5) + `editorial_hook` ALWAYS route to `city_review_queue` with citations and NEVER reach `cities` until an admin approves via `approve_city_review`/`reject_city_review` RPC (atomic, audited in `city_consensus_audit`, source stamped `llm+human`). Rating is only proposed when citation-backed, else null (no fake defaults). `safety_notes` is no longer LLM-generated — see the Safety Notes composer below. Admin surface: `/admin/cities` dashboard (`CityQualityPanel` + review queue with citations + approve/reject); CRUD stays at `/admin/content/cities`.

**City Fields Backfill (2026-08-01):** 13 city columns were at **literally 0%** because nothing had ever written them (`climate_type`, `local_language`, `mayor`, `postal_codes`, `area_codes`, `sister_cities`, `universities`, `economy_sectors`, `cost_of_living`, `transportation_info`, `demographics`, `best_time_to_visit`, `notable_landmarks`), and `city-factual-backfill` had filled **zero fields for 36 days** while visiting ~90 cities/day. **`best_time_to_visit` was never actually wired into this script — re-verified 2026-08-21, `backfill-city-fields.mjs` only names it in a header comment.** It is written by a completely different pipeline, the LLM narrative composer `city-agentic-enrich` (`CITY_MOAT_KEYS`, same call as `description`/`local_customs`), gated "produce if sources support it, else null." Measured across all 209 cities that composer has ever touched: 209/209 came back null for `best_time_to_visit` (local_customs 202/209), vs description filling reliably on the identical call — the model is simply conservative about citing a travel-timing recommendation, which is correct behavior for this codebase's no-fabrication stance, not a bug to route around. The stall was **selector starvation, not an API block**: once `never_refreshed` hit 0, `cities_due_for_refresh` collapsed to "placeholders first, lowest completeness first" and pinned itself to ~545 completeness-2 import shells whose names are region-qualified/German-localized (`Kapstadt, Südafrika`) or not cities (`Indonesien`, `—N/a`) — 2,921 log rows over 545 distinct cities, every miss logged as a bare `skipped` with no reason. **Selector v2** (`20260801133923`, ordering fixed again in `20260801140449`): content-bearing cities first, placeholder tier deleted, terminal sentinels excluded, and **`last_refreshed_at ASC NULLS FIRST` outranks `completeness_score`** — completeness is only recomputed nightly, so ordering by it re-selects a just-enriched city immediately. **`last_refreshed_at` is the selector's round-robin cursor, so it MUST be stamped on every visit** — an earlier "skip the no-op UPDATE" optimisation (to dodge the spine+profile upsert and `search_documents` HNSW churn) made unfillable cities a permanent queue head and dropped the sweep from 36/40 to 0/40 per batch. The real write saving is the terminal sentinel (`enrichment_status.<key>.state='data_unavailable'` after 3 attempts, ported from the countries engine) which removes exhausted rows from the pool; `reset_city_enrichment_state()` clears it. **`cities.wikidata_qid` + `wikipedia_title` are cached** (mirrored onto `geo_city_profiles` AND `sync_geo_spine_city()` — the drift check only compares name/slug/parent_id, so a missing profile column would never alarm); a repeat visit costs one `wbgetentities` instead of five requests, and Wikipedia is fetched **by cached sitelink title, never by `cities.name`**. `_shared/city-name-normalize.ts`: **comma-stripping alone** recovers the exonyms (`wbsearchentities` "Kapstadt" → Q5465 "Cape Town" under `language=en`); `name_en` is filled on 7 of 5,113 rows and is a dead end. **The BARE name must be searched first** — `wbsearchentities` matches labels, so "Buenos Aires, Argentina" returns only Q1537768, an obscure Córdoba town whose description also contains "Argentina", which satisfied the country check and beat the capital Q1486; Buenos Aires was published with that town's mayor. The country-qualified form is a fallback only, and a `relink` body flag re-resolves past the QID cache (otherwise a wrong QID is permanent). **`local_language` uses P37, falling back to P2936 only when it names ≤2 languages** — Wikidata fills P2936 ("language used") with minority/indigenous/historical languages, and of the 63 rows that fallback produced, every 3+-value answer was wrong (Jerusalem "Yevanic, Lishana Deni, Biblical Hebrew"; Delhi "Punjabi, Bauria, Central Tibetan") while every 1-2-value answer was right (Manila → Tagalog). Mexico City is the trap: its P37 is an unknown-value snak, so it fell through despite having an official language. **Staleness is judged against a provenance SNAPSHOT taken before the run** — `addCandidate` rewrites `field_provenance[field]` with the fresh value and runs before the write guard, so reading live provenance compares the NEW value to the OLD column and concludes a human curated it; Buenos Aires kept the wrong mayor through two relink passes while its own provenance already said "Jorge Macri". An array of all-NULLs counts as EMPTY (236 of 326 `airport_codes` rows held exactly `[null]`). A QID collision is kept as a duplicate signal, not discarded — the live collisions are duplicate ROWS for one city ("Athen"/"Athens") whose facts are correct for both. `_shared/wikidata-city.ts` is **rank-aware** — the old parser read `claims[P][0]` (array position), so Cape Town's population was 433,688 instead of the preferred 3,776,313 and Paris 2,145,906 instead of 2,103,778; `applyRankFix` corrects such values only when provenance proves we wrote them and nobody curated them since. Group-A fields (P281 postal, P473 area codes, P190 sister cities, P37→P2936 language, P6 mayor, P2564 Köppen, P452 industry) come free out of the `wbgetentities` call already being made, plus one batched 50-id label call. **`mayor` only publishes a term with no P582 end date** (every P6 statement probed carries one, so it fills rarely — that is correct, not a bug; >40% fill means the filter broke). Group B is WDQS behind its own `wikidata.sparql` breaker: airports via **P931** (`place served by` — an airport is usually outside the city boundary) with `wikibase:sitelinks` as the primary-airport tiebreaker (JFK/LHR/CDG/HND correct, exact ties leave the scalar NULL). **Measured and rejected:** transitive `P131*`+`P279*` = HTTP 500 at 60s; `notable_landmarks` = 42s/12 cities with a notability filter or junk churches without it, so it **stays empty**; Wikidata **P421 must never write `timezone`** (yields "UTC+02:00", the column holds IANA). `cost_of_living` is a coarse band derived from `countries.gdp_per_capita_usd` with an explicit "Country-level estimate, not city-specific" line and **no invented indices**; `demographics` stays empty (no free per-city source). `transportation_info` carries only the airports — both it and `cost_of_living` render EVERY jsonb key as a visible row, so provenance lives in `field_provenance`. Non-places are dispositioned **reversibly** via `archive_city_as_nonplace`/`unarchive_city` → `shell_status='ghost'` (never `duplicate_of_id`, which the 12 merge cores repoint content through), from the human-reviewed `cities_nonplace_candidates` view — **no cron**: Luxembourg, Singapore, Monaco and Panama are real cities named after countries. **`shell_status='ghost'` has two unrelated meanings and that collision is a live trap (found 2026-08-20, fixed same day, migration `20260820192709`):** the City Truth Engine's own `run_city_trust_recompute`/`run_city_completeness_recompute` (20260607100000/110000) route ANY zero-venue/zero-event city to `ghost` too — by design, per that migration's own comment, `'ghost (indexable, zero venues+events)'` — a completely different, still-`seo_indexable=true` tier for thin auto-created stub cities (144 live, avg completeness 19/100, unrelated to archival). Worse: neither recompute function excluded rows carrying the archive disposition marker (`enrichment_status.disposition.state='not_a_city'`), so the very next nightly trust recompute after an admin archival silently flipped `shell_status` back to `placeholder` — reproduced live on "Americas"/"Thebes", archived 2026-08-19, reverted by 2026-08-20 03:45 UTC. `seo_indexable` was untouched (neither function writes it) so there was no live SEO exposure, but `shell_status='ghost'` filters (admin views, `city_coverage_gaps`) silently lost the row. Both recompute functions now carry the same disposition guard `cities_due_for_refresh` already had. **Resolved 2026-08-21 (`20260821051221`):** the zero-content stub cities are now deindexed by design — the classifier no longer requires `seo_indexable=true` to route a row to `ghost` (it routes on `is_empty` alone and sets `seo_indexable=false` itself, restoring it only when a previously-ghost row gains real content). Applying it live reclassified 144→910 `shell_status='ghost'`: the extra 766 were zero-content cities that were already `seo_indexable=false` for unrelated reasons but had been silently mislabeled `shell_status='real'` under the old classifier the whole time — a pre-existing completeness-dashboard accuracy bug this fix incidentally closes too. Net newly-deindexed count is the original ~144. Driver `scripts/data-quality/backfill-city-fields.mjs` (`--phase link|sparql|derived`, via `pg_net` so the webhook secret stays in the DB). Breakers `wikipedia.api`/`wikidata.api`/`osm.nominatim` had **no rows at all** — `checkCircuit` allows by default, so they could never trip; now seeded.

**Non-place city shells — the `personality-birth-place` cohort (2026-08-25):** `cities` held a row **Hessen** — a German Bundesland, population 6,045,425, zero venues/events/hotels/news/villages — and it was **live in `search_documents`**, so a site search for "Hessen" returned a city card. The producer is `data_source='personality-birth-place'`, a path that mints a `cities` row from a personality's birth-place FREE TEXT: **1,832 rows, every one with a `tmp-` slug, and NOT ONE with a `wikidata_qid`** — nothing downstream ever corroborated any of them. **The reason given here until 2026-09-15 was WRONG and is corrected below** — it read "and `cities_due_for_refresh` never selects them because selector v2 deleted the placeholder tier". The selector selects them fine: measured live, `cities_due_for_refresh(1000,'qid_gap')` ranks **671** of this cohort in its top 1000 and **41 of the first 60**. What never reached them was the edge function, which silently rewrote the `qid_gap` scope away — see the scope entry below. 1,456 hold no place content at all. **`seo_indexable=false` does not hide them, and that half is still true**: it governs crawlers and the sitemap, not site search, so this cohort IS searchable on the site. **The second half of the original claim is now STALE and was corrected 2026-09-02** — it said `search_documents_index_cities` "filters only `duplicate_of_id`", so archived rows stayed searchable too; `20261016110000_archived_rows_leave_search` closed that, and the indexer now reads `duplicate_of_id is null AND coalesce(shell_status::text,'real') not in ('ghost','merged')`. Measured on prod, the split is sharp: **ghost 1,069 rows / 0 in search; placeholder 1,952 / 1,952; real 2,468 / 2,468.** So ARCHIVING a shell genuinely removes it from search — which is what makes `archive_city_as_nonplace` a fix rather than a flag — while an un-archived `placeholder` is fully searchable. This cohort is `placeholder`, so it remains the live problem; the archived rows no longer are. The cohort is **not homogeneous**, which is why a predicate cannot clean it: non-places (Bundesländer, US/BR/MX states, prefectures, counties, countries in German — `Russland`, `Frankreich`, `USA` —, the continent `Americas` at population 1,035,298,985, historic polities), **exonym/qualifier duplicates of real cities** (`Kapstadt`, `Teheran`, `Singapur`, `Brasília, Distrito Federal`, `Vienna, Austria` filed under `country_id=DE`), and a majority of correct small towns. **The DB's own reference lists cannot classify this** — `countries.name` + `regions.name` + `cities.region_name` + a suffix regex matches 44 of 1,456: `Hessen` misses because no German city carries `region_name='Hessen'`, `Russland`/`Singapur` miss because `countries.name` is English. `scripts/data-quality/classify-nonplace-cities.mjs` ranks against dr5hn's ISO-3166-2 subdivisions (with `native` + `translations`, the arm that catches Hessen), mledoze's multilingual country names, and — decisively — dr5hn's `countries+states+cities.json` gazetteer as a **demotion** signal: a candidate is dropped back out when a city of that name exists in that country (`Babylon` is an Iraqi governorate and a Long Island town), or when the matched subdivision **contains a city bearing the subdivision's own name** (Tehran province holds Tehran, Piacenza province Piacenza, every Thai province its capital, every Puerto Rican municipio its pueblo). That structural test is what makes the German exonyms tractable — `Teheran` matches no English gazetteer entry, but the province it resolves to is provably named after a city inside it. **A city-state is a city**: `Singapur` and `Luxemburg` match the country arm and would have been deleted, so a bridge resolves the name to an ISO2 and asks whether that country holds a city named after itself in any language (canonical `Singapore` carries 77 pieces of content, `Luxembourg` 15, `Hong Kong` 83). Arms **rank**; a human decides, and the nine rows taken back out — `Bursa`/`Malatya`/`Yalova` (real Turkish cities whose provinces are named after them, and dr5hn's TR city lists omit the central district so the gazetteer could not demote them), `Tucumán`, `Kohtla-Järve` (a real Estonian city under `country_id=RU`, a wrong-country row not a non-place), the London boroughs, `Thurrock`, `Saint Thomas` — are recorded with reasons in `scripts/data-quality/out/nonplace-city-review.json`. **57 rows deleted** by `20261001120000`, the **first hard DELETE on `cities` in this repo's history** (standing convention is the reversible `archive_city_as_nonplace`), so `nonplace_city_deletion_audit` carries a full jsonb snapshot of every deleted row and is the only way back. **`cities.id` has exactly TWO foreign keys left** — `cities.duplicate_of_id` and `queer_villages.city_id`; the ~26 FK columns in `00000000000000_baseline.sql` were dropped by the Geo P2 flips (`20260726114743`–`20260726115926`), so **read `pg_constraint`, never the baseline file**. A DELETE therefore neither cascades nor errors: it silently leaves dangling uuids.
