Measured and cleared by hand — `city_quality_signals` 494, `personalities.city_id` 88, `content_embeddings` 57, `city_coverage_gaps` 57, `image_asset_links` 20, `city_review_queue_legacy` 17, `personalities.death_city_id` 2 — while `geo_city_profiles` genuinely CASCADEs off `geo_places` and `entity_review_queue` is handled by `trg_erq_cascade`. **`personalities.birth_place` text is copied from `cities.name` BEFORE the delete** (6 of the 57's referrers had no birth-place text at all; 75 across the whole cohort), because the city name is otherwise the only human-readable record of where the person was born. Search needs no statement: the delete fires `trg_sync_geo_spine` → `trg_search_documents_city_del` → `search_reindex_drain` does `DELETE FROM search_documents` before re-indexing (verified: all 57 land in `search_reindex_queue`). **A reviewed row can also be merged away rather than gain content, and that happened here** — `Tunesien` was merged into `Tunis` by `20260929110000_city_exonym_merges` (concurrent work on the same table) between the review and the PR, which set `duplicate_of_id`, wrote a `city_merge_audit` row and turned the row into a redirect. Deleting it would have broken the redirect and made `unmerge_cities` impossible, so it came off the list (58 → 57) and the migration gained a **second abort guard** on `duplicate_of_id IS NOT NULL` — the shell predicate already excludes such rows, so re-running the classifier drops them automatically, but the migration carries a frozen id list and cannot. That concurrent pass also merged 101 exonym duplicates and shrank the empty-shell cohort 1,456 → 1,408, which is why the classifier's inputs must be re-pulled rather than reused from a snapshot. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/nonplaceCityDeletion.test.ts`. **Still open, deliberately out of scope:** the wrong-`country_id` rows need a repair pass, and the producer path is not sealed — though measured 2026-09-15 it minted **0 rows in the trailing week**, so it is dormant rather than actively regrowing. **The comma-qualified backlog this line used to carry was wrong by roughly fifty times and is now closed (`99100101100000`).** It read *"the ~359 comma-qualified names and exonym duplicates need `merge_cities`"*. Re-measured: **330** live comma-qualified rows, of which only **35** have ANY same-country twin under the bare head name — **0** corroborated by a shared `wikidata_qid`, **7** by proximity under 10 km (merged), **6** whose twin is a provably DIFFERENT place (19 km to 3,976 km), and **22** where one side has no coordinates so nothing corroborates. **A pass that merged "comma-qualified rows with a same-name twin" would therefore have merged six different places and twenty-two unverifiable pairs — precisely what the standing requirement forbids — and the recorded number would have made that pass look like routine backlog work.** **The seductive rule for the 22 is refused, and this cohort refutes it from the inside:** it is tempting to treat a qualifier naming a region of the row's own country (`Antwerp, Flanders` beside `Antwerp` in BE) as proof of sameness, but **`Schwerin, Brandenburg` sits beside `Schwerin` in DE and the two are 431 km apart**, because a Schwerin exists in Brandenburg as well as the Mecklenburg capital — and that row carries **Q1709**, the Mecklenburg city, so it is a WRONG IDENTIFIER rather than a duplicate and is left alone. Region agreement is not a second signal; coordinates and shared identifiers are. The 22 wait for `city_qid_gap_link` to supply identifiers. The seven were safe beyond the distance because every qualified row held **zero** venues and events while its twin held the content and usually the QID, so each merge moved a redirect and lost no editorial work. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/cityCommaQualifierMerges.test.ts`, mutation-tested **6/6** with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES, and dry-run on prod in a rolled-back transaction executing **the file's own `do` block** including its postconditions — merged 7, skipped none, all three passed. **No longer open:** this line used to end "the city search indexer still has no status filter, which is why the 986 pre-existing ghosts remain searchable" — `20261016110000` added the filter and 0 of 1,069 ghosts are in search as of 2026-09-02. What is still searchable is the 1,952 `placeholder` rows, which is a DISPOSITION backlog, not an indexer gap.
**Two crons worked the wrong list for their whole life, and the selector was never the problem (2026-09-15, PR follow-up to #3747):** `public.cities_due_for_refresh(p_limit, p_scope)` implements FIVE scopes — `content_first`, `content_only`, `all`, `qid_gap`, `alias_gap`. The edge function that is its only caller held `['content_first', 'content_only', 'all']` and rewrote anything else to `content_first`. **The two scopes missing from that list are exactly the two that exist ONLY to be driven by a cron**: `city_qid_gap_link` posts `{"scope":"qid_gap","batch_limit":60}` and `city_alias_harvest` posts `{"scope":"alias_gap","batch_limit":300}`. Both posted, both got a 200, both booked `last_run_status='success'`, and both spent every run re-working the same `content_first` list `city_factual_backfill` already works at batch 40. **That sentence is true of `city_qid_gap_link` and STALE for `city_alias_harvest`, corrected 2026-09-15:** that job auto-paused on 2026-08-28 after three consecutive `504 IDLE_TIMEOUT` responses (08-26/27/28 — a real edge 504 at the 150s idle limit, `timed_out:false`, so counting it as a failure was correct), and the nightly `automation_cron_sync` then unscheduled it. It is `enabled=false` with **no `cron.job` row at all**, i.e. `alias_gap` has no live driver and this change does not give it one. It sits in the *legitimate* half of the auto-pause rule — paused AND still failing, which only warns — rather than the paused-then-recovered shape that hard-fails CI. Note the 150s timeout is itself downstream of the defect: it was posting `batch_limit:300` and being handed the heavier `content_first` work, so the batch wants re-sizing before it is re-enabled. **Nothing could see it**: the registry is right, the cron is right, the selector is right, the run record says success, and the function's own response never named the scope it worked. **The measured cost is a cohort that was never once attempted.** The `qid_gap` scope ranks the 1,538 unlinked `personality-birth-place` cities first; of those, **1,526 are live in site search**, and every one carries **zero** `wikidata_link` state — `attempts` null, no miss, no terminal `data_unavailable` stamp. Not tried and found unresolvable: **never processed at all**, oldest visit 2026-06-07, newest 2026-08-01, while the engine resolved ~124 other cities a night at a ~95-100% hit rate. An engine with a healthy throughput number and a work list it cannot reach is indistinguishable from a healthy engine — the `would_merge: 0` lesson, one subsystem over. **THE OBVIOUS FIX IS HARMFUL ON ITS OWN AND THAT IS THE POINT.** Extending the allowlist alone turns the sweep loose on that cohort, and the `qid_gap` ordering is **population DESCENDING** — a country or a first-level subdivision always out-populates a city, so the ordering *systematically front-loads the non-places*. The live head of the batch is `Großbritannien` (63.8M), `Illinois`, `Long Island`, `Indiana`, `Tennessee`, `Missouri`, `Minnesota`, `Colorado`, `Yorkshire`, `Queensland`, `Distrito Federal`. The link phase's pre-existing suspect-name heuristic cannot stop them: it tests against `countries.name` + `regions.name`, which are **English**, so `Großbritannien` matches nothing — the same mechanism this file already records for `Hessen`/`Russland`/`Singapur`. Measured over the live `qid_gap` top 1000, that heuristic flags **1 row**. And a wrong QID here is permanent and self-reinforcing: `city_factual_sparql` and `city-corroboration` rebuild facts from the identifier weekly, so a plausible-but-wrong link regenerates wrong data forever while a null one regenerates nothing. **So the scope fix ships with a class gate**, `_shared/city-class-guard.ts`, reading P31 from the `claims` `fetchEntity` ALREADY returns (zero extra requests; class labels resolve through the existing batched, cached `resolveLabels`). **It is a WHITELIST, inverting `tag-wiki-guard.ts`'s polarity on purpose**: there an implausible class is refused and a miss costs a link, here the harm is asymmetric and permanent, so a QID is adopted only on a positively recognised settlement class and an unrecognised one is REFUSED and **reported verbatim**, so a gap in the vocabulary shows up as a rising count of namable labels rather than as silence. An OVERRIDE list runs over every label first, because the dangerous shape is the entity carrying BOTH — `megacity` + `federal entity of Mexico`, `city-state` — which a plain whitelist adopts on the settlement word alone. **THAT BLANKET VETO WAS WRONG AND THE FIXTURE JUSTIFYING IT WAS INVENTED — corrected 2026-09-15 by the first live dry run; see the next entry.** The override is per-LABEL now. **Adoption moved INSIDE the settlement branch**, which is the load-bearing edit rather than a tidy-up: `parseCityFacts` would otherwise copy Illinois's population and coordinates onto the row, and `enwikiTitle` would send step 3 to publish a US state's Wikipedia lead as a city description. **`undetermined` is a THIRD verdict and must never collapse into `refused`**: both decline to adopt, but only a decision about the entity may count an attempt toward the terminal sentinel — counting a Wikidata outage is how 6,498 venues were stamped `logo_fetched_at` while the token was dead and written off permanently. Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence. The run report now also states the `scope` it worked, the quantity whose invisibility was the whole defect. **Prevention is a static drift test** (`_shared/city-scope-drift.test.ts`): every `"scope"` a registered cron posts, read out of `supabase/migrations/`, must be one the function accepts — with a **positive control asserting at least 3 distinct scopes are found**, because an empty match set also satisfies "every scope is valid" and would turn the file green while the drift went unchecked. Guarded by `_shared/city-class-guard.test.ts` + `city-scope-drift.test.ts`, mutation-tested **4/4** (reintroducing the original allowlist, collapsing `undetermined` into `refused`, deleting the override loop, and neutering the drift regex) with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES. **Deliberately NOT done here:** nothing is enabled or disabled and no data is written by this change — the sweep's first real pass wants a `dry_run` read before it is trusted, and the 1,915 searchable `placeholder` rows remain a disposition backlog, not an indexer gap.

**The dry run earned its keep: the gate that shipped with the scope fix refused most of China and adopted a 120-person town in Kansas (2026-09-15, follow-up to #3752):** #3752 deployed (edge function v177, 17:28Z) and the verification it was written to require — post `{"phase":"link","scope":"qid_gap","batch_limit":20,"dry_run":true}` and READ the response — returned `"scope":"qid_gap"`, not `content_first`. **The substitution that was the entire defect is gone, confirmed on prod rather than inferred from a green deploy.** The same twenty rows then exposed two defects in the gate itself, neither of which any test caught, because both live in the gap between a vocabulary written from reasoning and the labels Wikidata actually uses.

**(1) THE OVERRIDE WAS A BLANKET VETO AND IT REFUSED REAL CITIES.** Three of six refusals were false, all one shape: Wikidata spells China's city classes `prefecture-level city of China` and `sub-province-level division`, so `\bprefecture\b` and `\bprovince\b` refused **Shijiazhuang, Nanning and Hangzhou** — every one of which ALSO carries a bare `big city` or `city`. Measured the same way, the veto also refuses **Berlin** (`metropolis`, `largest city`, beside `city-state`), **Hamburg** (`big city`), **Vienna** (`metropolis`) and **Singapore** (`city`) — four major destinations on this platform, and the last of them contradicts the capital-scope finding this file already records, that a city-state IS a city. **The justifying fixture was invented, not measured:** the old test asserted `['megacity','federal entity of Mexico']` must refuse, and no entity carries that pair — Mexico City's real P31 is `big city`/`city`/`metropolis`/`megacity`, and the dry run adopted it correctly. The override is now evaluated **per label**: a label carrying both a settlement word and a disqualifier is merely not EVIDENCE, and cannot veto a different label that is clean. It still does the job it was built for — an entity whose ONLY settlement evidence is qualified away (bare `city-state`) never reaches the adopt branch — and `France` (`sovereign state`/`country`) and `U.S. state` still refuse because they carry no settlement word at all. **A blanket veto here was never merely a missed link**: a refusal calls `bumpMiss`, three of them stamp the terminal `data_unavailable`, so three nights would have written most of China off permanently.

**(2) THE CLASS GATE CANNOT SEE A NAMESAKE, BECAUSE THE NAMESAKE IS A SETTLEMENT.** The dry run adopted **`Q2545992` — Long Island, a city in KANSAS, population ~120 — for the New York island row**, and **`Q1184769` — Indiana, a borough in PENNSYLVANIA — for the US-state row**, the latter filling that borough's population, area and elevation onto it. Both pass every class check cleanly and correctly; no class vocabulary can refuse them. This is the rule this file already states for `run_event_city_link` and the news linker, one entity later: never resolve by name alone when the reference table cannot represent the ambiguity — require a second, independent signal, and block when it disagrees. **The second signal is the row's own coordinates against the entity's P625**, which rides in the claims already fetched, so it costs no extra request. **The bound is measured, not chosen:** of the ten QIDs that dry run adopted, the eight correct ones sit **0.0-9.5 km** from the row's stored coordinates (London 0.0, Paris 0.0, Ahmedabad 0.9, Khartoum 2.9, Mexico City 8.8, Saint Petersburg 8.9, Ho Chi Minh City 9.5) and the two wrong ones sit **584.3 km** and **2216.1 km**. 100 km sits in that gap with 10x headroom over the largest correct distance and 5.8x margin under the smallest wrong one. It **fails open** when either side lacks coordinates and the run report COUNTS that (`coord_unchecked`), because refusing coordless rows would gut a sweep whose whole purpose is a cohort that has never resolved — measured, all 60 rows of the live batch carry coordinates, so failing open costs almost nothing today and a rising count stays visible if that changes.

**THE CRON WAS PAUSED AND THAT IS THE ONLY REASON THIS DID NOT SHIP INTO DATA.** `city_qid_gap_link` fires at **02:10**, and disabling the registry row alone does NOT stop it — `sync_automations_to_cron` only unschedules at **05:10**, so a paused job still fires for up to a day, exactly as the auto-pause entry above records. Both layers were therefore stopped: `admin_automations.enabled=false` AND `cron.alter_job(2384, active := false)`, with the prior state (`enabled`, `jobid`, `schedule`, `cron_active`) written into `conditions.paused_prior_state` so the restore is exact rather than remembered. **RESTORED 2026-09-15, and only after the deployed source was read rather than the deploy status.** The fix shipped as edge-function **v178** and the markers were grepped out of the DEPLOYED body (`cityCoordVerdict`, `CITY_COORD_MAX_KM`, `coord_refused`, `coord_unchecked`, `overriddenSettlement`) — a green deploy is not evidence the code is live. The same 20-row `dry_run` probe was then re-posted by `pg_net` and resolved **by request id** (106894), and **all twenty per-row `results` were read, not the tallies**: the three false refusals now ADOPT (Hangzhou Q4970, Shijiazhuang Q58401, Nanning Q179608), the two namesakes are now REFUSED by the coordinate arm at the distances that motivated it (`refused_coords:2216.1km` for Long Island, `refused_coords:584.3km` for Indiana), and the remaining refusals are each correct and namable — `override:\bprefecture\b:prefecture of Japan` on a **Chiba** row that resolved to the PREFECTURE rather than the city, `override:\bhistorical region\b` on Yorkshire, and `unrecognised:public research university` on an Illinois row that resolved to the university. `coord_unchecked: 0`, `class_undetermined: 0`, `failed: 0`, and the response's own `scope` still reads `qid_gap`. Both layers were then restored to the recorded prior state exactly — `enabled=true` AND `cron.alter_job(2384, active := true)`, jobid and schedule re-verified against the snapshot — and the cron command re-checked for `admin_automation_run_begin` + `automation_http_post` with **no raw `net.http_post`**, because a re-wrap that fell back to a raw post would give a job that works and records no runs. **One trap on the way back in: `conditions->'paused_prior_state'` returned NULL and read exactly like bookkeeping that was never written.** `admin_automations.conditions` is an ARRAY on **293 of 308 rows** (the 15 objects are the ones carrying a `mode`/`voice` lever), so `'[]'::jsonb || obj` yields `[{…}]` and a key lookup on the array finds nothing. Read the whole column before concluding a snapshot is missing — and restoring means putting `conditions` back to `[]`, since a stale pause note left on a live control surface is the comment-that-outlived-its-truth failure this file records elsewhere.

**Two method notes worth keeping.** A `refused` verdict and a false refusal are indistinguishable in the aggregate counters — `class_refused: 6` reads the same whether the six were right or half wrong — so the run report's `unrecognised_classes` list is not sufficient: it names only the *unrecognised* arm, and all three false refusals came through the *override* arm, which reports its pattern and label per row and not in any summary. **Read the per-row `results`, not the tallies.** And the guard's haversine clamp turned out to be load-bearing rather than defensive decoration: searching for a triggering input found a near-antipodal pair whose intermediate term is `1.0000000000000004`, so an unclamped `asin(sqrt(h))` is NaN — the verdict survives by luck (`NaN <= 100` is false) while `distanceKm` reaches `enrichment_status` as `refused_coords:nullkm`. Found by probing for the input, not by assuming. Guarded by `_shared/city-class-guard.test.ts`, mutation-tested **7/7** with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES — and the first control run read NOT-APPLIED because its anchor did not match the file, which is indistinguishable from a survivor and is the seventh time this file has recorded that check.

**The namesake problem INVERTED — a contained sub-entity passes all three guards, and no bound or vocabulary can refuse it (2026-09-18, `99600101100000`, #3774):** the restored `city_qid_gap_link` ran live on 09-16/17/18 and adopted 29 identifiers. All 29 were resolved against `wbgetentities` **by request rather than sampled**, and **28 are right** — including the exonyms the engine exists for (`Damaskus` → Damascus 1.6 km, `Ahwaz` → Ahvaz 1.7 km) and the three false refusals the previous entry's coordinate fix was built to rescue (Hangzhou `Q4970`, Shijiazhuang `Q58401`, Nanning `Q179608`). One is wrong: **`Kowloon, Hongkong` adopted `Q1022918` = Kowloon Walled City**, a settlement demolished in 1994 occupying ~2.6 ha *inside* Kowloon City district, for a row representing Kowloon, the urban area of roughly two million. **All three guards pass on it, and every one of them passes CORRECTLY** — the coordinate arm is for DISTANT namesakes and this entity sits **1.9 km** away (Long Island's Kansas town was 2216.1 km); `P31` is `Q486972` "human settlement", which is exactly the class the whitelist exists to *accept*; and the entity label contains the row's own name, so a name check agrees too. **Tightening any of the three is measured to be worse than the defect:** the eight correct adoptions of that first dry run sit 0.0–9.5 km out and two legitimate ones here sit at 18.5 km (Aguascalientes, the municipality) and 67.4 km (Cajamarca, where it is the ROW's coordinates that are wrong and the identifier that is right), so a radius under 100 km starts refusing correct work, and refusing "human settlement" disables the gate. **The rule this file already states for `run_event_city_link` and the news linker does not reach here either** — "require a second, independent signal" assumes the two candidates are *alternatives*; a contained entity is not a rival reading of the name, it is a smaller true thing inside the bigger true thing, and every independent signal agrees with it. **The harm was PENDING, not landed, and the cached title is the mechanism.** `cities.description` still held correct Kowloon prose, but `wikipedia_title` had been overwritten to "Kowloon Walled City", and `city-factual-backfill` fetches Wikipedia **by cached sitelink title, never by `cities.name`** — so the next weekly `city_factual_sparql` / `city-corroboration` pass would have rebuilt from the wrong entity and replaced correct prose with the Walled City's. Clearing the QID alone leaves that live: the `queerness` rule (`20360401100300`) on a third entity class, **nulling an identifier does not unpublish the prose it produced** — and inversely, leaving the cached TITLE keeps producing it. **NO REPOINT, and writing this tested that rule from the inside:** `Q216651` was recalled as "the correct Kowloon" and resolved live before being written into the migration header — **it is _Atmel AVR_, a family of microcontrollers.** The correct identifier is deliberately left unset rather than guessed, because a plausible-but-wrong QID regenerates wrong data weekly while a null one regenerates nothing. **The terminal state is part of the fix, not bookkeeping:** a bare null returns the row to the `qid_gap` cohort where the same three guards pass and the same entity is re-adopted — the rejection treadmill, one table over — and `cities_due_for_refresh` excludes `wikidata_link.state = 'data_unavailable'` **in its scope CTE, i.e. for every scope**, which is what stops it; the real reason is recorded beside the label and `reset_city_enrichment_state()` reverses it. Cost stated rather than hidden: the row gains no further facts, which is cheap because it is a placeholder shell (`tmp-` slug, `personality-birth-place`, 0 venues, 0 events, `seo_indexable=false`) whose description is already correct. **THE ENGINE IS DELIBERATELY NOT PAUSED, and a containment guard is deliberately NOT built.** Every test the pause rule names reads healthy on all three runs — `scope` is `qid_gap`, `coord_unchecked: 0`, `class_undetermined: 0`, `failed: 0`, and every entry in `unrecognised_classes` is a county, ceremonial county, university or administrative entity, i.e. correct — so the defect sits outside all of them, and pausing would stop legitimate work on a cohort that had never once been processed. Adoption is falling as the population-descending cohort head is consumed — **60 → 19 → 8 → 1** across 09-15/16/17/18 — so the exposure is shrinking on its own. **That series read "26 → 10 → 1" until 2026-09-18 and the two middle figures were WRONG**: they were copied out of the run reports rather than measured off the rows, which give 19 and 8. The corrected counts reconcile to the 29 exactly — 19 + 8 + 1 = 28 still carrying their identifier, plus Kowloon, whose own adoption stamp this repair overwrote, so the day it landed on is not recoverable. **Prefer a number the next reader can re-derive from the corpus to one taken from a run report.** **One instance is not a measurement**: a containment guard needs its own, and a SECOND instance is the signal to build it — spot-check weekly by resolving new adoptions `wbgetentities` BY REQUEST and asking whether each entity is the row's own place or something *inside* it, which is the one question the aggregate counters cannot answer. **THAT SPOT-CHECK HAS TO SEPARATE THE TWO ENGINES BY THE CLOCK, BECAUSE THE ROW DOES NOT RECORD WHICH ONE WROTE IT.** `enrichment_status.wikidata_link` carries `at` / `state` / `source` / `qid` / `attempts` and nothing else — measured over all 3,354 stamped rows, the only exceptions being the `by` / `reason` / `detail` keys this repair itself wrote onto the single Kowloon row — so there is **no per-row attribution** distinguishing `city_qid_gap_link` from `city_factual_backfill`, which writes the same key on the same column. The only separation available is the cron fire window — qid_gap **02:10**, factual-backfill **03:15** — and without it a blended daily count reads ~35 on a day the engine under test adopted **1**. It is fragile (move either schedule and the two become indistinguishable) and it is the row-level twin of the defect #3752 fixed by making the run report state its own scope. First spot-check under that rule (2026-09-18): the one 02:xx adoption was `Trujillo` → **Q214173**, resolved by request — "city of Peru, capital of La Libertad region", `P31` `Q1549591`, sitelink `Trujillo, Peru`, **0.0 km** from the row's own coordinates, i.e. the row's own place rather than something inside it. **1/1 correct, no second instance, guard still unbuilt.** Verified on prod after merge **by NAME**: `99600101100000` present as `city_kowloon_wrong_qid`, the row at `wikidata_qid` NULL / `wikipedia_title` NULL / state `data_unavailable` / reason `wrong_entity_contained` with both cleared values recorded, `description` intact at 373 chars and not the Walled City's, the three control identifiers still on their own rows, and the `qid_gap` pool returning **1000 rows with this row offered 0 times** — the pool size is the positive control, since "the row is absent" is equally satisfied by a selector returning nothing. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/cityKowloonWrongQid.test.ts`, mutation-tested **9/9** with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES — and its first draft sliced the UPDATE to the statement's first `;`, which sits **inside the statement's own detail string**, so two assertions failed against correct code: the venue-dedup trap (`[^;]*` across a message that itself contains one), now anchored on the first postcondition instead.

**The comma-qualified city name is DEAD, and that is a constraint rather than a convention (2026-09-19, `99991789823216`, #3828):** #3791 unlinked two events that `event-city-match` had resolved BY NAME ALONE onto the wrong same-name city — an Atlanta WNBA game on **College Park, MARYLAND**, a Derby **ENGLAND** IDAHOBIT event on **Derby, CONNECTICUT** — and named the obvious follow-up: create the real cities they belong to. Only ONE of the three is creatable, and finding out why is the entry. The plan was the corpus's own apparent convention (`Dearborn, Michigan`, `Hickory, North Carolina` and `Hudson, Wisconsin` are all live rows), and the dry run answered `23505 idx_cities_name_country_unique, Key (lower(name), country_id)=(college park, …)`. **The key evaluated to `college park`, not `college park, georgia`**: `trg_cities_aa_split_name` → `cities_split_qualified_name()` is a BEFORE trigger that splits a qualified name into base + qualifier, stamps the original into `field_provenance.name`, moves an unambiguous qualifier into `region_name` and recomputes `name_normalized`, so **the qualifier never reaches the index** and the ~330 comma-qualified rows in the corpus are LEGACY that predates it. With `idx_cities_name_country_unique` + `uk_cities_country_name_active` + `cities_country_canonical_key_uniq`, `cities` structurally holds **at most one row per (name, country)** — this file's "cities cannot represent same-name collisions" as an enforced constraint, and the real reason those rows were missing rather than merely unwritten. **Derby, England is creatable because GB is a different country** from Derby, Connecticut (`Q755197`); College Park, Georgia and Roseville, Minnesota are not, and **the workaround is forbidden in the migration's own text and asserted against by a postcondition** — a parenthesised or otherwise mangled name re-creates the duplicate the trigger exists to stop, under a spelling no resolver will ever match. Representing them needs the region in the uniqueness key, a schema change across ~5,700 rows. `Q43475` was resolved live (0.4 km from the event, against 6,649.4 km to College Park GA). **Two Minnesota venues sitting on the California Roseville row are unlinked, and `venues.state` alone could not have decided that**: measured across this cohort the column is near-random — "Quest" in Taos says Alabama, "Amtrak Raton" (New Mexico) says California, "Custer Beacon" says "Santa Fe" which is not a state, Colon Duty Free (**Panama**) says Michigan, the 250 Main Hotel in Rockland **Maine** says British Columbia — so the predicate requires the state text AND a 25 km coordinate arm, and the two are actionable only because their coordinates independently corroborate. **The producer is DORMANT, which is why no seal was built**: all 250 `event-city-match` rows were minted on one day, 2026-05-13. **Its sibling `venue-city-match` was audited and is NOT the same defect** — testing direction (venue clustering vs city distance) over ~100 rows gave 3 city-row suspects and only **Roseville** is real; the rest is venue-coordinate noise, so a sweep there would have manufactured work.

**`execute_sql` COMMITS, so a diagnostic query against prod is a WRITE (2026-09-19).** While diagnosing that 23505 the candidate INSERT was run through `execute_sql` to read back what the triggers did to `name_normalized` — with no trailing `raise exception` — and it committed a real `derby-england` row to production. It was confirmed mine rather than assumed (the only non-cron city created that day; every other was `geonames` on the `:24` cron), and kept rather than churned because it is correct; the migration asserts END STATE so it composes in both worlds. The house rule for validating a migration (`execute_sql` runs one call as a single transaction, so append `raise exception` to roll it back) applies to **every** exploratory write, not only to migration rehearsals.

**An e2e must assert the INVARIANT, not the repair's transient state — and writing one found a bigger defect than the one it was written for (2026-09-19, `99991789825952`).** `e2e/geo-namesake-city-links.spec.ts` asserts that each repaired event is EITHER unlinked OR on a city its own coordinates corroborate within 25 km; a spec pinned to `city_id is null` would have gone red the moment the follow-up it guards actually landed. It fetches `/city/derby` with a Googlebot UA to prove the England event is absent — and the served `<meta name="description">` read *"Derby is a cathedral city … on the River Derwent in Derbyshire, England … The population of Derby is 274,149 (2024)"* on the **Connecticut** row, indexable, four venues, `wikidata_qid` and `wikipedia_title` both CORRECT. That is the wrong-SUBJECT class `40000101100000` recorded for Cambria, and **no existing guard can see it**: `tag-wiki-guard` and the wrong-entity repair both key on the identifier, which here is right. **The population is what made the direction unarguable** — the column held `255394`, and resolved live `Q755197` P1082 = **12,325** while `Q43475` P1082 = **255,394 byte-exact**, so the number was copied off the namesake exactly as the prose was. **The correct text was already in the row, unpublished**, at `field_provenance.description.candidates[0]`: `city-factual-backfill` is fill-if-empty, so it recorded the right answer as a candidate and never overwrote the non-empty wrong column, which is why the row could never self-heal — check whether the right value is already in the row before repairing a wrong one. Corrected rather than retracted (live, indexable, content-bearing), published as a **VALUES literal** rather than the row's jsonb read at apply time so what lands is what was verified, content-guarded, prior text AND number preserved under `.corrected`, provenance built with `||` because `jsonb_set(create_missing)` creates only the LAST path element. **The cohort is measured and deliberately NOT swept: one description text published on two live city rows returns 43 shared texts over 86 rows, 65 indexable, 24 crossing a country boundary** — `Carlisle`/Pennsylvania serving Cumbria's lead, `Chester`/Pennsylvania serving Cheshire's, `Aberdeen`/South Dakota and `Dundee`/Oregon serving Scotland's, `Florence`/Colorado serving Tuscany's, and `Saint Augustine`/Florida serving a description of **Augustine of Hippo, the theologian**. Some of the 43 are instead genuine duplicate ROWS (Genoa/Genova, Ft Lauderdale) whose remedy is a merge, not a rewrite, so direction has to be decided per pair by hand. **Every assertion in that spec is an absence, so three positive controls carry it**: the events must be readable by anon at all, a same-name city must exist, and **the crawler page is shown to print its own venues** before "the event is not named" is trusted — a page that names nothing satisfies the absence equally. Guarded by `geoNamesakeCityRows.test.ts` (12/12) and `derbyConnecticutNamesakeProse.test.ts` (11/11), each with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES — and **three mutations first read as NOT-APPLIED because the patterns used a four-space indent where the WHERE clauses use three**, the seventh recorded instance of that check in this file.

**Place dedup rests on real sources, and that is now checked rather than observed (2026-09-15, `78000101100000`, #3747):** the standing requirement is that country/city/village dedup rest on real geographical sources and names and **never suggest merging two different places**. Countries, cities and villages currently suggest **nothing** — country 0 candidates, village 0, city 1 candidate (`Aguascalientes` vs `Aguas Calientes`, despace-identical, both MX, **642 km apart**) which the engine correctly skips on its <10 km gate. `skipped: 1` is what proves the engine sees a work list rather than being blind, and **an emergent zero is indistinguishable from a blind engine**, which is why the deliverable is a sentinel rather than the observation. **The literal rule is wrong and the corpus proves it.** Of **332** city merges on record, 11 are name-identical once case/accents/spaces/punctuation are stripped, 161 carry a comma qualifier, and **160 differ as strings** — but **146 of those 160** are the exonym/official-name class (Kapstadt/Cape Town, Teheran/Tehran, Venedig/Venice, Frankfurt/Frankfurt am Main), one real place under another language or its full legal name. Flagging them would condemn correct data, so the invariant is about **places, not strings**. `place_pair_corroboration()` is four arms — name identity · comma qualifier **whose tails agree** · shared `wikidata_qid` · same country within 10 km — and the wikidata arm is what carries the exonyms (Tokyo and 東京 share no characters and share **Q1490**), taking the residue from 160 to **12**. All 12 were read by hand: four correct-but-unverifiable (Venedig/Venice is 10.6 km apart, just past the gate; Biel/Bienne, Castelló/Castellón and Frankfurt am Main have no coordinates at all) and **the real defect class, a district merged into its parent city** (Harburg, Altenwerder, Altona-Ottensen, Groß Flottbek, Bergedorf → Hamburg; Freisenbruch → Essen). **Those are deliberately NOT reversed**: a district was never a city, so unmerging resurrects a non-place row rather than restoring a correct one — the defect #3745 had just finished archiving four of — and every one of these audits predates `schema:1`, so `unmerge_cities` cannot replay the reparenting and the personalities stay on Hamburg regardless. Gating: `suggested_uncorroborated` is a **zero-invariant hard fail** (a queued pair is a suggestion to a human, which is exactly what the requirement forbids), `merged_uncorroborated` is **advisory at its measured baseline of 12 and fails only on growth** (gating at the baseline ships red on arrival, the cry-wolf shape already removed once from the dedup backlog rule), and **`merges_total` is reported first** so zero merges reads as *the probe is measuring nothing* rather than as a clean graph. **Two defects the prod dry run caught that reading did not:** a plain `OR` in the comma arm let one side's own tail vouch for the pair, collapsing **"Springfield, Illinois" into "Springfield, Oregon"** — arm 2 is a `CASE` requiring the tails to agree, with a `Vienna, Austria`/`Vienna, AT` positive control so the both-qualified branch is not exercised only by a refusal; and `queer_villages` stores lat/lng as **`double precision`** while `cities`/`countries` use `numeric` and `haversine_m` takes numeric, so the **village branch alone** failed to resolve the call — all four call sites now cast explicitly, both sides, which the guard asserts by COUNT (8 and 8) because asserting one cast per site is satisfied by the surviving copy. Guard mutation-tested **13/13**; one mutation first SURVIVED because **`< 10000` is a prefix of `< 10000000`** — a 10,000 km radius corroborates any two points in one country — so the bound is anchored with `(?!\d)`. **The version is the other lesson.** It opened at `75000101100000`; #3744 merged four glossary migrations mid-review and one took that exact version. Different filenames, so git merged both with no conflict, but the version is what `db push` matches on and prod had already applied theirs under the name `tag_prose_says_nothing_and_abbreviation` — leaving it would have **silently skipped** this migration while every count-by-version check still read 1. Caught by `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=A HEAD origin/main -- supabase/migrations/` before pushing, not by any test. Renumbered above the then-live ceiling `77000101100000`, and **the version string lives in FOUR places** — filename, the test's `MIGRATION` constant, the test's header, and the health script's "not applied?" hint. Verified on prod after merge by NAME, not version: `78000101100000` present as `place_merge_name_corroboration`, and `place_merge_name_signals()` returning `probe_ok`, `merges_total` 332, `suggested_uncorroborated` 0, `merged_uncorroborated` 12 with the same twelve pairs.

**Capital scope — national vs Landeshauptstadt (2026-08-25):** `cities.is_capital` existed since the baseline as an **undifferentiated boolean with no scope column**, so nothing distinguished Berlin from Munich; all 16 German Land capitals sat at `false` and the words "Landeshauptstadt"/"Bundeshauptstadt" appeared **zero times in the repo**. `is_regional_capital` + `capital_of_region` are added as a SECOND boolean, not an enum, because Berlin/Vienna/Hamburg/Bremen are national AND regional at once. `capital_of_region` is not a display copy of `region_name`: that column is filled on 2,958 of 5,552 live rows and on many capitals holds numeric FIPS codes (`13`, `09`, `50`); the new column carries the Wikidata label that is the *evidence* for the flag. Adding it is a **six-object lockstep** — `cities` + `geo_city_profiles` + `sync_geo_spine_city()` + the `cities_admin` VIEW + `cities_directory()` + `commit_city_staging_item()` — because `geo_spine_drift_check()` only compares name/slug/parent_id and would never alarm on a missing satellite column. **The national side was 96% correct and the 4% was invisible**: 198 of 199 flags matched `countries.capital`, but 9 capitals existed as rows and were never flagged, 36 had no `cities` row at all, and 3 `countries.capital` values were wrong (HK `City of Victoria`, MO `Macao`, UM `Washington DC` — the *US* capital on a country with no capital). Every one is a dependent territory, which is why it hid. Nothing had ever written `is_capital` except `commit_city_staging_item` reading a literal `metadata.is_capital`, and **nothing ever revalidated the flag against `countries.capital`** — the same "derived field never rechecked against its input" class as the 86 wrong-country `safety_notes`. Repair `20261001110100` (validated in a rolled-back txn on prod: 199 → 246 flagged, 36 rows created, 246 countries with capital text → 246 flagged, exactly 1:1). **Comparison is unaccented via `public.city_name_key`** — Iceland's `countries.capital` is `Reykjavik` and the city is `Reykjavík`, both legitimate — and it needs the **TWO-ARG** `extensions.unaccent('extensions.unaccent'::regdictionary, …)` form, because these callers run `SET search_path TO 'public'` where the one-arg form cannot find its dictionary. **Resolution keys on the FLAG, never on name equality**: Bermuda's row is `City of Hamilton` against capital `Hamilton` and Macau's is `Macau/Macao` against `Macao`; a name test would report both as broken forever. Rot prevention rides the existing weekly `run_city_coverage_radar()` (`capital_flag_missing` in `missing_fields`) plus `city_capital_gaps()` for the country-level form the per-city table cannot represent — **"more than one flagged capital" is deliberately NOT a recurring check**, since South Africa and Bolivia really have several; it is asserted once in the repair, where the count was measured at 0. **The regional flag is backfilled from Wikidata P1376 in the existing `city-factual-backfill` sparql phase** (`capitalQuery`/`pickCapitals` in `_shared/wikidata-city.ts`, behind the `wikidata.sparql` breaker). National vs regional is decided WITHOUT a stored country QID — `countries` has no `wikidata_qid` column — by comparing the P1376 target against the city's own P17, plus a class arm (`P31/P279* Q6256`) for dependent territories where P17 is the parent state while P1376 points at the territory (Gibraltar). Regional additionally requires `P31/P279* Q10864048` (first-level subdivision) AND same-country corroboration, so district seats stay out. **Two traps, both found by running the query against live WDQS rather than reasoning about it.** (1) **A dissolved unit is not caught by the statement-level guards.** `pq:P582` and deprecated rank exclude a statement that ENDED, but Cologne's P1376 → *Electorate of Cologne* carries neither — the polity simply stopped existing in 1803, and it is a first-level subdivision by class, so Cologne would have published as a Landeshauptstadt (Düsseldorf is). Munich had the same shape twice (Kingdom + Electorate of Bavaria). `FILTER NOT EXISTS { ?unit wdt:P576 ?dissolved }` is what removes them; with it, live results are Munich→Bavaria, Düsseldorf→North Rhine-Westphalia, Stuttgart→Baden-Württemberg, Dresden→Saxony, Kiel→Schleswig-Holstein, Hannover→Lower Saxony, Magdeburg→Saxony-Anhalt, Mainz→Rhineland-Palatinate, Potsdam→Brandenburg, Saarbrücken→Saarland, Bremen→Bremen, and Austin→Texas / Barcelona→Catalonia / Lyon→Auvergne-Rhône-Alpes / Naples→Campania / Turin→Piedmont, with Bonn, Frankfurt, Cologne and Aachen correctly empty. (2) **MEASURED AND REJECTED: the inverse `?unit wdt:P36 ?city` direction.** The hypothesis was that some countries model the relation only on the subdivision, losing every US state capital; it does not hold (Boston→Massachusetts and Austin→Texas both carry P1376) and the union adds four junk units on Barcelona alone. Two cities that looked like coverage gaps in that measurement — Sacramento and Albany — turned out to be **wrong QIDs in our own `cities` table** (the stored "Albany" QID is Albany, *Texas*), not a Wikidata gap; Dresden, Erfurt, Potsdam, Saarbrücken and Wiesbaden carry wrong QIDs too and will silently resolve to "not a regional capital" until relinked. **The backfill is NOT fill-if-empty** — `is_regional_capital` is `NOT NULL DEFAULT false`, so an unprobed row is indistinguishable from a probed negative by its value; `enrichment_status.capital_scope` carries that distinction and decides first-write vs correction, and a city absent from the SPARQL result is a NEGATIVE finding (both catch branches return, so the loop only runs when every chunk succeeded) rather than a `bumpMiss`. **It never writes `is_capital`**: the national flag is derived from `countries.capital` and keeps one writer, so a Wikidata disagreement is recorded in `capital_scope.detail.national` and left for a human. UI: the Status line is a ranked ladder (national > regional > major city) and the "Capital of" row carries the regional half, so a city-state loses nothing; the `Crown` badge on directory/geo cards stays national-only. Migrations `20261001110000`–`20261001110200`; guarded by `supabase/functions/_shared/wikidata-city.test.ts` and `src/pages/city-detail/__tests__/CityOverviewTab.test.tsx`.

**Safety Notes composer (deterministic, tiered, 2026-06-08):** `safety_notes` is composed by one pure-SQL `compose_safety_note(jsonb)` (IMMUTABLE; migration `20260608000001`), not the LLM (the old path produced circular "Spain has a high equality score so Barcelona is no exception" blobs; 0/3830 cities were ever live because the human gate was never cleared). Derives every note from structured facts — country legal status (`lgbti_criminalization` legal/penalty/death_penalty + `lgbti_same_sex_unions`) + city LGBTQ+ density (venue/event/queer-village counts) — returning `{note, risk_tier, confidence, auto_publishable, components}`. Risk tier mirrors `useTripSafety.ts` (death→critical, criminalizing→high, eq<40→moderate, else low). **"death" is THREE states, not a boolean, and reading it as one understated five countries (fixed 2026-09-04, `20260904203201`).** ILGA splits the fact across `death_penalty` and `penalty` and neither is sufficient alone: Nigeria is `death_penalty='Yes'` with a prison-only `penalty`, while AE/AF/PK/QA/SO are `death_penalty='No legal certainty'` with `penalty='Death Penalty (possible)'` — ILGA recording that it CANNOT RULE OUT execution, which every `= 'yes'` test read as identical to `No`. Use `public.death_penalty_risk(jsonb)` / `deathPenaltyRisk()` (`confirmed|possible|none`), never the raw field; the two are drift-tested by `src/lib/__tests__/deathPenaltyRiskSqlParity.test.ts`, and the branch ORDER is load-bearing (the affirmative `death_penalty` test must precede the `penalty` fallback or Nigeria breaks). `confirmed` and `possible` stay distinct — a surface stating a fact uses `confirmed`, "should this reader be warned" uses `<> 'none'`. **`20260904203201` FIXED THE HELPER AND `location_is_high_risk()`, AND DID NOT TOUCH THE COMPOSER — for a year this line implied otherwise (corrected 2026-09-11, `20470922084500`).** That migration's own header predicted the outcome it did not deliver: *"compose_safety_note for Kabul today -> 'high', '...(penalty: Death Penalty (possible))'; with this fix -> 'critical', '...can carry the death penalty'; 8 published city notes sit in these five countries (AE 4, AF 2, PK 2)"*. `compose_safety_note` kept reading `death_penalty` as a BOOLEAN (`v_death := (p->>'death_penalty')::boolean`), and all four of its callers kept passing `(lgbti_criminalization->>'death_penalty')='Yes'`, which is FALSE for all five `possible` countries. Measured on prod a year later, all 8 were still `high` with the penalty buried, and **the split against the other cohort is what proves the boolean was the mechanism: the 7 `confirmed` countries buried 0 of their 6 notes (there `='Yes'` is true and the good branch fires) while the 5 `possible` countries buried 8 of 8.** The composer now RECONSTRUCTS the minimal `{death_penalty, penalty}` jsonb from the two fields it receives and routes it through `death_penalty_risk()` — one implementation of the rule, and **no caller changes**, because the reconstruction was measured to agree with the real jsonb on all 250 countries and the migration asserts that rather than trusting it. Prose is per-verdict and must stay so: `confirmed` → "can carry the death penalty" (a fact), `possible` → "where the death penalty cannot be ruled out" (what ILGA's "no legal certainty" means); collapsing them would overstate five countries, and the parenthetical `(penalty: …)` branch is now reachable only after both. The 8 published notes were `source='llm+human'`, which the backfill selector deliberately EXCLUDES, so nothing would ever have revisited them — they were corrected in-place rather than retracted (the facts were right and only the emphasis was wrong, so unpublishing would strip a death-penalty country's page of its safety note entirely), guarded on the composer's own `(penalty: … Death …)` signature so a human's real prose is never overwritten, with the prior text preserved at `field_provenance.safety_notes.corrected` and a review row opened. **The general lesson: a fix that changes a shared helper has not changed the surfaces that read the helper's INPUTS instead — enumerate the callers and check each one's own branch.** Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/safetyNoteDeathPenaltyStates.test.ts` (mutation-tested, 15/15). **Tiered publishing:** `risk_tier='low' AND equality_score≥75 AND not-criminalizing AND not-death-penalty AND confidence≥0.8` → auto-publish to `cities.safety_notes` (`field_provenance.source='derived'`); else → `city_review_queue`. **Outing-safety invariant (load-bearing): a criminalizing/death-penalty destination can NEVER auto-publish** — enforced inside the composer (forces `auto_publishable=false`) AND at `approve_city_review(p_id,p_note,p_confirm)` (criminalizing safety_notes approval needs `p_confirm=true`; UI shows a `Lock` + confirm dialog). Backfills `run_city_safety_backfill(p_batch≤300,p_force)` (hard cap 300/batch — **but not for the reason this line gave until 2026-08-19**: `trg_search_documents_city` has not existed on `cities` since Geo P3 `20260727083554` relocated it onto the spine, and re-reading the live catalog is what settles this — `cities` carries exactly five non-internal triggers, `trg_cities_aa_split_name` / `trg_cities_normalized` / `trg_cities_slug` / `trg_erq_cascade` / `trg_sync_geo_spine`, none of them search. **Search is still reached, one hop away**: the UPDATE fires `trg_sync_geo_spine` → writes `geo_places` → `trg_search_documents_city_ins` (`WHEN place_type='city'`) → `search_documents_sync('city')`, which since the pipeline overhaul **enqueues into `search_reindex_queue`** rather than indexing inline. So the cap survives — the chain is still per-row — but the 300 was sized against an inline index pass that no longer happens; measured end-to-end cost is now **2.6 ms/row** (`20260913114500`'s dry run, 500 rows in 1,324 ms). Do not cite the deleted trigger, and do not raise a cap by "verifying the trigger is gone" — it is gone and the work is not) + `run_hotel_safety_backfill(p_batch,p_hotel_id)` (regenerates `hotels.queer_safety_notes` from `amenities[]` signals — gay-district/host-tips/venues-nearby/clothing-optional — replacing the 18-distinct misterb&b boilerplate; no search trigger, unbounded). `triage_stuck_city_safety_reviews()` + `batch_approve_safe_city_reviews()` recompose stuck queue rows. Admin: batch-approve button + tier/`Lock` badges in `CityReviewQueue.tsx`, "Regenerate from signals" in `AdminHotels.tsx`; public render in `CityRightsTab.tsx`. Both backfills registered+enabled in `admin_automations` + pg_cron (`city_safety_backfill` `30 4 * * *`, `hotel_safety_backfill` `50 4 * * 0`). First pass (2026-06-08): cities 0→3273 live (557 gated), hotels deduped, 0 circular phrasing, invariant verified 0. **The invariant was verified at WRITE time only, and that was not enough (2026-08-16).** `city_review_queue` is a VIEW since `20260801130000` folded the five queues into `entity_review_queue`, and a view cannot carry `ON CONFLICT` — so `run_city_safety_backfill` had raised *"there is no unique or exclusion constraint matching the ON CONFLICT specification"* every night since, silently until run tracking landed. It writes `public.entity_review_queue` directly now, with the arbiter that exists (`uq_erq_open (entity_type, entity_id, field) WHERE status='open'`); it is the only function in the schema that INSERTs into any of the five compat views — the rest only UPDATE, which auto-updatable views do support. **The worse finding was in the data, not the code: 86 published notes described a DIFFERENT country's laws** — Aden, YEMEN (death penalty) read *"Same-sex relationships are legal in the United Kingdom, and same-sex marriage has been recognized since 2014"*, Kota Kinabalu/Malaysia got Australia's, Meknes/Morocco got Canada's; the other 83 were lower-stakes versions of the same defect (Bangkok as the UK, San Francisco as Thailand). The composer was never wrong — it was given the right facts for whatever country the city pointed at when it ran (all 86 stamped `2026-06-08T06:37`), and the city→country links were corrected *afterwards* by the repairs this file already documents (`20260801142627`, `20260802090844`, `20260807100200`). **Nothing recomposed them, because the job only ever filled EMPTY notes: a derived field written once and never revalidated against the input it was derived from will silently outlive that input.** Retracted in `20260816112824` (preserved under `field_provenance.safety_notes.retracted`, `needs_attention` raised — unpublishing removes a claim rather than making one), and the selection now treats a derived note that does not name its own country as *eligible work* rather than invisible state, so the class self-heals. Detection is deliberately the crude `safety_notes NOT ILIKE '%'||co.name||'%'` text test and not a stored key, because it also catches every note written before any key existed — which was all 4,495 of them; new writes additionally stamp `field_provenance.safety_notes.country_id` — and since `20260913114500` **every** derived note carries it (4,529/4,529; the 4,390 that predated the stamp would never have acquired it on their own, because the job only revisits notes that are empty or already stale). That backfill is **gated on the note naming its current country** rather than blanket-writing `c.country_id`: a row can rot between authoring a migration and CI applying it — Kołobrzeg was relinked Germany→Poland inside a 35-minute window and served German law on a Polish city — and an ungated stamp would bless the wrong country, making that row permanently invisible to the key check the stamp exists to enable. The key detects **relink** staleness only; fact drift (a country changing its law) left prose and key both intact and fired neither detector — **closed 2026-08-30**. `field_provenance.safety_notes.facts` now stamps the eight LEGAL composer inputs and a mismatch against the country's current values is eligible work (`20260830132243`, corrected by `20260830132442`, ordering-fixed by `20261103100000`). `density` is deliberately EXCLUDED from the fingerprint: venue/event counts churn continuously, so including them makes every city with any ingest activity eligible nightly through the `cities → geo_places → search_reindex_queue` chain, and density drift changes a note's tone, not its legal correctness. **An UNSTAMPED note is not a DRIFTED note** — `IS DISTINCT FROM` is true for both, and the first draft conflated them, which would have retracted correct notes for merely predating the stamp (latent, not active: measured 4,699 derived notes, all unstamped, zero below the auto-publish bar). `fact_unstamped` is eligible so it ACQUIRES a stamp but is never retractable; `fact_drift` is both. What it still cannot catch is a law change that alters none of the eight stamped fields — a narrower residue than "any fact change at all", recorded rather than implied away. Post-fix on prod: wrong-country 86→0, criminalizing-with-published-derived-note 3→0, eligible backlog 0, 662 open safety reviews, and the retracted criminalizing cities came back as *queued for human review*, never as published notes. **That self-heal only healed ONE of the job's two exits (fixed 2026-08-19, `20260912164200`).** Eligibility gets the row selected; the auto-publish branch overwrites `safety_notes` and so repairs itself, but the ELSE branch only INSERTed into `entity_review_queue` — so a note the composer had just judged unfit to publish **kept serving while it waited for a human**. Auto-publish needs equality ≥75 and a non-criminalizing country, which makes the gap the entire sub-75 half of the corpus, i.e. exactly where naming the wrong jurisdiction is most dangerous. Observed live while clearing the `city_country_mismatch` gate: after `apply_city_country_repair` fixed six cities, GB and GR (equality 100) republished themselves correctly while Novosibirsk (RU, 55) kept a note about **Germany** and Sendai (JP, 71) one about the **United States**. The ELSE branch now retracts under the same predicate and the same bookkeeping as part 1 of `20260816112824`, in **one** `UPDATE cities` (retraction + `needs_attention` together — two statements would double the spine→search chain above on the branch that runs on most of the corpus), and the run summary carries a `retracted` count. A one-shot in the same migration covers what the recurring job cannot reach: a city with an OPEN safety_notes review row is skipped by the selector's `NOT EXISTS` guard, so a note that went stale *after* being queued would never be re-examined. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/citySafetyBackfill.test.ts`, which parses the latest definition out of `supabase/migrations/` and fails if the ELSE branch stops clearing the column.
**Country Completeness Engine (data quality, 2026-06-07):** Per-country `content_completeness_score` (0-100, distinct from `equality_score` which is legal status) + `enrichment_status` jsonb (per-field state map; `data_unavailable` is terminal → credited by the scorer, skipped by the selector, so uninhabited territories aren't flagged forever). Pure-SQL `run_country_completeness_recompute()` (nightly `30 3 * * *`, registered in `admin_automations`) scores a uniform bar across editorial 20 / core facts 18 / stats 15 / legal coverage 18 / media+geo 8 / **practical travel facts 13** (calling code, TLD, driving side, timezone, government, airports, national day) / **encyclopaedic 8** (climate, resources, UNESCO, industries, exports, imports, symbols, religions) — re-weighted 2026-08-01 (`20260801165729` + `20260801165844`) because the original bands looked at none of those columns and so reported avg 92 while 15 of them were empty on all 250 rows. **The new bands score presence only:** crediting their `data_unavailable` state (as the economic fields do, where it is earned after 3 World Bank attempts) handed ~21 free points to exactly the thin territory pages the score exists to surface, and pushed the average UP to 96.5 — the first attempt did this and was corrected minutes later. Selector RPC `countries_due_for_enrichment(limit, phase)` ranks never-scored > low-score > stale. Three fillers: **`pipeline-enrich-country-editorial`** (LLM hook+paragraph grounded in facts + LGBTI legal context; hybrid-by-confidence via `_shared/editorial-confidence.ts` — high-conf safe → auto-publish to `description`/`editorial_hook`/`editorial_long`, criminalizing destinations + low-confidence → `editorial_drafts` review at `/admin/places-editorial`); **`pipeline-enrich-country-stats`** (fills `gdp_usd`/`gdp_per_capita_usd`/`life_expectancy`/`literacy_rate` from the FREE key-less World Bank Indicators API, terminal `data_unavailable` after 3 misses). It **replaced the old `enrich-wolfram` path**, which needed a paid `WOLFRAM_APP_ID` that was never set; the matching cron `wf-enrich-wolfram-countries` pointed at a workflow row the P0-P5 consolidation had already deleted, so it never once succeeded. `20260806160000` retired it and **the retirement did not hold** — `sync_automations_to_cron()` branch (d) recreates any `admin_automations` row that is `enabled` and carries an `action->>'command'` with no matching `cron.job`, so `cron.unschedule` alone is undone by the next reconciler pass (measured: replaying #2444's statement in a rolled-back txn makes the reconciler's own dry run report `recreated: ["wf-enrich-wolfram-countries"]`). Finished in `20260813100000`, which disables the registry row — branch (b) is then a kill switch, so it cannot return; a DELETE would instead make the live job "unregistered", which branch (a) reports and deliberately never auto-kills. **`sync_automations_to_cron(false)` reporting zero drift is not evidence that a retirement took**: it compares the registry against pg_cron, and here the two agreed on the stale intent. **Retiring a cron means retiring the registry row.** `human_development_index` is not sourced here (no free per-country HDI endpoint that the World Bank API exposes) but is no longer a gap — Wikidata `P1081`, latest value by `P585`, fills it via the country-facts backfill below (133 → 57 null, the residue being territories with no HDI at all). equality_score reconciled to one shared formula in `_shared/equality-score.ts` (re-applied nightly by the existing `wf-import-ilga-data` cron). Fill crons: `wf-enrich-country-stats` (`45 3 * * *`, direct `net.http_post`) + `wf-enrich-country-editorial` (`0 6 * * *`, via `enqueue_workflow`). Admin completeness column on `/admin/countries`; `editorial_hook` rendered on the country About card.

**Country facts backfill (one-shot, 2026-08-01):** 15 of the columns the country admin form exposes were **100% empty on all 250 rows** — nothing had ever targeted them (`calling_code`, `internet_tld`, `driving_side`, `national_day`, `national_anthem`, `major_religions`, `visa_requirements`, `climate_zones`, `natural_resources`, `unesco_sites`, `major_industries`, `exports`, `imports`, `major_airports`, `national_symbols`) — plus partial gaps on `government_type` (247), `airport_codes` (156), `human_development_index` (133) and `currency`/`languages`/`timezone` (51 each, the dependent-territory cohort). `scripts/data-quality/backfill-country-facts.mjs` fills them from five free key-less sources: **dr5hn/countries-states-cities-database** (calling code, TLD, currency, IANA timezone, capital), **mledoze/countries** (languages only — master dropped `car`/`population`/`timezones`), **OurAirports `airports.csv`** (IATA codes + airport names, scheduled-service large/medium), **Wikidata SPARQL** (`P1622` driving side, `P1081` HDI latest-by-`P585`, `P901` FIPS), and the **CIA World Factbook JSON** (the 10 prose fields + a stat rescue for rows the World Bank had parked at `data_unavailable`). Writes are strictly fill-if-empty (`--force <cols>` is the only escape, for re-deriving after a parser fix) and every column gets an explicit `enrichment_status` entry — `resolved` or `data_unavailable` — so a missing value is a recorded decision, never a silent null. `visa_requirements` is permanently `data_unavailable`: no free reliable per-nationality source exists, and inventing one is travel-safety-adjacent. **Traps:** Factbook filenames are FIPS 10-4 codes inside region folders (Germany = `europe/gm.json`), so ISO2 must be joined through Wikidata `P901`; a few ISO2 codes resolve to an umbrella entity with no FIPS (`NL` → *Kingdom of the Netherlands*) and fall back to ISO2-as-FIPS **validated by country name**, because FIPS `AU` is Austria while ISO2 `AU` is Australia. WDQS 502s on broad queries — keep them lean and retry. UNESCO's own list XML 403s; the Factbook "National heritage" field replaces it. Factbook economic series are year-keyed siblings (`"Real GDP per capita 2024"`) with no `text` at the parent, so a naive accessor silently returns nothing. `national_day` became `text` (migration `20260801130324`, `countries` **and** the geo-spine mirror `geo_country_profiles` together) — the fact is a named recurring day, not a date.

**Personhood Disposition (data quality, 2026-06-07):** Detects organizations / venues / teams misfiled in `personalities` (the bare-name residue the Wikidata resolver refuses because of its `P31=Q5` human filter — e.g. *Sisters of Perpetual Indulgence*, *SF Tsunami Water Polo*, *La Montaña*) and **reversibly soft-archives** confirmed non-persons (`visibility→draft` + `review_status='archived'` + `seo_indexable=false`, the Phase-1 adult-cohort convention; never hard-deletes, never reclassifies — no org table exists). Classifier `_shared/personhood-classifier.ts` fuses name/bio heuristics (reusing `entity-type-classifier.ts`) + Wikidata `P31` (`classifyWikidataPersonhood` in `_shared/wikidata-resolve.ts`) + LLM grounded in the bio, hybrid-by-confidence: a confident Wikidata-human match **vetoes** archiving; only `non_person` ≥0.8 archives, `uncertain` → `needs_attention`, `person` recorded + excluded from future runs. RPCs (migration `20260607400000`): `archive_personality_as_nonperson(id,reason,signals)`, `unarchive_personality(id)` (existing admin restore, extended to also restore visibility/seo from `enrichment_status.personhood.archived` snapshot), `set_personhood_verdict(id,verdict,payload)`, `personalities_nonperson_candidates(limit)`. Edge fn `pipeline-classify-personhood` (circuit-broken, daily-capped, internal-secret gated) on weekly cron `wf-classify-personhood` (`30 6 * * 1`). New critical `person_nonperson_public` gate in `release_gate_checks()`. Backfill driver `scripts/data-quality/classify-personhood.mjs`. First pass (2026-06-07): 12 archived, 6 flagged for triage, 62 confirmed persons, 0 public non-persons. Audit: `docs/audits/2026-06-07-personhood-disposition.md`.

**Amenity Truth Engine (data quality, 2026-06-08):** Venue amenities were garbage — only 2.9% of venues had any, and the 945 that did held **2,020 distinct uncontrolled values** (TripAdvisor scrape noise: adjectives `casual`/`trendy`, food `eggs`/`bacon`/`salads`, meal types, location words) because `normalizeAmenities` only ran for hotels. `accessibility_attributes` was 100% empty. The fix is a controlled vocabulary + cleanup + self-maintaining engine. **Vocabulary:** `public.amenities` is now the single category-aware vocabulary (was a dead 0-row catalog) — `slug`/`icon_name` (lucide)/`kind` (`amenity`|`accessibility`|`queer`)/`category_scope[]`; 61 seeded terms (migration `20260608100000`). `venue_amenities`/`attributes` deprecated. **Normalizer** `_shared/amenity-normalize.ts` (DB-backed, category-aware, default-reject) classifies each raw term → amenity (`amenities[]`) / accessibility (`accessibility_attributes[]`) / queer (`tags[]`) / noise (dropped); anything not in the vocab/aliases is dropped. `detectLgbtqMarkers` reused for queer markers. **Engine** (`amenity-truth-backfill` edge fn, `verify_jwt=false`, X-Webhook-Secret `amenity_quality_webhook_secret`): three sources per venue — `extract` (free re-classify of existing data, auto-applies), `places` (Google structured booleans → slugs, auto-applies; **deferred — but no longer for lack of ids: since 2026-08-22, 744 venues carry a real `platform_ids.google` place id for FREE**, backfilled by `20260822055141` from the patroc import's `venue_sources.payload->'normalized'->'metadata'->>'google_place_id'` (ChIJ… ids scraped from patroc's map buttons, PR #2940) — fill-if-empty, provenance in `venue_field_provenance` (`field='platform_ids.google'`, `source='patroc'`), 5 venues skipped because dedup merges left them holding two conflicting place ids; the remaining Phase-7 cost is only the Places Details fetch itself, not the ~$500 place_id resolution), `llm` (`extractVenueAmenitiesFromText` in `_shared/ai-enrichment.ts`, constrained to vocab, circuit-broken `llm.openai.amenity-extract`; amenities auto-apply ≥0.8, **accessibility ALWAYS review-gated** — never auto-published, a wrong access claim is real-world harm). Schema (migration `20260608110000`): `venue_quality_signals` ledger, `venue_review_queue` + `approve_venue_review`/`reject_venue_review` RPCs (array-union into the column on approve), `venues_due_for_amenity_backfill(limit)` selector (empty-first); reuses `venue_field_provenance`/`venue_consensus_audit`. Deliberately stores **no** recomputed score on venues — `trg_search_documents_venue` fires on every UPDATE, so a 32k-row nightly write would storm the search sync (disk-constrained DB); the selector ranks by `cardinality(amenities)` and the admin panel counts live. Extract+LLM sweep cron `amenity_truth_backfill` cleans (free) and mines descriptions for amenities (auto ≥0.8) + queues accessibility for review; weekly read-only `run_amenity_coverage_summary` health pulse. **The schedule here read `15 4 * * *`, batch 60, daily-cap 80 until 2026-09-13 and none of the three was right** — live it is `0 */3 * * *` posting `{"sources":["extract","llm"],"batch_limit":30,"daily_cap":200,"only_fillable":true}`, i.e. the LLM source fires **eight times a day**, not once, which is what made the queue-churn defect below routine rather than operator-only. Read `cron.job.command`, not this file, before reasoning about how often a sweep runs. Consensus: `amenities`/`accessibility_attributes` added to `VENUE_FIELDS` (`_shared/venue-consensus.ts`, array union). Admin at `/admin/content/venue-quality` (`AmenityQualityPanel` + `VenueReviewQueue`). Frontend: `AmenityDisplay` (vocab-driven lucide icons via `src/lib/amenityIcons.ts` + i18n, accessibility in its own prominent block) on venue + hotel detail; `VenueFilters` amenity facet now vocab-driven. **First cleanup pass (2026-06-08): 945 venues, 2,020 → 33 distinct values, 0 non-canonical, 201 pure-noise venues correctly emptied.**

**A review queue has TWO write patterns and one of them destroys the thing a reviewer is reading (2026-09-13).** Surveying every writer of the five compat queues found them split: **skip-if-open** (`personality-link-adult-profiles`, `event-agentic-enrich`) pre-selects the open rows and inserts only what is absent; **delete-then-insert** (`city-agentic-enrich`, `amenity-truth-backfill`) DELETEs the open row and writes a fresh one on every visit. The second shape is not idempotence — it is a silent overwrite of a pending human decision, one step earlier than the 40-day `ai_validation_status` incident: there the queue collected decisions and discarded them, here the PROPOSAL is discarded before any decision exists. **No queue-depth sentinel can see it**, because the depth is unchanged — one row leaves and one arrives. Nothing else holds a copy either: an auto-applied value gets a `venue_field_provenance` row, but a **gated** proposal gets none, so the queue row IS the proposal. `city-agentic-enrich` keeps the delete (its composer legitimately re-proposes as sources change) and now stamps the outgoing value into `city_consensus_audit.details.superseded` first, so a replacement is at least reconstructible; `amenity-truth-backfill` was switched to skip-if-open outright, because an accessibility proposal should not churn at all — the claim is about a physical door, not about prose the model can improve on.

**The first survey found four writers and there were SEVEN — re-run a sweep with the right query before believing its scope.** The initial grep matched only same-line `from('…_review_queue')…insert/delete` chains, which silently misses every multi-line chain; a multiline sweep found three more, and the largest was in a family nobody had looked at. **`pipeline-enrich-village` (cron `village_agentic_enrich` `40 5 * * *`, ACTIVE) was the worst case in the corpus** — 233 open proposals (history 68, editorial_hook 67, description 67, notable_landmarks 31) and **ZERO resolved in any status, ever**, so nothing had ever worked that queue, and it holds *less* than the others: a village proposal gets no provenance row and `village_quality_signals` records only a BOOLEAN (`queued: touched`), not the field and not the value. It is now skip-if-open. **Its own comment claimed the delete satisfied `uq_village_review_queue_open`, and that index is REAL but sits on `village_review_queue_legacy`** — the pre-fold table this code has not written to since `20260801130000` folded the five queues into `entity_review_queue`; the live constraint is `uq_erq_open`. A comment naming a real index is not evidence it governs the table you are writing to — check `pg_indexes.tablename`, not just the name. Reporting needed one extra distinction here that amenity did not: `touched` gates the `skipped++` branch meaning *"nothing actionable came back"*, so it must stay true for a skip, or a village whose proposals were all already queued reads as though the model returned nothing; whether the proposal was WRITTEN is carried by the queue counters instead. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/villageEnrichSkipIfOpen.test.ts` (mutation-tested 13/13 plus a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES).

**`marketplace-tag-backfill` carried the same shape PLUS an unscoped delete, and is now skip-if-open too.** Its delete matched `(listing_id, status='open')` with **no `.eq('field', …)`**, so it removed every open row for a listing whatever field it belonged to. That half is latent rather than active — `subcategory` is the only field this queue has ever held, 1,431 of 1,431 rows — but it is a defect waiting for the first writer that queues a second field. The gated field is now a named constant used by the pre-select, the insert and the skip key, so the three cannot drift; and because this function writes no `admin_automation_runs` row of its own (the cron posts through `automation_http_post`, which files the RESPONSE BODY against the run) the counters had to reach **both** return paths — the normal one and the circuit-open early return from inside the loop. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/marketplaceTagSkipIfOpen.test.ts` (mutation-tested 12/12 plus a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES).

**THIS FILE CLAIMED THAT QUEUE WAS "the one queue humans genuinely work (613 approved / 818 rejected)" AND THAT WAS WRONG — measured 2026-09-13, `reviewer_id` is NULL on all 1,431 rows and every `reviewer_note` reads `auto-triage …`/`autotriage: …`.** No human has ever decided a marketplace review row. A decision count is not evidence of human review; **read `reviewer_id`, not the count** — the same mistake the dedup queue's "152 approved / 591 rejected" turned out to be (the autoapprove cron and a scripted bulk rejection, both `reviewer_id IS NULL`). **What the numbers actually show is a machine-to-machine treadmill**: the nightly backfill proposes, an auto-triage pass decides a median ~3h later, and **126 listings have been rejected more than once** (max 3 rounds, one over six days). Skip-if-open does not stop that, and deliberately does not try to: `uq_erq_open` covers `status='open'` only, so a REJECTED row does not block a fresh proposal, and suppressing re-proposal after a rejection is a different decision — it would also suppress a legitimate re-proposal when the evidence genuinely changes. Recorded here as open rather than fixed silently.

**The last two were DORMANT and were fixed anyway — dormant is a reason to schedule the work last, not to leave it broken**, because the defect fires the instant either is switched on and both are one flag away from running. `venue-accessibility-osm` (disabled AND unscheduled) is the sharper of the two and the only one whose delete reached **ACROSS FUNCTIONS**: it gates the same `accessibility_attributes` field on which `amenity-truth-backfill` holds ~747 open proposals, so every conflict it found would have destroyed another job's pending work — and a gated accessibility proposal has no provenance row, so that queue row is the only copy. Skipping there loses nothing, because the finding is recorded independently: `needs_attention` is set on the venue and the conflict is stamped on the enrichment state whether or not the row is written. `venue-contact-enrich` (no registry row, no cron) gates two fields (`email`, `phone`) so its set is keyed `venueId:field`, and its row counters are deliberately SEPARATE from its pre-existing `queued`, which counts VENUES that produced review-worthy proposals — folding them together would change a number that already means something else. In both, an insert error used to propagate and abandon that venue's remaining fields; it is now classified and the loop continues. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/dormantVenueQueueSkipIfOpen.test.ts` (mutation-tested 14/14 plus a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES).

**All seven writers are now skip-if-open or audited, and the SQL side was always clean.** Final state: `city-agentic-enrich` keeps its delete and records the outgoing value; `amenity-truth-backfill`, `pipeline-enrich-village`, `marketplace-tag-backfill`, `venue-accessibility-osm` and `venue-contact-enrich` are skip-if-open; `personality-link-adult-profiles` and `event-agentic-enrich` already were.

**The rejection treadmill is now closed too, and the rule that made it safe came from the data.** `uq_erq_open` covers `status='open'` ONLY, so a REJECTED row blocks nothing and the producer re-offers the identical proposal on its next pass forever. The objection to fixing it was that suppression might also silence a legitimate re-proposal after the evidence changed — so that was measured before it was believed: across **every repeat rejection on record (126 groups, 273 rows), ALL 126 re-proposed a byte-identical value and NONE differed.** So the key is **value equality**, which suppresses pure repeats and leaves a changed proposal free to reach a human. One implementation, `_shared/review-queue-guard.ts`, replaces the five ad-hoc `alreadyQueued` Sets; `personality-link-adult-profiles` is deliberately NOT wired (it reads the BASE table with an `entity_type` filter rather than a compat view, aborts the whole run on a failed read rather than degrading, and has recorded zero rejections) and its comment, which asserted the exact opposite — *"Rejected rows are not 'open', so they are never re-suggested"* — is corrected in place. `event-agentic-enrich` is out of scope for a real reason: it writes the generic `review_queue` table with `status='pending'` and no `proposed_value`, so none of this applies.

**`canonicalJson` is the load-bearing part and the reason is invisible from the code: jsonb does NOT preserve key insertion order.** It stores keys sorted by length then bytewise, so a proposal written as `{subcategory, from_rating, to_rank, rationale}` reads back as `{to_rank, rationale, from_rating, subcategory}` — a plain `JSON.stringify` comparison would therefore **never** match a stored row, and the guard would silently block nothing while every structural test of it still passed. Verified against a real row before the comparison was written, not assumed.

**REJECTED only, never APPROVED.** An approved proposal is applied to the entity, so the producer's next run sees the new state and usually stops on its own; blocking it would also stop a legitimate re-application if someone later edits that value back out. A rejection is the case where nothing about the entity changes, so identical evidence regenerates an identical proposal indefinitely. **A failed REJECTED read has no database backstop** — unlike the open read, which `uq_erq_open` still catches — so both reads report through `precheckFailed` and runs surface `queue_rejected_before`. `city-agentic-enrich` uses only the rejected half: overwriting an OPEN row is its design, and the guard question is asked BEFORE the delete, or the refused proposal would be destroyed and rewritten before anyone noticed.
