Guarded by `_shared/review-queue-guard.test.ts` (10 behavioural tests against a fake client, mutation-tested 10/10) plus the five per-function adoption suites (mutation-tested 10/10 across the call sites — including passing the field instead of the value, comparing a different shape than the one inserted, and asking the guard after the delete). **The SQL side is clean** — only `erq_cascade_delete` (the entity-cascade trigger) deletes, and the sole SQL inserter `run_city_safety_backfill` uses `ON CONFLICT`, the database-level equivalent of skip-if-open. One thing that looks like a precedent and is not: the 535 `personality` rows at `status='superseded'` come from the dedup/merge machinery (`approve_dedup_review`, `run_dedup_truth_sweep`) when an entity is merged away — **there is no tombstone path for a replaced proposal anywhere in this system.**

**`uq_erq_open` is the reason both halves are needed.** It is a PARTIAL unique index (`(entity_type, entity_id, field) WHERE status='open'`) and **PostgREST cannot target a partial index with `ON CONFLICT`**, which is why the delete existed in the first place and why idempotence has to be enforced in the function. The index still backs it: a pre-select that fails must NOT be read as "nothing is queued" (that is the defect one layer down), so a failed read leaves the set NULL, the insert is attempted, and a **23505 is classified as a skip rather than a fault** — the database enforcing the invariant the pre-check could not see, leaving the existing row intact. The old insert ended `.then(() => {}, () => {})`, so a 23505, a genuine write failure and a success were the same silence; runs now report `queued` / `queue_skipped` / `queue_errors` / `queue_precheck_failed`, omitted when zero so their presence carries meaning.

**The quality queue was 3,997 rows sorted worst-first, and 347 of them could not be approved at all (2026-09-14):** the Quality tab of `/admin/inbox` is one flat list over five `triage_src_quality_*` views. Measured: **3,997 open against 242 human decisions in the queue's entire life — 231 of them in a single burst on 2026-07-26, and FIVE in the last sixty days.** Three separate defects made it that way. **(1) "Priority" ranked the LEAST decidable rows first.** `get_unified_triage_queue`'s score adds **+15 when confidence < 0.5 and +8 when < 0.7**, which is right for staging and moderation — there a human holds context the classifier lacked — and exactly backwards for a quality row, where the evidence is already ON the row, so an unsure producer means nobody can resolve it. `quality-personality` also carries the registry's highest `priority_weight` (40), so the adult-profile probe's 0.40–0.60 review tiers swept the top: the live top five were `Sergeant Miles`, `Ty Roderick`, `Gabe Bradshaw` (pornhub), `Stevie` (xhamster) and `Michael Lucas`, all at 0.40 `display_name_mismatch` — the tier whose own producer comment says the platform's display name does NOT match ours — while 749 accessibility proposals at 0.88 and 346 criminalizing-destination safety notes at 1.00 sorted below all of it. Fixed by a **`triage_sources.low_confidence_weight` multiplier** (1 = a human knows more; **-1** = all the evidence is on the row), not an `IN ('quality-…')` list — the property belongs to the SOURCE, and hardcoding five keys is how `search_reindex_drain`'s sentinel ended up pinned to one slug out of ~240. **(2) `p_confirm` was never sent by any component.** `approve_entity_review` RAISEs `42501 high-risk destination: <field> approval requires explicit confirmation` whenever `_review_risk_blocked` holds; `triage_action` has forwarded the flag since it was written and `useTriageAction` has always had the parameter, but `TriageView.handleAction` never passed one — **so 347 open rows (346 criminalizing-destination safety notes, i.e. the highest-stakes content in the queue) threw on approve for every reviewer, forever.** Same shape as dedup's `keep_id`: reachable from SQL, reachable from the hook, reachable from no button. The view's `risk_flags.confirm_may_be_required` had likewise been emitted since that view existed and read by nothing — the third such unread flag after `namesake` and `risk_flags.namesake`. **(3) It was one list, not a dozen campaigns.** `review_queue_cohorts()` (admin/moderator, aggregates only) splits it, and reports **`decidable` separately from `n`** — a cohort of 692 where 346 need a confirmation is not the same work as one where none do. **The producer fix must land BEFORE its closer, and that ordering is the whole design.** `run_city_safety_backfill` had no `shell_status` filter, so 253 of its 692 open proposals target deindexed ghost shells — pages absent from the sitemap, from `search_documents` and from every link. It is NOT actively spamming them (its own `NOT EXISTS (… status='open')` guard means it currently matches **three** cities, i.e. it is idle behind its own backlog); the trap is that closing those 253 rows RELEASES that guard and it would re-compose all 253 over the following nights at 300/batch — the marketplace rejection treadmill again. `'placeholder'` is deliberately NOT excluded: thin-but-reachable is exactly who a safety note is for, and the migration asserts placeholders survive. Closers (`run_review_queue_close_unactionable` 06:35, verified on prod at **282 closed — 253 deindexed shells, 28 merged away, 1 closed venue**) write `status='rejected'` and **never a new status**, because every producer's idempotency keys on `status='open'` and `uq_erq_open` is partial over open rows, so any other value is re-inserted as open next run. Machine closes stay legible as `reviewer_id IS NULL` + an `auto-…:` note carrying the reason. **The adult-links cohort is undecidable by construction, and the gate that produced it MUST NOT be "fixed".** 1,735 rows (43% of the queue) came from one 2026-08-15 batch; its biggest tier, `encyclopedic_provenance` (1,169), exists to protect real-named people from a name-only match against a porn directory. Reading the provenance shows what it actually is: **`Jack` → `Jack_(given_name)`, `Griffin` → `Griffin_(surname)`, `Bobby Clark` → `Bobby_Clark_(rugby_union)`, `Cole Turner` → `List_of_Charmed_characters`, `Ryan Patrick` → `Ryan_Murphy_(producer)`, `Jong` → `Yang_(surname)`** — two hand-read samples, **42 of 42** a name page, a cast list or a different public figure, and **0 of 1,169 have a `wikipedia_url`** while 690 carry a `SKIP_<uuid>` pseudo-QID that `fetchAdultPerformerQids`'s `/^Q\d+$/` filter can never resolve. **The seductive wrong move is to narrow `encyclopedic` so a disambiguation page stops counting. Do not:** it is the LAST arm of `decideTier`, so those rows would fall straight through to **`auto`** — they already passed exact-name-match and curated-directory — and `Bobby Clark` would be auto-linked to a porn profile with no human in the loop, which is the defamation the tier exists to prevent. The gate is broad on purpose. `run_close_undecidable_adult_link_reviews` (06:45) instead REJECTS the 222 provable ones via the IMMUTABLE `wikipedia_url_cannot_identify_person()` (name/surname page, `List_of_`, non-adult occupation parenthetical), spares 547, flags the personality `enrichment_status.wrong_entity_candidate` because the junk link is its own namesake-chimera defect, and is proven treadmill-free by asserting all 1,169 sit at `state='review_queued'`, which `personalities_due_for_adult_links` excludes. `Heavy` → `Heavy_metal_music` and `Adultery` → `Adultery` are left alone: a regex cannot prove those, and under-reaching is the correct error. **A first pass got this backwards and the correction is the lesson:** seeing 1,149 of 1,169 carry `profession='Adult performer'` in our own DB, the obvious read was "the gate is misfiring on rows that are plainly performers, so auto-apply is safe" — then one `personality_sources` URL turned out to be **`Ángel_Di_María`**, a footballer, and the real finding inverted: the *personality rows themselves* are contaminated, and approving would have published exactly the claim the gate was built to stop. **Sentinel:** `review_queue_signals()` (service_role only — a DEFINER aggregate granted to `authenticated` is granted to every member) reports the human decision RATE, not depth, because depth cannot tell a queue being worked from one being abandoned; `check-pipeline-health.mjs` §16 hard-fails on a broken probe, an absent key, a missing or **auto-paused-then-recovered** closer, an unregistered field, or `unactionable_open > 600`, and only warns on depth. **Deliberately NOT built: a bulk-approve button.** `approve_entity_review_batch` exists and stays unexposed — the batchable cohorts are LLM prose (village description/history/hook, city editorial_hook) at 0.50–0.90, and bulk-publishing machine prose is the experiment this repo already ran and retired when the tag prose judge retracted 16 of its first 18 rows with 13 of them wrong. **THAT SENTENCE STOOD FOR FIVE DAYS AND WAS THEN CONTRADICTED BY `50600101100100`, which built exactly that — corrected 2026-09-19.** `run_review_queue_autoapprove` approves every open row at confidence ≥ 0.90 with no human, and it is not a button but a cron (`2-59/5 * * * *`). That was a deliberate, well-guarded operator decision and is NOT a defect; what makes it worth recording is the SHAPE. **It was sized against a ONE-OFF backlog of 1,409 rows and left running after the backlog drained** (`still_open_at_threshold` measured 0), so a one-shot drain silently became a standing publish path for whatever the enrichers emit next. **Measured over ten days: 1,725 machine approvals, of which 723 were `venue.accessibility_*` and 285 `city.lgbt_friendly_rating`** — both fields this file documents as never-auto-published, plus 7 `city.best_time_to_visit`, gated nine days earlier precisely because ≥0.8 confidence did not mean correct (the Busan Pride date). **A threshold that spans every field silently overrides every per-field invariant that is not ALSO expressed as a threshold**, and none of these were. `review_field_registry.batchable` already encoded all of it and `approve_entity_review_batch()` already honoured it — the autoapprove pass was the only consumer that joined the registry for `active` and ignored the rest of the row, so the fix is `AND g.batchable` and no hardcoded exclusion list. **One exception, `city.safety_notes`, KEEPS auto-approving**: `batchable` governs the GENERIC approver, and this function carries two measured guards for that field (unreachable entity, note not naming its own country), so withdrawing it would retract a decision made WITH those guards. `city.lgbt_friendly_rating` is demoted in the REGISTRY rather than the predicate, so one UPDATE reverses it. The run summary gains `held_for_human_not_batchable` because without it the output reads identically whether the predicate is present or dropped by the next `CREATE OR REPLACE`. **The migration deliberately asserts NO postcondition on the function's SELECT** — a text check there aborts `db push` for the whole repo on a rewrite that preserves the condition (`20810101100100`); the predicate is asserted in the repo test, where a false alarm costs one PR. Migration `99991789843323`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/autoapproveHonoursBatchable.test.ts`, mutation-tested 6/6 with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES. Migrations `50200101100000`–`50200101100400`; guarded by `QualityCohortBar.test.tsx`, `TriageDetailPanelSafetyConfirm.test.tsx` (mutation-tested 3/3 on the confirm path) and `reviewQueueSignals.test.ts` (mutation-tested 13/13).

**The inbox's biggest queue was unreachable, and the drain reported success every ten minutes while it was (2026-09-14):** `/admin/inbox` holds **5,184 open items across 10 queues**. The 2,308 `entity_review_queue` rows are the documented residue above. The largest unexamined one was **news-quality at 782**, and it decomposes into two structurally different things. **436 are a real human queue** — each carries a `quality_decision`, a `relevance_score`, a pipeline version and a stated blocked reason. **346 carry NO VERDICT AT ALL**: `auto_publish_blocked_reasons` IS NULL (not an empty array), `quality_decision`, `relevance_score`, `quality_score_before` and `sentiment` all NULL. Nothing had ever judged them, yet they sat in a HUMAN queue as though a machine had decided a human was needed — absence of evidence filed as evidence — and they are invisible meanwhile: **0 of 782 are in `search_documents` and all 782 are `seo_indexable=false`**, so neither a reader nor a crawler can reach them. **`news_quality_enqueue_candidates(200000, 3)` returned ZERO rows corpus-wide** while the cron `news_verdict_geo_backfill` (*/10) posted enqueue+run and booked `last_run_status='success'` on every firing. **An engine with an empty work list and a non-empty queue is indistinguishable from a healthy one** — the same shape as `would_merge: 0` on the dedup engines, one subsystem over. **The ceiling that locked them out was right to exist and is NOT reversed:** the selector excludes an article with ≥3 failed jobs counted over ALL TIME, added after 29,181 failed jobs across 1,159 articles burned ~$41/month re-asking questions that had already returned no answer. What it cannot express is that **an attempt which produced no verdict is not evidence ABOUT THE ARTICLE when the provider was the broken thing.** The dates settle it: `no_decision` for this cohort runs 1,585 in July, **17,461 in August**, 57 in September (corpus-wide 21,857 in August), and **only 8 failed jobs exist corpus-wide at or after 2026-09-04** — August being exactly the NVIDIA window this file already documents, where every reachable model narrates instead of answering without `chat_template_kwargs:{thinking:false}`, the default tier 404'd, and a completion pinned at `max_tokens` returned prose and was recorded as a SUCCESS. A response the parser cannot read arrives as `no_decision`, byte-identical to "considered it and could not decide". 279 of the 346 hold exactly 3 failures; the long tail holds **151 to 805 each**. **DECIDABILITY WAS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED — BUT THE DRY-RUN YIELD IS A SAMPLE, NOT A PROPERTY.** 20 of the cohort were re-run with `dry_run` (the run path returns before any article write; all 20 articles verified untouched), and **18/18 that completed came back decided at confidence 0.65–0.93 with ZERO `no_decision`**, 16 judged publishable and 2 correctly routed to manual review. **That 100% did not survive contact with the live path, and the re-measurement is the honest number.** Joining those same 20 articles against their live attempts after the epoch moved: of the 13 that have reached a terminal live state, **8 completed and 5 came back `no_decision` (38%)**, and one article the dry run had lost to `stale_reaped` completed live — **the flip is BIDIRECTIONAL on identical inputs, so it is model non-determinism, not an unrepresentative sample.** Across the whole first drain the live per-attempt yield settles near **50% (116 completed vs 117 `no_decision` of 233 terminal attempts)**, against the 100% the dry run reported. **The decidability conclusion survives anyway, which is why the watermark is still right:** the preserved 3-failure ceiling lets the selector re-offer a `no_decision` article, so at ~50% per attempt expected coverage across attempts is **~87%**, not 50% — against a cohort that had produced nothing for months. **The general rule: a dry-run yield on a non-deterministic model path is a sample, not a property — quote it with its sample size and re-measure against the live path before sizing anything on it.** **The fix is a WATERMARK and the alternatives were rejected for stated reasons:** raising `p_max_failures` forgives nothing structurally and re-asks the genuinely undecidable as eagerly as the merely unlucky; a ROLLING WINDOW is the marketplace rejection treadmill one table over, where an undecidable article becomes eligible again every window forever. `news_quality_settings.attempt_epoch` moves only when a human moves it. Blast radius measured before the date was chosen: **0 eligible → 341**, which is the cohort and essentially nothing else. **A NULL epoch counts every failure**, so the column being absent or unset can never silently widen the gate — verified on prod as the middle arm of a three-state dry run: with the new selector installed and the epoch UNSET, eligible stayed 0 and the gate stayed 340, i.e. **the code change alone is inert and only the deliberate epoch move changes behaviour.** A second, smaller lock was found alongside it: **9 rows corpus-wide carry a `quality_pipeline_version` with no verdict behind it** — a stamp asserting a run that produced nothing — excluded by the selector's *other* arm and so unreachable even with the epoch; cleared by the incoherence predicate itself, never an id list. **The sentinel's first draft was VACUOUS and the prod dry run is what caught it.** `unjudged_unreachable` also required "no attempt at or after the epoch"; with the epoch unset every attempt ever counts as recent, so against the real incident — 346 unjudged, 0 eligible — **it read ONE**. The gate would have stayed green through the exact fault it exists for, and reading the code could not show that. It now gates on unjudged AND not-offered AND not-in-flight, which fired at **340** on the broken state and **0** after the fix. Depth (`judged_in_review`) is a genuine human queue and NEVER gates. `news_quality_signals()` is standalone and service_role-only; `check-pipeline-health.mjs` §18 hard-fails on it, on a missing RPC, and on a review row leaking into search. Migrations `61000301100000`/`61000301100100`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/newsQualityAttemptEpoch.test.ts`, mutation-tested **14/14 with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES** — and one mutation first survived because the assertion matched the error MESSAGE (`review_rows_in_search … FAILED = true`) rather than the `if` condition, so neutering the branch to `if (false)` left it green. **Anchor a health-script assertion on the condition, not on the text it prints.**

**Approving a tag near-duplicate warning assigned a tag TO A TAG, 185 times (2026-09-14):** all 67 pending rows in the inbox's Tags queue are `source='duplicate_warning'` with `entity_type='tag'` — `entity_id` the flagged tag, `tag_id` the one it resembles, and `suggested_tag_name` a MESSAGE rather than a name (`Near-duplicate of "Harness" (similarity: 0.73)`). **`approve_tag_suggestions` neither creates nor merges anything**: it inserts into `unified_tag_assignments(tag_id, entity_id, entity_type)` and then recomputes `usage_count` to include the result. For these rows that means assigning a tag to a tag — a shape `unified_tag_assignments` was never meant to hold, since tag-to-tag relationships live in `tag_relations`. **Not latent: 41 such rows exist** — `Swallowing` on `Swallowing Cum`, `Appetizers` on `Apetizers`, `Cock & Ball Torture (CBT)` on `Cock And Ball Torture`, and `Protocol` on `Protocol` — each having silently added 1 to a real tag's usage count. **Approve is the wrong action for every row in the queue**, which is why the fix refuses rather than doing something cleverer: reading the pending pairs, some are genuine duplicates whose remedy is a MERGE (`Gas Mask`/`Gas Masks`, `Dominance And Submission` with and without `(D/S)`) and some are false positives whose remedy is REJECT — most sharply **`Women Who Have Sex With Women` flagged at 0.79 against `Men Who Have Sex With Men`**, surface-form similarity between opposite meanings (the `Sexting↔Stretching` 0.93 problem), which approving would have tagged the WSW entry with MSM. The guard keys on **`entity_type`, the structural truth, not on `source='duplicate_warning'`**, so a future producer emitting the same shape is caught too. The queue was also undecidable as presented — the view rendered only the message, naming one side of the pair — so `triage_src_tags` now resolves both ids (`Hip Harness ⇄ Harness`), the same half-a-pair defect the dedup queue's `? ⇄ ?` rendering had. **THE 41 JUNK ROWS ARE DELIBERATELY NOT DELETED, and that is the finding worth keeping.** The obvious cleanup — delete them, recompute `usage_count` truthfully — drops **28 tags to zero usage, six of them `active` with `human_reviewed=false`**: Ball Busting, Cock & Ball Torture (CBT), Epididymis, Femboy/Femboi, Fetnight, Pillow Princess. That is exactly the tuple `deprecate_unused_tags()` selects, so **the truthful cleanup would arm the deletion of six real glossary terms** — and the counter that function reads is the MATERIALIZED VIEW `tag_usage_summary`, **not** the `usage_count` column, so the exposure only appears after the next refresh. `deprecate_unused_tags` has no cron (measured: 0 matching jobs), so nothing fires on its own. **Check what consumes a counter before correcting it** — the same rule that stopped nine empty-bodied terms being stamped `human_reviewed=false`. Migration `61000301100200`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/tagSuggestionApproveGuard.test.ts`, mutation-tested **14/14 with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES**, and the migration's own postcondition **claims admin via `set_config('request.jwt.claims', …, true)` so it can assert the guard's OWN `22023`** — a migration carries no JWT, so the admin check raises `42501` first and accepting that as proof would have been vacuous.

**ONE EMPTY VIEW TOOK THE WHOLE INBOX DOWN FOR SIX WEEKS, AND EVERY DEEP LINK KEPT WORKING (2026-09-14, `20260914084603`):** `/admin/inbox` served nothing but *"Failed to load triage queue: UNION types text and editorial_entity_type cannot be matched"*, hiding **7,525 items** across every queue (staging 1,238 · dedup-review 1,378 · quality-personality 1,738 · quality-venue 1,197 · news-quality 782 · quality-city 829). `get_unified_triage_queue()` builds its result by `UNION ALL`-ing `SELECT *` over every ACTIVE `triage_sources` view; sixteen of the seventeen emit `text` for `content_type`/`subtitle`/`entity_table`/`status`, and `triage_src_editorial` emitted `editorial_drafts`' own enum types on all four. **A UNION type mismatch is a PLAN-time failure, so it does not care how many rows a member contributes** — `editorial_drafts` holds 241 rows and **0** at `status='pending'`, so a view with nothing in it killed a union of seventeen. **No queue-depth, row-count or per-queue check can see this**, which is why it survived from `20260801050000`. **The error named THREE columns and there were FOUR** — `status` would have raised the identical error on the next page load, so fix the class, not the message.

**IT ONLY EVER FAILED UNFILTERED, which is the whole reason nobody hit it.** Every `?queue=<one>` deep link builds a ONE-view union and worked perfectly, so every deep-linked screenshot, every per-queue count and every triage RPC exercised by another session looked healthy. **A per-queue probe is not a probe of the inbox.** `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` cannot change a column's type ("cannot change data type of view column"), so the repair is DROP + CREATE — and in this database the `public` schema's default privileges grant `anon=awd`/`authenticated=arwd` on a newly created relation, so **a drop-and-recreate SILENTLY RE-EXPOSES a view whose grants were previously revoked**; the migration re-revokes and asserts the absence via `aclexplode`.

**NOTHING CAUGHT IT AND ONE THING SHOULD HAVE.** `e2e/a11y-admin.spec.ts` visits this route on every nightly — and **an error banner has no accessibility violations**, so it passed, green, for six weeks on a page rendering zero rows. **A sweep that measures how a page is BUILT cannot tell you whether it has anything IN it.** `e2e/admin-inbox-renders.spec.ts` is the missing half: it opens the inbox UNFILTERED (the only shape that failed), asserts no banner, asserts rows, and asserts the unfiltered total exceeds one queue's — a regression that silently narrowed the union to a single view would still render rows and pass a naive check. It probes for the `<h1>Inbox</h1>` FIRST, because measured on prod an anonymous visit to `/admin/inbox` lands on `/auth`, where "no error banner" is trivially true — **without that positive control the whole file is vacuous in exactly the environment it ships to**. Admin-only, so it runs in the nightly (`e2e-nightly.yml` passes `E2E_ADMIN_*`) and skips everywhere else.

**The sentinel RUNS the union rather than inspecting it.** `triage_queue_signals()` (service_role only; `check-pipeline-health.mjs`) rebuilds the union from `triage_sources` exactly as the RPC does and EXECUTES it, so it also catches a dropped column, a reordered `SELECT` or a vanished view — proven against the broken state before the fix. It compares **base types (`atttypid::regtype`), never `format_type()`**: `confidence_score` is legitimately `numeric`, `numeric(3,2)` and `numeric(4,3)` across the set and all three unify, so a formatted-type comparison reports permanent drift. `rows` stays **NULL** on a failed probe — a zero would read as an empty inbox — and the health script hard-fails on a MISSING `probe_ok` separately from a false one, because a broken probe must never read as a healthy inbox; **depth is described and never gated**, since a drained inbox is the goal. **Its scope is stated rather than implied: it proves the union EXECUTES, not that it is COMPLETE.** A source deactivated in `triage_sources` moves `views_active` 17 → 16 and the probe stays green. The completeness check that is worth knowing and is NOT automated: **the unfiltered total must equal the sum of the per-queue totals** — measured 2026-09-19, 4,356 = 676+2+520+62+89+1384+478+240+838+67 exactly, across 10 non-empty and 7 legitimately empty queues.

**The a11y fix that had to ride along, because the bug was hiding the defect.** With the inbox returning an error banner, **no triage row had ever mounted in CI**, so axe had nothing to fault; making 7,525 rows render turned `/admin/inbox` red on `nested-interactive` (serious, WCAG 4.1.2) and `target-size` (WCAG 2.5.8) — red on the fixing PR, green on `main` only because `main`'s inbox was broken. `TriageItemRow` was a `role="button" tabIndex={0}` div wrapping a focusable `Checkbox`; it now uses the repo's documented overlay-sibling pattern. `KNOWN_VIOLATIONS` was deliberately NOT extended — that weakens a gate on real findings. **`min-h-0` on the overlay is load-bearing and no test would have found it**: `@layer base` in `src/index.css` gives every `<button>` `min-height: 44px`, which BEATS the height an `inset-0` box resolves to, so on any row shorter than 44px the overlay hangs past its own row and swallows clicks on the next one. Rows are ~58px today, which is exactly what would make the regression baffling later.

**Two process traps worth more than the fix.** (1) The inbox was fully down, so this was applied live via MCP `apply_migration`, **which stamps `schema_migrations` with its OWN call timestamp** — hence a file numbered `20260914084603`, sorting BELOW the repo's `5001*` block, which must not be "corrected". (2) Checking whether that row existed with `select version, name from schema_migrations order by version desc limit 10` returned **nothing**, because the version sorts below the `2026092*` block and **fell off the LIMIT** — absence looked confirmed, and CI's drift check caught the lie. **Query the version you expect BY NAME, never from the head of a descending list.** Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/triageQueueUnionTypes.test.ts` and `TriageItemRow.test.tsx`; the e2e spec's three borrowed string literals (banner, row `aria-label`, empty state) are pinned to their producers there, over **comment-stripped** source — the spec's own header quotes all three, and mutation testing showed a `toContain` over the raw file stays green with the executable line deleted. Mutation-tested **11/11 with two comment-only controls that correctly SURVIVE**, and one mutation first read as a survivor because `getByText(ERROR_BANNER)` occurs TWICE and a single-occurrence replace left the second standing — the replace-without-`/g` trap, now recorded here for the eighth time.

**The rest of the inbox is human work or is already being worked, and the numbers say which (2026-09-14):** `dedup-review` 1,254 has a closer (`dedup_close_distinct`, 12 ticks/hour, 0 failures) and a 6.6-day median, i.e. it is churning, not stuck. `staging` 709 (703 `ai_validation_status='needs_review'`; venues 355, personalities 315; 653 of them 7–30 days old) is a genuine human queue that humans HAVE worked — 2,053 decisions on record. `org-link-review` 62 needs a human per row by design (auto-linking a business on name alone is what this codebase refuses), though its producer has emitted nothing since 2026-08-22 and all 62 date from five days in July and August. Of the news rows that DO carry a verdict, **341 are blocked on `image_unusable` whose `image_url` is now NULL** — the 2026-09-08 podcast-audio repair nulled the column and nothing re-judges a row that already has a pipeline version, so the stated reason outlived what it described — and **315 on `truncated_body` are genuinely still short**. Both are reported by `news_quality_signals().stale_image_block` rather than fixed here.

**The schema trap worth keeping: `entity_review_queue.entity_type` is NOT NULL with no column default, yet inserts through `venue_review_queue` omit it and succeed.** There is no INSTEAD OF trigger and no rule — all five compat views carry their own `ALTER VIEW … ALTER COLUMN entity_type SET DEFAULT`, which `information_schema.columns` on the BASE table does not show. Read `pg_attrdef` for the VIEW's relation before concluding a writer is broken.

**The historical loss is unrecoverable by construction and is not estimated here.** A deleted proposal left no tombstone, so "how many were destroyed" has no answer — which is itself the argument for the fix. What is measurable: **1,011 open gated proposals across 749 venues** (accessibility_attributes 747, accessibility_notes 264), oldest 2026-06-08, and **6 resolved ever, all approved, none rejected** — a queue nothing has worked in three months, every row of which was overwritable on any of the eight daily LLM passes. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/amenityTruthSkipIfOpen.test.ts` (mutation-tested 12/12, plus a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES — this function's own comments quote the old delete-then-insert shape verbatim, which would make several assertions vacuous against unstripped source).

**Accessibility contract gap — FIVE layers, and commit was the one nobody named (2026-09-02):** `venues.accessibility_attributes` was populated on **6 of 26,867 venues (0.02%)** while 20,600 non-toilet venues carried coordinates. Not a data gap — a CONTRACT gap. `NormalizedItem` (`_shared/source-adapter.ts`) had no accessibility field, so no `source-*` could express one; `source-osm-venue` read `tags.wheelchair === 'yes'` and pushed the result into `venues.tags`, where `normalize_venue_tags` (the controlled QUEER vocabulary) default-rejected it at commit — which is why **0 venues ever carried even that**; `pipeline-normalize` never emitted the field; `_shared/venue-consensus.ts:57` had been READING the path for months. `docs/architecture/open-data-integration.md` §1.7 traces those four. **The fifth is `commit_venue_staging_item`, which writes name/geo/contacts/category/tags/images/hours/relevance and nothing else** — so with all four upstream layers fixed a staged claim still terminated there. **When a column is empty end-to-end, walk the chain to the WRITER before believing any single layer is the cause.**

**Array consensus fields can never conflict, and for accessibility that was live harm.** `kind:'array'` in `venue-consensus.ts` UNIONs contributors and reports `conflicting: []` unconditionally, so OSM `wheelchair=no` and Google `wheelchairAccessibleEntrance=true` both survive on one venue and auto-commit at high confidence; `HIGH_RISK_FIELDS` did not list the field either, and **neither half works alone** — listing it before the comparator could report a conflict would have gated nothing. `FieldSpec` now takes `contradictions` (`_shared/accessibility-vocab.ts`, mirrored in `public.amenities.contradicts` + `is_negative_assertion`, drift-tested against the migration). **It was NOT purely latent, which the doc assumed:** 0 venues but **1 event** was already publishing `accessible-restroom` AND `no-accessible-restroom` — a Dalston Superstore workshop whose own `accessibility_notes` read *"the toilet is not wheelchair accessible"*. `event-agentic-enrich` extracted `level-access-toilet` and `no-wheelchair-access-to-toilet` from one page and `normalize_event_accessibility` preserved both, exactly as designed; nothing then asked whether both could be true.

**Resolution keeps the NEGATIVE.** A traveller wrongly told a door is step-free arrives and cannot get in; one wrongly told it is not merely goes elsewhere — the errors are not symmetric. The conflict is never swallowed: the dropped pair is stamped on `enrichment_status.accessibility_conflict`, `needs_attention` is raised, and the enrichment path opens a `venue_review_queue` row. Enforced by an **UNSCOPED** BEFORE trigger on `venues` AND `events` (`resolve_entity_accessibility`), not in a writer: the column has four writers and `events` has its own, and a column-scoped `UPDATE OF` fires on the columns named in the STATEMENT, not on what another BEFORE trigger wrote (same shape as `20260807100200`). Sentinel `pipeline_hygiene_stats().accessibility_contradictions` is zero-tolerance with **no baseline**, and `check-pipeline-health.mjs` reports an ABSENT key separately from a zero count — an undeployed sentinel must never read as a clean corpus. Migrations `20261201100000`–`20261201100200`; e2e `e2e/accessibility-contradictions.spec.ts` asserts through the ANON role (what a user is actually served) with two positive controls, because "zero pairs" also passes on an empty table or a corpus stripped of negatives.

**OSM inverted from discovery to coordinate-keyed ENRICHMENT.** Discovery is saturated (five nightly `vn_fill_osm` runs: 120 fetched, 120 skipped). `venue-accessibility-osm` (`*/20`, batch 25) queries BY COORDINATE for venues already held. Identity needs an OSM id we hold or a NAME match inside 60 m — **proximity alone may never attribute an access claim** — and two same-named candidates BLOCK (fired live on *Gran Cafe Latino*, Maspalomas: flagged, wrote nothing). All four `wheelchair` values are read; `limited` gets its own term rather than being rounded to a promise or a refusal; a `no` is a measurement and is never collapsed into absence. **Measured on the first 220 probed: 12 venues resolved, ADDING 13 slug-values — `wheelchair-accessible` ×3, `limited-wheelchair-access` ×5, `not-wheelchair-accessible` ×4, `no-accessible-restroom` ×1. Only the 3 positives were expressible by the old `=== 'yes'` code**; the other 10, every negative among them, could not previously be represented at all. Corpus totals moved 6 → 18 venues and 7 → 20 values, but **quote the DELTA against the old path, not the totals** — the totals fold in the 7 pre-existing values and overstate the comparison (a first draft of this line said "7 of 20" for exactly that reason).

**Three OSM operational traps, all found by WATCHING PROD, none by any test** (42 unit tests and a 4/4 e2e passed throughout). (1) **A busy upstream is not our failure.** The per-venue path had the rule right — 429/5xx and remark-bearing 200s are "ask again later" — then the RUN recorded `status='error'` when every mirror came back busy, feeding `consecutive_failures` against `auto_pause_threshold=3`. Transient all-busy is now a success; five consecutive escalate to error so a permanent outage still goes red. (2) **A single-shot control probe condemns a flapping mirror** — `overpass-api.de` was measured answering 504 then 200 to the identical query seconds apart. (3) **Probing every endpoint after finding a healthy one burns the wall clock**: measured, overpass-api.de answered in 1.1s while kumi.systems hung to timeout, spending ~120s of a 240s budget before the first venue. Fixing (3) took throughput from **8.5 → 17.8 venues per fire (34% → 71% of batch, 2.1×)**, confirmed by two independent watchers against a baseline taken BEFORE the deploy. **A run that finds a good mirror and still writes nothing is indistinguishable from the upstream being down** — which is why the diagnosis needed a rate, not a status.

**Two process lessons from the same session.** (a) **`scripts/check-migration-versions.mjs` without `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` cannot see the real ceiling.** Local max read `20261103100000`; the true applied max was `20261127100000`, and two successive renumbers (`20261106*`, `20261111*`) both sorted BELOW it. CI has the token and says so definitively — **do not trust a locally-computed migration ceiling.** (b) **Auto-pause is a ONE-WAY DOOR, so resumption disproves it.** A 70-minute silence across three cron boundaries plus one healthy Overpass probe *from a laptop* was read as "almost certainly auto-paused"; the job then resumed unaided. A probe from a different network at a different instant says nothing about the upstream from Supabase's egress at the fire moment. **Judge a cron's health by whether its effects resume, not by a one-shot reachability check from somewhere else.**

**Tag diagnostic codes (clinical classification on the glossary, 2026-08-15):** `/tags/:slug` carries a **Diagnostic codes** band — ICD-9/9-CM/10/10-CM/11, ICPC-2, DiseasesDB, SNOMED CT, ICD-O, DSM-IV/5, ICD-10-PCS, HCPCS Level II, OPS-301, LOINC, ATC, NDC — each code linking to the issuing body. Two tables (migration `20260815111727`): `medical_code_systems` (the vocabulary AND the link mechanism — `code_group`, `wikidata_property`, `url_template`, `home_url`, `code_pattern`) and `tag_medical_codes` (row per code). Pure-SQL weekly `run_tag_medical_codes_sync` (cron `tag_medical_codes_sync` `30 5 * * 1`, registered in `admin_automations`) copies the batched-`wbgetentities` shape of `20260724260000_tag_wikidata_hierarchy.sql`; anon reads via `get_tag_medical_codes(uuid)`, which composes the URL server-side. First run: **303 codes over 95 tags, 11 systems, 0 API errors**. **"Health tag" is SELF-SELECTING — the category cannot express it:** `Sexual Health` holds ACT UP, Chastity Cage and Lipstick Lesbian (re-verified 2026-08-29), and of the **167 active tags in `Substances & Recovery` only 32 carry a diagnostic code** (re-measured 2026-09-08) — membership predicts clinical-ness in neither direction. **The category's NAME is `Substances & Recovery`; `substances-harm-reduction` is its SLUG**, and this line called it by the slug until 2026-09-08, which is a string that matches nothing in `unified_tags.category`. A tag is clinical iff its Wikidata item carries a registered code property. **Until 2026-08-29 this line offered mayonnaise, popcorn and shipping as that category's absurd members; all three now read `Venues & Nightlife` on all three filing surfaces** (`category` text, `category_id`, primary junction row). The anecdote was repaired out from under the claim while the claim stayed — prefer the ratio, which anyone can re-measure, over an example that decays silently. **MeSH `P486` and UMLS `P2892` are deliberately NOT registered** — they sit on 40/50 and 38/50 of a health sample *including yoga and meditation*; they are cross-reference terminology and would both dilute the band and mark non-clinical tags as clinical. **All 16 unsourceable systems are rows with `enabled=false` + `unavailable_reason`** (NANDA, NIC, Read codes, OPCS-4, CCAM, ICHI, ICSD, ICHD, ILDS, BPA, CCMD-3, OSIICS, DIN — no Wikidata property; **CPT — AMA copyright, can never be stored here**; ICD-9-CM Vol 3 — `P1692` is diagnoses, not procedures; SNOMED D/P/C axis — SNOMED RT, retired 2002, superseded by `P5806`), so a gap is a recorded decision rather than a silent omission. **Traps:** `P7329` is the readable ICD-11 **MMS** code and `P7807` the numeric **Foundation** id — both are labelled "ICD-11 ID", and only the Foundation id addresses the WHO browser (`#1858812010` resolves; `#GA10` silently does nothing). That is why `link_property`/`link_code` exist and why a code with no link key yields `url = null` instead of a dead link that looks live. The two statement lists are **independent with no pairing**, so a tag with two MMS codes and one Foundation id (HIV/AIDS) gets NO link rather than pointing both at one concept. `code_pattern` rejects malformed source values (measured across all 1,409 QIDs, it fires on exactly 4, every one the same upstream defect — a code concatenated with itself: ICD-10 `"B2424."`/`"F65.365.3"`, ICD-9 `"302.81302.81"`/`"302.82302.82"`); deprecated-rank statements are skipped because Wikidata retracts wrong identifiers by deprecating, not deleting. **SNOMED CT links use `https://snomed.info/id/{code}`, not the `browser.ihtsdotools.org/?perspective=full&conceptId1=` app URL** — the SNOMED CT URI Standard §3 defines the former as the persistent concept identifier and SNOMED International resolves it to that same public browser, so do not "fix" it back to the query-string form (`20260815161044`). `min(uuid)` does not exist — use `(array_agg(id order by id))[1]`, the same fix as `20260724260500`. Parse each API response ONCE into `_ent`: the naive `_map cross join _sys` shape re-parses a multi-MB body ~28k times at 18 properties. **The sync causes zero search churn and zero audit noise** (measured: queue 7→7, 0 new `tag_change_log` rows) precisely *because* it writes a separate table — `unified_tags_audit` is unscoped and `trg_search_documents_tag` is column-scoped, so a jsonb column on `unified_tags` would have hit both. Frontend: `TagDiagnosticCodes` band + `#codes` route-strip stop + a count row in the rail's "Elsewhere" card + a read-only admin `extraPanels` tab (`source='editorial'` rows are never deleted by the sync, so a curated lane can open later without a schema change).

**Tag source of law (legal citations on the glossary, 2026-08-15):** `/tags/:slug` carries a **Source of law** rail card for law-related tags, above "Elsewhere". **Wikidata cannot source this and the measurement is the whole point: across the 44 law tags carrying a `wikidata_id`, `P1031` (legal citation) is present on 0 and `P953` (full-text URL) on 3** — the UN conventions and nothing else. There is nothing to derive, so **do not build an enrichment sweep here**; anything that "fills in" citations is inventing them. `P31` is useful only as a manual triage signal (is this QID even a legal instrument?), never as a writer. Two tracks. **A — the tag IS a named instrument** (18 rows): a hand-researched citation in `tag_sources` (the pre-existing table, extended rather than replaced — it also holds ~8,700 rows of a 2026-04-27 wikipedia/wikidata backfill, which is what `is_public` separates). `source_type` gained `statute|treaty|case_law|constitution|resolution`; `instrument_status` is `in_force|repealed|superseded|partially_invalidated` and is load-bearing — "adopted 1993" with no repeal marker is a *wrong* claim (DADT is repealed; Uganda's AHA 2023 is in force *partly struck down*), and it is NULL for the UDHR and GA resolutions because a declaration does not commence or lapse. CHECK `tag_sources_public_requires_citation` makes an incomplete row unpublishable. **B — the tag is a CLASS of law** (`marriage-equality` is 38 national statutes): no citation exists, so it says so and links the ILGA per-country ledger at `/rights#<topic>`; whole-field tags (`lgbtqia-rights`, 2,093 records) get an umbrella branch to `/rights` instead — mapping `transgender-rights` to `gender-recognition` would assert trans rights ARE that one right. The mapping is a **TS const** (`src/lib/rights/tagRightTopics.ts`) because `RIGHT_TOPICS` is TS and Postgres cannot FK-constrain to it. **`/rights/<slug>` is NOT a route** (only `rights` + `rights/sources`), and the topic cards mount only after the all-countries fetch, so `Rights.tsx` re-runs the fragment jump on a timer — `requestAnimationFrame` is wrong there (paused in a background tab, which is how these links get opened). **Both JSON-LD documents must be fed:** the SPA's `useMeta` one and the separate bot-UA one in `functions/_lib/detail.ts`; the latter is all a non-JS crawler sees, and `fetchRows` uses the service role so `is_public=eq.true` must be repeated in the query — RLS filters nothing there. **Link rot is guarded** by `scripts/check-legal-citation-links.mjs` (schedule-only step in `data-quality-gates.yml` — never on `pull_request`, the failure is on third-party servers). **Status codes catch nothing on these hosts**: `treaties.un.org` and `govinfo.gov` both answer a missing document with **HTTP 200** + a redirect to an error *path* on the *same host*, so `res.ok` and hostname comparison both pass; body scanning is worse (the live Refugee Convention page contains the string "PageNotFound"). 403 = UNVERIFIABLE, not dead (ohchr/ulii/ilo sit behind a browser challenge). Migrations `20260906100000`–`20260909164500`; also repaired 4 tags pointing at the wrong Wikidata entity (`hate-crimes`→a TV episode, `don-t-ask-don-t-tell`→a film, `bathroom-bills`→a paper, `legal`→`jurist`) and `uganda-anti-homosexuality-act`, whose prose described the annulled 2014 Act while citing the in-force 2023 one.

**Tag category — THREE representations, and each reader surface reads a DIFFERENT one (2026-08-29):** a tag states its category in `unified_tags.category_id`, the denormalized `unified_tags.category` TEXT column, and a `tag_category_assignments` row with `is_primary`. **`/tags/:slug` renders the JUNCTION** — `TagDetail.tsx:184` `tag?.categories?.find(c => c.is_primary)`, fed by `fetchTagWithCategories`; the TEXT column is used nowhere on that page. **The search facet renders the TEXT** — `search_documents_index_tags` emits `facets->>'category'` from `t.category`. **`category_id` is the single lever and it moves BOTH**: `sync_tag_category_assignment` (BEFORE) derives the text from it, `sync_tag_category_assignment_after` (AFTER) demotes the old primary junction row and promotes `category_id`'s. Both are guarded `new.category_id is distinct from old.category_id`, so **writing the text alone, or inserting a junction row alone, fires no trigger at all** — nothing propagates back. That cuts both ways: it is why a junction-only filing leaves the column NULL forever and why drift never self-corrects, and equally why a **text-only** UPDATE is the SAFE repair when only the facet is stale (`run_tag_category_resync()` writes text from the primary junction and moves no page). Reach for `category_id` only when the JUNCTION itself is wrong. **Applied migration `20260829072807:28` says the TEXT is "the value a reader actually sees" and that is WRONG** — it will be read as precedent, so do not copy it. The practical warning it drew (anchoring on `category_id` moves live pages) still holds, but for the other reason: the AFTER trigger moves the junction, and the junction is what the page renders. Anchoring on text is not free either — reconciling to text means writing `category_id`, which fires the same AFTER trigger; whichever value you pick, if the junction disagrees today the page moves. Precedent for repairing by writing `category_id` alone and letting both triggers reconcile: `20261006110000` (21 rows, verified on prod with page, text and facet all agreeing).

**Wrong-entity Wikidata links on the glossary — the namesake chimera at corpus scale (2026-08-29):** `tag-enrichment-sweep` resolved a tag's identity by fetching `en.wikipedia.org/api/rest_v1/page/summary/<RAW TAG NAME>` and adopting whatever `wikibase_item` came back; its own header called that "always source-grounded", which was **false — it is grounded in a NAME lookup**. The REST endpoint follows redirects and the code skipped only a disambiguation *page*, so a redirect to a real article passed: `golden-shower` → **Cassia fistula**, `passing` → **Q4 death**, `amateur` → **Indianapolis**, `anal` → **"Analyst" the journal**, `bear`/`bottom`/`toy` → **family names**, `latex`/`gimp`/`disco` → **LaTeX, GIMP, Discogs**, plus a whole cohort of 2–3 letter tags resolved as ISO codes (`dj`→Djibouti, `cub`→Cuba, `spa`→Spanish, `cat`→Catalan, `miss`→Missouri). **`wikipedia_url` is the proof — it is literally the redirect target.** Measured by pulling all 4,437 distinct stored QIDs through `wbgetentities` and classifying P31: **1,535 of 4,772 linked tags (560 active) point at a class a glossary term can never be**; hand-read stratified samples ran 45/45 and then 30/30 genuine. **The prose may NOT vouch for the link** — `short_description`/`long_description` were generated FROM the wrong entity, so overlap between a tag's prose and the wrong entity's description is *contamination, not corroboration*; a first draft used it as evidence and kept `disco`→Discogs and `seafood`→**COVID-19** because their prose had already been overwritten. **The concept class is deliberately NOT auto-cleared**: a concept QID may legitimately be broader (`soccer`→association football, `accessible`→accessibility), and auto-clearing it was measured to destroy ~70% correct links, so its 267 mismatches are recorded for review and 21 were cleared by hand with per-row reasons. **Clinical codes are the sharpest harm**, the same shape as `trauma` in the health pass: `passing` published ICPC-2 **A96 (death)** on a trans glossary entry, `seafood` published **U07.1/U07.2**, `bearded` **L68.0 hirsutism**. **Prefer NULL to a guess** — nothing is re-resolved, because `tag_medical_codes_sync` and `tag_wikidata_hierarchy` rebuild from this identifier weekly, so a plausible-but-wrong QID regenerates wrong data forever while a null one regenerates nothing; 707 rows also had prose retracted where it contradicted the tag's own `description`. Every prior value is in `tag_wikidata_repair_audit` (+ full rows in `tag_change_log`). Migrations `20261008100000`/`20261008100100`; the repair **re-checks the live identifier against the audited one and skips any row that moved**, which is what lets it compose with the concurrent 44-row hand pass `20261007160200` (25 rows skipped, nothing repaired twice). Producer sealed by `_shared/tag-wiki-guard.ts` — **both** gates required, because name agreement is exactly what the namesake bug produces (the article `Pep` really is titled "Pep"; it is a male given name): the resolved title must agree with the tag name, AND the entity's P31 must not be an implausible class. Regression watched by `tag_wikidata_repair_regressions()` in `check-pipeline-health.mjs`, keyed on **the same id coming back** so a corrected human relink is never reported. E2E `e2e/tags-wrong-entity.spec.ts` asserts over the crawler HTML with paired positive/negative fingerprints; mutation-tested 11-fail/1-pass before the repair.

**Glossary content quality — descriptions, synonyms, related terms (2026-08-29):** one programme, five parts, migrations `20261012090000`–`20261012090500`. **(1) Synonym display is approved-only** — `useTagAliases` was the ONE reader of `tag_aliases` with no `review_status` filter, so all 15,391 aliases published as synonyms while auto-tagging (`20260910151200`) and the search bridge trusted `approved` only; 14,950 were unreviewed Wikidata sitelinks of a sometimes-wrong entity (`Neptunic` displayed ship registration "IMO 8805614"). Display now agrees with the load-bearing semantics; admin-created aliases land `approved` (the admin IS the review). **(2) The alias truth pass** (`20261012090100`): every typed alias hand-read — ~1/3 of typed-`synonym` rows were not synonyms. The dominant non-synonym is a **narrower term deliberately routed to its covering tag** (`2C-T-7`→`2C-T-X`, `Crack Cocaine`→Cocaine, `Lean`→Codeine), which is load-bearing routing, so it got its own `alias_type='covers'` ("Also covers") instead of deletion — `tag_relations` cannot hold these because the narrower side has no tag. Translations retyped `multilingual` (a foreign street name like `Shabu` stays a synonym — it names the substance, it is not a translation). **Approval = auto-tagging rule, so ordinary-word street names (Speed, Acid, Ice, Emma, Vitamin K, ART, CBT) stay `auto` forever under the single gate** — true synonyms, catastrophic tagging rules; decoupling display from tagging is the open follow-up. Three twin-tag pairs surfaced for the merge queue (RACK pair, Facesitting/Face Sitting, Reagent Testing/Drug Checking). **(3) The WRONG-SENSE class is distinct from the namesake class and the repair above cannot catch it**: title agrees AND the entity class is plausible — it is simply the generic dictionary sense on a kink/venue vocabulary page (`Vacuum Pump` under Fetishes published Otto von Guericke's 1650 device; `Furniture` under Gear published IKEA prose; ~20% of a random prose sample). Producer sealed by a third guard gate (`generic-sense` in `tag-wiki-guard.ts`): on a tag in a SENSE category (`isSenseCategory` in `_shared/tag-style.ts` — Fetishes/Dynamics & Roles/Gear/Slang/…, deliberately NOT Venue Types or Substances where the generic sense is right), the extract must corroborate the queer/community sense. Recurring detector: `tag-enrichment-sweep mode='prose'` (cron `tag_prose_pass`, `30 */2`, cursor `prose_reviewed_at` stamped on EVERY visit — the selector-starvation lesson) judges subject and rewrites voice per `TAG_STYLE_SYSTEM` (direct, factual, queer-audience, no consent-boilerplate padding — 112 rows carried "essential to prioritize consent…"); **both auto-apply paths are RETIRED and the cron is DISABLED — the judge was measured and it is not reliable.** Its first live batch (18 tags, 2026-08-29) retracted 16 and **13 of those were WRONG**: it nulled correct definitions of `soft-limits`, `outing`, `deadnaming`, `anxiety`, `genital-warts`, `safe-sane-and-consensual-ssc`, `heteronormativity`, `pillow-princess` and more, while only `maler` and `tanzer` (literal surname disambiguation lists) were genuinely wrong-subject. **The mechanism is that the model answers `wrong_subject` at HIGH confidence for prose that is merely SHORT** ("Passive sexual partner", "Teaching role"), so a `≥0.9` gate filters the model's self-report — the broken part — and bounds nothing. Of 2 rewrites, 1 was a downgrade into the exact register `TAG_STYLE_SYSTEM` bans (`ghosting`: "Ending contact with someone by simply stopping — no reply, no explanation, no block" → "Ghosting refers to the practice of suddenly and without explanation ceasing all communication"). All 18 rows were restored byte-exact from `tag_change_log.before_data`, which is the **only** reason this was recoverable and is why content writes go through an attributed actor. Rewrites now queue to `ai_suggestions`; the wrong-subject verdict is counted and logged and **touches nothing**, and is deliberately NOT queued either — at ~19% precision five of six review rows would be noise, which is how a queue teaches reviewers to rubber-stamp, and `entity_review_queue` APPLIES on approval. `tag_prose_apply` lost its retract branch at the DB layer so it cannot be re-enabled by accident (`20261018094000`). **Content writes go through `tag_prose_apply()` (SECURITY DEFINER, service_role-only), which declares `app.actor='llm:tag-prose-pass'`** — `log_unified_tag_change()` RAISEs when an UNDECLARED (`system:%`) actor modifies a `human_reviewed` row, and 79% of prose-bearing active tags carry that largely bulk-stamped flag, so a direct PostgREST update cannot do this job (found by dry-running the wave in a rolled-back txn on prod); the RPC hard-refuses sensitive/adult rows whatever the caller claims, and `prose_reviewed_at` joined the trigger's derived-columns exemption. `long_description` is NEVER LLM-rewritten (curated kinktionary/drgay bodies). Retraction only ever REMOVES; new prose re-enters through grounded/queued fill paths, and the fill prompt is now sense-anchored with an UNKNOWN exit (name-only prompting is how the wrong-sense prose got written). **(4) Related terms are curated-only**: the `relatedByEmbedding` footer on `/tags/:slug` is gone — hand-measured pool precision ~70% at ≥0.90, ~50% at 0.85–0.90, ~25% at 0.80–0.85 ("Sexting↔Stretching" hits 0.93 on surface form), so no automatic floor is display-clean. `mode='relations'` (cron `tag_relation_verify`) LLM-verified ≥0.85 pairs into `tag_relations` with a NAMED type as `review_status='pending'` (new state, `20261012090300`) — **also DISABLED, same failure, measured independently**: its `broader` arm (stored child→parent) was ~29% correct across its first 46 proposals, asserting SIBLINGS as parent/child (`Heterosexual`→`Homosexual`, `Intersex Female`→`Intersex Male`, `Chastity Belt`→`Chastity Cage`), two BACKWARDS (`Primal`→`Primal Top`), and `HIV Transmission`→`AIDS`, which conflates HIV with AIDS on a queer health glossary — every one at **confidence 1.000**. Nothing published (the display gate below), and the 46 rows are marked `rejected` rather than deleted so the unique key makes each a TOMBSTONE the verifier's `ignoreDuplicates` upsert cannot re-propose. **Two independent measurements of one rule: a self-reported confidence score cannot gate a write.** Re-enabling either engine needs a better precision number on a fresh sample, never a tuned threshold; `get_tag_ontology` is now review-aware — broader shows auto+approved (Wikidata provenance edges keep displaying), **related shows approved ONLY** (also hid the 120 unreviewed co-occurrence rows), rejected never displays and doubles as a re-proposal tombstone via the unique key. **(5) Sentinels**: `tag_hygiene_stats()` gained `alias_equals_name`/`alias_mojibake`/`refusal_prose_active` (zero-invariants; `20261012090000` deleted 47 alias==name rows and nulled 175 "No information available" stamps + 38 LLM refusal essays — a stamp reads as content and defeats both `indexable_without_description` and the thin-page deindexer, a blank is honest and self-heals) plus three advisory queue depths (`unreviewed_typed_alias`, `relations_pending_review`, `prose_unreviewed`). Four hand-verified wrong-sense rows retracted in `20261012090500` (only the wrong FIELDS — `casting`'s `description` is correct kink prose and kept, its metal-casting `long_description` cleared); E2E `e2e/tags-wrong-sense.spec.ts` mirrors the wrong-entity spec's paired-fingerprint discipline.

**raveitsafe.ch glossary comparison (2026-09-08):** compared the glossary against the five CONTACT/raveitsafe harm-reduction pages (Drug-Set-Setting, Mischkonsum, Safe Use, Gesetzliches, Substanzen A-Z). **Coverage was already strong and the reason is on the rows themselves** — `deprecation_reason` on `crystal-meth` and `nicotine` still names `admin:saferparty-substance-import`, saferparty.ch being raveitsafe's Zurich sibling, so this vocabulary was imported once already: 30 of the 33 A-Z substances present, and the two conceptual pages map onto `set-and-setting` and `polydrug-use`, both carrying accurate bodies that needed no correction. **`Nachtschattendrogen` was deliberately NOT created** — `deliriants` already covers that class, and a second row for one concept is what the slug guard exists to stop. Residue only: `2c-i`, `hawaiian-baby-woodrose` and `redosing` created, `impaired-driving` revived from the zero-usage sweep and its Wikidata-derived body replaced (it cited its own source to the reader — *"According to Wikidata, impaired driving involves…"* — and defined the term with the term). Licence stance is the Kinktionary's: only the TERM LIST was used as a signal for what is absent, no wording copied or translated. **Reviving is where the trap is: a deprecated row can still be `seo_indexable=true`** (this one was), so clearing `status` without clearing that flag publishes an unreviewed machine-written body to crawlers the moment the row goes active — the assertion covers the revived row and the new ones together for that reason. Migrations `20360101101300`/`20360101101400`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/raveitsafeGlossaryPass.test.ts`, mutation-tested 9/9.

**The honest review flag is unusable on an already-live row, and that is a general rule, not a detail of this pass.** Nine ACTIVE, indexable, `human_reviewed=true` terms had an EMPTY `long_description` — `/tags/cannabis` rendered one sentence and stopped (cannabis, tobacco, gbl, salvia-divinorum, comedown, g-hole, cocaethylene, ketamine-bladder, booty-bumping). Filling a NULL is not the LLM-rewrite the prose judge is banned from doing; nothing is destroyed and the UPDATE is guarded on emptiness. But stamping the truthful `human_reviewed=false` on the result **would have deleted the pages**: `deprecate_unused_tags` selects exactly `status='active' AND human_reviewed=false AND usage_count=0`, and seven of the nine have zero usage — the same mechanism that culled `impaired-driving`, `methadone` and `salvia` in the first place. The signal that works costs nothing because it is already set: `prose_reviewed_at` is NULL on all nine, so they were already counted by `tag_hygiene_stats().prose_unreviewed` and writing a body leaves them exactly there. **Check what consumes a flag before setting it truthfully.** Second trap, unrelated and cheaper: **`\n` inside a plain single-quoted PostgreSQL literal is a literal backslash-n, not a newline** — the corpus convention is real newlines inside the quoted string (`20261007120000`), and measured, 0 active bodies contain a backslash-n. A body written the intuitive way renders the escape sequence to the reader.

**The CombiChecker was asked for and could NOT be imported — and the reachable substitute is 81% redundant (2026-09-08):** the CombiChecker is combi-checker.ch, a tool by the **SubsDance** association in Basel; raveitsafe only links to it. It was unreachable — DNS resolves (168.119.175.170) but TLS is refused, and a `site:` search returns zero indexed pages — so its data was never seen and its licence was never established. **Nothing in `substance_interactions` comes from it, and nothing may be labelled as if it did**: `substance_interaction_matrix()` credits sources computed from the cells it returns, so a wrong `source` reaches the page footer as a false claim about who made a safety assessment. What WAS imported is rave it safe's own *Gefährliche Mischungen* PDF, a different and much smaller artifact, under the source name `rave it safe`. **The measurement is the point: the PDF is 16 rows expanding to 33 pairs, 27 of which map onto tags we hold, and 22 of those 27 ALREADY EXIST** (18 tripsit, 4 eve&rave). It is the standard harm-reduction consensus, not an independent voice, so it corroborates nothing — and the unique index on `(tag_a_id, tag_b_id)` is what mechanically prevents anyone importing it as though it did. Five rows were genuinely new (`cannabis+psychedelics`, `cocaine+methcathinone`, `mdma+methcathinone`, `ephedrine+caffeine`, `poppers+cannabis`). **The PDF has NO risk scale** — every row in it is "dangerous" by inclusion — so grades were anchored to the nearest existing sibling row rather than to its wording, and `ephedrine+caffeine` is deliberately graded DOWN to `caution` because every caffeine+stimulant pair we hold is caution; inflating one row above all its siblings is how a risk scale stops meaning anything. **No `ingestion_sources` row was added, deliberately**: §9's watched set is derived from `target_table`, a PDF has no refresher, and a row there would arm a 14-day staleness gate that can never be satisfied — which is why eve&rave and FDA have none either. Migration `20360101101500`.

**Wikipedia LGBTQ category comparison — most of the "missing" vocabulary was DEPRECATED, not missing (2026-09-08):** compared the glossary against `Category:LGBTQ_and_disability` / `_health` / `_society` (96 direct pages, 34 subcategories). 48 titles had no tag, but ~18 more existed as **deprecated** rows, culled by the same two passes: `auto: zero usage` and the 2026-06-05 orphan audit ("no entity assignments, relations, synonyms, or aliases"). **Both premises are false for a glossary term** — the finding `20261211100000` already recorded when it revived `femdom` and `voyeur` — and what was lost is not marginal: `men-who-have-sex-with-men` is the standard public-health category for HIV epidemiology and was absent from an LGBTQ+ health platform; `heterosexism`, `transmisogyny` and `bi-erasure` are core vocabulary. All carried real 323–558-character bodies, so nothing was invented; 15 revived unpublished with their existing prose UNCHANGED (rewriting it would be the banned LLM rewrite). `lesbophobia` is among them and is the reason the pass does NOT create a "discrimination against lesbians" row: the concept already had a row under its proper name, and adding a descriptive second one would have been the duplicate the alias guard exists to prevent. **Two candidates were blocked by `tag_reject_alias_shadow()` and the two cases are different findings, which is why they must not be lumped together:** `pinkwashing` is an APPROVED alias of the active `rainbow-washing`, so it was never a gap and a future comparison must not re-propose it; `queer-theory` is an alias of `queerness`, which is simply WRONG (an academic field is not a synonym for queerness, and it is typed `multilingual` as though it were a translation) — deferred to its own change, **now made, and it found the alias was a symptom rather than the defect** (see the next entry). Two details of this sentence were corrected there: there is **no** `search_synonyms` row (0 for all nine of `queerness`'s aliases), and `genre-queer-theory` is not a stopgap for a missing term — `genre-%` is a real namespace of 16 book-genre tags. **`trauma` was repaired**: its `short_description` read "Physical harm to living tissue" — the injury sense — on a row filed under Mental Health whose own `long_description` correctly describes psychological trauma. Only the summary line was wrong, so only it was rewritten; this is NOT the wrong-sense class and the Wikidata id was left alone. 20 genuinely-absent concepts created unpublished, triaged away from films, journals, TLDs and article-shaped topics, and away from near-duplicates of `bullying`, `suicide`, `hate-speech`, `neuroqueer` and `mental-health`. **Two PL/pgSQL traps, both found by dry-running rather than by reading:** a loop variable named `r` makes `r.slug` in a guard that aliases `from _revive r` resolve to the UNASSIGNED RECORD, not the table (`55000 record "r" is not assigned yet`) — name the variable `rec`; and a long explanatory header makes a text-based guard test **satisfiable by the prose while the guard is gone**, which mutation testing caught, so those tests assert against comment-stripped SQL. Migrations `20360101101600`/`20360101101700`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/wikipediaSocietyPass.test.ts`, mutation-tested 16/16.

**Queer theory is not queerness, and one enrichment run wrote the whole `queerness` row from the wrong entity (2026-09-08):** following up the alias deferred above found it was one of **NINE aliases on `queerness`, and all nine are translations of QUEER THEORY** — `queer teorie` (cs), `Queer-Theorie`/`Queertheorie`/`Queer-Theory` (de/en), `teoría cuir`/`kuir`/`queer`/`torcida` (es), `théorie queer` (fr). Not one translates "queerness". They are the Wikidata sitelink labels of **Q658022, which is Queer theory**, harvested wholesale onto the wrong tag, and the rest of the row matched: `wikidata_id` Q658022, `wikipedia_url` .../wiki/Queer_theory, and both `tag_sources` rows citing that same entity. **The prose is the live harm, not the aliases** — all nine are `review_status='auto'` and since `20261012090000` display, auto-tagging and the search bridge are ALL approved-only, so the aliases are inert; but `queerness` is ACTIVE, `seo_indexable`, 55 uses, and its summary read "Critical theory on non-heterosexual practices" over a `long_description` opening "Queer theory is a field of post-structuralist critical theory…". Both fields are **byte-identical to the deprecated `queer-theory` row's own**, which is what proves one run wrote both from one entity rather than two authors coinciding. **The 2026-08-29 wrong-entity repair could not have caught it and that is by design**: it classified by P31 and deliberately did not auto-clear the CONCEPT class (a concept QID may legitimately be broader), Q658022 is a field of study, and `tag_wikidata_repair_audit` holds no row for `queerness` — so it was never dispositioned by hand either.

**TWO SESSIONS FOUND THIS INDEPENDENTLY ON THE SAME DAY, AND THAT IS THE LESSON WORTH KEEPING.** #3553 (`20360401100100`) and #3554 repaired the same rows from opposite ends and were **mutually exclusive by construction**: #3554 nulled `queerness.wikidata_id`, which #3553's pre-flight refuses (`wikidata_id is distinct from 'Q658022'`), while #3553 revived `queer-theory`, which #3554's guards refused (`status = 'deprecated'`, exactly 9 aliases). **Whichever merged second would have failed `db push` on `main`, which blocks every migration queued behind it** — a repo-wide blast radius from two individually-correct changes. #3553 merged first and owns the identity half: alias deleted, QID and URL nulled, `queer-theory` revived PUBLISHED under a purpose-built **Theory & Scholarship** category, `genre-queer-theory` facet-clone merged in. **A migration must be SOFT ON PRECONDITIONS AND HARD ON POSTCONDITIONS.** An exact-match premise (`must be deprecated`, `must carry exactly 9 aliases`) is not protection — a concurrent session can legitimately move it between authoring and merge, and the abort then punishes the whole repo rather than the change. Verify what you are about to overwrite, no-op what is already done, and RAISE only on the state the file exists to reach.

**NULLING THE IDENTIFIER DOES NOT UNPUBLISH THE PROSE IT PRODUCED**, and measured on prod after #3553 applied, that is exactly what was left: `queerness` still served queer theory's summary and body, still carried the **eight** remaining translations (`queer-theory` was live with ZERO aliases — its own translations sitting on a row that no longer claimed the concept), and still carried the two Q658022 provenance rows. `20360401100300` is that residue and nothing else: retract the two prose fields, re-parent the eight, delete the two sources. **The retraction is CONTENT-guarded, not a blanket null** — an `ilike` on the wrong text, so a human who writes real queerness prose first keeps it — and the re-parent is **by explicit slug**, because "every alias on `queerness`" would drag along any genuine translation of *queerness* the row gains later. **`description` is KEPT**, the `casting`/`trauma` rule of repairing only the wrong FIELDS, and here it is load-bearing rather than tidy: `tag_has_prose(description, short_description)` is a hard zero for an active indexable row, so over-retracting would leave a 55-use page unpublishable instead of just thinner. **The English alias was DELETED rather than re-parented, and that asymmetry still matters**: re-pointing `queer-theory` at the revived `queer-theory` would mint the corpus's first **self-alias**, a state `20270601200100` measured at 0 and calls "junk of a different kind" — and **both existing guards miss that shape**, since the shadow trigger explicitly excludes `canonical_tag_id = NEW.id` and `alias_equals_name` compares NAMES, where "Queer-Theory" ≠ "Queer Theory" on the hyphen. **`deprecate_unused_tags()` has no cron and no `admin_automations` row** (measured, not assumed); #3553 additionally stamps `human_reviewed=true` on what it revives, which is that function's documented escape hatch. Migration `20360401100300`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/queernessProseRetraction.test.ts`, mutation-tested 12/12 — **and one of those twelve first read as "not caught" because `perl` without `/g` mutated the phrase's occurrence in the HEADER COMMENT, which the test's comment-stripper correctly ignores.** Same trap as the venue-dedup pass: verify a mutation hit the STATEMENT, not merely that the file changed.

**A correct Wikidata identifier does not make the prose derived from it correct — two independent failures, and only one is fixed by clearing a QID (2026-09-09):** `/tags/methadone` opened *"Methadone refers to a group of stereoisomers, which are molecules that have the same molecular formula and sequence of bonded atoms…"* and went on to list German trade names. **This is NOT the wrong-entity class and must not be repaired as one.** `wikidata_id` Q179996 is genuinely methadone — resolved live rather than assumed: label "(RS)-methadone", sitelinks to en:Methadone, and `wbsearchentities` returns no separate medication item (the alternatives are methadone hydrochloride, a punk band, and "methadone clinic"). What happened is that **Q179996's own English DESCRIPTION is the string "group of stereoisomers"**, and the enrichment sweep wrote `short_description` and `long_description` from it. Chemically true, and a harm-reduction glossary entry for methadone that never mentions its half-life is not a thin page but a wrong one. So nothing was repointed and nothing nulled; the prose was replaced and `wikipedia_url` filled (corroborated both ways — the sitelink resolves AND the article title agrees with the tag name, the pair `tag-wiki-guard.ts` requires). Written to match its siblings `buprenorphine` and `naloxone`, which were already active and reviewed while this row sat deprecated — all three are one clinical picture and the corpus carried two thirds of it. The load-bearing fact is the gap between how long methadone lasts and how long it *feels* like it lasts (subjective effect ~4–8h against an 8–60h elimination half-life that varies widely between people), which is why substitution deaths cluster in the first two weeks, why chasing a faded dose kills hours later, and why naloxone — which does work on it, unlike on buprenorphine — wears off long before the methadone does. `description` was serviceable and was still rewritten, because its tail ("high potential for abuse and addiction") is the moralising register `TAG_STYLE_SYSTEM` bans; that is a deliberate rewrite of correct-but-weak prose, stated rather than folded in silently.

**The same pass merged the Lavender Scare duplicate, and `merge_tag_concept` REPOINTS the loser's category rows rather than dropping them (2026-09-09).** The concept had two rows and the wrong one was live: `lavender-scare` DEPRECATED with the correct slug, correct QID (Q2896360), correct Wikipedia link and ~1,200 chars of accurate prose, against `lavenderscare` ACTIVE — a despaced scrape artefact with no prose at all, deindexed `seo_deindex_reason='thin'`, which is exactly right for a row with nothing on it. Both carried **zero** `unified_tag_assignments`, so the direction was not a judgement call: the row with the prose wins. Merged rather than deprecated because the merge mints an alias from the loser's name, so "lavenderscare" keeps routing somewhere, and because `unmerge_tag_concept(audit_id)` reverses it. **The 23505 is the thing to know**: `merge_tag_concept` deletes the loser's category row only when the winner holds the SAME `category_id`; filed differently it runs `update tag_category_assignments set tag_id = p_canonical_id`, and two `is_primary` rows on one tag violate `tag_category_assignments_one_primary_per_tag`. 20360401100100's header had already recorded this and **it was still missed on the first draft — the dry run caught it, reading did not.** Demote the loser's primary junction BEFORE the merge, then delete the inherited membership afterwards (per 20361001100100's rule: drop only the membership that is actually wrong, and a filing inherited from a contentless artefact carries no editorial intent). Refiled `Slang & Language` → `Movements & Milestones`: a 1950s federal purge is not a slang term, and the target is where its siblings already sit (`stonewall`, `stonewall-riots`, `daughters-of-bilitis`), not the obvious-sounding `History & Rights`, which measured out as mostly demonyms. **Both revives set `seo_indexable` EXPLICITLY** — 20361001100100 is the precedent, where a revive that moved status and review flags but not that column left eight published terms invisible to crawlers with nothing to self-heal them — and both set `human_reviewed=true`, the documented escape hatch from `deprecate_unused_tags`, since both rows have `usage_count=0` and would otherwise be re-offered to the next sweep. Migration `20361124161700`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/methadoneLavenderScare.test.ts`, mutation-tested 12/12 — **and once again one mutation first read as "not caught" because the phrase occurs in BOTH the header comment and the SQL string, and `perl` without `/g` hit the comment**, which the test's comment-stripper correctly ignores.

**Berlin harm-reduction and anti-violence comparison — it found MISFILING, not gaps (2026-09-14):** compared the glossary against sidekicks.berlin (a project of Schwulenberatung Berlin — `/en/sex/`, `/en/drugs/`, `/en/advice-services/`, plus its Sex Acts & Fetishes index and the Darkroom Cruising Vocabulary) and six MANEO artifacts (the K.O.-Tropfen handout, the Taschendiebe, IPV, Schutzbefohlene and Zwangsverheiratung flyers, and the Gedächtnisprotokoll tips page). **Substance and STI coverage was already near-complete** — every one of sidekicks' 15 substance pages and 9 STI pages resolved to a live tag or an approved alias — so the comparison's value was almost entirely in what it found filed against the wrong thing. **`darkroom` IS THE PHOTOGRAPHY SENSE, on 176 uses — the highest-usage wrong-sense row found so far.** `wikidata_id` Q601413 (verified live: label "darkroom", description "workshop used by photographers make prints and otherwise handle photographic film"), `wikipedia_url` en/Darkroom, and a `description` opening *"A darkroom is used to process photographic film, make prints and carry out other associated tasks"* — on a row filed `Practices & Play`, flagged `is_adult`. **Both existing guards pass it**: `tag-wiki-guard.ts` requires the resolved title to agree with the tag name, which here it does exactly, and the entity class (a room) is entirely plausible, so the `generic-sense` arm has nothing to catch. `seo_indexable` was already false, so no crawler saw it; site visitors and site search did. Identifier NULLED rather than repointed (the weekly `tag_medical_codes_sync` / `tag_wikidata_hierarchy` rebuild is why), but prose **replaced rather than retracted** — the row is ACTIVE and rendering, so a retraction leaves a 176-use page blank where a replacement leaves it correct. **`stis-stds` published a scrape timestamp as its definition** — `description` read, in full, *"Updated May  4, 2023  9:52pmUpdated May  4, 2023  9:52pm"* — ACTIVE and `seo_indexable`, a facet-clone duplicate of `sti` (usage 95, Q12198); merged into it. **A merge is what removes a row from search, measured: 0 of 288 merged and 0 of 5,271 deprecated tags are in `search_documents`, against 4,725 of 4,725 active**, so the loser's leftover `seo_indexable=true` is inert rather than sloppy. **`safer-injecting` carried seven wrong-entity aliases**, all harvested from one acronym: "Saint Louis Art Museum" in six spellings (SLAM) plus **"moshing"** (slam dancing) — sitting in the same list as the real `Slam` and `Slamming`;
