all `auto`, so inert for display and auto-tagging, and deleted on that basis. **`spiking` had an empty body and the material for it was in the MANEO handout** (a NULL fill, not the banned rewrite): the Berlin case series of ~181 cases 1995–2012, the bystander signature, the short detection window for urine and blood, and the recommendation to write a record while memory is fresh. **The German `K.O.-Tropfen` was moved off `ghb`, and that is a correction rather than a preference** — the term names the covert ADMINISTRATION, not a substance, and MANEO's own series records sedatives and medicines alongside G, including a 2001 homicide by methadone overdose. `tag_aliases.alias_slug` is **globally UNIQUE**, so the row has to be UPDATEd across; an INSERT fails and leaving both splits one phrase across two tags. `intimate-partner-violence` and `domestic-violence` were both **deprecated by the 2026-06-05 orphan audit again** (the same false premise as `femdom`/`men-who-have-sex-with-men`), both with correct Wikidata (Q5153528 / Q156537, verified), and revived unpublished into `Violence & Hate`; IPV's NULL `description` was filled with the repertoire MANEO documents and general definitions miss — outing threats, HIV-status lies, pressure into unwanted or unprotected sex, and phone/inbox/finance surveillance. `forced-marriage` was **absent under every spelling** and is created unpublished with `Zwangsverheiratung`/`Zwangsheirat` aliases. `watersports` pointed at **Q61065, aquatic sports**, with a body about swimming and kayaking over its own correct urine-play `description`; only the wrong FIELDS were cleared (the `casting`/`queerness` rule) and it was NOT revived, because `piss-play` holds the same concept and choosing between them is a merge decision.

**The systemic finding is a trigger gap, and the dry run found it — reading the schema did not.** `trg_sync_tag_category` is **BEFORE UPDATE** and `trg_sync_tag_category_after` is **AFTER UPDATE OF category_id**; neither fires on INSERT. So a tag created with `category_id` set derives no `category` TEXT and mints no `tag_category_assignments` row — and since `/tags/:slug` renders the JUNCTION while the search facet renders the TEXT, such a row is uncategorised on its own page and categorised in search. Measured corpus-wide: **194 active tags carry a `category_id` with no junction row**, including all three terms `20360101101300` created. The first dry run of this pass produced exactly that shape, and **the obvious postcondition (`category_id is null`) would have passed it** — assert all three representations, not the lever. The 194 are NOT repaired here: that is its own change with its own per-row category decision. **Five things were deliberately NOT done**, each measured rather than assumed: **`bdo`** is not a gap (sidekicks titles its page "GHB / GBL / BDO", but BDO is already an APPROVED `covers` alias of `ghb` whose own prose explains the conversion); **pickpocketing** is general crime prevention, not queer vocabulary, and its one darkroom-specific line (valuables belong at the bar) was folded into `darkroom` instead; **Gedächtnisprotokoll** has no established English term and minting one is inventing vocabulary, so its substance was folded into `spiking` instead; the chemsex sense of **`chill`** cannot be added as an alias on `chemsex` because "Chill" is an existing tag NAME and `tag_reject_alias_shadow()` rejects it — the live `chill` row is an empty `Venue Types` stub, left open rather than half-fixed; and **`sti-testing`** stays deprecated because reviving it means rewriting prose that cites its own source to the reader (*"According to a scientific article published on August 21, 2013"*), which is a separate decision. Migrations `50100101100000`/`50100101100100`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/berlinHarmReductionGlossaryPass.test.ts`, mutation-tested 19/19 with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES.

**Rope/kink glossary comparison — the prose described a different subject while the row's own `description` was right (2026-09-14):** compared the glossary against seven external glossaries (rebornropes.com's A-Z rope glossary, "A is for" and positions pages; skillfullybound.com; fetbomb.com; naturallynaughty.shop's Kinkipedia; lioness.io). **The general kink vocabulary was already in good shape** — impact/sensation/temperature/wax/knife/breath/fire/blood play, protocol, high protocol, collaring, subspace, sub drop, munch, play party, dungeon monitor, primal/pony/puppy play, objectification, human furniture, key holder, chastity device and free use all ACTIVE, and the consent layer complete (consent, safe word, hard/soft limits, SSC, PRICK, RACK, safe call, vetting, spotter) — so, as with the Berlin pass, the value was almost entirely in what was filed wrong.

**THE CLASS IS NEW AND NO EXISTING DETECTOR COULD SEE IT: `description` is CORRECT and `short_description`/`long_description` describe a different subject.** `/tags/suspension` — active, `seo_indexable`, usage 3 — led with "Partially or fully lifting a person off the ground using rope, chains, or harnesses" and then ran four sentences explaining that *"Suspension is an action taken by a website's administration to restrict or block a user's account."* `src/pages/TagDetail.tsx` renders `description` as the lead paragraph and `long_description` as the body directly beneath it, so the reader gets a correct one-liner followed by prose about something else; `functions/_lib/detail.ts` selects all three columns, so crawlers get it too. Twenty-six rows: `grounding` published a ship running aground on a kink aftercare term, `redding-out` (calling the red safeword) published "a social event ... for the LGBTQ+ community" while `seo_indexable`, `rope-eel` a fish of the family Moringuidae, `service-rigger` a person who sets up stage lighting, `domspace` "a community or physical space" for a headspace, `rope-model` an **invented** "framework used to understand and communicate about sexual boundaries" (no such model exists), `harness` body armor (Q485027), `rope-bottom` a **drawing in the National Gallery of Art** (Q64555914, "Rope Bottom Chair", NGA 16762), `bottom` "Family name or surname", `pincushion` "Small cushion for storing pins or needles", `fucktoy` "2025 American surrealist film", `dependency` "Detached secondary building of a residential complex", and `bondage`'s body ended *"...can be found in various sources, including the 2014 edition of Wikidata and Wikipedia"* — citing its own source to the reader, off a QID (Q61286643) that is a bibliographic EDITION.

**Dated from `tag_change_log`, not inferred.** One enrichment sweep on **2026-04-27 between 16:55 and 18:56** wrote all of it: the trigger set `wikidata_id`, then the same sweep wrote prose from that entity 40–90 minutes later. Rows that never had a QID (`rope-eel`, `redding-out`, `service-rigger`, `domspace`, `rope-model`, `spotter`) got prose from a **name-only** lookup — the namesake mechanism `tag-wiki-guard.ts` was later built to seal at the producer, which does nothing for prose already written.

**WHY THE 2026-08-29 REPAIR MISSED IT, AND THE PART THAT REPEATS A LESSON ALREADY IN THIS FILE.** `20261008100000` classified by the **P31 of the stored QID**, and **3,179 of 4,727 active tags carry no QID at all**, so it was structurally blind to them. For the two rows it did touch it removed the identifier and left the prose: `spotter` (Q122307053, a 2018 video game) on 2026-08-29, and `suspension` on 2026-09-04 under a hand-written reason that diagnosed it exactly right — *"Rope suspension. The QID is an administrative account ban."* `tag_change_log` shows `short_description` **unchanged** across both writes. That is `queerness`'s rule (`20360401100300`) on a third entity class: **nulling the identifier does not unpublish the prose it produced.** `tag_wikidata_repair_regressions()` watches the identifier coming back; nothing watched the text.

**MEASURED RESIDUE, and it is an upper bound rather than a defect count.** **364 active tags still carry the `short_description` a now-disowned entity produced, 339 of them `seo_indexable`.** A hand-read sample of 24 came back **11 clearly wrong, 3 borderline, 10 genuinely fine** — the repair deliberately retracted only prose that CONTRADICTED the row's own description, so some of what survives is correct. Roughly 45%, about 165 rows; 26 are repaired here and the rest is unexamined, not clean. New sentinel `tag_disowned_prose_signals()` (standalone — `tag_hygiene_stats` is a long CREATE OR REPLACE and a new key there is a merge-collision surface; service_role only) reports `probe_ok` and `audit_rows` **separately from the counts**, because an empty audit table, a revoked grant and a cleaned corpus otherwise all give the same reassuring zero. `check-pipeline-health.mjs` **warns on the count and hard-fails on growth**: the backlog can only be worked down by hand, so a zero-invariant would be red from the day it shipped and get scrolled past, while growth means a producer is writing NEW prose from a disowned entity. **Re-measure with a fresh hand-read sample before believing the 45%, including this sentence.**

**Prose REPLACED, never retracted, and identifiers NULLED, never repointed.** Every row is active and rendering, so nulling would leave a live page thinner instead of correct (the `methadone`/`darkroom` rule). `long_description` is nulled only where no full body was written, which is safe and **asserted** rather than assumed: `enforce_tag_thin_page_gate` reads `tag_has_prose(description, short_description)` only. **`crotch-rope` and `breast-bondage` are the deliberate exception — their QIDs are KEPT because they are RIGHT**; what was wrong is that the prose was copied from Wikidata's own gendered descriptions ("a woman's waist ... the labia ... the female genitals", "a woman's breasts"), which on this platform writes most of the audience out of their own glossary. Correct entity, narrow prose: fix the prose, keep the identifier. **`steer` and `warlord` were deliberately NOT touched** — both are wrong for a Fetishes tag but the INTENDED sense cannot be established from the row, and guessing a sense is how this class arose.

**The 2026-06-05 orphan audit took thirteen more core terms, and reading them before reviving changed the plan.** Deprecated with no active row holding the concept: `negotiation`, `consent-violation`, `flogging`, `whip`, `obedience`, `orgasm-control`, `predicament-bondage`, `play-piercing`, `mummification`, `hemp-rope`, `anal-play`, `pvc`, `g-spot` — the same false premise ("orphan tag: no entity assignments") already recorded for `femdom`/`voyeur` and `men-who-have-sex-with-men`, now the **third** time. **`description` is correct on all thirteen and the PUBLISHED prose is the generic or wrong sense on eight**: `negotiation`'s body is *business* negotiation ("resolve differences, gain an advantage") on the consent term, `whip` is horse training ("commonly used on horses to give subtle cues") with `description` the literal string "Toys tag", `pvc` ends *"PVC is also the name of a Norwegian musical group"*, `mummification` is "Preserved dead body or animal", `hemp-rope` is "sailing and landscaping", `obedience` is Milgram social psychology, and `flogging`/`anal-play` carry the "it's essential to prioritize consent" padding `TAG_STYLE_SYSTEM` bans. **A blanket revive would have published all eight — reviving is the cheap half, reading what you are about to republish is the half that matters.** Revived **unpublished** (`active`, `seo_indexable=false` set EXPLICITLY, per `20361001100100`): the gap being closed is SITE SEARCH (0 of 5,271 deprecated tags are in `search_documents` against 4,725 of 4,725 active), and putting thirteen pages into the crawler index is a separate decision. `human_reviewed=true` is load-bearing, not decorative — `deprecate_unused_tags()` selects exactly `status='active' AND human_reviewed=false AND usage_count=0` and all thirteen are usage 0. **The dedupe removed 21 of 34 candidates**, each checked individually: the whole `intimate-*` family (9 rows, empty prose, every one with a live active twin — Fisting u=31, Leather u=17, Spanking u=9) is correctly deprecated; `watersports`/`dom-space`/`ballbusting`/`latex`/`rubber` have active twins; `enema`/`hanky-code`/`kinbaku`/`cbt` are approved aliases of active rows. **The deprecated `cbt` row is Q1147152, cognitive behavioural therapy** filed under Community Life & Support — a different concept sharing an acronym with the active `Cock & Ball Torture (CBT)`, correctly separated, recorded so the next comparison does not re-propose it as a missing kink term.

**The real gap was rope TECHNIQUE and rope SAFETY, and the corpus shape shows why it was invisible.** `Practices & Play` holds Rope Bat, Rope Eel, Rope Gremlin, Rope Kitten, Rope Menace, Rope Monkey, Rope Princess, Rope Slut, Rope Dummy, Rope Toy and Rope Coven — persona coinages, most at usage 0 — while a reader could not look up a single column tie, a hip harness, a partial suspension or nerve compression. **The glossary over-indexed on identity words and under-indexed on the things that leave marks.** 16 terms created (single/double column tie, futomomo, kikkou, chest harness, hip harness, partial suspension, semenawa, newaza, nerve compression, rope marks, safety shears, jute, plus the three asymmetries: `kitten-play` where pony and puppy play were both active, `day-collar` where `Collaring` was, `topspace` where `subspace` and `domspace` were), `hardpoint` revived from prose that defined the term with the term (`description` "Toys tag", `long_description` "Hardpoint"), and 11 aliases. **Most of the Japanese vocabulary was NOT created, because the concepts are already here under English names**: takatekote/gote IS `box-tie` (whose body already names takate kote and the radial nerve), matanawa IS `crotch-rope`, hishi IS the diamond pattern of `karada`, nawashi IS `rigger` — aliases, not rows. **The ~200 further Japanese tie names on the rebornropes index are deliberately not created**: named figures within one tradition are not glossary concepts, and minting 200 one-sentence rows is how the "rope <animal>" cohort happened in the first place. Aliases are `approved`, not `auto` (since `20261012090000` display, auto-tagging and the search bridge are ALL approved-only, so an `auto` alias routes nothing), and typed `synonym` rather than `multilingual` by the same call `20261012090100` made for `Shabu` — these are the names the practice uses, and "box tie" is the translation, not the original. **The same sweep contaminated `tag_relations`, and nothing had ever looked there.** 53 relations sit on tags whose Wikidata entity a repair later disowned; **21 of those are `broader` and currently DISPLAY** (`get_tag_ontology` shows broader at auto+approved). All 21 were read by hand, and the result is the argument against a blanket clear: **17 are CORRECT.** The taxonomic parent usually survives a wrong QID because it was chosen from the tag's real category context rather than from the entity — `salvia-divinorum`→`psychedelics` and `tobacco`→`stimulants` are right even though both QIDs were botanical species, and `fentanyl-test-strips`→`harm-reduction` is right even though its QID was a journal article. **What separates the three that are wrong is a statable signature: the parent makes sense only for the DISOWNED entity, not for the tag.** `suspension`→**`punishment`** (the QID was "account suspension", and a suspension IS a punishment in that sense — and the prose fix alone would have left this standing, which is the point); `aftercare`→**`recreational`** (the QID was "after-school activity", which is recreation — and `aftercare` is core consent vocabulary, active and indexable); `vers`→**`genre-poetry`** (the QID was "poem" — *vers*, as in VERSE — on the queer term for versatile, indexable). Marked `rejected` rather than deleted, because the UNIQUE key on `(source, target, relation_type)` makes a rejected row the TOMBSTONE that stops the verifier re-proposing it. **Two were read and deliberately left alone**: `schoolgirl`→`student` is borderline, and a borderline call is not grounds for a tombstone that blocks it forever; `workshop`→`workshops` is junk but it is a singular/plural DUPLICATE-TAG problem, and rejecting the link would paper over the duplicate. **22 curated links added** (6 `broader`, 16 `related`, all `approved` — `related` displays approved-only, so an `auto` row would be stored and never rendered). `broader` is restricted to unambiguous "X is a kind of Y" because `tag_relation_verify` was measured at ~29% on that arm and disabled for asserting SIBLINGS as parent/child: `jute`/`hemp-rope` and `chest-harness`/`hip-harness` and `topspace`/`subspace` are siblings and are `related`, never one under the other. **The dry run exposed a defect in the migration's own postconditions**: `on conflict do nothing` is idempotent, so the inserts can add NOTHING and still succeed — the first run reported "20 of 22 added" and establishing that the other two merely pre-existed took a separate query. **Counting insertions proves nothing on a re-run; assert the END STATE** — that every link exists in the exact direction — which is what the file does now.

Migrations `50500101100000`–`50500101100400`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/ropeGlossaryPass.test.ts`, mutation-tested **41/41** across two rounds, each with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES. **All three data migrations were dry-run on prod in a transaction forced to roll back** (26 rows corrected, 13 revived, 16 created + 11 aliases, every postcondition passing) — `execute_sql` runs one call as a single transaction, so appending a `raise exception` validates a migration against live data and commits nothing.

**Eight canonical terminology glossaries — the comparison found a CULL, not a gap (2026-09-14):** compared `unified_tags` against the Safe Zone Project vocabulary, Wikipedia's *LGBTQ slang*, Stonewall's term list, UH Hilo's queer terminology page, the UConn Rainbow Center dictionary (PDF), Florence Ashley's *Queering Our Vocabulary*, Studio Inclusie's *Queer Glossary* (PDF) and SLCC's terminology sheet (PDF). **458 extracted headwords → 454 distinct slugs, which match the corpus 151 active / 78 deprecated with a real body / 18 deprecated and empty / 11 alias-only / 185 unmatched.** Those five numbers are the MECHANICAL ones and are re-derivable from `all-terms.json` by slugifying and joining; quote them rather than the hand-triaged 71/51/73 an earlier draft of this entry carried, which summed to 195 and so could not have been a split of 458. The 185 unmatched is not 185 gaps: it is mostly extraction residue (`a-queer-glossary`, `facebook`, `you`, `or`, `including`, `category`) and compound headwords the sources join with a slash (`fag-faggot`, `ftm-f2m`, `latine-latinx`, `shemale-or-she-male`), which is why the triage is by hand and why it ends at 4 creations rather than 185. **48 of the 78 deprecated-with-body rows are revived**; the other 30 were read and left: 3 alias-shadowed, ~12 a compound or abbreviated SPELLING of a concept already live (`fag-faggot`, `ftm-f2m`, `same-gender-loving-sgl`, `sex-reassignment-surgery-srs`, `binders`, `aro`, `binary`, `fluid`, `transition` — alias candidates, not terms), and ~15 a generic word or scrape residue whose filing is its own decision (`sex` and `fruit` both sit under **Fetishes**; `clock` is already retracted as the wrong sense). **UCSF's glossary (lgbtq.ucsf.edu) was NEVER READ and nothing is attributed to it** — curl, `r.jina.ai` (which returned Cloudflare's "Attention Required!" interstitial), WebFetch and headless Chromium all failed; `$HTTPS_PROXY/__agentproxy/status` reported `enabled: true, bundleCoversEveryHost: true`, so the block is on their side, not an environment fault. A source that could not be read is recorded as unread rather than quietly dropped from the source list, and the guard test asserts the string `ucsf` appears nowhere in either migration.

**The finding is that the same two blind sweeps culled CORE vocabulary, for the third time on record.** `auto: zero usage` and the 2026-06-05 orphan audit ("no entity assignments, relations, synonyms, or aliases") had already been shown false premises for a glossary term when they took `femdom`/`voyeur` (`20261211100000`) and then `men-who-have-sex-with-men`/`heterosexism`/`transmisogyny`/`bi-erasure` (`20360101101600`). This pass finds them holding **`gender-binary`, `sexual-attraction`, `romantic-attraction`, `romantic-orientation`, `microaggression`, `cissexism`, `monosexism`, `assigned-sex`, `intersexuality`, `pansexuality` and `kinsey-scale`**. Corroboration across the 48 is **33 in one reference, 10 in two, 5 in three** (`gender-binary`, `hermaphrodite`, `romantic-attraction`, `skoliosexual`, `transvestite`) — counted on EXACT headword, so a concept the sources spell differently is undercounted, and a low count is not evidence a term is marginal. A first draft of this line claimed five of the eleven named above sit in three or more references; the real number is two, which is why the distribution is quoted instead of a flattering subset. 48 revived unpublished with their existing bodies UNCHANGED (rewriting them would be the LLM rewrite both auto-apply paths are retired for). **41 of the 48 were `seo_indexable=true` WHILE deprecated**, so clearing `status` without clearing that flag publishes an unreviewed machine-written body to crawlers the instant the row goes active — the trap `20360101101300` recorded when it revived a single row, here on 41 of them at once.

**Reviving the slurs is the HOUSE CONVENTION, not a new decision, and that was measured before acting.** `faggot`, `shemale` and `breeder` are already ACTIVE with `is_sensitive=true`, `is_adult=true`, `seo_indexable=false` — so `tranny`, `muff-diver`, `bulldagger`, `batty-boy`, `bull-dyke` and `copenhagen-capon` revive on exactly those terms. A glossary that documents the word a reader was called is doing its job; deindexing is what keeps that from being publication.

**Three candidates are blocked by `tag_reject_alias_shadow()` and each is a different answer, so they must not be lumped together** — `berdache` is an alias of `two-spirit`, `deadname` of `deadnaming`, `genderfluid` of `gender-fluid`. None is a gap; all three are the guard working. The postcondition asserts they are still deprecated, so a later pass cannot re-propose them.

**Most of the 73 "absent" terms were NOT gaps — they are spellings the glossary already holds under another name, and reading them one by one is what kept this small.** 18 aliases route `Transgender Man`/`Transman`, `Transgender Woman`/`Transwoman`, `Transitioning`, `Crossdressing`, `HRT`/`Hormone Replacement Therapy`, `Sex Reassignment Surgery`, `Gender Reassignment` (the UK Equality Act's protected-characteristic wording), `Gender Affirming`, `Sex Assigned At Birth`, `Hermaphroditism`, `LGBTQIA`, `Bare-backing` and `Latine` onto live rows. **`AFAB` and `AMAB` take `alias_type='covers'`, not `synonym`**: they are not other names for "assigned sex", they are its two VALUES — the narrower-term-routed-to-its-covering-tag relation `20261012090100` created that type for (`Crack Cocaine` → Cocaine) — and `tag_relations` cannot hold them because neither narrower side has a row. **`latinx` was revived rather than a `latine` row created**: the English coinage already had a deprecated row with a 377-character body, and the candidate list simply never matched it because the reference that carries the concept spells it `latine`. **`lezbo` and `lezzie` are DELIBERATELY NOT aliased** — an approved alias is an auto-tagging RULE as well as a displayed synonym, so pointing two derogatory diminutives at `lesbian` tags anything containing them as the identity itself; the routing gain is small, the failure mode is not, and the postcondition refuses them corpus-wide so the decision cannot be quietly reversed.

**Four terms are genuinely absent and are created unpublished with authored prose** — `internalized-oppression`, `institutional-oppression` and `gender-cues` (each carried by ≥2 of the eight references, none with a parent to alias onto) plus `dyadic`, which is the counterpart to `intersex`: the glossary carried one half of that pair and not the other, which is exactly the asymmetry the word exists to remove. **All three category representations are set by hand on every new row**, because neither category trigger fires on INSERT — the 194-row finding of `50100101100100`, which is still not repaired.

**The migrations were restructured to be SOFT ON PRECONDITIONS AND HARD ON POSTCONDITIONS, and the first drafts were not.** Both opened with exact-match premises — *all 48 must be deprecated*, *exactly 48 revived*, *no new slug may already exist* — and an exact-match premise is not protection: a concurrent session that legitimately revives or merges one row between authoring and merge turns the abort into a `db push` failure on `main` that blocks **every** migration queued behind it, which is the repo-wide blast radius `20360401100100` recorded from two individually-correct changes. Every guard now REPORTS and EXCLUDES, and only a category slug that does not exist still aborts — a typo in the file itself, which no later corpus state repairs. **The postcondition then had to be inverted, and the obvious form is vacuous**: counting rows in a BAD state returns zero for a slug that has gone missing from the corpus entirely, which is precisely what the softened guards now let through, so it counts the REACHED state positively (`if v_bad <> 48`).

**Three extraction traps, none of them about the glossary.** **Wikipedia's rendered HTML is not a term list** — the article body yielded **0** `/wiki/` links, `<b>` gave 3 terms and `<i>` gave 10 junk ones, and `api.php` answered **429**; `index.php?title=LGBTQ_slang&action=raw` with a real User-Agent plus a bullet parser (`* term – definition`, stripping `[[link|display]]`, refs and templates) gave **121**. **pypdf cannot be installed here** — the system python's `cryptography` raises a `pyo3_runtime.PanicException` — so the three PDFs were read with the npm `pdf-parse` package. And **a category slug guessed from its display name is wrong**: `gender` and `expression-style` do not exist; the real slugs are **`gender-identity`** ("Gender") and **`expression-presentation`** ("Expression & Style"), which the dry run caught and reading did not.

Migrations `50300101100000`/`50300101100100`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/canonicalGlossaryPass.test.ts`, mutation-tested **24/24 with two comment-only controls that correctly SURVIVE** — and **two mutations first read as survivors because the perl pattern silently did not match** (a 14-space run where the file has 15, a 7-space indent where the file has 6), which is indistinguishable from a guard that does not fire. Verify a mutation APPLIED before believing it was missed; this is the fourth entry in this file to record that same check.

**Seven HIV/STI clinical references — the glossary had the vocabulary and had filed it wrong (2026-09-14):** compared `unified_tags` against the CDC HIV glossary, the CDC STI-treatment term list, the clinicalinfo.hiv.gov HIV/AIDS glossary (133pp), aidsmap (834 headwords), the UNAIDS 2024 Terminology Guidelines, the PHAC STI booklet (100pp) and the WHO STI fact sheet. **Three of the seven are unreachable from this egress and were read from the Internet Archive instead** — `www.cdc.gov` and `clinicalinfo.hiv.gov` answer 403 to curl, to `r.jina.ai` and to headless Chromium (Cloudflare's "Just a moment..."). **The proof that it is their block and not our environment is a sibling host: `npin.cdc.gov` answers 200 from the same egress in the same minute**, and the proxy reports `bundleCoversEveryHost: true`. The working form is `https://web.archive.org/web/<year>id_/<url>` — **the RAW `id_` variant**; the ordinary replay form 403s, the availability API answers 429, and the CDX endpoint is blocked by egress policy. The CDC HIV glossary specifically had to come from the **2024** capture because the 2025 one archived CDC's own 403. Anything attributed to those three is as of its snapshot, which the migration header records.

**The corpus slice is 432 rows matching an HIV/STI pattern: 157 active, 258 DEPRECATED, 17 merged** — so this pass found almost nothing genuinely missing and a great deal filed where no reader would meet it.

**TWO ACTIVE, INDEXABLE STI PAGES WERE FILED UNDER `Fetishes`.** `pelvic-inflammatory-disease` — what untreated chlamydia or gonorrhoea becomes, and the leading preventable cause of infertility and ectopic pregnancy — and `lymphogranuloma-venereum`, whose current European epidemiology is almost entirely among men who have sex with men and which the WHO fact sheet read for this pass names among the "re-emerging neglected STIs". Both `seo_indexable`. This is the `mayonnaise under Sexual Health` class running in the harmful direction: a diagnosis published as a kink, on the platform least able to afford the confusion. Two more sit there deprecated (`epididymitis`, `sti-screening`). The category is moved by writing `category_id` ALONE and letting both triggers reconcile (`20261006110000`); the revive in the sibling migration moved `epididymitis`'s junction row the same way, verified.

**HPV HAD NO LIVE PAGE AT ALL, AND THE REASON IS A MERGE THAT POINTS AT A DEPRECATED ROW.** The glossary holds FIVE HPV rows — `hpv` (deprecated), `hpv-human-papillomavirus` (**merged INTO `hpv`**), `human-papillomavirus-hpv` (deprecated), `genital-warts-hpv` (deprecated), `hpv-vaccination` (deprecated, with a 1,355-character description) — and the only live row in the family was `genital-warts`, a SYMPTOM. So the virus behind almost all anal cancer, the cancer whose risk is most elevated in men who have sex with men and highest of all in men living with HIV, was absent while the wart it sometimes causes was published.

**THE GENERAL FINDING IS THAT A MERGE TARGET MUST BE ACTIVE AND NOTHING ANYWHERE CHECKS IT.** A merge mints a redirect from the loser's slug to the winner's page, so a winner that is deprecated or itself merged is a redirect to a page that does not render. Measured corpus-wide: **of 289 merges, 8 point at a deprecated row and 5 at another merged row.** Found by following ONE term. Seven are repaired — the five merged→merged chains are collapsed to their terminal, which is safe because every one resolves to an ACTIVE row in a single hop (`coffee-bar`→`cafe`, `enby-slang-for-non-binary`→`non-binary`, `night-clubs`/`nightclubs`→`nightclub`, `step--brother`→`stepbrother`), `hpv` is fixed by reviving it, and `sexually-transmitted-infections-stis` is repointed off the deprecated `sexually-transmitted-infection` onto the live `sti` where `50100101100000` already sent `stis-stds`. **The remaining six are NAMED rather than silently counted** (`fluctuating/evolving`, `sensation-stimulation-devices`, `projector`, and the mojibake person slugs `jan-mikolasek`, `kirsten-plotz`, `preistrager`), because each needs an editorial decision on a row outside this pass's subject.

**THE SENTINEL WAS PROMISED IN A HEADER AND NOT SHIPPED, WHICH IS THE FAILURE THE SAME PR DOCUMENTS ONE MIGRATION LATER.** `50400101100100`'s header said the invariant "is added as a reported signal"; it was not — the file repaired seven rows, asserted its own postconditions, and left nothing watching. Caught by re-reading the claim against the diff rather than by any test, because a comment that outlived its truth is invisible to every gate. `tag_merge_graph_signals()` (`50400101100300`) is the real thing, wired into `check-pipeline-health.mjs`. **It is a STANDALONE RPC, not a key on `tag_hygiene_stats()`** — that body is 210 lines and restating it to add a counter is a merge-collision surface, which is why `glossary_link_signals` and the news/venue/event signals are all separate functions. **Nothing existing covered it, checked rather than assumed:** `redirect_to_non_canonical` reads the redirect TABLE, not the merge graph, and `merged_but_not_status_merged` reads the LOSER's status, never the target's. The keys SPLIT on purpose — `target_merged` / `target_missing` / `self_merged` are structural, are driven to zero by the repair and gate there; `target_deprecated` is editorial, stands at six by design, and only warns while printing the pairs so a NEW one is distinguishable from the six known ones. `merges_total` is reported first, because four zeroes from a corpus with no merges must never read as a clean merge graph.

**THREE ACTIVE, INDEXABLE ROWS PUBLISHED NO BODY** — `mpox` (**39 uses**), `shigella` and `hepatitis-a`. Each already carried a good, queer-specific `description`, so `/tags/mpox` rendered two sentences and stopped. Filling a NULL is not the banned LLM rewrite. **The obvious shortcut on mpox was measured and REFUSED**: a merged `monkeypox` row still holds a 416-character body, but that text is generic zoonosis prose that never mentions sex, and WHO renamed the disease in 2022 precisely because the old name was used in racist and stigmatising ways — recovering it would re-import both the framing and the name. This is the mirror of the `cambria` lesson: check whether the right answer is already in the row, and then check whether it is actually right. **`human_reviewed=false` is deliberately NOT stamped on the three**, because `deprecate_unused_tags` selects exactly `active AND human_reviewed=false AND usage_count=0` and two of them qualify — the honest flag would queue the pages for deletion, which is `20360101101300`'s rule verbatim.

**`/tags/aids` DEFINED HIV — an active, indexable page with 106 assignments whose `description` AND `long_description` both opened "The human immunodeficiency virus (HIV) is a retrovirus that attacks the immune system".** Only the `short_description` was about the relationship between the two at all. **Neither existing guard could have caught it**: this is the wrong-SENSE class, and `wikidata_id` and `wikipedia_url` are both NULL on that row, so there is no identifier to be wrong and nothing for `tag-wiki-guard.ts` to check — comparing against the seven references is the only thing that would have found it. It matters more than a typo because conflating the virus with the syndrome is the commonest error in HIV communication and the one every source takes most care over, and because `hiv` is a separate live row with 580 uses already carrying the correct virus definition — **so the corpus stated the virus twice and the AIDS page said nothing**, in particular nothing about AIDS being a STAGE with criteria (an AIDS-defining condition **or** a CD4 count under 200 cells/mm³, whichever comes first), nor that immune recovery on treatment is ordinary. **Replaced, not retracted** — the `darkroom` rule, because the row is live and rendering — and **content-guarded**, so a human who fixes it first keeps their work; the prior text survives in `tag_change_log.before_data`, which is why content writes go through an attributed actor. The same migration fixes `hiv-aids-crisis`, whose `description` was an encyclopaedic paragraph on the virus's PHYLOGENY (Léopoldville, 1920s) over a `short_description` and `long_description` that are correctly about the crisis and queer communities — **only the wrong FIELD is touched**, the `casting`/`trauma`/`watersports` rule. **`hiv-aids` (289 assignments) is deliberately NOT touched**: it describes itself as "HIV/AIDS related news and research", which is what it is used as — a news facet, not a definition — and although UNAIDS prefers "HIV and AIDS" to "HIV/AIDS", renaming or merging a 289-assignment tag is an editorial decision about the news taxonomy rather than a prose correction.

**40 rows revived unpublished with their prose unchanged, and 32 of the 40 were `seo_indexable=true` WHILE deprecated.** (A first draft of this line said "41 of 43", carried over from the previous pass's entry rather than measured for this one — re-measure a number before reusing it, even from the paragraph above.) What was holding: `viral-load` (the quantity U=U, treatment and monitoring are all defined in terms of — `viral-load-monitoring` was live while the thing being monitored was not), `viral-suppression`, `seroconversion`, `serodiscordant`, `opportunistic-infections`, `dental-dam` (the only barrier for oral-vaginal and oral-anal sex, i.e. the one that matters most to the readers least served by condom advice), `pubic-lice`, `molluscum-contagiosum`, `chancroid`, `candidiasis`, `herpes`, `anal-warts`, `anal-cancer-screening`, and **all five syphilis stages** while `syphilis` itself stayed live — staging is what treatment turns on, so a page that cannot say which stage is a page that cannot help. **U=U itself was already correct and needed nothing**: `u-equals-u` is active with `undetectable`, `tasp` and `treatment-as-prevention` as aliases and two spelled-out duplicates properly merged into it. **Two candidates were refused for different reasons**: `serosorting` is already an APPROVED alias of the active `seroadaptation`, so it was never a gap (the `pinkwashing` lesson — a future comparison must not re-propose it); and `female-condom` would publish outdated gendered terminology, so `condom-internal` was revived instead, with `condom-external` for the pair.

**UNAIDS IS A LANGUAGE STANDARD, NOT A GLOSSARY, SO IT BELONGS IN `styleguide_terms` — and checking ours against theirs CORROBORATED it more than it corrected it.** Six UNAIDS rules were already present, arrived at independently: `living-with-hiv` already avoids "HIV-infected"/"AIDS victim"/"AIDS sufferer", `hiv-negative` already avoids "clean"/"DDF", `people-who-use-drugs` already avoids "addict"/"substance abuser", `sex-worker` already avoids "prostitute", `condomless` already avoids "unprotected sex", and `u-equals-u` already refuses to hedge U=U. **Five were genuinely missing and are added, each measured against our own voice first**: `safe sex` **22 hits**, `sexually transmitted disease` 3, `mother-to-child transmission` 2, `HIV virus` 1, `high-risk group` **0** (prevention, not cleanup). **The v1.2.0 precision rule shaped the list and rejected three UNAIDS entries**: "target" and "intervention" are ordinary English and ordinary health English (UNAIDS itself says "intervention" is correct in structural and health-systems contexts), and bare **"STD" measures 0** in our own voice so it buys nothing as a matcher and lives in the context note instead. The one entry that could have been noise was checked rather than assumed — "safe sex" is a substring of "unsafe sex", a different word with a different problem, and **0 of the 22 hits are "unsafe sex"**.

**PUBLISHING IS PART OF THE CHANGE, AND THE GATED FUNCTION CANNOT DO IT.** `styleguide_compile()` freezes every profile into an immutable version row AT PUBLISH TIME and `getVoicePrompt()` serves the frozen row, so inserting terms without publishing leaves the fleet on the old prompt while every check reports success — the "shipped and wired to nothing" failure this system was built after. `styleguide_publish()` is SECURITY DEFINER whose first statement is `IF NOT has_role_jwt('admin') THEN RAISE 'unauthorized'`, and a migration has no JWT; **the gate and the work are deliberately separate functions, so the migration calls `_styleguide_publish_core(bump, note, NULL)`** — which is also honest, because no human published it. v1.2.0 → **v1.3.0**, full 30,194 → 31,260 chars, compact 13,457 → 13,838, 36 → 41 active terms. The postcondition asserts the PUBLISHED TEXT and both profiles, never the rows, because asserting `styleguide_terms` passes in exactly the un-published state. **The standard is added and the corpus is NOT bulk-rewritten**: four hits are repaired by single-phrase substitution on rows this pass was already correcting, and **the two `mother-to-child transmission` hits on `zidovudine` are deliberately left standing** — that phrasing is the historical name of the trial the drug is known for — so the term ships with a known, named, non-zero backlog rather than a reassuring zero.

Migrations `50400101100000`–`50400101100400`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/hivStiGlossaryPass.test.ts`, mutation-tested **25/25 with three comment-only controls that correctly SURVIVE**. **One assertion was vacuous and the mutation is what found it**: the STI-repoint test sliced from the statement's WHERE clause, and `slug = 'sti' and status = 'active'` occurs there too in the statement's own guard — so it matched the guard and passed with the merge target repointed at the deprecated row. Anchor on the ASSIGNMENT, not on the statement.

**Glossary residue — closing a recorded backlog found half of it already closed, and that is the first lesson (2026-09-14):** the HIV/STI and eight-glossary passes each ended by NAMING what they did not fix rather than counting it silently. Re-measuring that list before working it found **two of the six items already done by other sessions** — the 195-row category junction gap (`50700101100000`) and the wrong-subject prose cohort (`50700101100200`, eight rows, none of them these). **A recorded open item is not still open because it was once written down**; re-measure before writing a line of SQL, or you ship a migration that repairs nothing and asserts it did.

**`sti-testing` had BOTH prose fields broken, in different ways, and the row had been revived under me.** Its `description` was a definition of **STI** — the wrong-SENSE class, identical in shape to `/tags/aids` opening with the definition of HIV — while `sti` is a separate live row with 95 uses already carrying that text; the corpus stated STI twice and the testing page said nothing about testing. Its `long_description` had the right subject but cited its own source to the reader (*"According to a scientific article published on August 21, 2013"*) and padded with *"It is recommended to consult a healthcare provider"* — which is exactly why the HIV/STI pass left the row DEPRECATED rather than reviving it. Something revived it since, so the deferral no longer held. Both fields replaced (the `darkroom` rule — the row is live), content-guarded, prior text in `tag_change_log`.

**`cialis` and `levitra` are BRAND rows and merging them into their generics was rejected, not overlooked.** `viagra` already exists as its own brand row with its own body, so brand-as-a-row is the established shape; merging would change a pattern rather than fill a hole. Each body therefore carries what the BRAND adds over the generic instead of paraphrasing it — for Cialis, once-daily dosing, which **inverts the generic page's safety advice**: the 48-hour nitrate interval is only reachable by waiting if dosing is on-demand, so someone on the daily schedule is never outside it. Tadalafil is also licensed for BPH, so a person can be taking Cialis daily for their prostate and not consider themselves to be taking an erection drug at all.

**Two of the six dead merge targets were revivable-looking and neither was.** 50400101100100 named six `target_deprecated` merges and left them as editorial decisions; reading the targets split them in two, and the split is about whether a correct LIVE destination exists — never about whether the target has text on it. `fluctuating/evolving` has a 353-character body, and is still not vocabulary: its `description` is **byte-identical** to `abrosexual`'s ("Sexual orientation that changes over time"), which is active with 27 uses, so reviving it mints the duplicate the `lesbophobia` and `deliriants` findings exist to prevent; its **slug contains a slash**, and measured corpus-wide six tag slugs do, five of them `merged` and therefore never rendered — so it is the only one a revive could publish, at a URL `/tags/:tagName` cannot match; and its body is the affirmation boilerplate `20261012090000` stripped from 112 rows. `sensation-stimulation-devices` has a 357-character body and a **scrape timestamp as its description** ("Updated July  6, 2023 12:57pm…", the `stis-stds` defect), closing on the same banned register. So: one REPOINT onto the live row already holding the concept (the `sexually-transmitted-infections-stis` → `sti` move), five WITHDRAWN — `merged_into_id` cleared, both sides left deprecated — because **a merge whose target holds nothing a reader can use is not a merge, it is two dead rows pretending to redirect**, and reviving a bodiless target trades a redirect-to-nothing for an indexable page-with-nothing, which is worse.

**DEMOTING `merged` → `deprecated` CONVERTS A HARMLESS FLAG INTO A LIVE ONE, and the migration's own postcondition is what caught it.** A merged row is inert to crawlers (measured: 0 of 288 merged tags are in `search_documents`), so `seo_indexable=true` on one is untidy; on a DEPRECATED row it is the publish-on-revive trap. `projectors` carries exactly that flag, so the first draft — which moved status and left the flag — was rejected by its own "nothing here may publish" check on the dry run. The signal goes `target_deprecated` **6 → 0** and `merges_total` 289 → 284, so a baseline six people scroll past becomes a number where any non-zero reading is new.

**`LIKE '%\n%'` DOES NOT TEST FOR A LITERAL BACKSLASH-N — in a LIKE pattern the backslash is the ESCAPE character**, so that pattern means "contains the letter n" and fired on all five rows on the first dry run. The corpus convention (real newlines in the quoted string, `20261007120000`) is worth asserting, but `position('\n' in x) > 0` is what asserts it; a pattern-matching operator interprets the very thing being searched for. The guard was live, not vacuous — it failed loudly on its own defect.

**`chill` is filled as a venue type and the chemsex sense stays unaliasable, deliberately.** `50100101100100` recorded the empty `Venue Types` stub as blocking "Chill" from being aliased onto `chemsex` via `tag_reject_alias_shadow()`. **Its one assignment is a VENUE**, so the venue sense is the one in live use; and the vocabulary is not badly served, because `chemsex` already carries `Chems` (covers, approved) plus `Party & Play`, `PnP` and `Chem-Sex`. Deprecating a tag that is in use to free a slug for an alias is a trade this pass declines.

**Two items are deliberately NOT closed and neither is a fix.** `hiv-aids` (289 assignments) is a news facet rather than a definition, so renaming or merging it is a taxonomy decision. And `styleguide_content_drift()` stands at **615 flagged rows over 138 active phrases** (`vibrant` 374, `explore` 174, concentrated in `city.description` at 356) — **up from 571 because v1.3.0 ADDED terms, which is new phrases finding old hits, not a regression**. Bulk-rewriting it is the experiment this repo already ran and retired when the prose judge retracted 16 rows and got 13 wrong.

Migrations `50900101100000`/`50900101100100`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/glossaryResiduePass.test.ts`, mutation-tested **18/18 with two comment-only controls that correctly SURVIVE** — and one mutation first survived for the fifth recorded time in this file: the repoint test matched `a.slug = 'abrosexual' and a.status = 'active'` in the statement's SET subquery rather than in the WHERE guard the mutation deleted. Scope the assertion to the half of the statement it is about.

**The last two recorded open items, closed — and one of them closed as a REFUSAL (2026-09-14):** `hiv-aids` and the styleguide drift backlog were the two things `50900101100000`'s entry left standing as decisions rather than defects. Both were decided.

**`hiv-aids` is NOT merged, and the data settles it rather than taste: of its 289 assignments, 288 are NEWS** and the one remainder is a venue (the World AIDS Museum) that is itself about both. `hiv` (580 uses) defines the virus and `aids` (106) the syndrome, so an article covering both belongs to neither alone; merging points a topic at a definition and destroys 288 topical links, the journalism-deleting move `20260809100000` refused for news duplicates.

**`hiv-aids` is also NOT renamed, and that is the finding worth keeping.** The intended change was `name` "HIV/AIDS" → "HIV and AIDS", the UNAIDS form this repo started publishing at styleguide v1.3.0. It was written, dry-run on prod, and abandoned, because a name update on `unified_tags` does two things nobody asks for. **(1) IT MOVES THE SLUG** — `trg_unified_tags_normalize_slug` is `BEFORE UPDATE OF name, slug`, and the observed result of setting the name was `slug = 'hiv-and-aids'`, i.e. the canonical URL of an indexable page with 289 assignments. **Reading the function suggests the opposite**: its ASCII branch is `normalize_tag_slug(coalesce(NEW.slug, NEW.name))`, which should preserve an existing slug. **The source reads one way and the row moves anyway — trust the dry run, not the function body.** **(2) IT TITLE-CASES THE NAME**: the value written was "HIV and AIDS", the value stored was **"HIV And AIDS"** — not English, and not the form the rename existed to adopt, so the rename cannot even achieve its own purpose. A slug move is survivable (`unified_tags_slug_redirect` exists) but is a disproportionate price for a solidus. **Both mechanical faults are now fixed — the title-caser by `83000101143000` and the slug re-derivation by `99960101100200` — so this line's "do not re-propose this rename without first fixing the title-caser and the slug re-derivation" no longer blocks it.** What stands is the EDITORIAL reason recorded above: 288 of its 289 assignments are news, so it is a topic facet rather than a definition, and renaming it is a decision about the news taxonomy. A future pass reading only "UNAIDS prefers HIV and AIDS" must still answer that, not the two mechanics. What WAS repaired is the `description`, which **defined the term with the term** ("HIV/AIDS related news and research"), the third recorded instance of that defect after `impaired-driving` and `hardpoint`.

**The drift backlog: two hypotheses were measured and BOTH were false, which is the whole reason the scope is what it is.** (1) *"Most of it sits on unreachable rows, so narrow the counter."* FALSE — `styleguide_content_drift()` **already filters tags to `status='active'`**, so the 118 deprecated tag rows carrying drift were never in the 615; cities are filtered only on `duplicate_of_id`, leaving **38 ghost/deindexed rows, about 6%**. Narrowing would have bought almost nothing and would have hidden prose a revive can make live again — which this repo does routinely, 40 tags in `50400101100100` alone. **The counter is left exactly as it is.** (2) *"`vibrant` can be deleted globally."* FALSE — the collocations are grammatically varied (`a vibrant and X` 162, `the vibrant capital` 25, `a vibrant city` 14, `and vibrant X` 34) and a blanket deletion yields "a and diverse city" and "beaches markets".

**So the sweep removes the word only where deletion needs no judgement, and the skipped shapes are asserted to SURVIVE.** Four rules on reachable rows only (indexable, not ghost/merged, not duplicate): `a vibrant and <consonant>`, `the vibrant <word>`, `its`/`'s vibrant <word>`, `a vibrant <consonant>`. **Rule ORDER removes the need for a negative lookahead** — rule 1 consumes every consonant-initial `a vibrant and`, and "and" cannot then match `[b-df-hj-np-tv-z]`. **`a vibrant and <vowel>` (37 rows) is SKIPPED** because removing the adjective strands the article ("a iconic port") and repairing it means rewriting "a"→"an", where "a unique"/"a historic" are exactly where the vowel-letter test is wrong. **`and vibrant X` (34) is SKIPPED as genuinely ambiguous**: "beaches and vibrant markets" wants the adjective gone, "beautiful and vibrant city" wants "and vibrant" gone, and nothing in the string distinguishes them. Measured on prod: **196 descriptions corrected, 0 ungrammatical, reachable rows carrying the word 284 → 88.** **Both skipped counts are REACHABLE-row counts — corpus-wide they are 38 and 45**, so a later re-measurement that drops the reachability filter, or that requires a letter before `and`, reads as a discrepancy that is not there (a post-merge check reported 23 against the documented 34 for exactly that reason — the regex, not the data). Original text is preserved at `field_provenance.description.styleguide_corrected.from`, built with `||` rather than `jsonb_set(create_missing)` — the trap `21050101100000` recorded.

**This is deliberately NOT the retired experiment and the distinction is mechanical, not rhetorical.** `tag-enrichment-sweep mode='prose'` had an LLM *judge and rewrite*; its first live batch of 18 retracted 16 rows with **13 wrong**, and both auto-apply paths are disabled. This is a literal, reversible string deletion with no model in the loop. **The residue is named rather than zeroed**: ~88 reachable `vibrant` rows plus `explore` 174 and the rest of the 138-phrase vocabulary remain, and closing those means rewriting prose, which is the decision this file already records as retired.

**Verified on prod after the merge, and the delta histogram is what proves the RULES behaved rather than merely that the totals look right.** Both migrations are in `schema_migrations` under their OWN names (`hiv_aids_topic_facet` / `styleguide_drift_vibrant_sweep`) — the check that matters after three forced renumbers, since a version present under another PR's name means yours never ran and every count-by-version check still returns 1. `hiv-aids` kept its slug, its name `HIV/AIDS` and all 289 assignments; 196 rows corrected with 196 originals preserved; reachable `vibrant` 88; both skipped shapes still present at 37 and 34. Every deletion is one of exactly three legal lengths — **8 chars × 77** (`vibrant `), **12 × 118** (`vibrant and `) and **20 × 1** (one row matching both rules) — with no other length anywhere in the set, which is the evidence a totals check cannot give. **97 corrected rows match `a <vowel>` and all 97 matched it BEFORE the sweep**: that is the "a unique"/"a historic" false positive the vowel rule was skipped to avoid, not an artifact the sweep created, and counting it without diffing against the preserved original would have manufactured a defect.

Migrations `60000101160000`/`60000101160100` — renumbered THREE times as the live ceiling was overtaken, and the sweep file carries its version string in **three** places (the `app.actor` stamp, the `field_provenance.description.styleguide_corrected.by` literal, and the postcondition counting by that literal), one more than the two of `40000101100000`, so a rename that misses one reports 0 corrections AFTER the rows have already been rewritten; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/glossaryOpenItemsClosed.test.ts`, mutation-tested **21/21 with two comment-only controls that correctly SURVIVE** — and two mutations first survived because the assertions matched the regex and the RAISE message while the `IF` condition was neutered to `false`. **Assert the CONDITION, not the needle inside it.**

**Disowned prose, rounds two and three — the wrong subjects CLUSTER, and "surviving" is not "wrong" (2026-09-14):** two more tranches off the backlog `tag_disowned_prose_signals()` counts (352 surviving summaries at the time of round three, 291 bodies, 327 indexable). Round two took 24 rows, round three 11. **The selection rule is unchanged and is what bounds both: repair ONLY where the row's own `description` establishes a sense the short/long description contradict** — evidence on the row, not taste. **The number worth carrying forward is the HIT RATE: 64 candidates were hand-read in usage order for round three and 11 qualified.** The other 53 are not defects at all — "surviving" only means the prose is unchanged since the identifier was taken away, which for most rows means it was right all along (`lgbtq`, `drag`, `acceptance`, `stigma`, `resilience`, `chosen-family`, `act-up` and the whole STI cohort are correct and untouched). `50500101100300`'s ~45% is a hand-read UPPER BOUND measured at the top of the backlog, not a rate to extrapolate over the tail; re-measure before planning the remainder.

**Sorting the qualifying rows by what the body is ABOUT is what made the pass fast**, and the families are worth knowing because they recur: four are PLACES (`ally` → the commune of Ally in Cantal, `baby` → the commune of Baby in Seine-et-Marne, `coven` → **Coventry University**, `reading` → the market town in Berkshire), three are a BAND, a SHOW and a SPECIES (`shame` → the British band, `big-brother` → the US reality show, `bear` → Ursidae), and two are NARROWED rather than wrong (`femme`, `butch`). **`nudist` is the sharpest and needed no inference at all**: a human had already corrected its summary to *"Someone who goes without clothes by preference — a person, not a place"*, and the body directly beneath it is 400 characters about nude BEACHES. The row argued with itself on the page, at 571 uses, indexable. **`shame` asserts its own defect** — its body closed *"In the context of this platform, the tag 'Shame' primarily refers to the British band"*, which is false about this platform and is why a 12-use row was not deferred. **`femme` was incoherent, not merely narrow**: *"also used by some non-lesbian individuals, including gay men, bisexuals, and non-binary or transgender individuals **who identify as lesbian**"* — the crotch-rope/breast-bondage class, correct concept with prose that writes most of the audience out.

**`queen` is the deliberate non-repair and the reasoning generalises.** The rock band is wrong under any reading, so leaving it was not an option — but THREE SIGNALS DISAGREE about the intended sense: `description` says "Female ruler", `category` says Slang & Language, and the live queer sense is drag. Guessing a sense is how this entire class arose, so the false claim is REMOVED and no vocabulary is minted, the half-measure round two took with `lioness`. **`ally` keeps its generic `description` ("An ally is a member of an alliance.") as a stated limitation** — this file never writes `description` because it is the evidence that justified the repair — and its NULL `short_description` is FILLED, which is not the banned rewrite.

**`long_description` is NOT in `trg_search_documents_tag`'s column list** (`name, short_description, description, category, slug, image_url, entity_kind, merged_into_id, deprecated_at, status`), so a body-only repair causes ZERO search churn and nulling a body cannot trip `enforce_tag_thin_page_gate`, which is scoped to `description, short_description`. Read off `pg_get_triggerdef`, not inferred. Nine of round three's eleven rows are `human_reviewed`, and the actor declaration was verified load-bearing rather than assumed: the undeclared UPDATE returns `human_reviewed tag … cannot be modified by system:trigger`. Migrations `51500101143000`/`51500101144000`; guarded by `tagProseRoundTwo.test.ts` and `tagProseRoundThree.test.ts`, mutation-tested 13/13 and 13/13 with comment-only controls that correctly SURVIVE.

**Round four is THREE rows, and the number is the result (2026-09-14, `51500101160000`).** 66 candidates hand-read in usage order, excluding the 49 the four previous passes repaired and the 3 they named; **3 qualified, against round three's 11 of 64** — and the second batch of 33 yielded **zero**. The high-usage head of this backlog is worked out, so plan the remaining ~320 against THIS rate rather than `50500101100300`'s ~45%, which was a hand-read upper bound measured at the very top. **All three rows have `description IS NULL`, so the ORIGINAL rule reaches none of them** — this pass runs entirely on the widening `51500101152700` introduced (the sense may also be established by a CATEGORY that admits exactly one reading of the tag's own name), which is why the file declares that rather than leaving a reader to conclude three rows were repaired without evidence. `rooftop` (Venue Types, 16 uses) published roofing material — summary "Top covering of a building" over a body about "a barrier against rain, snow, sunlight" — the `gym` shape exactly, and the only one of the three whose SUMMARY is replaced too. `casual` (355 uses) is the group-B move of `51500101143000`: the summary is already right, so the body is derived from it and no sense is chosen. `drag-show` (71 uses) is the `femme`/`crotch-rope` NARROWING class — "impersonate men or women" writes drag kings and nonbinary performers out of their own entry — correct concept, prose that excludes. **The actor declaration is NOT load-bearing on this tranche and the file says so**: all three rows are `human_reviewed = false`, so `log_unified_tag_change()` would not RAISE and `set_config` is attribution only. Round three's nine-of-eleven are the opposite case, and recording which is which is what stops the next pass copying the wrong precedent from whichever file it happens to open. Deferred and named: `potato-salad` (Gear, correct potato-salad prose — a FILING question, the `warlord` disposition), `locker-room` (the generic-sense cohort `teacher`/`priest`/`acolyte` sit in, a whole-cohort decision), `tea` (its own `description` establishes the beverage and the body agrees; description and category disagree, which the widened rule does not adjudicate). Guarded by `tagProseRoundFour.test.ts`, mutation-tested **17/17** with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES.

**Round five corrects round four's own headline, and the correction is the reusable part (2026-09-14, `60000301100000`).** Round four concluded *"the high-usage head of this backlog is worked out"*. Round five re-read the **whole surviving list** instead of the usage head and found five more wrong subjects, the largest being **`identity` at 541 uses** — Gender category, indexable, whose own `description` establishes "gender identity, sexual orientation" while the body ran through *"philosophy, MATHEMATICS, and social sciences"* and then **identity documents**. So round four's claim held for the top twenty and is false as a general statement: **a hit rate measured over one ordering does not transfer to another, and re-reading a list you have already sampled is not redundant when the sample was taken by usage and the defect does not correlate with usage.** Nine rows in **three labelled classes**, kept labelled because the risk of a mixed migration is that a later reader takes the loosest group's licence and applies it to the rest. **A — the ORIGINAL rule** (`description` contradicts the prose): `identity`; `solidarity` (45, whose body is the **Polish trade union federation** on a row whose description establishes solidarity between marginalised groups); `awareness` (38, EPISTEMOLOGY — "true belief distinct from opinion or guesswork" — against a description that says "raising consciousness about issues"); `dyke` (1, human_reviewed, body opens *"can refer to a FAMILY NAME, as listed on Wikidata"*); `man` (10, human_reviewed, the `femme`/`drag-show` NARROWING class — its own summary already says "held by trans and cis men alike" and the body opened by centring people assigned male at birth). **B — the WIDENED rule**: `old-theatre` (`description` IS NULL, so the category carries it; the body was ONE named building in Stamford plus unresolved Singapore disambiguation residue). **C — NOT a wrong subject at all, a REGISTER defect named as its own class**: `pride-events` (250), `gay-men` (162) and `workplace-equality` (55) each carried one sentence CITING ITS OWN SOURCE TO THE READER (*"According to a scientific article published on 2 March 2022…"*), the register `impaired-driving` and `sti-testing` were repaired for. **The Group C repair deletes that sentence and keeps every other sentence BYTE-IDENTICAL**, and the test asserts the survivors byte-for-byte — a "the citation is gone" check alone passes against a full rewrite, which is the retired bulk-rewrite experiment wearing a fix's clothes. **The actor declaration IS load-bearing here, the OPPOSITE of round four, which is exactly why both files state which case they are in**: `man` and `dyke` are `human_reviewed`, verified live rather than assumed — the undeclared UPDATE returns `human_reviewed tag … cannot be modified by system:trigger`. Deferred and named: `diversity` (165, correct prose filed under **Slang & Language** — a filing question, the `warlord` disposition); the Dynamics & Roles / Fetishes **generic-sense cohort** for the third pass running (`teacher`, `priest`, `acolyte`, `angel`, `frog`, `goat`, `villain`, `devotee`, `humiliation`), where the original rule structurally cannot reach them because each row's `description` AGREES with its body ("Amphibian role" over prose about the order Anura); `ice-cream`/`tapas`, deliberately left because unlike `rooftop` — where "the top covering of a building" is no venue under any reading — what an ice cream shop sells IS ice cream, and generic is not wrong; and `dyke`'s `seo_indexable`, since the house convention for reclaimed slurs (`faggot`, `shemale`, `breeder`) is `is_sensitive` + `is_adult` + deindexed, and whether a word many people use for themselves belongs in that cohort is an editorial call rather than a prose fix. Guarded by `tagProseRoundFive.test.ts`, mutation-tested **24/24** with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES — and **four mutations first read as survivors or no-ops for three separate reasons, all previously recorded in this file**: `man`'s body contains SEMICOLONS so a `[^;]*` deletion pattern silently matched nothing (the venue-dedup trap), two multi-line patterns did not match the file's real whitespace, and `perl` without `/g` mutated only one of the **two** occurrences of `541`. **The test's own first draft was vacuous in the mirror direction**: `expect(statements).not.toContain('According to a scientific article…')` FAILED on correct code, because every UPDATE is content-guarded and therefore quotes the defect verbatim in its own WHERE clause — the negative assertions are scoped to the SET clauses now. Anchor a "must not contain" on what is WRITTEN, not on the statement.

**The tail, and a defect class five passes could not see: a HALF-REPAIRED row (2026-09-14, `60000301100100`).** Rounds two to five worked this backlog in USAGE ORDER and stopped where the usage ran out, which is why 25 rows survived all five: almost every one is **usage 0**, so it sorted below everything anyone ever read. Searching the surviving set for the SIGNATURES the earlier rounds had already found by hand — "family name", "commune in", "studio album", "genus", "may refer to", "according to Wikidata" — returned **57 rows in six shapes**, and hand-reading those 57 gave 25 repairs. **A signature is not a defect**: `boyfriend` matched on "can refer to a wide range of relationships", which is ordinary correct prose, and `frog`/`goat`/`tiger`/`duck`/`dragon`/`bunny`/`strawberry` matched the species arm while being the deferred generic-sense cohort. The regex narrows what a human reads; it does not decide. **THE NEW CLASS is `queerness`'s rule one layer further in — nulling an identifier does not unpublish its prose, and repairing one PROSE FIELD does not repair the other. The result is invisible to any check that reads either field alone**, which is exactly why a usage-ordered sweep and a spot-check both miss it. `collar` is the clearest: its `description` AND `long_description` are both careful hand-written kink prose ("in dominance and submission a collar most often marks an ownership dynamic") while its `short_description` — the LEAD LINE on the page — still read **"Family name or surname"**. Someone repaired that row and fixed two fields of three. `humbler` ("Family name of origin") and `gainer` ("Surname or rare term") are the same shape; `bottom`, `babyboy`, `toy` and `possum` are the mirror, a good summary over a junk body. **Seven of the 25 are half-repaired and every one would pass a reviewer who spot-checked the field that had already been fixed.** What the rest published, all indexable: `domme` → a **commune in the Dordogne**; `sissy` → a **commune in Aisne**; `servant` → a **commune in Puy-de-Dôme**; `fister` → a **village in Norway**; `knife-play` → **Xiu Xiu's 2002 debut album**; `business-suits` → a **Hole song**; `vixen` → **Dariusz Szlagor, a Polish rapper**; `bottom` (core kink vocabulary) → "can refer to a family name or surname"; `babyboy` → the **birth-certificate placeholder name for an unnamed male child**, on an age-play row. **`masc` is the sharpest and the reason its group was not deferred**: its own description reads "A masculine gender expression, REGARDLESS OF GENDER IDENTITY" and the body published BIOLOGICAL SEX — "a male is an organism that produces sperm" — which is precisely the claim that description exists to refuse. **Nine rows MINT NOTHING and that is a group, not an omission:** `doe`, `fae`, `flock`, `handler`, `minion`, `tickler`, `whipper`, `vixen`, `cunt` carry a real but one-line `description` ("Person who tickles") — enough to state what the tag is, not enough to write a body from — so the summary is set to exactly what that description supports and **the body is NULLED**, the `steer`/`lion`/`office` treatment of `51500101152700` and the `queen` rule of `51500101144000`. Nulling is safe and asserted rather than assumed (`enforce_tag_thin_page_gate` reads `tag_has_prose(description, short_description)` only, and all nine keep a description). Deferred WITH the reason each cannot be reached, which is the distinction worth copying: **`ebony` and `bicon` have no clean evidence on the row** — `bicon`'s `description` is ITSELF a disambiguation list ("Bicon may refer to: BiCon (UK) … Bicon Dental Implants") and this file never writes `description`, so it is unrepairable under the rule rather than overlooked; **`host` and `unicorn` name TWO senses at once** ("Mythical creature or third person" — the second being the polyamory sense), and choosing between them is the guess this whole class came from; and the generic-sense cohort for the **fourth** pass running, where each row's `description` AGREES with its body. 24 of the 25 rows are `human_reviewed`, so the actor declaration is load-bearing, verified live on this corpus. Guarded by `tagProseDisownedTail.test.ts`, mutation-tested **24/24** with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES — including mutations that give a Group C row a body, that make a half-repaired row rewrite the field which was already correct, and that strip `masc`'s refusal of the biological-sex claim or `sissy`'s separation of the role from trans identity.

**Two sessions worked one seam from opposite ends, and the file that merged second had to be cut down rather than shipped (2026-09-14, `60000301100000`/`60000301100100`).** While round five and the tail sat in review, a concurrent session merged and APPLIED `51700101143000_tag_prose_namesake_artifacts` — the same backlog, the same selection rule, the same evidence — repairing **18 of the 34 rows those two files targeted**: `awareness` and `dyke` from round five, and 16 of the tail's 25 (`collar`, `humbler`, `bottom`, `babyboy`, `doe`, `handler`, `minion`, `tickler`, `whipper`, `vixen`, `domme`, `fister`, `sissy`, `servant`, `knife-play`, `business-suits`). This is the `queerness`/`queer-theory` collision (`20360401100100`) for the **third** time on this table, and the second time in one day.

**The overlapping UPDATEs were DELETED, not left to no-op, and the reasoning generalises.** Every UPDATE in both files is content-guarded on the junk text it removes, so once the other session's repair landed they would simply have matched nothing — harmless at run time. Two reasons not to leave them anyway: **a file that claims 25 rows and changes 9 misreports itself to the next reader**, who has no way to tell a deliberate scope from a silent no-op; and re-writing prose that is already correct is exactly the LLM rewrite both auto-apply paths were retired for. Round five went 9 → **7 rows**, the tail 25 → **9**.

**The POSTCONDITIONS were deliberately NOT narrowed to match, and that is what made the collision survivable.** Both files' verify blocks were already written against the **WRONG text** rather than against their own writes — round five's says *"Tests for the WRONG text, so a better fix written by someone else also satisfies it"* — so after the cut they assert the whole **34-row seam** is clean, ours and theirs together, and would catch a regression in either session's rows. Had they instead asserted "25 rows were updated" or "row X now reads my exact wording", the second migration would have RAISEd on `main`, and `db push` aborts on the first failing file **and takes every migration queued behind it**, which is a repo-wide blast radius from two individually-correct changes. **Soft on preconditions, hard on postconditions, is not a style rule — it is the thing that decides whether a concurrent repair costs you a diff or costs the whole queue.** The tail's Group-C invariant is the sharpest example: it asserts all **nine** bodies are null, six of which the other session nulled and three of which this file does, so one check covers both authors.

**The check that actually found this was `git diff --name-only HEAD origin/main -- supabase/migrations/`, run before updating the branch** — not a test, not CI. Nothing in CI compares a pending migration's target rows against what another merged migration already changed; the dry run would have caught it, but only after the wasted work. **On any data-repair migration that has sat in review, re-read the live rows before merging rather than trusting the diff**, and diff the migrations directory against the base branch first: the collision is visible there in one command, and every other route to it is slower.

The migrations also had to be renumbered a **fourth** time (`51500101170000`→`51600101110000`→`51800101110000`→`60000301100000`, and the tail alongside), because `51700101143000` and then #3715's `60000101100000` each moved the ceiling again. The fourth move is the one that names the mechanism: #3715 was ARMED FOR AUTO-MERGE by another session and landed out of version order while this PR sat in review, so an ordering agreed between queued PRs is not binding on a PR that is already up to date. Under strict branch protection the only order that holds is the one the merges actually happen in. Free and mandatory, and the escalation is the coordination problem this file already records — picking a bigger number is not a fix.

**The tag title-caser had no concept of a function word, and it DEGRADED correct input (2026-09-15, `83000101143000`):** `normalize_tag_name()` ran a three-rung ladder — explicit acronym, then all-uppercase run, then capitalise — so every "and", "of" and "with" was capitalised. Measured on prod: **557 rows carried a title-cased function word, 116 active, 72 active AND indexable**, and the worst are proper names on a rights platform (`Convention On The Rights Of Persons With Disabilities`, `Convention On The Elimination Of All Forms Of Discrimination Against Women`, `UN High Commissioner For Human Rights`) plus **`Men Who Have Sex With Men` at 83 assignments**, the standard public-health term spelled wrong. **It did not merely fail to fix these, it created them**: `normalize_tag_name('Men Who Have Sex with Men')` returned `... With Men`, so a human typing the correct form had it degraded by the trigger on save — which is why this is a producer fix and not only a repair, since repairing the rows alone would be undone by the next edit to any of them. The new rung goes **THIRD, below the all-uppercase rung**, and that order is the whole protection for a deliberate all-caps `OR` (Oregon) or `IN` (Indiana); two such rows exist. **First and last run are never lowercased**, which is ordinary title case and is also what protects real rows here — `Hepatitis A` (the letter, last run), `Strap On` / `Crushing On` / `Sidle Up` (phrasal particles), `Dine-In` (a hyphen separates runs, so `In` is last), `In Service To` (first and last both protected); `In A Hive With` shows all three rules at once → `In a Hive With`. Knowing which run is LAST needs the count before emission, so it is taken in a pre-pass repeating the emit loop's boundary condition **verbatim rather than approximating it with a regex**, and the per-run ladder was extracted into `tag_name_cap_run()` because the old body carried it TWICE — once in the loop and once in the trailing flush, **which is exactly where the last run usually lands**, so a rule added to one copy and not the other fails on precisely the run the protection exists for. The repair re-normalises through the fixed function rather than from a frozen list, so it cannot drift from the producer; `status='merged'` rows are excluded (three carry legacy slugs with punctuation the normaliser strips — `clit-and-pussy-torture-(cpt)`, `dining-at-the-y-(daty)`, `princess-by-day,-slut-by-night` — so touching their name would move a redirect trail for a row that renders nothing), and every row additionally has to keep its slug. Dry-run on prod in a rolled-back transaction: **521 rows changed (107 active, 68 indexable), differing-beyond-case 0, slugs moved 0, unrepaired 0.**

**THE POSTCONDITION WOULD HAVE ABORTED `db push` ON MAIN, AND THE FIRST DRY RUN COULD NOT SEE IT.** The verify block asserted the proxy *"name agrees with slug on every non-merged row"* — and **130 non-merged rows already fail that** for unrelated reasons (hand-set slugs, punctuation), so it would have RAISEd and taken every migration queued behind it, the `20810101100100` failure again. It survived the first dry run because **that dry run exercised my own measurements and never executed the file's `do $verify$` block at all** — the block that runs at apply time is the one that can block the repo, so dry-run THAT, not a paraphrase of it. The fix is to assert what the file actually promises: snapshot every slug into a temp table before the UPDATE and compare after, so the claim is "this migration moved no page" rather than a derived condition the file neither created nor can repair. The reached-state count is likewise scoped to rows the file UNDERTOOK to repair, since a row the slug guard deliberately declines is not a failure. Measured after the fix: verify block passes on live data, `declined_by_slug_guard = 0`.

**This removes half of the `hiv-aids` rename blocker** — `normalize_tag_name('HIV and AIDS')` now returns `HIV and AIDS`. **The other half is now fixed too, by `99960101100200` — see the next entry**; this line read *"the other half is untouched and is a decision, not a bug to fix in passing: a rename still re-derives the slug, so the page's URL moves"* until then. **And the earlier entry named the wrong writer** — it blamed `trg_unified_tags_normalize_slug`, but `unified_tags_normalize_slug()` runs LATER and merely re-reads a slug that **`normalize_tag_input()`** has already overwritten via `OR (TG_OP = 'UPDATE' AND OLD.name IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.name)`. That is why reading the function the entry pointed at suggested the slug was preserved. The question this line posed for whoever took the slug half — whether a display-name edit should move a canonical URL at all — was answered no. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/tagNameFunctionWords.test.ts`, mutation-tested **14/14 with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES** — and one mutation first read as NOT-APPLIED because the perl pattern used a four-space indent where the emit site has eight, the sixth recorded instance of that check in this file.
