**A display-name edit moved the page's canonical URL, and the admin form had no slug field to decline it with (2026-09-19, `99960101100200`):** `normalize_tag_input()` re-derived the slug on EVERY update that changed the name — `IF NEW.slug IS NULL OR NEW.slug = '' OR (TG_OP = 'UPDATE' AND OLD.name IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.name)` — so correcting a display name silently moved its page. Measured on prod: setting `hiv-aids` to `HIV and AIDS` moved the slug to `hiv-and-aids` and minted a redirect, on an indexable row carrying **289 assignments**. `AdminTags.tsx` submits only `{name, category, description}` and **has no slug field at all**, so an editor could neither see nor refuse the move. **The rule: a statement that does not write the slug never changes the slug — except where the non-ASCII seal already claims it.**

**CI CAUGHT A REAL REGRESSION AND IT CHANGED THE DESIGN, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING.** The first draft put the unwritten-slug early return at the TOP of `unified_tags_normalize_slug`, and `src/lib/__tests__/tagSlugSeal.test.ts` — a guard another session shipped for an unrelated reason — went red and was right. `20261211120000_tag_slug_seal.sql` makes the NAME win for a non-ASCII name because `source-tags-extract` slugifies with a regex that never transliterates (`Bühne` → `b-hne`) and upserts that string as the `ON CONFLICT` key. **On the re-upsert path the caller supplies the same bad slug the row already carries**, so `NEW.slug IS NOT DISTINCT FROM OLD.slug` holds — and an early return above the seal swallows exactly the case the seal exists for, leaving a stale `b-hne` row unable to heal on every future weekly run. So the seal is untouched and still FIRST, and this rule is the `ELSIF` underneath it. **A guard from another session going red is evidence about your design, not an obstacle to route around** — and narrowing the seal to make room would have reversed, in passing, a decision that ships with its own one-shot repair and blast-radius caps.

**TWO WRITERS, NOT ONE.** `trg_normalize_tag_input` fires first and carried the name-triggered branch; `trg_unified_tags_normalize_slug` fires later and is shared with the seal. Fixing only the first would have looked complete and not been — but the correct fix to the second turned out to be an ORDERING question, not a deletion. **The softener was measured and rejected**: "follow the name only while the slug still matches what the old name derives to" does not fix the motivating case, because `normalize_tag_slug('HIV/AIDS')` is exactly `hiv-aids`, so that row reads as auto-tracking and would still have moved; anything conditioned on reachability (indexable, has assignments) fails worse, since the same edit then behaves differently on two rows for reasons invisible at the call site. **Safe on this corpus, measured rather than assumed:** slugs already lowercase and non-null **10,305 / 10,305** (so the surviving `lower()` branch is a corpus-wide no-op), rows whose slug ALREADY differs from `normalize_tag_slug(name)` **176** (104 active, 13 active+indexable — slug-follows-name was never an invariant), non-ASCII names **88** (11 active). **Cost stated rather than hidden:** for a non-ASCII name a display-name edit still moves the slug, those 88 rows; the ASCII corpus is 10,217 of 10,305 and includes `hiv-aids`. A tag created with a typo keeps the typo in its URL after the name is corrected — the remedy is to write the slug explicitly (`20261013110100` already does, and that mints the redirect through the existing `unified_tags_slug_redirect` trigger), and `slug = ''` remains the explicit re-derive escape hatch.

**Verified on prod AFTER the merge, against the DEPLOYED functions rather than the file** — `schema_migrations` carries `99960101100200` under the NAME `tag_slug_does_not_follow_name`, which is the check that a version applied under another PR's name cannot pass. Eight probes in a transaction forced to roll back (0 probe rows and 0 probe redirects left afterwards): an INSERT still derives; a name-only edit changes the name and HOLDS the slug; an explicit slug write still moves it and mints 1 redirect; `slug = ''` re-derives; a non-ASCII name-only edit still re-derives and the seal still beats a caller-supplied slug. **Both source checks assert the seal is first BY OFFSET** (live: seal@219, rule@382), because both branches exist in either order and presence proves nothing — and the non-ASCII name-ONLY probe is the one that pins the composition, since `NEW.slug` equals `OLD.slug` there, precisely the shape a pre-empting early return swallows. **Two traps the runs caught that reading did not:** `CREATE OR REPLACE` forces the whole body to be restated and the `translate()` literal maps en-dash to a SPACE and em-dash to a HYPHEN (a scratch retype got that wrong by one character, so the verify block asserts the BEHAVIOUR instead of trusting the transcription); and the name-normalization probe had to expect the END of the pipeline, not `translate()`'s output, because `unified_tags_normalize_name` fires LAST and title-cases the result. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/tagSlugDoesNotFollowName.test.ts`, mutation-tested **15/15 against both guard suites with two comment-only controls that correctly SURVIVE** — and **two mutations first read as survivors because the file's own header quotes the seal's condition verbatim**, so a first-occurrence replace landed in PROSE while the statement stood and a "did the file change?" check passed with nothing executable moved; the harness compares comment-stripped text now. Seventh recorded instance of that check in this file. Every assertion is scoped to the half of the file it is about, because the header quotes the removed disjunct verbatim AND the verify block greps for it — a first draft asserting over the whole file failed on CORRECT code, and the mirror form would have passed with the statement deleted.

**The mirror defect was on the CREATE path, and the client deleted characters the database can never restore (2026-09-19, #3817):** `99960101100200` stopped a name edit from moving a slug; auditing the create path found `AdminTags.tsx` building the slug itself with `.replace(/[^a-z0-9-]/g, '')` — a regex that **strips** rather than separates. Measured against the live trigger chain (INSERT in a transaction forced to roll back, nothing committed): `HIV/AIDS` stored as **`hivaids`**, `D/s` as **`ds`**, `U=U` as **`uu`**, where Postgres alone gives `hiv-aids` / `d-s` / `u-u`. Creating "HIV/AIDS" through the admin form would have published it at `/tags/hivaids` — the exact canonical URL the two preceding PRs on that branch existed to protect. **The rule: a benign pre-normalisation is repaired downstream, a lossy one cannot be.** `normalize_tag_slug()` turns `/` into a separator, but nothing restores a character the client already deleted. **The hook carried a SECOND, different derivation and that one was harmless** — `useCentralizedTags.createTag`'s fallback replaced only spaces, so `hiv/aids` reached the database and came back `hiv-aids`, correct on all six probes. It is removed anyway: two client implementations of `normalize_tag_slug()` are free to drift from it and from each other, which is exactly what had happened; `createTag` now sends `''`, the documented derive-from-the-name escape hatch `normalize_tag_input()` honours, and forwards a slug only when one is passed deliberately. **The non-ASCII seal hid this from precisely the inputs a reviewer reaches for**: `Bühne` → `buhne` and `Café Society` → `cafe-society` through BOTH client paths, because the seal overrides the caller's slug outright — so an accented spot-check finds nothing wrong, and the live defect is ASCII names carrying punctuation.

**THE SWEEP FOUND TWO MORE, AND THAT IS WHY "the one surface that creates tags" WAS WRONG.** Grepping for every client-side slugifier that feeds a tag write turned up four writers, three of them lossy: `AdminTags.tsx` (live), `useCentralizedTags.createTag` (benign — spaces only — removed anyway), **`useUnifiedTags.createTag`** (identically lossy but **DORMANT**: its only production consumer `useVenueFilters` takes `{ tags, loading, fetchTags }` and never `createTag` — fixed regardless, because dormant is a reason to schedule the work last, not to leave it armed) and **`useTagAliases.createAlias`**, which is LIVE and **worse than the tag path, because both things that softened the tag defect are absent**: `tag_aliases.alias_slug` is `NOT NULL` with no default and **no BEFORE trigger deriving it** (read off `information_schema` + `pg_trigger` — the only BEFORE trigger there is `tag_alias_reject_shadow`), so `''` is not an escape hatch and the caller genuinely must supply a value; and there is **no non-ASCII seal**, so the accent damage is real and stored. Measured against the live function: `Café Society` → **`caf-society`** against `cafe-society`, `dominación sexual masculina` → **`dominacin-…`** against `dominacion-…`, and that second value is **already the stored `alias_slug`** on rows of that shape. It now calls `normalize_tag_slug` **over RPC** — `has_function_privilege('authenticated', …, 'EXECUTE')` is true and a live PostgREST call returns `cafe-society` / `hiv-aids` / `pirate-s-bounty` / `buhne` at HTTP 200 — so there is ONE implementation and nothing to drift, and it **THROWS on failure rather than falling back to a local slugifier**: a lossy value cannot be repaired downstream, so no slug beats a wrong one the admin cannot see. **The corpus-wide alias number is deliberately NOT attributed to this path**: 3,445 of 12,596 aliases differ from canonical and 3,275 of those are accented, but only **18** are `approved` — the rest are the machine-minted Wikidata sitelink pool with its own slugifier, so quoting 3,445 as this form's damage would be the flattering number.

**IT REACHED PRODUCTION DATA, AND THE REPAIR WAS MEASURED AND REFUSED — which is the half worth keeping.** Six active tags carry the exact slug that regex produces. **Five are apostrophes, and for those the stripped form is the BETTER url**: `normalize_tag_slug` turns `'` into a separator, so the canonical of `Pirate's Bounty` is `pirate-s-bounty` against the stored `pirates-bounty`, and of `Let's Talk About...` is `let-s-talk-about`. **There is no convention to restore, either — the corpus splits 7/6** across the 13 active apostrophe names. Only **one** row is the slash class the fix is about (`Master/Slave Dynamics` → `masterslave-dynamics`), out of **33** active slash names, because almost no tag is born in the admin form. **Both rows whose canonical slug is already taken are the SAME NAME TWICE, one row per path, and the chronology inverts the obvious reading**: the stripped row came FIRST (2025-07-18 / 07-20) and the separated row later (2026-02-23 / 04-26), and the corpus then resolved BOTH in favour of the stripped row — `ma-am` is `merged` away with 0 assignments while `maam` is active, indexable and in search; `master-slave-dynamics` is `deprecated` while `masterslave-dynamics` is active and in search. So repair-to-canonical is **blocked by construction on the only two rows where it could have mattered** (the target slug is a live redirect trail) and **unjustified on the other four**. Under-reaching is the correct error. **Named rather than silently counted:** `masterslave-dynamics` is active, `seo_indexable` and in `search_documents` with a fused slug whose clean form is held by a deprecated empty row — freeing it requires the corrupt row to die, and here the corrupt row is the one carrying the content and the search presence, which `20261211120000`'s rule does not reach.

**Verified on prod against the DEPLOYED functions, not the files.** Five probes in a transaction forced to roll back (0 probe rows left afterwards): `slug=''` on `Zzp/Probe Alpha` derives `zzp-probe-alpha` with the separator intact; a pre-stripped `zzpprobe-beta` survives unchanged, confirming the database cannot undelete; a name-only UPDATE HOLDS the slug; `Zzp Hiv and Aids Probe` title-cases to `Zzp HIV and AIDS Probe`, so the acronym rung and the function-word rung of `83000101143000` both fire; an explicit slug write still moves it. At the HTTP layer with a crawler UA, `/tags/hiv-aids` serves 200 with its own title and `rel=canonical`, while `/tags/hivaids` and `/tags/hiv-and-aids` are both hard 404s **beside a nonsense-slug control that also 404s** — without that control a 404 is equally consistent with the route being dead. **The browser layer could not be tested here and that is recorded rather than glossed**: Chromium reads trust anchors from NSS, `$HOME/.pki/nssdb` does not carry the egress interception CA, and installing it was correctly refused as a TLS weakening — so `net::ERR_CERT_AUTHORITY_INVALID` blocks every browser-driven spec in this container while `curl` (which reads `/etc/ssl/certs`) succeeds. A grep of `cert9.db` for the CA name is NOT a substitute test: the file yields no readable strings at all, so it reports ABSENT for everything including a CA that is present — the vacuous-check class again, settled only by launching the browser. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/tagSlugClientDerivation.test.ts`, which asserts the **CALL SITE** (no behavioural test of the hook can see a caller that passes its own slug) over **comment-stripped** source, because the guard's own header quotes the deleted regex verbatim and an unstripped search would match the prose while the statement was back; mutation-tested **3/3** with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES.

**A THIRD ordering, and it found a seam every earlier one was blind to: the SUMMARY alone (2026-09-15, `64000101100000`).** Rounds two to five worked this backlog in usage order; round seven re-sorted it by what the prose is ABOUT after round four's hit rate was shown not to transfer. This pass ordered the surviving set on a third axis that needs no judgement to compute — the **lexical overlap between a row's own `description` and its published short/long description, lowest first**. A row whose description shares no content word with its own summary is either about a different subject or says nothing, and neither correlates with usage. **The ordering narrows what a human reads and does not decide**: `chubby-chaser` and `intimate-partner-abuse` both score ZERO shared words and are correct, and `dogging`, `monogamish` and `solo-poly` are surviving-but-fine — the fourth entry in this file to record that *surviving* means only that the prose is unchanged since the identifier was taken away. **What it surfaced is the half-repaired class of `60000301100100` seen from the other side: FOURTEEN of the fifteen rows already have `long_description IS NULL`** — an earlier pass removed the junk body and left the junk SUMMARY standing — and `short_description` is the LEAD LINE on `/tags/:slug` *and* is in `trg_search_documents_tag`'s column list, so it is also the search facet. A reader met **"Scottish dog breed" on a kink role**, and a reviewer spot-checking the body would have found nothing wrong. **Two labelled groups, and the separation is the point.** **A — the summary names a different thing** (8 rows, the row's own description establishes the sense): `golden-retriever` "Scottish dog breed"; `hussy` "Derogatory term for a sex worker" over a description whose own second sentence says the word is RECLAIMED and "worn proudly" — the summary asserting exactly what the description refuses, the `masc` shape; `paraboy` "Term for a male-identified person" over "a partial or near connection ... but NOT entirely"; `androx` "a term related to male hormones", the ANDROGEN namesake, on a non-binary gender identity; `under-consideration` "Term introduced in 1968", encyclopedic residue that does not even name a subject; `hand-feeding` "Feeding animals by hand"; `sword-play` "Fencing and sword fighting"; and `mama-bear` "LGBTQ+ term for a protective mother figure", which is a REAL and different sense on a row whose description establishes a nurturing dominant — the `darkroom` shape. **B — the summary states nothing a reader can use** (7 rows): `medical-play` "medical play" and `vetted` "Vetted" define the term with the term (the `hardpoint`/`impaired-driving`/`hiv-aids` defect, **fourth recorded instance**), and `slutface` "A term with complex connotations", `fluffing` "Fluffing refers to various practices", `shallowing` "Shallowing refers to a sexual practice", `foot-play` "Refers to foot-related activities" and `masturbating` "Masturbation discussion" say only that the term exists. **Group B CHOOSES NO SENSE** — each replacement restates the row's own `description` and nothing else, so it is strictly weaker than group A and there is no reading to get wrong; merging the two into one count of fifteen is how a later pass takes group A's licence and applies it to a row with no evidence. **No body is written or removed**: the fifteenth row, `masturbating`, has a correct body and is untouched, and minting a body from a one-line description is the guess this whole class came from. **The actor declaration IS load-bearing here** — all 15 rows are `human_reviewed` and the undeclared UPDATE returns `human_reviewed tag … cannot be modified by system:trigger`, verified live rather than assumed; round four's tranche was the opposite case, which is why each file states which one it is in. Deferred with the reason each cannot be reached: `warrior-princess` ("Term for strong female figures" is THIN, not wrong — **under-reaching is the correct error**), `feeder` (describes the community rather than the role, a filing-shaped mismatch), `chubby-chaser` (its summary is BETTER than its own description, so the description is not evidence), `sacred-play`/`anal-creampie` (bodies citing their own source or carrying the banned consent padding — the REGISTER class of `60000301100000`'s group C, which belongs in its own pass rather than muddying a summary seam), and the generic-sense cohort for the **fifth** pass running. Guarded by `tagProseSummarySeam.test.ts`, mutation-tested **11/11** with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES — including mutations that write `description` instead of `short_description` (the evidence the whole class rests on), that null a body, that drop a content guard, and that turn the positive `if v_bad <> 15` postcondition into the vacuous form that counts rows in a BAD state and so returns zero for a slug that has gone missing from the corpus. **The seam is fifteen rows and this file writes THIRTEEN, because a FIFTH concurrent collision on this table landed mid-review.** `63000101171500_tag_prose_half_repaired_and_self_citation` (#3733) worked the identical backlog at the same time, also calling itself round eight, and merged first — carrying `vetted` and `masturbating`. Both overlapping UPDATEs were DELETED rather than left to no-op: content-guarded, they would have matched nothing and been harmless at run time, but **a file that claims fifteen rows and changes thirteen misreports itself to the next reader**, and rewriting already-correct prose is the LLM rewrite both auto-apply paths were retired for. **The postconditions were deliberately NOT narrowed to thirteen** — they still assert the wrong text is gone across all fifteen slugs and that all fifteen carry a usable summary, so one check now covers both authors and would catch a regression in either half; narrowing them to a file's own writes is exactly what turns a rival repair into a `db push` abort on `main`. Verified by re-running the cut file against the post-#3733 corpus in a rolled-back transaction: 13 UPDATEs matched, all four postconditions passed. **Two details worth keeping.** The other session's header had already reasoned the composition out and its conclusion was right, but it was READ rather than trusted — the load-bearing fact is that it writes only `short_description`, which is what keeps `masturbating`'s body (and this file's postcondition on it) intact. And the collision cost a renumber, not the queue, because the branch was diffed against `main` before updating: `62000201100000` sorted **below** the newly applied `63000101171500`, which `check-migration-versions` rejects — the failure arrived as one red check on one PR rather than as an aborted `db push` taking every queued migration with it.

**The cohort five passes deferred as unreachable was reachable all along, and the difference is ONE WORD (2026-09-15, `65000101100000`).** Rounds four, five, the tail and round eight each ended by deferring "the generic-sense cohort" on the stated ground that each row's `description` AGREES with its prose, so the original rule structurally cannot reach it. **That is true of some of those rows and FALSE of others.** Round nine re-read them in full instead of re-reading the deferral, and the split is sharp: `bunny` description **"Cute animal role"** against summary "Small mammal in Leporidae family"; `doll` **"Toy-like role"** against "Model of a human or humanoid character"; `meerkat` **"Small mammal role"** against "Small mongoose species found in southern Africa"; `balloon` **"Balloon fetish role"** against "Inflatable bag made of flexible material"; `thrall` **"Bound servant"** against "Historical Scandinavian slave or serf". Read quickly, "Cute animal role" and "Small mammal in Leporidae family" look like two ways of saying rabbit — which is how the cohort was waved past five times. They are not. **The description TYPES the tag (this row is a ROLE a person takes) while the summary asserts the tag names an ANIMAL, an OBJECT or a HISTORICAL INSTITUTION**, and that is a contradiction about what kind of thing the tag is, which is exactly what the original rule is for. The word `role` in the description was the evidence, sitting on the row the whole time. **The general lesson is that a deferral is a claim about the data and decays like any other** — re-read the rows, not the sentence that deferred them; a reason that was right about seven rows was carried to nineteen. **The replacements say only what the description already says** — that this is a role — plus which animal or object the persona is named for, which comes from the tag's own name. No scene detail is invented. **Three bodies are NULLED rather than replaced**: `bunny` published rabbits as "herbivores, prey animals, livestock, and pets", `doll` ran to religious rituals, `meerkat` to brindled coat patterns — pure zoology and object encyclopedia on role pages, while each row's `description` is a single line, enough to state what the tag is and not enough to write a body from, so minting one would be the guess this class came from (the `doe`/`fae`/`flock` treatment of `60000301100100`). Nulling is safe and ASSERTED rather than assumed, and `long_description` is absent from `trg_search_documents_tag`'s column list so it causes no search churn. **`edge-switch` and `lactation-play` have CORRECT bodies and the file asserts they are NOT destroyed** — nulling a good body is the exact mirror of leaving a wrong one, so the verify block checks both directions. Group B is the established shape (`chauffeur` "Chauffeur" and `dawg` "Dawg" define the term with the term — the `hardpoint`/`impaired-driving`/`hiv-aids` defect, **fifth recorded instance**; `edge-switch` "BDSM practice" names no subject; `lactation-play` "Lactation play is a form of erotic play" says only that the term exists) and CHOOSES NO SENSE, each replacement restating the row's own description. **The deferral that remains is now SPLIT, because the old blanket label was hiding two different things**: `wolf` ("Pack animal"), `knight` ("Chivalrous warrior"), `reynard`, `familiar`, `satyress`, `goblin` and `villain` have a description that is ITSELF the generic sense, so there is genuinely no contradiction on the row and nothing to derive from — those are the rows the earlier deferrals correctly describe; while `angel`, `bimbo`, `catgirl`, `butler`, `acolyte` and `bootblack` ARE typed as roles but their summaries are NARROW rather than a different subject (an angel IS a supernatural entity, a butler IS a domestic worker), and **under-reaching is the correct error**. `pet` names TWO senses at once — description "Animal companion role" against a body about pet-friendly travel — the `host`/`unicorn` shape. The actor declaration IS load-bearing (all 9 rows `human_reviewed`, verified live: the undeclared UPDATE returns `human_reviewed tag … cannot be modified by system:trigger`). Dry-run on prod in a rolled-back transaction: 9 UPDATEs matched, all five postconditions passed. Guarded by `tagProseRoleTyped.test.ts`, mutation-tested **7/7** with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES — and **one mutation first read as a survivor because the target phrase WRAPS ACROSS TWO COMMENT LINES**, which the test's normalized `prose` helper correctly sees and a single-line pattern cannot; the assertion that the mutation had applied is what caught it, the sixth time this file has recorded that check.

**Round nine drew its own split in the wrong place, and the wider cohort is what showed it (2026-09-15, `74500101100000`).** Round nine repaired nine role-typed rows and then SPLIT the remaining deferral, keeping `angel`, `acolyte`, `butler`, `bimbo`, `catgirl` and `bootblack` back on the ground that their summaries are *"NARROW rather than a different subject — an angel IS a supernatural entity, a butler IS a domestic worker"*. **That test asks whether the REFERENT is correctly named, and it tracks nothing a reader cares about.** Selecting the cohort mechanically instead of by eye — `description ~* '(role|persona)'` over the surviving set, **27 rows** against the 19 round nine happened to look at — puts the two failure modes side by side: `lamb` description "Innocent sheep role" against summary **"Young sheep or sheep meat"**, and `butler` description "Service role" against **"Domestic worker in a large household"**. Both name their referent correctly; neither tells a reader of a kink glossary that the tag names a role a person takes. **The test that works is the one round nine's own group A used: DOES THE SUMMARY SAY THIS IS A ROLE?** Under it `angel`, `acolyte` and `butler` fail exactly as `bunny` did, and 20 rows are repaired here. **The lesson is the one round nine coined, applied to round nine: a deferral is a claim about the data and decays like any other — including a deferral made three hours earlier by the pass that coined the rule.** What made the reversal legitimate is NOT a re-reading of the same rows, which would just be thrash; it is the wider cohort, which is what makes the old split visible as arbitrary. **Widen the selection before re-litigating a judgement call.** Corroboration that the kink-role reading is right, measured rather than assumed: all 20 rows are `is_adult = true`, `human_reviewed = true`, and every one sits in `Dynamics & Roles` — **a zoology entry does not get filed adult**. Three rows are worth keeping individually. **`catgirl`'s body closes *"The concept of catgirls is not related to any real-world culture or identity"*** — on a row whose own description says it is a role people take, the prose denies the tag's subject outright. **`pet`'s body is not encyclopedia at all but a TAG-ADMIN NOTE addressed to nobody** (*"This tag is for discussions and information related to pets and animals in the context of LGBTQ+ travel… The tag is intended to provide a space for community members"*), and round nine's deferral of it as "two senses at once" is settled by measurement rather than judgement: **`pet` has ZERO live rows in `unified_tag_assignments`**, so there is no pet-friendly-travel usage to protect. **`guru` is the plain namesake shape** — summary "American rapper and term for mentor or guide", i.e. Guru of Gang Starr, over a description that is a full BDSM mentor-role definition. **Three labelled groups, because the licence differs**: A1 (14 rows) has a one-line description and the replacement adds only which animal, being or relationship the persona is named for, taken from the tag's own name; A2 (`guru`, `pharaoh`, `huntress`) has a full description and the replacement restates it; **A3 (`frog`, `goat`, `strawberry`) has a NULL summary and the literal-referent BODY is what a reader meets** — filling a null is not the LLM rewrite both auto-apply paths were retired for, and those three are additionally guarded on the body they are about to remove. **17 bodies are NULLED** (family Anatidae, genus Panthera, "the meat of sheep", "endemic to China", "the 2022 video game Acolyte", "also used as a family name"), safe and asserted because `enforce_tag_thin_page_gate` reads `tag_has_prose(description, short_description)` only and all 20 keep both; `beast`, `guru` and `pharaoh` already carry none and **are not given a redundant `long_description = null`**, which would be a no-op that misreports the diff. **The mirror assertion is on rows OUTSIDE the pass** — `aftercare` and `chosen-family`, same backlog, real hand-written bodies — because "the seventeen are gone" is equally satisfied by a sweep that took everything. Deferred, each with its own reason: the `wolf`/`knight`/`reynard`/`familiar`/`satyress`/`goblin`/`villain` set, where the description is ITSELF the generic sense (round nine was right about exactly these); `parent`, `event-organizer` and `pillow-prince`, where the summary describes a kind of PERSON and **under-reaching is the correct error**; `diva`, where a replacement derivable from "Prima donna role" plus the tag's own name would **define the term with the term**, the defect this backlog keeps finding; `bootblack`, where the leather-community role and the shoeshine trade are genuinely two things; and **`bimbo`, deferred for a DIFFERENT reason that must not be collapsed into the others — the defect there is GENDERING, not subject** ("Slang term for a stereotypical attractive, sexualized woman" writes every non-woman out of a role page), which is the `femme`/`man`/`drag-show` class and belongs in a pass with its siblings. Actor declaration load-bearing, verified live. Dry-run on prod in a rolled-back transaction: 20 UPDATEs matched, all five postconditions passed, rollback confirmed by re-reading three rows. Guarded by `tagProseRoleTypedCompletion.test.ts`, mutation-tested **13/13** with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES — and **two mutations first read as survivors because their target phrases WRAP ACROSS TWO COMMENT LINES**, which the test's normalized `prose` helper sees and a single-line pattern cannot; the assert-it-applied check caught both, the **seventh** time this file has recorded that trap.

**An abbreviation collided with an ordinary word, and the sweep took the word (2026-09-15, `75000101100000`).** Round eleven takes the class every earlier ordering surfaced and none finished — **the summary that states nothing a reader can use** — selected corpus-wide over the surviving set rather than from wherever the previous ordering happened to reach. 21 rows in three labelled groups. **The two sharpest defects in this backlog since `queerness` are both abbreviations**: `/tags/pov` published **"Lack of financial resources for basic needs"** over a full paragraph about POVERTY, on an indexable `Fetishes` row; `/tags/uncut` published **"Uncut refers to unedited content"**, the film-editing sense, where the word means uncircumcised. **Both have `description IS NULL`**, so the original rule cannot reach them and they are repaired under the widening `51500101152700` introduced — the sense may be established by a CATEGORY that admits exactly one reading of the tag's own name. **The REFUSAL is the load-bearing half of that rule and is what keeps it honest.** `solo` looks identical (Fetishes, "Traveling alone", a body about solo travel) and was **not** repaired: this is a TRAVEL platform, so "solo" has a live ordinary reading here and the category settles nothing. `black`, `peaches` and `ebony` are refused on the same ground. **A category only establishes a sense where the site itself has no second reading for that word.** Group B (14 rows) restates the row's own description and **chooses no sense**, so it carries none of group A's licence: `breeder`'s summary was the single word "Breeder" (define-the-term-with-the-term, **sixth** recorded instance, with `uncut`); `warrior-princess`, `alpha-pet` and `freak` opened "Term for…", which says only that the term exists; and **`drag` — 3,897 assignments, the largest row in the backlog — published "Refers to drag culture or performance"**. **`hard-limits` is the one whose evidence is its own sibling**: `soft-limits` correctly publishes "Boundaries that are flexible or negotiable" while `hard-limits` published "Boundaries in BDSM practices", dropping **non-negotiable** — the only thing distinguishing the two terms, and on a consent page that is not thinness but the whole content. Group C fills a NULL summary from the row's own description, which destroys nothing; **`lgbtq` is why the group exists — 5,340 assignments, indexable, and no summary at all**, so the highest-usage row in the backlog had no lead line and no search-facet text. **THE THIN-PAGE GATE IS AN `OR`, NOT AN `AND`, and that is what makes group A legal** — `tag_has_prose` is `coalesce(nullif(btrim(description),''), short_description) is not null`, read off the live catalog — so a row with no description stays publishable precisely because the summary is written. The postcondition **calls the real function** instead of re-implementing it; the stricter hand-rolled "both present" form used one migration earlier would have failed on `pov` and `uncut` and read as a defect in the repair. **Call the predicate, do not restate it.** **Only TWO bodies are nulled and the restraint is the point**: this tranche keeps nineteen it could have swept, including `mademoiselle`'s generic French-dictionary body while `monsieur`'s is already in the D/s register — an asymmetry inside one pair, left standing because under-reaching is the correct error (the `casting` rule: repair only the wrong FIELDS). The mirror assertion names six rows whose bodies must survive. Deferred with reasons that are not "we did not get to them": **`titica`** — an Angolan musician on a Fetishes row where the sweep overwrote the **description** as well, so the evidence itself is contaminated and there is nothing to derive from; **`zaddy`**, whose description is the truncated fragment *"Zaddy can be defined in two ways:"* with nothing after the colon, making it unusable as evidence when the summary is serviceable; and `stepdad`, `parent`, `locker-room`, `interracial`, `hairy`, `young`, `potato-salad` as generic-but-not-wrong. **One new finding is recorded rather than folded in: a summary pasted onto SEVERAL rows.** `priest` and `priestess` share "Religious leader performing sacred rituals" verbatim, and `algolagnia`, `algophilia` and `masochist` all share "Deriving pleasure from pain or humiliation" — one Wikidata description stamped across a family, which is a different defect shape from anything this series has worked and deserves its own pass. Dry-run on prod in a rolled-back transaction: 21 UPDATEs matched, all five postconditions passed. Guarded by `tagProseSaysNothing.test.ts`, mutation-tested **12/12** with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES — including re-implementing the thin-page gate as an AND, sweeping a control body, and taking the refused `solo` row anyway.

**One summary stamped across a family, and two tags that published a FRUIT-FLY PROTEIN (2026-09-15, `76000101100000`).** Round eleven ended by naming a defect shape it had found and deliberately did not work. Round twelve works it, found by query rather than by eye — `group by short_description having count(*) > 1` over active tags — and **the result SPLITS IN TWO, which is the finding**. Most shared summaries are **not a prose defect at all**: `marriage`/`married`, `apparel`/`clothing`, `educator`/`teacher`, `anal-master`/`ass-master` share a summary because they are the same concept twice. That is a **duplicate-TAG problem for a merge pass**, and rewriting one side to make them differ would paper over it. What IS a defect is one string stamped across rows that are **different things**, where the shared summary erases the distinction the family exists to draw: **five gender identities — `boyflux`, `girlflux`, `glitchgender`, `pupgender`, `versandrogyne` — all published "A non-binary gender identity"**, true of every one and distinguishing none; four sadist rows published one line whose qualifiers (dominant, sensual, sexual) are the entire reason the four rows exist. 20 rows repaired. **`futch` and `crumbs` both published "Protein found in Drosophila melanogaster"** — and unlike everything else in this series they are live **wrong-ENTITY** rows the 2026-08-29 repair never dispositioned, still carrying the protein's QID. **Both identifiers were resolved live before the file was written, not inferred from the number**: Q29813072 is **"Futsch Dmel_CG34387"** and Q29809512 "Crumbs Dmel_CG6383", both `P31 Q8054` (protein). **The label is the lesson: the sweep matched the tag `futch` to a gene named FUTSCH, one letter apart** — close enough that a name check passes, and `tag-wiki-guard.ts`'s plausibility arm has nothing to object to either, because a protein is a perfectly plausible thing for a word to denote. Identifiers **NULLED, never repointed** (the weekly `tag_medical_codes_sync` / `tag_wikidata_hierarchy` rebuild is why), after measuring that neither row carries a `wikipedia_url`, a medical code or a relation. **NOTHING is written to `tag_wikidata_repair_audit`, deliberately**: that table is the INPUT to `tag_disowned_prose_signals()`, so a row there would make the tag "repaired" and its prose-at-repair-time the baseline the sentinel compares against — perturbing a live metric to record what the file already records. The prior values survive in `tag_change_log.before_data`. **Check what consumes a table before writing to it.** **The METRIC is narrower than the defect, and the entry says so rather than quoting a flattering number**: only **3 of the 20** rows (`algolagnia`, `algophilia`, `masochist`) sit inside the sentinel's set, because it keys on that audit table and the other 17 were never dispositioned. `sd_surviving` falls by 3, not by 20. `foot-bottom` and `sensation-bottom` both published **"A term for the receptive partner in anal sex"** — the wrong PRACTICE on a glossary where `bottom`, `top` and `switch` are separate live entries — and `foot-bottom`'s body opened *"The term 'foot bottom' is not a widely recognized or standard term"*, prose denying the subject of its own row, the `catgirl` shape. **`sadist` is deliberately NOT repaired and is asserted as a control**: its summary is correct for the unqualified term, and repairing the three qualified rows is what ends the collision — an assertion that only checked "the family no longer shares a string" would be satisfied by rewriting the one row that was already right. **The positive postcondition is `count(distinct short_description) = 20`**, which states the file's purpose directly. Four bodies nulled, sixteen kept. `pupgender`'s description carries mojibake; the replacement is written in clean text and the description is left alone, because this series never writes that column. Guarded by `tagProseOneSummaryManyRows.test.ts`, mutation-tested **15/15** with comment-only controls that correctly SURVIVE — **and two mutations SURVIVED the first round and both were real gaps in the guard, not false alarms**. (1) Widening postcondition 1 from the file's own 20 slugs to the whole corpus passed, because the assertion `where slug in \([^)]*'futch'` matched the slug list of a *later* postcondition — the vacuous-assertion class again, and the mutation is dangerous rather than cosmetic: corpus-wide, `sadist` legitimately keeps its string, so the check would RAISE on apply and abort `db push` for the whole repo. Scope an assertion to the half of the statement it is about. (2) Neutering `if v_bad <> 0` to `if v_bad < 0` left the RAISE text, the slug list and the string list all intact, so every assertion anchored on those still passed while the check had stopped checking — the trap `60000101160000` already recorded. The guard now counts the CONDITIONS (`if v_bad <> 0` exactly six times, `if v_bad <> 20` once) and refuses any loosened comparison anywhere in the block.

**Round thirteen is three rows and a REFUSAL, and the refusal is the substance (2026-09-15, `77000101100000`).** The class this round set out to work — prose written in the register `TAG_STYLE_SYSTEM` bans — was measured, hand-read, and mostly **declined**. **The count was wrong twice before it was right.** An earlier note put the consent-padding cohort at 93 rows; re-measuring gives **157 active rows** carrying a sentence of the form *"it's essential to prioritize / remember / note / understand …"*. A first grouping then reported **two distinct sentences**, which would have made this stamped boilerplate and a clean mechanical deletion — **and that was an artifact of the regex**, because `substring` returns the first match and the grouping collapsed on the truncation. Grouped correctly the 157 rows carry roughly **150 DISTINCT sentences**. **Re-measure before believing a number that makes the work easy.** Hand-reading all of them splits the class in two, and the split is why nothing is swept. Some are contentless exhortation the style guide is right to ban. But many are **real content wearing the banned register, on exactly the pages where it matters most**: `rape-play` (*"rape play is not the same as actual non-consensual acts, and it should never be used to normalize or trivialize sexual violence"*), `piss-drinker` (*"carries health risks if not practiced safely"*), `hiv-transmission`, `water-bondage`, `electrostimulation`, `self-bondage`, `vajazzle`, `ampallang` (safety facts), `morosexual` and `novosexual` (*"not an officially recognized term in the scientific community"* — an epistemic caveat), `latino` (the Latino/Hispanic distinction), `alloromantic` (romantic vs sexual orientation), `lipstick-lesbian` and `exclusive` (how the term is contested). **No regex separates the two groups** — both are one trailing sentence in the same voice — so a sweep keyed on phrasing strips harm-reduction and consent-boundary content from a kink and sexual-health glossary. **The refusal is made ENFORCEABLE rather than merely written down**: a postcondition asserts that eight named content-bearing rows still carry their sentences, so a later pass reaching for the easy sweep breaks this file's own check. That is the same conclusion `styleguide_content_drift()` reached for `vibrant` — a counter, not a rewrite — and the same one the tag prose judge earned by retracting 16 of 18 rows with 13 of them wrong. **What IS repaired: `trampling`, and it is the original rule at its sharpest.** Category `Fetishes`, `is_adult`, **indexable**, description "Walking or standing on someone" — and the published summary was **"Cause of death by being walked on"** over a body about crowd disasters, animal stampedes and the German and French words for being crushed. Summary replaced, body nulled. Plus `puberty-blockers` (17 uses, indexable) and `polyandrous`, which closed by **citing their own source to the reader** (*"According to scientific research published in 2020…"*) — the `impaired-driving` / `sti-testing` register, now its fourth recorded instance. **The offending sentences are deleted and every other sentence is kept BYTE-IDENTICAL, and the postcondition asserts each survivor in full** — a check that merely confirms the citation is gone passes equally against a complete rewrite, which is the retired experiment wearing a fix's clothes. Guarded by `tagProseRegisterAndRefusal.test.ts`, mutation-tested **6/6** with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES, including mutations that delete the refusal postcondition, sweep a protected row, and rewrite a body instead of trimming one sentence from it.
**Rounds six, seven and eight — 61 more rows, and each round's METHOD is the part worth keeping (2026-09-15, `61000101174500` / `62000101163000` / `63000101171500`).** Selection rule unchanged throughout: repair only where the row's own evidence establishes a sense the published prose contradicts, derive the summary FROM that evidence, never write `description`.

**Round six (30 rows) found that the corpus had already decided three of them.** `51500101143000` repaired `woman` because its body was gamete essentialism; `man` and `femme` followed. Their siblings were never touched, so `masc` still published *"a male is an organism that produces sperm"* against its OWN description reading "A masculine gender expression, REGARDLESS OF GENDER IDENTITY", and `girl`/`boy` the same shape. **This is not a matter of taste and the repo says so independently**: `styleguide_terms` rates "born a woman | biologically female | natal sex" at severity `never`, so these were a live violation of the published standard on indexable pages. The replacement summaries MIRROR the already-repaired siblings rather than being authored, and the bodies are NULLED — `woman` has a real body and is the obvious model for writing the other three properly, but writing three identity definitions is authoring, not repair. Also `femminiello`, a Neapolitan social role collapsed into "a trans woman" (erasing the distinction the word exists to carry); `whore`/`floozy`, which published sex-work prose on rows whose description says "Promiscuous person" **and** used the `never`-rated "prostitute" in our own voice; and 22 namesake artifacts (`scat`→adipose tissue, `owner`→landlord, `toymaker`→toys, `host`→TV presenter, `cunt`→the vulva).

**THE POSTCONDITION IN ROUND SIX'S FIRST DRAFT WOULD HAVE ABORTED `db push` ON MAIN, and that is the transferable part.** It asserted `long_description IS NULL` — an assertion about ITS OWN IMPLEMENTATION. `#3716`'s `disowned_tail` had already applied and overlaps four of the 30 rows, and on `masc` it writes a real body where this file nulls one, so the assertion would have RAISEd on somebody else's BETTER fix and stranded every migration queued behind it. Re-keying it on the DEFECT TEXT (*"still publish the essentialist body"*) let both compose; verified live afterwards — `masc` and `cunt` kept #3716's wording and the guarded UPDATEs correctly no-opped. **Soft on preconditions, hard on postconditions, is what decides whether a concurrent repair costs a diff or costs the queue.**

**Round seven (13 rows) corrected the ORDERING, not the rule.** Rounds two–five worked the backlog in usage order and round four declared the head "worked out"; round five already corrected that once. This pass re-read the surviving set sorted by WHAT THE PROSE IS ABOUT and **12 of its 13 rows are usage 0**, so every usage-ordered sweep had sorted them below anything anyone ever read. `pet-owner` published "dogs, cats, birds" against a description opening "In BDSM"; `rough-sex` published the definition of **BDSM**, which is a separate live tag, so the corpus stated BDSM twice and the rough-sex page said nothing about rough sex; `older-women` published **"Women aged 50+"**, a demographic with an invented numeric threshold appearing nowhere on the row, against a description establishing an attraction.

**Round eight (18 rows) made the half-repaired class a QUERY instead of a hand-search.** Round six found such rows one signature at a time; the shape is exactly expressible against the sentinel's own CTE — **the summary still matches what the disowned entity produced while the body no longer does**, i.e. someone repaired the body and left the lead line. Five rows, and in each the row's own body is correct authored prose, so the evidence sits on the row twice and no inference is needed: `face-fucking` (indexable, careful negotiated-play body) led with **"Adult film production company"**; `pretzel` led with **"Baked pastry made from dough"** over "A flexible bottom who enjoys being folded into demanding positions". Plus seven thin/meta summaries — `vetted`'s summary was literally the term, `power-bottom` published the definition of `bottom` (the `rough-sex` shape again) — and six rows CITING THEIR OWN SOURCE to the reader (*"According to a scientific article published in 1971…"*), which is **exactly six rows corpus-wide**, so that cohort went to zero rather than being sampled, with a corpus-wide postcondition so a seventh fails too. The citation removal is a `replace()` of one exact sentence and **never a literal body**: a `replace()` cannot author prose, so every other sentence survives byte-identical by construction rather than by retyping. One judgement call stated rather than buried — `hormone-blockers` and `anal-warts` lose a substantive-sounding claim with the citation, taken deliberately because *"According to a scientific article published on 09 October 2020"* identifies no article, so it is the APPEARANCE of a citation, and an unverifiable medical claim on a trans-health page is worse than none.

**THE CONCURRENCY OUTCOME INVERTED THE PLAN AND COMPOSED ANYWAY, which is the strongest evidence yet for the discipline.** A concurrent session's `tag_prose_summary_seam` was found on an unmerged branch by `git diff --name-only origin/main -- supabase/migrations/` **before pushing** — the one-command check — overlapping on `masturbating` and `vetted`. Its version sorted BELOW round eight's, so the plan assumed it would apply first and round eight's two UPDATEs would no-op. It then **renumbered ABOVE** (to `64000101100000`) to clear a moved ceiling, so the order reversed and round eight applied first. It still composed, because both sides content-guard on the same defect text and both postconditions key on the wrong text being gone: verified live, both rows carry round eight's wording and the other file's UPDATEs no-opped. **The two rows were deliberately KEPT rather than dropped** — `60000301100000` dropped its overlaps because the other migration had already APPLIED, but where neither has merged, dropping a row on the assumption the other session lands it is how BOTH sessions drop it and nobody fixes it.

**ROUND NINE THEN CORRECTED ROUND EIGHT'S OWN DEFERRAL REASONING, and the correction is the reusable part.** Rounds four, five, the tail and round eight each deferred "the generic-sense cohort" on the stated ground that the row's `description` AGREES with its prose. `65000101100000` shows that is **false for a large part of it, and the difference is one word**: "Cute animal role" against "Small mammal in Leporidae family" reads like two ways of saying rabbit, and is not — **the description TYPES the tag as a ROLE while the summary asserts it names an ANIMAL, an OBJECT or a HISTORICAL INSTITUTION**, which is a contradiction about what kind of thing the tag is, exactly what the original rule is for. The word `role` was in the description all along. `bunny`, `doll`, `meerkat`, `balloon` and `thrall` were repaired on that reading, and `balloon`/`edge-switch`/`lactation-play` were rows round eight had explicitly named as "generic but not wrong". **A deferral reason is a claim, and five rounds repeating it did not make it true.**

**Measured trajectory across the three rounds** (`tag_disowned_prose_signals()`, each delta matching its statement counts exactly and each dry-run on prod in a rolled-back transaction first): sd_surviving 277 → 251 → 240 → 228, ld_surviving 216 → 194 → 182 → 175, indexable 236 → 210 → 201 → 191; with rounds nine to thirteen since, the live figures measured after #3744 applied are **170 / 152 / 161** and self-citation **0 corpus-wide** — that cohort is closed, not sampled. Guarded by `tagProseEssentialismAndNamesakes.test.ts` (12/12 + control), `tagProseRoleAndVenueSenses.test.ts` (17/17) and `tagProseHalfRepairedAndSelfCitation.test.ts` (25/25 across two rounds), every suite with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES. **Round seven had three assertions SURVIVE their first mutation round, all one recorded class**: every defect string appears TWICE — in an UPDATE's content guard and in the postcondition — so asserting over the whole file matched the guard and passed while the postcondition was gutted; scope the assertion to the half of the statement it is about. **Standing and deliberately not closed**: the advice-register padding (*"it is essential to…"*) measured **385 rows** and sits at 383 (two fell out incidentally when round eight nulled two bodies that carried it) — a whole-corpus register decision, not a prose repair, and bulk-rewriting it is the experiment this repo already ran and retired.

**Round fourteen — the GENDERING class, and the deferral re-read that mostly held (2026-09-15, `79000101100000`).** Round ten found this class, named it, and deliberately sent it to a pass with its siblings: `bimbo` was deferred because *"the defect there is GENDERING, not subject"*. This is that pass — 22 rows where the CONCEPT is right and the prose writes most of the audience out of their own glossary entry, the `femme` / `man` / `drag-show` / `masc` / `crotch-rope` class. **Selected mechanically rather than by eye, which is round ten's own lesson**: the summary names a gender while the row's own `description` does not, over the role and identity categories. That returned 35 rows; hand-reading gave 22. **The regex narrows what a human reads; it does not decide, and what it found and a human REFUSED is the more useful half** — `cuckoldress`, `satyress`, `nun`, `brother`, `big-sister`, `little-sister` and `milkmaid` carry gender in their own NAMES the way the `-ess` and sibling words do; `cottaging`'s own description also says gay men; `final-girl` and `nymphic-domme` are gendered in the field the term comes from, where **under-reaching is the correct error**; `genderqueer`, `transfeminism` and `gender-gap` are correct as written; and `transmasc` was refused because its description is the GENERIC trans definition and so establishes nothing about that tag — the generic-sense shape. **The sharpest row is `brat-queen`, whose description says "their" and "They" twice over while the summary answered "a dominant, youthful woman"**; `masculinization`'s description says "a person of ANY GENDER EXPRESSION" in as many words against a summary reading "Biological development of male characteristics", the `masc` shape exactly; and **`switchtress` carried two defects, the worse being the wrong PRACTICE** — "a female dominant" on a glossary where top, bottom and switch are separate live entries, the `foot-bottom` shape. **A second labelled group is anatomy published as gender** (`cock-and-ball-torture`, `cock-worshipper`, `yoni-massage`): the row's own description is anatomical and the summary restated it as a gender, and on a platform whose readers include trans people "male genitals" and "the penis and testicles" are not interchangeable — the description had already chosen the right one. **BODY DISCIPLINE IS STATED ONCE AND HELD TO: the body is touched in exactly two ways and no others** — nulled where it is the wrong subject or adds nothing beyond the gendered claim (16 rows, safe because `enforce_tag_thin_page_gate` reads `tag_has_prose(description, short_description)` only and every one keeps a description), or altered by an **exact-phrase `replace()`** for an anatomical mis-gendering (2 rows). **A `replace()` cannot author prose**, so every other sentence survives byte-identical by construction rather than by retyping. No body is authored; `bimbo` (real etymology), `yoni-massage` (careful correct prose whose only fault was the summary above it) and `gender-variance` keep bodies this file does not touch, and the verify block asserts they SURVIVE — nulling a good body is the exact mirror of leaving a wrong one. **Round thirteen's refusal is re-asserted inside this file**, because sixteen body nulls are exactly the shape that could quietly take a protected row with them: one check, two authors. **The deferral re-read is the other half of the round, and it mostly HELD, which is worth recording as a result rather than as a non-event.** Of nine rows deferred with stated reasons, eight still stand on re-reading the ROWS rather than the sentence that deferred them — `titica`'s evidence is still contaminated (the sweep overwrote its `description` too), `zaddy`'s description is still the truncated fragment "Zaddy can be defined in two ways:", and `wolf`/`knight`/`reynard`/`familiar`/`goblin`/`villain`/`satyress` still have a description that IS the generic sense, which round nine was right about and round ten confirmed. **One became reachable, and only because a rule that did not exist at deferral time now does**: `unicorn` was deferred by `60000301100100` as naming two senses at once, and rounds nine and ten then established that a description typing the row as a ROLE contradicts a summary asserting the tag names an ANIMAL — it is filed `Dynamics & Roles`, its own description names the third-person sense, and the summary answered with the horned beast. `host` had meanwhile repaired itself. **A deferral is a claim about the data and decays like any other — including when re-reading confirms it.** `diva` is the second reversal: round ten deferred it because any summary derivable from "Prima donna role" plus the tag's own name would define the term with the term, and under the gendering lens it is reachable, because a prima donna is a leading performer and that is statable without the word diva. **The METRIC moves by 3, not 22, and the entry says so rather than quoting a flattering number**: only `unicorn`, `bimbo` and `diva` sit inside `tag_disowned_prose_signals()`'s set, so `sd_surviving` goes **170 → 167** and `ld_surviving` **152 → 151** (only `unicorn` leaves the body set, because `bimbo`'s body is deliberately kept). The other 19 were never dispositioned by the 2026-08-29 repair and the sentinel is structurally blind to them. Actor declaration IS load-bearing (21 of 22 rows `human_reviewed`; `daddy` is the exception), verified live rather than assumed — the undeclared UPDATE returns `human_reviewed tag 5da4bb24-… cannot be modified by system:trigger`. Dry-run on prod in a rolled-back transaction: 22 UPDATEs matched, all eight postconditions passed, rollback confirmed by re-reading four rows. **Deliberately NOT done and named rather than counted:** `gender-variance`'s BODY carries the same pathologising framing as its summary and repairing it means authoring prose, which is the line this series does not cross; and the two standing refusals (the 156-row banned-register cohort, the 405-row advice-padding class) are untouched. Guarded by `tagProseGenderingAndNarrowing.test.ts`, mutation-tested **11/11 with two comment-only controls that correctly SURVIVE** — and **one mutation SURVIVED the first round and was a real gap**: short-circuiting a postcondition with `where false and slug in (…)` leaves the slug list, the regex AND the `if v_bad <> 8` all intact, so every string-anchored assertion stayed green while the check had stopped counting. **Anchoring on the CONDITION is not enough when the PREDICATE can be neutered instead** — the guard now refuses `false` anywhere in the verify block, refuses a pre-seeded counter, and counts that all eight postconditions actually read `unified_tags`.

**Round fifteen — the BODY-SIDE SEAM, and the first axis that is not the summary (2026-09-15, `82000101100000`).** Rounds two to fourteen worked this backlog on nine orderings and **every one of them SELECTED on `short_description`**; the body was only ever touched as a CONSEQUENCE, nulled when the summary above it was wrong. **Nothing had ever selected on the body itself**, and that is the axis here — new in the only sense that matters, because it reaches rows no earlier ordering could. Five body-side signatures were measured over the 2,811 active rows carrying a body (anatomy-as-gender 13, the prose denies its own subject 3, the body cites Wikidata/Wikipedia to the reader 12, the body is a disambiguation list 6, the body names an album/game/genus 5) giving **39 distinct rows; hand-reading all 39 gives 15**. **Most of the 39 are the signature matching CORRECT prose** — `intersex-rights`, `intersexuality`, `intersex-male` and `vulva` all say "male or female bodies", which is the correct intersex phrasing — so once again the regex narrows what a human reads and does not decide. **THE CLASS THAT ONLY THIS AXIS CAN FIND is group B: the summary is already correct and the body alone is wrong**, which a summary-ordered sweep reads as clean and moves past. It is round eight's half-repaired class seen from the other side — there the body had been fixed and the summary left standing. `vivisector` carried a careful authored summary over a body that is a disambiguation list (vivisection, a Marvel superhero, a Patrick White novel, a first-person shooter); **`female` had a correct summary over a body opening "an organism that produces the ovum"** — gamete essentialism, the register `styleguide_terms` rates `never` and the class round six repaired on `woman`, `man`, `masc`, `girl` and `boy`, with `female` the sibling that pass missed; `dance-floor` published a flooring-products dump ending "the term has multiple meanings across different contexts"; `cake-and-cunnilingus-day` asserted "its origins are unclear" where the row's OWN description states the origin exactly; and `nantaimori` opened by conflating itself with `nyotaimori`, the one distinction the row exists to draw. **No summary is written in that group** — rewriting a correct summary is the LLM rewrite both auto-apply paths were retired for. Group A is the original rule at its plainest (`girlboss` published **South Park's Wendy Testaburger**, `unsure` published **Wikidata's "disputed" QUALIFIER value**, `rearrange-guts` published gastrointestinal surgery, `cock-worship` the anthropology of phallic religion, `androgynous` the naming sense) and **`foot-top` is settled by its own SIBLING**: `foot-bottom` already read "The receiving partner in foot play." with no body while `foot-top` still published foot-hygiene advice plus a sentence denying the term exists. `hunk` is kept in its own group because it rests on the widened rule (`description IS NULL`, sense established by the category) and **the REFUSAL half of that rule was applied, not just cited** — `solo` was refused in round eleven because a TRAVEL platform has a live ordinary reading of that word, and "Hunk" filed under Fetishes and flagged `is_adult` has no second reading here. **SEVEN IDENTIFIERS ARE NULLED AND TWO ARE DELIBERATELY KEPT, which is the distinction the round exists to draw, and all eleven were resolved live against `wbgetentities` rather than inferred from the prose.** Nulled as namesakes, with the `wikipedia_url` as the proof: Q47788 *Wendy Testaburger* (`/wiki/Wendy_Testaburger`), Q18912752 *disputed*, Q3378002 *phallus cult*, Q3409032 *unisex given name*, Q18039772 ***HUNK, protein-coding gene in Homo sapiens*** (the third and fourth gene namesakes after `futch` and `crumbs`), Q7937924 *Vivisector, Marvel character*, Q74107953 ***Dance Floor, vocal track by Zapp, 1982***. **KEPT because they are RIGHT: Q1243210 really is the sex position and Q10940 really is the garment — the `methadone` rule, that a correct identifier does not make the prose derived from it correct.** On both, the ENTITY'S OWN English description is the defect verbatim ("penetrated both vaginally and anally by two men"; "undergarment originally designed for supporting the male genitalia"), which is exactly how that prose got written, and the fix is the prose. A sweep that cleared all nine would satisfy "the seven are gone", so **both halves are asserted**. Identifiers are NULLED and never repointed, because the weekly `tag_medical_codes_sync` / `tag_wikidata_hierarchy` rebuild from them; measured first, none of the seven carries a medical code or a relation. **Nothing is written to `tag_wikidata_repair_audit`** — it is the INPUT to `tag_disowned_prose_signals()`, so a row there would make these tags "repaired" and perturb the live metric to record what the file already records (round twelve's rule). Body discipline unchanged: 13 nulls, one exact-phrase `replace()` on `jockstrap`, no body authored, and `manties` and `jockstrap` asserted to SURVIVE alongside round fourteen's `bimbo` and `yoni-massage`. `manties` is the one row where a NULL summary is FILLED from the row's own description — it is indexable with a real hand-written body and no lead line at all. **THE METRIC MOVES BY ONE, NOT FIFTEEN.** Replaying the sentinel's own CTE over the 15 slugs returns exactly two rows and only `nantaimori` counts as surviving; its summary is untouched and its body nulled, so **`ld_surviving` 147 → 146 while `sd_surviving` stays at 167** and `indexable_surviving` stays at 158. The other 14 were never dispositioned by the 2026-08-29 repair. **Residue named rather than swept:** the 22 `tag_sources` rows citing the seven disowned entities stay, because all 22 are `is_public = false` and none renders. Actor declaration load-bearing (13 of 15 rows `human_reviewed`), verified live. Dry-run on prod in a rolled-back transaction: 10/10 summaries, 13/13 bodies nulled, 7/7 identifiers cleared, 2/2 bodies kept, all ten postconditions passed, rollback confirmed by re-reading four rows. Guarded by `tagProseBodySideSeam.test.ts`, mutation-tested **13/13 with two comment-only controls that correctly SURVIVE** — and one control first reported as broken rather than as a survivor, because its pattern was not present in the file. **That is the check this file has now recorded seven times working as intended**: a mutation that does not apply is indistinguishable from a guard that does not fire unless the harness says which happened.

**Round sixteen — the concept published as a COMMUNITY, and the first round whose metric moves by ZERO (2026-09-19, `99960101100000`).** Rounds two to fifteen worked the disowned-prose backlog on ten orderings. **This one is not a slice of that backlog and the file says so up front:** all thirteen rows carry NO `wikidata_id` and NO `tag_wikidata_repair_audit` row, so `tag_disowned_prose_signals()` is structurally blind to them and **`ld_surviving`, `sd_surviving` and `indexable_surviving` do not move at all** — 146 / 167 / 158 before and after. They are the OTHER half of the same producer: CLAUDE.md already records that rows which never had a QID got prose from a **name-only lookup**, and this is that cohort. Quoting a sentinel movement here would have been the flattering number. **THE AXIS: the row's own `description` defines a TERM — a person-type, a behaviour, an orientation — and the published prose reframes it as a COMMUNITY, a PLACE or a TAG.** It is round nine's role-typing one step out: there the contradiction was "a role" against "an animal", here it is "a person or a practice" against "a group you can join". **`uranic` is the sharpest row and is a factual error as well as a wrong subject** — its body describes URANIAN, the 19th-century historic term, and attributes it to **Karl Maria Kertbeny, who coined "homosexual"/"heterosexual"; the Uranian vocabulary is Karl Heinrich Ulrichs'** — while the row's own description defines the modern orientation label whose feminine counterpart is `neptunic`, a tag this corpus already holds separately. `sex-favorable` is an asexual-spectrum identity published as a venue facet, its body ending *"This tag is used to indicate destinations or spaces"*; `vulturing` is INVERTED by its own summary ("Unwanted, attention-seeking behavior") where the description establishes circling someone emotionally vulnerable — a vulture targets vulnerability, it does not seek attention; `queer-for-queer-q4q` turns a dating preference into a mutual-aid initiative; `horny-net-geek-hng` publishes *"appears to be a group … Without specific sources, it's difficult to provide more detailed information"*, the model's own uncertainty shipped as a definition. **THE SPLIT IS WHAT MAKES THE SIGNATURE TRUSTWORTHY and most of what it returns is CORRECT prose**: `trans-community` IS a community, `rope-dojo` IS a space, `sexual-assault-resources` IS a set of resources, `peer-rope` IS a community and a practice, `needle-exchange-program-nep` IS a programme — none is touched and all five are asserted to survive. **Group B (3) CHOOSES NO SENSE**: `ukete`, `sensual-hedonist` and `smart-ass-sadomasochist` have a one-line description, so the replacement restates it and nothing more — `ukete` had published the summary **"Ukete is a term"** (define-the-term-with-the-term, **seventh** recorded instance) over a body admitting *"its meaning and usage are unclear"*. Body discipline: **12 nulls and ONE kept** — `aesthetic-fetishist` carries correct generic prose whose only fault was the summary above it (the `casting` rule), and nulling it would be the exact mirror of leaving a wrong body standing. No body is authored. Nulling is safe and asserted by **calling `tag_has_prose` rather than restating it** — the predicate is an OR, and the stricter hand-rolled "both present" form is a different check. **Three refusals are re-asserted inside the file rather than merely written down:** the *"This tag is for …"* REGISTER cohort (`lgbtq-support`, `comedy`, `outdoor`, `family-friendly`) whose bodies are addressed to a content manager but carry correct facts — round thirteen's measured refusal, and removing the opener would mean authoring prose; **`queer` (13,119 uses)**, whose summary is THIN against a rich authored description rather than wrong, where under-reaching is the correct error; and the anatomy-phrase residue `futanari`/`pussy-worship`/`shemale`, **re-read on the rows rather than re-reading the deferral** — on all three the row's OWN description carries the same phrasing as the summary, so there is no contradicting evidence and this series never writes that column. **The actor declaration is load-bearing (12 of 13 rows `human_reviewed`) and the probe that proved it was WRONG the first time**: writing `short_description || ''` is an IDENTICAL value, so nothing changed and nothing fired, which reads exactly like a permissive trigger; with a genuinely different value the undeclared write returns `human_reviewed tag c8d492ae-… cannot be modified by system:trigger`. **Verify a probe changes something before believing what it reports.** Dry-run on prod in a rolled-back transaction: 13 summaries, 12 bodies nulled, 1 kept, 0 thin-page failures, 5+3+1 controls intact, all seven postconditions passed. Guarded by `tagProseConceptAsCommunity.test.ts`, mutation-tested **11/11 with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES** — and the test's own first draft carried the recorded reach-forward defect, slicing from `slug = 'aesthetic-fetishist'` into the group B statements that legitimately DO null a body, so the assertion is scoped to the one statement it is about.

**Round seventeen — the CODES a disowned entity left behind, and the documented remedy is what freezes them (2026-09-19, `99980101100100`).** Rounds two to sixteen worked the glossary's PROSE. This is the same failure in a different artifact: `tag_medical_codes`. **Six ACTIVE, INDEXABLE tags published clinical codes derived from a Wikidata entity that had since been disowned**, and the weekly sync had never removed them. Every code was resolved LIVE before the file was written, never inferred from the tag name: **`passing` published ICPC-2 `A96` = Q4 "death"** on a trans glossary entry; `seafood` ICD-10 `U07.1`/`U07.2` = COVID-19 (six codes); `bearded` `L68.0` = hirsutism; **`s-a-m` SNOMED `223688001` = Q30 "United States"** on a gay leather-scene term; `scat` ICD-11 `XA5CS6` = Q30015788 "subcutaneous adipose tissue"; `whore` SNOMED `137116002` = Q14915751 "prostitute". CLAUDE.md already named passing/seafood/bearded as "the sharpest harm" of the 2026-08-29 wrong-entity repair and recorded their identifiers as nulled. **The identifiers were. The codes were not, and had rendered ever since** — `get_tag_medical_codes` filters on `tag_id` and `s.enabled` and nothing about the identifier. **THE MECHANISM IS THE REPAIR ITSELF.** `run_tag_medical_codes_sync` builds its work set as `where status = 'active' and wikidata_id ~ '^Q[0-9]+$'`, so **clearing a wrong identifier removes the tag from the work set entirely**: the sync can no longer refresh OR retract it, and its only `DELETE` is against a temp table. This is `queerness`'s rule (`20360401100300`) one artifact class further on, and the sharpest version of it yet, because **the admin panel's docblock recommended precisely the action that causes it** — *"the way to change what appears here is to fix the tag's `wikidata_id`"* — a comment that was true of refreshes and false of retractions, corrected in place rather than deleted so the correction does not read as a fresh claim. **THE REAPER'S PREDICATE IS THE EXACT COMPLEMENT OF THE SYNC'S WORK SET**, so no row can be both unrefreshable and retained, and the one-shot repair IS the reaper called once — the recurring fix and the backfill are the same code path, so applying the file proves the scheduled call works. **THE NULL TRAP FIRED TWICE IN ONE FILE and both times the dry run caught what reading did not.** (1) `not (status = 'active' and wikidata_id ~ '…')` is NULL when `wikidata_id` IS NULL, so a bare `NOT` is not true and **all six defective rows fall out** — the reaper would have deleted only the inert merged row, reported success, and left every wrong code rendering; `coalesce(…, false)` is load-bearing and the guard refuses the bare form. (2) The first draft folded the reap into `admin_automations.action.command` by reflex — **but `tag_medical_codes_sync` is an `action->>'type'='rpc'` row with NO `command` key at all**, so the registry cannot carry it and `sync_automations_to_cron()` branch (d) structurally cannot schedule it (CLAUDE.md records that every rpc automation is scheduled by its own migration). The guard was `action->>'command' not ilike '%reap_orphans%'`, which is NULL on that row, so the UPDATE matched zero rows **silently** and only a postcondition caught it. Editing `cron.job` directly is the convention for an rpc row and is NOT the `detect_stale_venues` mistake — there is no reconciler branch that can overwrite it — and the postcondition asserts the LIVE cron carries sync-then-reap **on its original `30 5 * * 1` schedule**, per this file's own rule to verify a rescheduled cron live rather than assume it from the migration. **Scope: 19 code rows over 7 tags** — 14 on the six active indexable tags (the live harm) plus 5 on `sexual-pain-penetration-disorder`, which is `status='merged'` and renders nowhere; the merged row is included because it is unreachable by the same mechanism and nothing is lost (an unmerge returns it to the work set and the next Monday re-adds its codes). **The editorial lane is exempt and holds ZERO rows today**, so that exclusion is a forward-looking guard and is stated as doing no work rather than quoted as protection. Sentinel `tag_medical_code_signals()` is standalone and service_role-only, reports `code_rows_total` FIRST (zero orphans over an empty table is not a clean corpus) and `editorial_rows` separately from the invariant; `check-pipeline-health.mjs` hard-fails on `orphan_code_rows > 0` and on a broken probe, with a 404 carve-out — and **the reason first written here was WRONG and is corrected**: it said the carve-out keeps the introducing PR from deadlocking against its own gate, copying the `city_scalar_defects` framing. **`check-pipeline-health.mjs` does not run on `pull_request` at all** — `pipeline-health.yml` is `schedule: '0 6 * * *'` plus `workflow_dispatch`, and the PR-time `Critical data-quality gates` job runs `check-data-quality-gates` / `check-tag-hygiene` / the grant checks and never this script, so no PR can deadlock against it. (The same misattribution sits in this file's `city_scalar_defects` entry, which names that job; both sentinels live only in `check-pipeline-health.mjs`. Verified by `grep -rln` over `scripts/` and by reading the workflow's `on:` block.) The carve-out is still load-bearing for the case that actually exists: the NIGHTLY run checks out `main` and calls the LIVE database, so in any window where main carries the newer script and prod has not applied the migration the gate would go red for something that is not a defect — **which is not hypothetical, because `db push` was broken on `main` for 4.5 hours the same morning** on two other PRs' version collisions. **Deliberately NOT done:** no identifier is repointed (the sync rebuilds codes from it weekly, so a plausible-but-wrong QID regenerates wrong codes forever while a null one regenerates nothing) and no prose is touched — `bearded` and `seafood` carry a NULL `description`, which is a separate seam. **Two recorded open items were found ALREADY CLOSED by other sessions before any SQL was written** — the 194-row category-junction gap and text-vs-junction disagreement both measure **0** — which is this file's own rule paying for itself: a recorded open item is not still open because it was once written down. Dry-run on prod in a transaction forced to roll back: 19 rows reaped, `healthy=411 total=411`, all seven postconditions passed, rollback confirmed by re-reading the table, the six tags and the live cron command. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/tagMedicalCodeOrphans.test.ts`, mutation-tested **16/16 with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES** — and one mutation SURVIVED the first round as a real gap: the health-script assertion was `toContain('tag_medical_code_signals')`, which a name in a comment satisfies, so an "unwired sentinel" mutation passed; it is anchored on the fetch URL now. **Anchor on the call that does the work, not on a name that also appears in prose.** **Verified on prod after merge BY NAME**, which is the check a version-only read cannot pass: `schema_migrations` carries `99980101100100` under the name `tag_medical_codes_orphan_reap`, `tag_medical_code_signals()` returns `probe_ok` true with `orphan_code_rows` 0 / `orphan_tags` 0 / `editorial_rows` 0, the corpus survived at **411 rows over 135 tags** (the mirror a totals-only read cannot give), all seven repaired slugs read zero, and the live `cron.job` row is `30 5 * * 1`, active, running sync-then-reap in that order. **The file shipped at a version it did not start with**: it was authored as `99980101100000`, #3796 took that number while the PR sat in review, and a concurrent session renumbered it to `99980101100100` — the fifth recorded collision of the week, and the reason the `next-migration-version.mjs` allocator now exists.

**Round eighteen — THE BAND IS TITLED "DIAGNOSTIC CODES" AND SOME OF IT IS NOT DIAGNOSTIC (2026-09-19, `99991789851492` / `99991789851494`).** Round seventeen removed codes left behind by a DISOWNED entity. This is the opposite case, and it overturns the framing it inherited: **every identifier is CORRECT.** `/tags/australia` really is Q408, `/tags/jockstrap` really is Q10940 — all 135 code-bearing identifiers were re-resolved live. What is wrong is that **SNOMED CT, ICD-11 and ICPC-2 are not diagnosis vocabularies**; they are reference terminologies with whole axes for geography, occupations, kinship, physical objects, places and social circumstances, and the sync copies a code from any registered property without asking what KIND of thing the entity is.
