Measured on prod, fifteen ACTIVE tags — eight of them `seo_indexable` — published: SNOMED geography on `australia` `223621005`, `south-africa`, `espana`, `estonia`, `south-america` and `california`; SNOMED environment on `bakery` `224838001`; SNOMED occupations on `nurses` `106292003`, `doctor` `112247003`, `jurist` `106303002`, `anthropologist` `50835006`; a SNOMED kinship concept on `nephew` `83559000`; ICD-11 extension codes on `jockstrap` `XE16J` (object) and `prison` `XE30E` (place); and the ICPC-2 social chapter on `poverty` `Z01`. Nothing here is a wrong FACT — a reader is simply told that "Bakery" and "Nephew" have diagnostic codes. It is the `mayonnaise under Sexual Health` class in the clinical register, and the harm is to the band's credibility.

**MEASURED AND REJECTED: A CODE-PATTERN RULE, which is the cheap fix and under-reaches by exactly half.** SNOMED `223*`/`224*` is geography, ICD-11 `XE` is the object/place extension, ICPC-2 `Z` is the social chapter — run against the real rows that catches **10 of the 15** and misses the entire occupation/kinship half, because **SNOMED concept ids are OPAQUE**: `50835006`, `106292003`, `106303002`, `112247003` and `83559000` share no prefix with each other or with the geography block. So the discriminator has to be the ENTITY, not the code — which is also the honest framing, since the code is real and the entity is not clinical. **P31/P279 ride in the `claims` blob `wikidata_entities_fetch` already returns**, so the gate costs zero extra requests (the `_shared/city-class-guard.ts` precedent).

**IT IS A DENYLIST, INVERTING city-class-guard's POLARITY ON PURPOSE.** There a QID is adopted only on a positively recognised settlement class, because a wrong link is permanent and self-reinforcing. Here the asymmetry runs the other way: refusing wrongly DELETES a legitimate clinical code from a health page, while missing one leaves a single odd row until the vocabulary is extended — and the next run then removes it, because nothing is frozen. **Over-refusal is the expensive error, so an unrecognised class is ALLOWED and COUNTED, never refused** (24 of 135 today, reported so a vocabulary gap is a rising count of namable QIDs rather than silence). **The clinical override is tested FIRST and the order is load-bearing** — the `\bprefecture\b` lesson: a disqualifying class must not veto an entity that ALSO carries a clinical one. It rescues exactly two, `substance abuse` (Q3184856) and `substance dependence` (Q3378593), both classed `social issue` AND `type of disease` and both carrying real ICD codes; a blanket deny on `social issue` would have stripped them, and `poverty` carries no clinical class at all, which is what separates the three. **MEASURED AND REJECTED: adding `crime`.** Only two code-bearing entities carry a crime class — `embezzlement` (a wrong identifier, below) and `substance abuse` (rescued anyway) — so it would cost nothing TODAY and is still left out, because this platform documents `spiking`, `sexual-assault` and `stealthing`, and an abuse concept that legitimately holds an ICD code is classed a crime with no clinical sibling. **Under-reaching is the correct error.**

**A REAPER, NOT A PRODUCER GATE, and the alternative was real.** Gating inside `run_tag_medical_codes_sync` where `_codes` is built would be internally consistent by construction — one snapshot decides both the codes and their verdict — and would retract for free, since a refused tag stays in `_covered`. It is rejected because `CREATE OR REPLACE` restates the whole body and **that body is the SOLE WRITER of clinical codes on a health platform**: a slip in a 100-line transcription breaks the pipeline silently, where a missed reaper week leaves fifteen already-known rows in place seven more days. The blast radius decides it, it matches this subsystem's own precedent (round seventeen added a reaper rather than touching the sync) and CLAUDE.md's `*_tick()` rule that wrappers exist so reviewed bodies are not restated. **The insert-then-reap churn is invisible because pg_cron runs the whole command string in ONE transaction**, so no reader sees the intermediate state and a newly-coded tag is gated the same week it appears.

**THE VERDICT IS STORED, and that is what makes the sentinel possible at all.** `tag_medical_code_signals()` is pure SQL and STABLE; it cannot fetch, so without `tag_entity_class_probe` the invariant could only be recomputed by the thing it audits. **Its COVERAGE is reported separately and never folded in** — `nonclinical_code_rows = 0` over an unprobed corpus is vacuous, not clean, and only `unprobed_qids` tells the two apart. `check-pipeline-health.mjs` hard-fails on a non-zero count and on an EMPTY probe, warns on incomplete coverage (a schedule gap, not a defect) and warns on an absent key, for the same reason as round seventeen's 404 carve-out: the nightly run checks out `main` and calls the LIVE backend.

**NO NETWORK IN THE MIGRATION, and the seed is evidence rather than an id list.** `db push` aborts the whole repo on a failing file, so a migration that waits on api.wikidata.org is a repo-wide outage waiting for a slow upstream. The probe is seeded with the classes measured for all 135 entities and the deletion is driven by the same verdict function the cron uses — **so if the vocabulary is wrong the seeded rows do not refuse and nothing is deleted**, the self-check a frozen slug list cannot give. The seed was then diffed against a live fetch: **135/135 qids identical, ZERO verdict disagreements.**

**THE DRY RUN CAUGHT WHAT READING COULD NOT, AND IT WOULD HAVE ABORTED `db push` ON MAIN.** In a `VALUES` list a bare `'{Q1,Q2}'` literal resolves to **`text`, not `text[]`**, so the seed's call to the verdict function fails `42883: function medical_code_entity_class_verdict(text) does not exist` — and the FIRST dry run could not see it, because that run substituted the live reaper for the literal seed. **A dry run that paraphrases the file does not exercise the file.** Fixed with an explicit `s.classes::text[]` at the use site. Live dry runs, both in transactions forced to roll back: the reaper probed **135, fetch_errors 0**, refused exactly the sixteen measured slugs, `code_rows_total` **411 → 395**, then the sibling took it to **394**, `orphan_code_rows` 0, `nonclinical_code_rows` 0, `unprobed_qids` 0, and the live cron chains sync → both reapers on `30 5 * * 1`.

**TWO IDENTIFIERS WERE GENUINELY WRONG, and the class gate reaches only one of them.** `justice` → **Q16533 = JUDGE** (`/wiki/Judge`) and `infidelity` → **Q157833 = EMBEZZLEMENT** (`/wiki/Embezzlement`), both ACTIVE and `seo_indexable`, and on both **the row argues with itself**: the `description` is correct while everything derived from the identifier is about something else. `/tags/justice` (usage 75) described "The principle that people should be treated fairly" under a summary reading *"Official presiding over court proceedings"* and a body defining a judge. `/tags/infidelity` (usage 13) described a kink under *"Theft of entrusted assets"* and a body opening **"Infidelity, ALSO KNOWN AS EMBEZZLEMENT, refers to the theft of assets…"** — the body does not merely describe the wrong thing, it ASSERTS THE IDENTITY, telling a kink-glossary reader the two words are synonyms. Q16533 is classed `legal profession`, so the gate reaps its code as an occupation; **Q157833 is classed `crime`, which the vocabulary deliberately does not carry**, so it survives the gate and is removed by nulling the identifier — the correct mechanism for a wrong entity anyway. **NO REPOINT** (the weekly rebuild regenerates wrong data from a plausible-but-wrong QID forever while a null one regenerates nothing), **prose REPLACED not retracted** for the summary since both rows render, **bodies NULLED** because each row's description is one line — enough to state what the tag is, not enough to write a body from, and minting one is the guess this class came from. Nulling is asserted safe by CALLING `tag_has_prose` rather than restating its OR. **Nothing is written to `tag_wikidata_repair_audit`** — it is the INPUT to `tag_disowned_prose_signals()`, so a row there would perturb a live metric to record what the file already records (round twelve's rule).

**The actor declaration is load-bearing for ONE of the two, and the probe that proves it must write a DIFFERENT value.** Measured live: `infidelity` is `human_reviewed` and an undeclared write returns `human_reviewed tag 48c08116-… cannot be modified by system:trigger`; `justice` is not, and the same write is ALLOWED. A self-assignment changes no column and fires no trigger, which reads exactly like a permissive one — the round-sixteen trap. **Each file states which case it is in**, so the next pass does not copy the wrong precedent from whichever it happens to open.

Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/tagMedicalCodeNonclinical.test.ts`, mutation-tested **22/22 with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES** — and two mutations failed that bar on the first round, both recorded classes. One **SURVIVED**: the editorial exemption appears TWICE in the reaper (building the work list and in the delete), so a single-match assertion was satisfied by the surviving copy while the delete had lost it — asserted by COUNT now, the haversine-cast rule, **eighth recorded instance**. One read **NOT-APPLIED** because its anchor omitted a leading `description,` and so matched nothing, which is indistinguishable from a guard that does not fire — **the sixth time this file has recorded that check.** **Verified on prod after merge BY NAME**, which a version-only read cannot do: `schema_migrations` carries `99991789851492` as `tag_medical_codes_nonclinical_entities` and `99991789851494` as `tag_justice_infidelity_wrong_entity`, so both ran rather than a sibling PR's file landing under one of the versions. `tag_medical_code_signals()` returns `probe_ok` true with **probe_rows 135, code_rows_total 394** and all four invariants at zero (`nonclinical_code_rows`, `orphan_code_rows`, `nonclinical_tags`, `unprobed_qids`); the live `cron.job` row is `30 5 * * 1`, active, chaining sync → reap_orphans → reap_nonclinical in that order; `justice` and `infidelity` both read QID null, body null and a replaced summary; and the four reaped slugs (`australia`, `nephew`, `jockstrap`, `poverty`) hold 0 codes each. **THE MIRROR ASSERTION FAILED AND THE MIGRATION WAS RIGHT — the query was wrong, which is a new trap worth its own line.** The clinical override is documented as rescuing *substance abuse* and *substance dependence*, and a mirror keyed on `substance-dependence` returned **0 code rows**. Those are Wikidata ENTITY LABELS, not our slugs: `Q3378593` is carried by THREE rows — `dependence` (**active, 3 codes**), plus `drug-addiction` and `substance-dependence`, both **deprecated**, and `run_tag_medical_codes_sync` builds its work set from `status = 'active'`, so a deprecated row never receives codes at all. Both QIDs read verdict `clinical` when asked directly. **A mirror assertion keyed on a slug guessed from an entity's label reads as a regression against correct code** — resolve the slug from `unified_tags` by `wikidata_id`, never from the label. Finally `unknown_verdict_qids` reads **23** against the documented 24, and that delta is the repair rather than drift: `Q157833` is still seeded in `tag_entity_class_probe` but is now held by **zero** tags, because this migration nulled `infidelity`'s identifier.
**Round nineteen — SIX DIFFERENT DRUGS PUBLISHED ONE SENTENCE, and the thing it erased is how long to wait before poppers (2026-09-20, `99991789887254`).** Round twelve found this class and named it — *one string stamped across rows that are different things, where the shared summary erases the distinction the family exists to draw* — and repaired the sadist family and five gender identities. This is the same class continued into the CLINICAL families. Measured on prod, fifteen ACTIVE rows share five flattened summaries — **and this line read "ACTIVE, `seo_indexable`" until 2026-09-25, which is FALSE: all fifteen are `seo_indexable = false` and `publication_role = 'utility'`** (see the corrected render-surface paragraph below): **"Medication for erectile dysfunction" x6** (`sildenafil` `tadalafil` `vardenafil` `avanafil` `cialis` `levitra`), **"Antidepressant medication" x3** (`fluoxetine` `paroxetine` `sertraline`), **"Medication for HIV prevention" x2** (`descovy` `truvada`), plus two HIV-treatment pairs. Every one of those rows already carries a CORRECT, detailed `description` and a 275-1183 character body; only `short_description` is collapsed.

**THE SEAM WAS FOUND BY A QUERY AND IT OVERTURNED THE FRAMING IT INHERITED.** Both round twelve and the sex-glossary pass deferred this to *a merge pass* for duplicate tags, so the search was written for duplicates — `group by short_description having count(*) > 1`, plus a suffix-twin arm. **Most of what it returns is not a duplicate-tag problem at all**, and the largest groups are families of genuinely different drugs. `pansexual`/`pansexuality`, `marriage`/`married`, `educator`/`teacher` are the real merge questions and are untouched here; `queer`/`queerness` is a suffix-twin FALSE POSITIVE (one is an identity, the other the quality, and this file documents `queerness` at length). **Run the query the deferral implies before trusting the deferral's own label for what it will find.**

**WHERE IT RENDERS — AND THE ROUND'S OWN ANSWER TO THAT WAS WRONG ON EVERY COUNT (corrected 2026-09-25, five days after the merge).** The paragraph here used to read: *"`search_documents_index_tags` is the opposite: it emits `coalesce(t.short_description, t.description)`, so the flattened line **WINS in site search**. Verified on prod against `search_documents` rather than inferred: six ED drugs, three SSRIs and both PrEP formulations return byte-identical snippets."* **Three separate things in that are false, and the last of them is the one that makes it a lesson rather than a typo.** (1) The live `search_documents_index_tags` selects **`t.description`** — `short_description` appears nowhere in its body, so it never reaches site search for ANY tag. (2) It filters `status='active' AND merged_into_id is null AND deprecated_at is null AND publication_role='article' AND NOT restoration_review_required`, and all fifteen rows are **`publication_role = 'utility'`**, so **0 of 15 are in `search_documents` at all** — the join returns NULL for every one, controls included. (3) **The "verified against `search_documents`" claim therefore cannot have happened.** What was almost certainly measured is `coalesce(short_description, description)` computed off `unified_tags` — i.e. what an indexer *would* emit — and then reported as a read of the index. That is the vacuous-measurement class this file records over and over, committed in the very sentence asserting the opposite. **A claim that names the table it read is not evidence it read that table; the join has to return rows.**

**THE REPAIR IS STILL JUSTIFIED, and the real surface is more prominent than the one claimed.** `short_description` IS preferred over `description`, on three reader surfaces: `TagIndexCard.tsx:81` and `TagIndexRow` (the glossary index at `/tags`, fed by `useCentralizedTags`' `TAG_INDEX_COLUMNS`), `TagDefinitionCard.tsx:34` (the inline/hover definition card), and `FromTheGlossary.tsx:11`, which SCORES a preview 2 for having a `short_description` against 1 for a `description` — it actively up-ranks exactly the rows this defect sat on. **`fetchTagPreviews` filters `status='active'` and NOTHING else** (no `seo_indexable`, no `publication_role`), so all fifteen reach the definition card; `useHomeGlossaryPool` is the one preview path that excludes them, on `.eq('seo_indexable', true)`. Meanwhile `description` wins on the detail-page meta (`TagDetail.tsx:444`), on both crawler paths (`functions/_lib/detail.ts:1726`/`1732`) and in search — and `description` is correct on all fifteen, so **there was never any crawler or SEO harm**, which is the one half of the original paragraph that survives. **Read which field each surface actually prefers before sizing a prose defect** — and enumerate the readers with a grep over `src/` and `functions/`, not from one indexer you believe you remember. **`99991789887254`'s own header lines 9 and 24-25 still carry the false claim and are deliberately NOT rewritten** — an applied migration is the record of what ran (the `99930101100000` rule) — so this paragraph is the correction, and a reader who opens that file should discount its "ACTIVE and seo_indexable" and "WINS in site search" lines against what is measured here. The guard test never asserted either claim, so it needed no change.

**WHY IT IS WORSE THAN A DUPLICATE SNIPPET.** What separates those six drugs ON THIS PLATFORM is the nitrite interval, and each row already states its own answer: tadalafil and cialis **48 hours**, avanafil **12**, sildenafil **"unknown"**, vardenafil and levitra **"no safe interval determined"**. The summary that erases it is the first thing a reader meets in search. `descovy`/`truvada` is the same shape and the reason a reader would search one name rather than the other: Descovy's PrEP approval **excludes people at risk through receptive vaginal sex** and Truvada's does not. Every replacement restates the row's OWN description and adds no fact.

**Scope stated and PROVEN rather than asserted:** `short_description` only. The migration snapshots `description` and `long_description` into a temp table before the writes and a postcondition compares after, so *collateral = 0* is a measurement, not a promise. Nothing is written to `tag_wikidata_repair_audit` — it is the INPUT to `tag_disowned_prose_signals()` (round twelve's rule). The actor declaration IS load-bearing: all fifteen rows are `human_reviewed` and six are `is_sensitive`, so `tag_prose_apply()` is unavailable (it hard-refuses sensitive rows whatever the caller claims). Verified live with a REAL value change, since a self-assignment fires no trigger and reads exactly like a permissive one — undeclared returns `human_reviewed tag 57ad63d4-… cannot be modified by system:trigger`, declared is allowed. **The postcondition that states the purpose is DISTINCTNESS, not absence**: "the flattened text is gone" is satisfied by stamping a *different* single string across all fifteen. Dry-run on prod in a transaction forced to roll back: scope 15, distinct 15, flattened 0, thin 0, collateral 0, controls 2.

**Deliberately NOT done, each named rather than counted:** the corpus holds **34 shared-summary groups over 74 rows, 63 of them indexable**, and this round takes 15. The rest need their own decisions — `trans-man`/`transmasculine` is the GENDERING class ("Man assigned female at birth" is wrong for transmasculine, which includes non-binary people); `sexual-orientation`/`sexuality`/`sexual-preference` share a line and `sexual-preference` is a styleguide-**rejected** term; `priest`/`priestess`, `deity`/`god`, `apparel`/`clothing` are merge questions. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/tagProseClinicalFamilySummaries.test.ts`, mutation-tested **14/14 with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES** — and **two mutations SURVIVED the first round and both were real gaps**: a pre-seeded counter is declared `v_touched   int := 0`, so a `v_name\s*:=` pattern never matched it (the DECLARE block is banned from carrying any initialiser now), and dropping half the `viagra` control left `(slug = 'viagra')` standing, which satisfied a bare `toContain` while the control had stopped controlling. **The test also failed honestly against CORRECT code first**: three replacement summaries contain a semicolon of their own (*"Sold as Cialis; the longest-acting of its class"*), so slicing each statement at `indexOf(';')` truncated mid-literal and every slug assertion read as a missing statement — the venue-dedup `[^;]*` trap, now **ninth** recorded, and the first time it has fired inside a guard rather than a mutation.
**A phrase assertion against crawler HTML is one glossary term away from red, and the sentence is never the thing that broke (2026-09-19, `e2e/support/glossaryProse.ts`).** `/tags/:slug` prose is rewritten on render so any word that is itself a glossary term becomes `<a href="/tags/consent" data-glossary-link="consent">consent</a>`. FOUR specs were red on prod for exactly this and nothing else — `/tags/stealthing` *"consent to protected sex is not consent to unprotected sex"*, `/tags/k-hole` *"cannot consent to anything"*, `/tags/doxy-pep` *"cisgender women"*, `/tags/lgbti` *"lesbian, gay, bisexual, trans and intersex"* — every sentence published, correct and complete, with `consent`, `cisgender` and `intersex` having simply become linked terms since the specs were written. **THE FOURTH WAS FOUND BY THE VERIFICATION RUN FOR THE FIRST THREE, which is the strongest evidence the premise is live rather than historical**: the `@smoke` sweep that confirmed the fix went 166 passed / 1 failed, and the one failure was a fresh instance of the same defect in a file the fix had not yet reached. This is not a backlog that was cleared — it regenerates every time a word inside an asserted phrase becomes a tag, so a spec asserting prose against crawler HTML should adopt the helper on the way past rather than waiting to go red. **The failure names the content and the defect is in the assertion**, which is the shape that gets a correct page "fixed". `unlinkGlossary()` removes those anchors and keeps their inner text before any phrase assertion runs. **It strips ONE element class rather than all tags, deliberately**: a blanket tag strip also removes `<article>` boundaries, href values and attribute text, which would weaken every NEGATIVE assertion in the same file — a check that silently starts passing is worse than the red it replaced. Removing an anchor and keeping its text cannot ADD prose, so a positive assertion can only go from wrongly-failing to passing. **`e2e/glossary-inline-links.spec.ts` must never use it** — that spec asserts ON those anchors, so the helper would gut it. Each adopting spec carries a **positive control** that the raw HTML contained `data-glossary-link` at all and that the strip removed it, because if the renderer renames the attribute the helper becomes a silent no-op and the phrase assertions go back to being one glossary term from red. Proven load-bearing by mutation rather than by the green run: with `unlinkGlossary` neutered to `return html`, **6 of 68 fail** (the 3 phrase assertions plus the 3 controls, each in its own direction); restored, **68/68 pass against prod**. The mutation's first attempt did not apply and reported as a survivor — asserted to have taken before it was trusted, the **ninth** recorded instance of that check in this file.
**The brief was to rewrite all 3,615 descriptions, and the corpus said the STANDARD was missing instead (2026-09-18, `99700101100000`-`99700101100200`).** Asked to standardize and rewrite every glossary description for consistency, the first move was to band them rather than sweep them, and the shape settles the question: **under 60 chars, 1,112 rows, 11.7% ending in a terminal mark and ZERO multi-sentence; over 60, 2,503 rows at 93-96%.** The corpus is **BIMODAL, not chaotic** — a LABEL register ("Pack animal", "Cute animal role", "Learner", "Dominant in latex play") and a SENTENCE register, each near-uniform inside itself. So "1,122 rows lack terminal punctuation" is not 1,122 defects; **~982 are correctly obeying their own register**, and a rule forcing one register would rewrite roughly a thousand correct rows. **The label register is what the whole repair series reads**: `65000101100000` turned on the word *role* in bunny's "Cute animal role", and the retired judge's documented failure mode is answering `wrong_subject` at HIGH confidence for prose that is merely SHORT. **REGISTER IS GRAMMATICAL, NOT LENGTH-BASED, and the first draft of this pass got that wrong** — the <60 band also holds real sentences ("A man who was assigned female at birth.", "A gender-neutral form of priest or priestess."), so length cannot tell a noun phrase from a sentence and the rule is stated for a human and a model and enforced mechanically NOWHERE. **THE MOST DANGEROUS AVAILABLE "STANDARDIZATION" IS ADDING A FULL STOP, and it is a NEW defect class**: the rows that are provably sentence-register yet unpunctuated are dominated by descriptions **EXACTLY 500 CHARACTERS long ending mid-clause** — `rumpus-room` ("...A rumpus room may also"), `kink-dispenser` ("...reciprocity, or building"), `sensual-sadist` ("...often but not always Dom"), `alliteration` ("...during a BDSM scene to en") — **23 of the 24 rows at that cap are cut off.** They are TRUNCATED, not missing a period, and appending one converts a visibly incomplete entry into a plausibly complete one, destroying the only evidence the text was lost; repairing them means regenerating content, so they are counted and never touched. **WHAT WAS ALREADY CLEAN IS WHY NO PROSE WAS WRITTEN**: measured across 3,615 active descriptions, self-citation **0**, advice-register padding **0**, define-the-term-with-the-term **0**, description-equals-name **0**, literal backslash-n **0** — twenty prior passes closed those, and the residue is 19 whitespace rows, 2 scrape timestamps, 2 disambiguation lists, the 23 truncations and 46 drift rows. **So the gap was the standard, not the corpus** — `20470922084600`'s finding one entity later. `styleguide_rules` held 30 active rules, **21 scoped `all` and exactly TWO scoped `tag`, neither governing STRUCTURE**: nothing stated what the three fields are for, which registers are legal, or that a truncation must not be punctuated. Five rules added there rather than as a new document (a second standard beside the published one re-creates the three-prose-copies problem the styleguide system exists to end), published **v1.4.0**. Sentinel `tag_prose_standard_signals()` is standalone and service_role-only, reporting `rows_scanned` FIRST because an empty scan and a clean corpus return the same zeros; `whitespace_dirty`/`stamp_as_definition` are zero-invariants while `truncated_description`/`unresolved_disambiguation` gate on GROWTH, since a rule red on arrival is one people scroll past. The repair is **21 rows and no sentence rewritten**: a whitespace trim, plus NULLing two rows (`conditions-disorders`, `kidnapping`) whose entire description is "Updated June 28, 2023  3:59pmUpdated June 28, 2023  3:59pm" — the `stis-stds` defect on active rows, nulled per `20261012090000`'s finding that a stamp READS AS CONTENT while a blank is honest and self-heals, asserted safe by CALLING `tag_has_prose` rather than restating its OR. **Two traps, both found by running rather than reading.** (1) **`btrim()` WITH NO CHARACTER SET TRIMS SPACES ONLY, NOT NEWLINES** — a first pass found 12 rows and missed 7, every Wikipedia-derived row carrying a `\n\n\n` prefix (`orgasm`, `gender-identity`, `pride-parade`, `tokenism`, `crimes-against-humanity`); the explicit `E' \t\n\r'` set is load-bearing, and only `[ \t]{2,}` is collapsed so the INTERNAL newlines `paragraphsHtml()` splits crawler prose on survive (verified per row by the length delta, asserted by a mirror postcondition). (2) **A bare `(may|could) refer to` matches CORRECT prose** — `pansexuality`'s "Pansexual people may refer to themselves as gender-blind" — so the disambiguation regex is anchored to the head plus a colon, measured live to match `bicon` + `flamer` and nothing else. **The health-gate wiring needed the `city_scalar_defects` split, not a hard fail**: `Critical data-quality gates` builds the BRANCH and calls the LIVE backend, so a hard fail on a missing RPC could only go green after the merge it blocks — a deadlock, not a guard, the same trap this file records on `Critical paths`. 404 WARNS and names the migration; every other non-ok status hard-fails, because collapsing both fails open exactly where the gate is needed. **Deliberately NOT done, each named:** the 23 truncations, `bicon`/`flamer` (a description cannot be evidence for itself — already recorded), `basorexic`'s real typo "inlcude" (spelling inside someone's prose is its own decision), and the 46 `tag.description` drift rows, where the house answer is a counter and the deterministic half already ran. Guarded by `tagProseStructuralStandard.test.ts`, mutation-tested **31/31 with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES** — and **three mutations first SURVIVED and every one was a real gap**: a mid-line `--` left the publish call's text intact (the comment stripper only removes comments at LINE START, so the assertion is line-anchored now), the mirror assertion matched `pride-parade` in the **RAISE MESSAGE** rather than the WHERE condition (the class this file already records — anchor on the condition, not the text it prints), and the field-contract rule body could be inverted into "keep fields roughly equal in length" while its title stood.

**The rewrite half of that brief ran as five measured batches, and THE ORDERING is the finding (2026-09-18, `99700101100300` + `99930101100000`-`100400`):** once the standard existed, the corpus half was worked as hand-read tranches rather than a sweep — **55 rewrites across six migrations, every row read against the row's own evidence**, plus 4 voice corrections re-touching rows batch 1 had already rewritten (18 + 10 + 7 + 15 + 5, counted off the `update unified_tags` statements rather than from the PR titles, which count this PR's 37 + 4). The bulk alternative is not hypothetical: it is the experiment this repo already ran and retired when the tag prose judge retracted 16 of its first 18 rows with **13 of them WRONG**, and a 3,615-row sweep is that at two hundred times the scale on pages that rank. **The yield per tranche is the number to carry forward, and it does not transfer across orderings:** batches 1-3 walked the encyclopedic pool in a fixed seeded order and found **35% / 36% / 25%**; batch 4 took the SAME pool ordered by assignment count and found **15 of 22 (68%)**; batch 5 continued that ordering and found **5 of 30 (17%)**. The jump is mechanical rather than lucky — the heavily-used tags are the ones an early import gave a Wikipedia lead, so ordering by usage concentrates them — and the collapse is the head being worked out. **This is the third independent confirmation of the rule the disowned-prose rounds coined** (`60000301100000` corrected `51500101160000`'s "the high-usage head is worked out" by re-reading on a different axis): **a hit rate measured over one ordering is a property of that ordering, not of the backlog.** Quote it with its sample size and re-measure before sizing anything on it. **The batch-5 number is reported rather than padded**, because the temptation at 17% is to add generic-but-not-wrong rows to make a batch look worth shipping, and that is the sweep wearing a measured pass's clothes.

**A precision number is only as complete as the criteria it was scored against, and batch 1's were not.** `99700101100300` (#3777) hand-read 18 rows against pre-registered verdict categories covering SUBJECT, SENSE, SPECIFICITY and FABRICATION — and **not conformance to the published Tone of Voice**, which had shipped as `styleguide_rules` v1.4.0 in the same programme. Running the 18 shipped texts against the live rules afterwards found **four violations**: `fundraiser` and `tokenism` shipped *organisation*, `nala` *characterised*, `facial-feminization-surgery` *non-binary*. The rows were individually defensible and the 19/20 was honestly measured; the criteria set was the defect. **Score against the standard you publish, not only against the defect class you set out to find.** Corrected in `99930101100000`, which does NOT edit the applied `99700101100300` — an applied migration is the record of what ran.

**`nonbinary` is a genuine corpus-vs-standard disagreement and is resolved TOWARD THE STANDARD, with the losing cohort protected.** `trans-language` (must) and `styleguide_terms.preferred` both write it unhyphenated; the corpus majority is the **rejected** spelling, 34 hyphenated to 9. The four repaired rows move and **the other 33 are deliberately not swept**, with a postcondition asserting that cohort stays >=20 — so a later corpus-derived pass cannot quietly read the majority as consent. A majority in the data is not a vote against a published rule.

**Two rows in the series are accuracy fixes on health content and must not be re-read as voice fixes.** `bictegravir` published a **2016 Phase-3 trial status for a long-approved HIV medication**, and `misgendering` published the definition of **`transphobia`** — a separate live row with 356 assignments, asserted in both directions so the split cannot regress. A register-shaped sweep finds neither, because both read fluently.

**THE ACTOR-DECLARATION PROBE WAS VACUOUS AND NEARLY SHIPPED AS EVIDENCE.** Batch 5 touches two `human_reviewed` rows, so `log_unified_tag_change()`'s refusal is load-bearing. The first probe ran `update unified_tags set description = description` and returned **ALLOWED** — reading exactly like "the declaration is not needed here". It is not: **a self-assignment changes no column, so a trigger gating on a change never fires.** Re-run with a real value change it returns `human_reviewed tag … cannot be modified by system:trigger`. Each batch file states which case it is in, because batch 4's tranche was the opposite (all rows `human_reviewed=false`, declaration attribution-only) and the next pass should not copy the wrong precedent from whichever file it happens to open.

**FIVE MANDATORY RENUMBERS IN ONE DAY, on one branch, and two of them were collisions with PRs that merged while this one sat in review** (#3778 took `99800101100000` outright; #3783 took `99920101100000` within the hour; #3772 and #3778 each raised the ceiling). Each move is free (`VERSION_RE` is any 14 digits) and each is mandatory (`db push` aborts on the first file sorting below remote `max(version)` and takes every queued migration repo-wide), **which is exactly why nothing stops the escalation** — the same coordination problem this file already records at 2070 → 3500, now inside a single afternoon. Two things that worked: `git diff --name-only HEAD origin/main -- supabase/migrations/` before every push, which is the one command that shows a collision; and re-reading BOTH ceilings (`origin/main` and prod `schema_migrations`) immediately before merging rather than trusting the constant recorded at authoring time. **A concurrent session renumbered this branch twice while this session held a stale local HEAD** — `git fetch` before assuming your own branch state is current.

**Refusals, recorded so a later pass does not reverse them rather than merely omitted:** `youth` (a correct definition of the word, merely generic on an `Audiences` row — the ice-cream/tapas disposition, not the rooftop one); `lesbian`/`gay` (an identity definition is an editorial decision, not a defect, and guessing a sense is how the wrong-sense class arose); `naturist` (encyclopedic but accurate, and its *lifestyle* is the protected community-vocabulary sense `styleguide_content_drift` deliberately does not match); `writer` ("Author or scribe" is THIN, not wrong — under-reaching is the correct error); the safety rows `anal-sex`/`poppers`/`chemsex`/`soft-limits`; and the three 500-character truncations, which must stay exactly 500 chars and unpunctuated. Batch 5 asserts `youth` and `writer` are still deferred, so a sweep has to break the file's own check first. **`accessibility` is the one row repaired as a CLAUSE DELETION and it is labelled as its own group** — it is a NEWS TOPIC tag (1,766 of 1,768 assignments, not the venue facet its category suggests) whose prose is generic-but-not-wrong except for a trailing *"people with disabilities, including LGBTQIA+ individuals"*, which asserts LGBTQIA+ people are a SUBSET of people with disabilities; the edit is a `replace()`, which **cannot author prose**, so every other sentence survives by construction rather than by retyping. Guarded by `tagProseRewriteBatch2..5.test.ts` + `tagDescriptionVoiceCorrections.test.ts`, batch 5 mutation-tested **14/14 with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES**; every batch dry-run on prod in a transaction forced to roll back before merge.

**Sex & sexual-health glossary — 34 external sources, and the headline find is a PUBLISHED page (2026-09-19, `99991789812138`–`99991789812141`):** compared `unified_tags` against NSW Health Play Safe (9 A–Z pages), Planned Parenthood, WebMD, sexetc.org (409 headwords, paginated), the Advocates for Youth NSES PDF, GLAAD, PFLAG SF, Stonewall, the Equality Network and UConn PDFs, MSU GSCC, Sexual Health Dorset, sexualdiversity.org, three shibari glossaries, and eight consumer listicles. **1,973 distinct headwords; 598 formed the working set** (in a topical source, or corroborated by ≥2). **Two of the 34 could not be read and nothing is attributed to them** — pridetraining.org.au and clwbnawa.co.uk are JS-only and returned no glossary content over curl, the Internet Archive, or a real browser; recorded as unread rather than dropped from the source list. **`/tags/lubricant` was ACTIVE and `seo_indexable` and published INDUSTRIAL LUBRICANT** — *"transmitting forces, transporting foreign particles… the property of reducing friction is known as lubricity"* — filed under `Substances & Recovery`. That is the `darkroom`/`trampling` wrong-SENSE class: plausible entity, matching name, **no identifier to be wrong**, so neither `tag-wiki-guard.ts` arm nor `tag_disowned_prose_signals()` could ever see it — only comparing the row against what the word means HERE. The correct sexual-lubricant body **already existed on the deprecated sibling `lube`**, which stays down because `tag_reject_alias_shadow()` refuses an alias whose text is an existing tag NAME and that row is named "Lube". Also live: `praise-kink` summarised itself as *"a term from a podcast episode"*, `primal-play` as *"a form of self-expression"*, `androgyny` was filed under **Orientation** (it is a presentation), `erectile-dysfunction` said *"in males"* (the `masc`/`femme` gendering class — ED happens to anyone with a penis), and **`lgbti` carried 1,289 assignments with BOTH prose columns NULL**. **THE TWO BLIND SWEEPS TOOK THE ANATOMY VOCABULARY, for the fourth recorded time** — `auto: zero usage` and the 2026-06-05 orphan audit had deprecated clitoris, vagina, penis, hymen, labia, foreskin, perineum, puberty, menstruation, pregnancy, morning-after-pill and sex-worker; 46 revived. **Reading before republishing caught six wrong subjects with a CORRECT description underneath**: `anus` → *"Village in Papua, Indonesia"* (Q26235245), `semen` → *"District in East Java, Indonesia"* (Q7887443), `fluid` → the physics sense (Q102205), `ovaries` → *"Female reproductive cell or gamete"* (an OVUM), plus `butt-plug` → "Butt plug" and `quickie` → "Term for brief activity". **`Q9384` sat on THREE rows at once** (egg + testes + testicle — it is the testicle) and `Q782623` on two (autoeroticism + autosexual). Identifiers **nulled, never repointed**, and **nothing written to `tag_wikidata_repair_audit`**, which is the INPUT to the disowned-prose sentinel. Four health topics were filed under **Fetishes** with `is_adult=true` — menstruation, pregnancy, libido, multiple-orgasms — plus `ovaries` under *Dynamics & Roles*; the `pelvic-inflammatory-disease` class again. **29 of the 46 carried `seo_indexable=true` WHILE DEPRECATED**, so the revive sets it false explicitly. **TWO MECHANICAL CHECKS STOPPED THIRTEEN DUPLICATES AND NEITHER WAS READING.** (1) `tag_reject_alias_shadow()` refused four revivals — `blow-job`, `butt-plug`, `semen` and `transition` are each held as an alias of an ACTIVE row that already carries the concept (`blowjob`, `anal-plug`, `jizz`, `gender-transition`); the first draft tried to revive all four and the dry run stopped on the first. (2) A **singular/plural/de-hyphenated twin probe** run before any INSERT stopped nine creations — `metamour`, `ddlg`, `footjob`, `handjob`, `fraysexual`, `fallopian-tubes`, `hookup`, `packers`, `seminal-vesicles` all existed under another spelling; five moved to the revive file, four are aliased, and `handjob`'s EMPTY description is filled. **A slug being absent is not evidence the concept is** — the absent-term query had looked up `fraysexuality`, `hook-up`, `packer`, all genuinely absent as slugs and all the wrong spelling of a live row. `hookup`'s own description was additionally WRONG (*"A casual sexual encounter facilitated by chemsex"* — nothing about the term is chemsex-specific). 55 created, 28 aliases, all `approved` rather than `auto` since display, auto-tagging and the search bridge are approved-only. Every new row sets **all three category representations by hand** — neither category trigger fires on INSERT, the still-unrepaired 194-row finding of `50100101100100`. **Refused with stated reasons rather than counted:** 12 single-listicle coinages with no corroboration in 34 sources (`doppelbanger`, `hotline-bling`, `mastuwaiting`, `venus-butterfly`… — the "rope <animal>" lesson), ~50 general clinical terms from WebMD and Planned Parenthood (seminiferous tubules, corpus luteum, laparoscopy — general health glossaries, not this one), the social-justice and sexual-violence sets (their own pass; a thin row on that subject is worse than none), ~20 compound headwords the sources join with a slash, and **`sex-change-operation` because creating it publishes outdated terminology as a headword**. Left open and named: `pansexual` (u=34) and `pansexuality` (u=9) are both ACTIVE and are one concept — a duplicate-TAG problem for a merge pass, and rewriting one side to make them differ would paper over it. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/sexGlossaryPass.test.ts`, mutation-tested **27/27 with three comment-only controls that correctly SURVIVE** — and **one mutation first SURVIVED because `insert into tag_category_assignments` is a PREFIX of `..._SKIPPED`**, so a bare `toContain()` passed against an insert that writes nothing (the `< 10000` / `< 10000000` trap, one table later); anchored with `(?![\w])`. Two of the guard's own assertions also failed on correct code first: `[\s\S]*?;` truncated the lubricant statement at the **semicolon inside its own prose**, and a `wikidata_id = 'Q` ban matched the WHERE guards — scope the assertion to the half of the statement it is about. All three dry-run on prod in rolled-back transactions, then **re-validated against `main` after #3797 landed mid-review** (46/46 still deprecated, 55/55 still absent). **THE PROD E2E AFTER THE MERGE FOUND A GAP THAT PASS HAD CREATED, and only rendered pages could show it (2026-09-19, `99991789823744`):** it de-gendered `penis`, `vagina` and `uncircumcised` under the crotch-rope rule and left **eleven siblings** untouched, because those sat in the part-1 file whose working rule was "description is the evidence, only the summary is rewritten" — so `/tags/clitoris` led with *"a vital female sex organ"* and `/tags/ovaries` with *"a gonad in the female reproductive system"* while `/tags/penis`, repaired hours earlier, read *"Trans women, non-binary people and intersex people have penises too"*. **Applying a rule to three of fourteen rows is not under-reaching, it is drift**, and it is invisible in a diff — you have to look at the pages side by side. 13 rows repaired: `erectile-dysfunction` is the only INDEXABLE one and said *"sexual dysfunction in males"* twice (ED is most likely to be met by trans women on oestrogen and anyone on anti-androgens — exactly this glossary's reader); `pregnancy` said *"inside a woman's uterus"*; **the sharpest row is not anatomy at all** — `sex-worker` closed *"Sex workers are typically female, but there are some male and transgender sex workers"*, an unsourced demographic claim with trans sex workers appended as an exception, removed by an exact-sentence `replace()` so the other clauses survive byte-identically. `sperm`, `perineum` and `vagina` additionally shed zoology (red algae, ferns, *"in placental mammals"*). **The selection regex returned 17 and FIVE are correct as written** — `ceterosexual` (*"neither exclusively male nor female"* IS the definition), `feminism`, `hymen` (*"assigned female at birth"* is already the inclusive phrasing), `breasts`, and the already-repaired `penis` — so they are asserted to SURVIVE, which is the half of the postcondition that stops an over-reaching sweep from satisfying "the excluding prose is gone". Guarded by `glossaryAnatomyDegender.test.ts`, mutation-tested **11/11** with a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES.

**A TAG HAS THREE PROSE FIELDS AND I REPAIRED THEM ONE AT A TIME, FOUR TIMES RUNNING (2026-09-19, `99991789826279` / `99991789833238` / `99991789836833`).** The ledger, all one author, each follow-up caused by the previous one's scope: #3807 fixed two namesake SUMMARIES and left the BODIES → `99991789826279` cleaned up five; #3810 fixed thirteen gendered DESCRIPTIONS and left five summaries and eleven bodies → `99991789833238`; that file's regex sweep then missed two more → `99991789836833`. **`anus` alone needed three separate repairs** — `short_description` was *"Village in Papua, Indonesia"*, `description` was zoology, and `long_description` opened *"Anus is a village located in Sarmi Regency"* and only then reached the anatomy. Each pass selected on whatever signature it was hunting and never looked at the other two columns. **WHEN A ROW IS WRONG IN ONE FIELD, READ ALL THREE BEFORE MOVING ON**, and make the postcondition assert all three — that check is what was missing and its absence let the same defect through four times.

**THE FIELD YOU REPAIR IS PROBABLY NOT THE FIELD THE READER SEES.** `functions/_lib/detail.ts` renders `article = long_description ?? description ?? short_description` but `summary = description ?? short_description ?? long_description`, and that file's own comment already warned the two diverge (*"the crawler and the reader were served different fields for the same URL — invisible unless the two are compared directly"*, with `/tags/hiv` serving humans *"cannot be transmitted sexually"* while Google indexed *"survival time is 9 to 11 years"*). So #3810's de-gendering **never reached a single reader** on any row that had a body: `/tags/penis` was `seo_indexable` and published *"a male reproductive organ … part of the male genitalia"* directly beneath its own description reading *"Trans women, non-binary people and intersex people have penises too — this is anatomy, not gender."* The row argued with itself. **A repair is not shipped until you have looked at the rendered page**, not the column and not the meta description.

**A REGEX SWEEP OVER A LIVE TABLE REPAIRS WHAT MATCHED WHEN IT RAN.** `99991789833238` selected the 13 target slugs whose body matched `\m(male|female|men|women)\M` and got ELEVEN; `penis` was not among them though its body plainly contains "male", so the corpus moved between the SELECT and the UPDATE. The count a migration reports is not a statement about the corpus afterwards — **re-measure after merging**, which is why `99991789836833` re-runs the whole check inside its own postcondition, after its own write.

**TWO ROWS ARE DELIBERATELY LEFT AND MUST NOT BE SWEPT.** `female-ejaculation` keeps *"during female orgasm"* because the word is in the TERM'S OWN NAME — the `ceterosexual` case, where removing it makes the entry describe something else — and its description is already the inclusive one; whether the headword should change is a vocabulary decision, not a prose repair. `sexual-arousal` is a THIRD shape and got an exact-phrase `replace()` rather than a null: its body was accurate physiology named by gender rather than by anatomy (*"In males, arousal leads to erection … while in females, engorged sexual tissues, cervical changes"*), so the clinical detail is worth keeping and only the naming changed. Both are asserted as controls.

**THE GUARD TESTS CAUGHT ME LOOSENING MY OWN INVARIANTS TWICE, and scoping beat relaxing both times.** Adding the `anus`/`labia` repairs broke *"never writes description"* and *"guards every statement on gendered text"* — those repairs deliberately do both — and adding `sexual-arousal` broke *"nulls both bodies"*. Each was re-scoped to the statements it was actually about, with the exception asserted explicitly, rather than widened until it passed.

**The prod e2e is what found all of it, and it was VACUOUS TWICE before it was honest (`e2e/tags-sex-glossary-pass.spec.ts`).** (1) A first draft treated any page containing "Sign in to view" as gated and **skipped 13 of 19 cases** — ordinary pages carry that phrase in a rail notice, so the check matched its own escape hatch; scope it to `<title>`. (2) A bash script asserting only *"the bad text is gone"* reported **28/0** while nine rows were still wrong: these rows are deindexed, the crawler emits no `<article>`, and **absence of the defect and absence of the page are the same green**. Every case must pair a negative with a POSITIVE fingerprint. (3) **The age gate is STICKY PER BROWSER CONTEXT** — `/tags/ovaries` is `is_adult=false` AND `is_sensitive=false` and still showed *"Adult content gated"* once `/tags/praise-kink` had been loaded in the same worker — so an SPA-reading spec's verdict depends on test ORDER and fails as *"still publishes the defect"* on correct data. The spec now reads the crawler `<article>` for indexable rows and the META DESCRIPTION for deindexed ones, naming `long_description` as the gap the migrations' own postconditions cover; it went from 28s with four order-dependent failures to **19/19 in 2.4s**. Faster because it became honest, not despite it.

**A zero-invariant was broken by an individually-correct change, and the fix was to tell two kinds of merge apart (2026-09-14, `51500101145000`):** `50900101100100` withdrew five merges whose target was deprecated, on the rule that *"a merge whose target holds nothing a reader can use is not a merge, it is two dead rows pretending to redirect"*. Three of the five were `jan-mikol-ek`, `kirsten-pl-tz` and `preistr-ger`, and withdrawing them took `tag_hygiene_stats().slug_diacritic_lossy` from 0 to 3 — **a documented zero-invariant, which then failed `Critical data-quality gates` on EVERY open PR in the repo, none of which touched tags.** **The withdrawal reversed a SLUG REPAIR, not an editorial merge.** `20261211120000` created those three, and its own header says a collision has exactly one resolution: *"merge_tag_concept leaves the loser's slug on the loser as its redirect trail, so a corrected slug can never be freed by merging the other way. The corrupt row must be the one that dies."* So the merge was the MECHANISM for repairing a lossy slug whose corrected form is already held by a twin — which is also why the metric excludes `status='merged'`. Each of the three pairs shares a **byte-identical `name`**, both sides deprecated, 0-usage, prose empty, and the loser's slug is a corrupt SPELLING of the winner's. **Withdrawing a de-duplication is a different act from withdrawing a semantic redirect**, and `50900101100100`'s rule still holds for the two that really are semantic (`fluctuating/evolving`, `sensation-stimulation-devices`). Restoring the three costs a print-only advisory (`target_deprecated` 0 → 3) and moves `redirect_to_non_canonical` by **nothing** — 58 → 58, because the `tag_slug_redirects` rows still exist (the withdrawal cleared `merged_into_id` and left the trail standing) so `on conflict (old_slug) do update` rewrites them with the same `tag_id`. **Three alternatives were rejected on evidence:** re-baselining (a zero-invariant, and the check's own output says never loosen a number to make CI pass); renaming the slug (IMPOSSIBLE — the corrected slug is already held by the twin and `slug` is UNIQUE); and DELETING the lossy rows, which was the first proposal and the worst — a hard DELETE on `unified_tags` is unprecedented and irreversible where a merge is undone by `unmerge_tag_concept(audit_id)`. **And the re-baseline happened anyway, in a concurrent session, the same hour (#3709) — which is the part worth keeping.** That PR traced the cause correctly and moved the baseline 0 → 3, dropping `zero: true` from the metric and the test's expectation in lockstep, on the stated ground that *repair was not available* because transliterating collides with the correctly-spelled twin. **The transliteration half of that is right and the conclusion was not: the third option is to restore the merge**, which takes the live value to 0 and makes the loosening unnecessary. Two sessions, opposite conclusions, same invariant, same hour — the `queerness`/`queer-theory` shape again. All three artifacts are restored to the zero-invariant here, and the baseline note keeps the excursion rather than erasing it, because "a repair looked unavailable" is a claim worth being able to re-check: **an option you did not think of is indistinguishable from an option that does not exist.**

**That fix could not be delivered by its own PR, and the reason is a deadlock worth recognising.** `check-tag-hygiene.mjs` reads the **LIVE** database, so a migration repairing a live invariant cannot turn its own PR green: the gate stays red until the file applies, which happens on merge, which the gate blocks — the same shape CLAUDE.md already records for `Critical paths`. The repair was therefore executed against prod directly after a rolled-back dry run — which makes the committed file a **repair shim**, named for the version prod stamped (`20260914175649`) so `db push` skips it on merge. **The first version of this entry said `apply_migration` recorded NO `schema_migrations` row, "checked rather than trusted", and that was FALSE — the check was the thing that was wrong.** It read `... where version like '2026091%' order by version desc limit 5`, and the row's version sorts BELOW several `20260919*` rows, so the limit cut off the one row being looked for and a truncated result read as an absence. CI caught it in minutes (`a version applied to prod has no repo file: 20260914175649`), which is exactly the drift that otherwise makes `db push` skip a merged migration forever. **A query that cannot see the answer is not a check** — bound the predicate, not the row count, and prefer `where version = '<exact>'` to any ordered LIMIT when asking whether one specific row exists. Note also that `statements` holds ONE row for this migration though THREE statements ran, so the column is provably incomplete and the file, not the recorded SQL, is the record.

**The same function timed out a THIRD time, and the arm everyone was looking at was not the cause (2026-09-15, `70000101100000`).** `tag_hygiene_stats()` spent **8.3s** against the 8s ceiling and failed PR #3719 with `57014`; it passed on re-run, and three other PRs called it against the same database inside 40 seconds and passed — so the diff was never the variable. **PostgREST connects as `authenticator`, whose `rolconfig` pins `statement_timeout=8s`, and `service_role` has no `rolconfig`, so `set role service_role` does NOT raise it.** It had already been optimised once (the 2026-08-24 OR-to-two-arms split) and had crept back to 20–40% of the ceiling. **The diagnosis that failed was made from WALL TIME, and wall time is the wrong instrument here**: a warm block is a `shared_buffers` hit, so warm milliseconds hide physical I/O, and an arm timed standalone additionally gets parallel workers that the same arm does NOT get as an InitPlan inside the function. Measured by BUFFERS over the whole body: `totals.assignments` **153,102 blk**, `assignment_to_non_active_tag` **153,102 blk**, `event_tag_pairs_unlinked` 76,631, `events_with_tags_unlinked` 62,288, `event_tag_strings_unresolved` 18,005, everything else 31,869 — **494,997 total**. Two thirds of the function is `unified_tag_assignments`, NOT `events`, and **the two 153,102 entries are the SAME index-only scan run twice**, 210,470 heap fetches each, against a heap that is only **3,597 blocks**: a plain seq scan of that table is 3,597 blocks / 77 ms, so the index-only scan is **42× worse than reading the whole table**. **The planner is not at fault — its inputs are.** `relallvisible` equals a stale `relpages` (2,926 against a real 3,597), so the planner believes every heap page is all-visible and the index-only scan needs no heap access at all. **The first version of this entry blamed that on the table having "never been vacuumed or analysed", read off four NULL `pg_stat_user_tables` timestamps, and that inference does not hold — corrected 2026-09-15 the same day.** Those counters live in the stats collector and **do not survive a restart**: this instance came up 2026-09-05, and **497 of 585 user tables** read NULL for exactly that reason while autovacuum was demonstrably alive throughout (88 tables vacuumed and 113 analysed since start, the newest minutes before the measurement). `last_autovacuum IS NULL` therefore means *"not in the last 10 days"*, never *"not ever"*, and `n_live_tup` reading **12,404 for a table holding 264,261 rows** is the same artifact — a counter accumulating from zero since the restart, not a census. **The durable evidence is `pg_class`, which DOES survive a restart**, and it says something narrower and still bad: `relpages` 2,926 against a real 3,612 means the last vacuum or analyze ran when the table was **23% smaller**, and `relallvisible` has been frozen at that same 2,926 ever since. `age(relfrozenxid)` is 53.6M. **Read `pg_class.relpages`/`relallvisible` when you want to know when a table was last vacuumed; read `pg_stat_user_tables` only for what happened since the current postmaster started.** The condition is real but NOT an autovacuum outage: 13 tables sit over the autovacuum threshold un-vacuumed, most of them tiny (`venue_sources` 1,014 dead tuples, `tag_aliases` 520) and most with `relallvisible = relpages`, i.e. an accurate VM. `unified_tag_assignments` is the outlier on both axes at once — **31,360 dead tuples, 12.4× over its threshold, and one of the few whose VM is provably stale** — which is why it, and not the other twelve, fooled the planner. That is an operational condition and is deliberately NOT "fixed" in the migration — a migration runs inside a transaction and `VACUUM` cannot, and an `ANALYZE` would be undone by the next bulk write. **What a migration CAN own is not doing the same expensive scan five times**, which is cheaper whether or not the visibility map is healthy. Three shared CTEs (`uta_rollup` folding the three whole-table counters into one pass with `count(*) filter`, `ev_assign` holding the `entity_type='event'` slice, `ev` holding events with non-empty tags): **`events` 3 scans → 1, `unified_tag_assignments` 5 → 2 (and seq, not index-only), 494,997 → 57,585 blocks (8.6×), warm 1,750 → 1,390 ms.** **Quote BLOCKS when re-measuring this, never the warm milliseconds** — warm time moves only 20% because the failure mode is a COLD call, where those 437,000 extra blocks are physical reads. Equivalence is not by inspection: `active` is keyed on the `unified_tags` primary key, so `not exists` and `left join … where t.id is null` count the same rows, and all three event counters are already blind to a tagless event (`unnest` of an empty array yields nothing), so restricting the shared set to non-empty `tags` is exact. Output was verified **byte-identical by evaluating the old function and the new body in ONE statement**, so both see one snapshot — re-run that comparison rather than eyeballing counts, because the corpus moves between calls. **A non-recursive CTE referenced more than once is not inlined**, so these materialise on their own; `materialized` is written out anyway, and `work_mem` is 12 MB against ~3.5 MB and ~3 MB tuplestores. Guarded twice, because the migration's own `do $verify$` binds only the migration carrying it while `create or replace` lets the NEXT restatement silently reinstate five scans: the verify block asserts the CTEs exist, that `unified_tag_assignments` appears exactly twice and `events` exactly once, and that the function still EXECUTES and returns 27 keys (`assert_admin_or_internal()` returns early for a session with no JWT claims, which is what `db push` is); `src/lib/__tests__/tagHygieneStats.test.ts` asserts the same against the latest definition. Both mutation-tested (6 DB mutations, 4 repo mutations, all caught, plus a comment-only control that correctly SURVIVES) — **and one mutation first read as a survivor because `str.replace` with no count rewrote the GUARD as well as the statement**, the same trap this file has now recorded six times: mutate the body only, and verify the mutation landed where you meant. **It timed out a FOURTH time (2026-09-18, PR #3779) and the recorded cause did NOT recur — which is the point of re-measuring rather than re-applying the last fix.** `unified_tag_assignments` was HEALTHY this time (`relpages` 3,675 = `relallvisible` 3,675, autovacuumed that morning), so the stale-VM/index-only-scan mechanism above was absent. What WAS stale was **`unified_tags`**: `relallvisible` 1,845 of 2,671 (69%), 2,049 dead tuples. **The producer of that bloat is this repo's own standing practice — a migration dry-run on prod in a transaction forced to roll back STILL leaves dead tuples**, so a session that dry-runs a dozen data-repair migrations against `unified_tags` bloats the very table the glossary sentinels read. `VACUUM (ANALYZE)` took it to 100% visible / 0 dead and warm time **2,126 → 1,446 ms (32%)** — **and blocks moved FOUR, 102,759 → 102,755.** By this entry's own rule (quote blocks, never warm milliseconds, because the failure is a COLD call) **the VACUUM was therefore not the fix**, and treating the 32% as the fix would have been the wall-time mistake recorded above, one table over. **Blocks have grown 57,585 → 102,755 (78%) and it is CORPUS growth, not a restatement regression** — checked rather than assumed, because that is exactly what the verify block exists to catch: `uta_rollup` and `ev_assign` are both still present, `unified_tag_assignments` still appears exactly twice and `events` exactly once. The growth sits in `events`, which now carries **36,940 rows with non-empty `tags`**: materialising the `ev` CTE is **16,112 blocks**. **THE SECOND FIGURE I FIRST WROTE HERE WAS WRONG AND THE ERROR IS THE REUSABLE PART.** I read `event_tag_strings_unresolved` as "~18,000 blocks on its own" beside ev's 16,112, as if the two were independent costs to be added. **`EXPLAIN` reports buffers CUMULATIVELY up the plan tree**, so 17,974 is the arm's whole subtree *including* ev — it arithmetically decomposes as 16,112 (ev) + 1,275 (`idx_unified_tags_lower_name`) + 587 (`idx_unified_tags_lower_slug`), matching each intermediate node exactly. The arm's **marginal** cost beyond ev is ~1,878 blocks, roughly a tenth of what I quoted. **A rolled-up figure reads exactly like an independent one; subtract the children before quoting a node as a cost.** **RESOLVED BY #3784, WHICH REACHED THE OPPOSITE CONCLUSION AND IS RIGHT.** A concurrent session measured the same function at **1.3s warm / 1.6s cold against the 8s ceiling — 6x headroom** — and made the same cumulative-buffers mistake in the other direction (reading one rolled-up 45,945 as two independent scans), built the shared-CTE optimisation it implied, measured it at **102,783 -> 102,048 blocks, 0.7%**, and threw it away. So the diagnosis is **contention, not cost**: one call took 8.3s while three other PRs hit the same database inside 40 seconds and passed, and a 20% optimisation does not survive a 6x spike. The fix is `check-tag-hygiene.mjs` retrying **once** on 57014 and saying so loudly — the same handling `check-data-quality-gates.mjs` and `check-search-facets-parity.mjs` already had, and this script was the one that never got it. **My "the next optimisation is the `ev` set" was therefore wrong**, and it was wrong because I sized the target from a cumulative number. **A green re-run is not a fix, and neither is a VACUUM whose block count did not move — but a retry IS the fix when the cost is fine and the contention is not.** **IT TIMED OUT A FIFTH TIME, AND THE SENTENCE ABOVE RESTS ON A PREMISE NOBODY MEASURED (2026-09-19, `99991789807686`).** "A retry IS the fix when the cost is fine" assumed the cost was fine. It was not. Measured as whole-query TOP-NODE totals -- the one reading that cannot double-count -- the function is **101,228 blocks with `enable_indexonlyscan` on and 54,921 with it off, 1.84x**, and the whole difference is ONE leaf node. `ev_assign` reads the 88,152-row `entity_type='event'` subset of `unified_tag_assignments` through `unified_tag_assignments_tag_id_entity_id_entity_type_key`, where `entity_type` is the THIRD column, so `Index Cond` is a filter over the entire 24 MB index plus **65,895 heap fetches** -- **46,992 blocks to read a SUBSET of a table whose whole heap is 3,675 blocks, 12.8x worse than reading all of it.** Fix is one line on the function, `SET enable_indexonlyscan TO 'off'`. **It is the SAME pathology `70000101100000` removed from `uta_rollup`, in the CTE immediately below it** -- third recorded occurrence of stale-VM index-only thrash on this instance, which is why it is a blanket directive and not another hand-folded CTE: every arm here is a whole-table aggregation or a small indexed lookup, none benefits from an index-only scan, and each is a latent 12x regression waiting on the next autovacuum lag. A migration still cannot fix the visibility map, but it can stop the function DEPENDING on it. **WHAT BOTH PRIOR SESSIONS GOT RIGHT, AND THE EXACT LINE BETWEEN THEM.** Both misread cumulative buffers, in opposite directions, and the surviving rule is narrower than either: **buffer counts roll up through the plan tree, so per-node attribution is valid ONLY on a LEAF** (`not (node ? 'Plans')` -- a childless scan has nothing beneath it to roll up). #3784's two 45,945 figures were interior nodes sharing a parent's total, so folding them bought 0.7% and was correctly thrown away; the 46,992 here is an Index Only Scan with no sub-plan, so it is genuinely its own, and removing it moves the whole-query total by 46%. **Same number, different structural position -- check the position before trusting OR dismissing a figure.** #3784 was also right that `ev` is not the target: measured and rejected here too, a covering partial index on `events` takes `ev` 16,112 -> 6,294 blocks (9.6% of the total) and **buys back the exact dependency this fix removes** -- its reading already carried 5,868 heap fetches 7 minutes after an autovacuum -- for a write-blocking SHARE lock on a 126 MB ingest table. If the events half ever needs attention, fix autovacuum on `events`, not the query. **THE RETRY STAYS AND THE TWO COMPOSE**: #3784 stops the gate MISREPORTING a timeout as a hygiene regression, this removes the cause so the retry stops being load-bearing -- its own note is the argument for both, "a retry that quietly succeeds is how a function creeps back toward the ceiling unnoticed". **VERIFY THE SHIPPED MECHANISM, NOT A SIMULATION OF IT:** a session-level SET proves nothing about a function-level one, so it was re-checked with the session forced to the OPPOSITE value -- `enable_indexonlyscan=on` at session level and the call still reads **57,641 blocks**, i.e. the function's own SET wins at call time. Live e2e under the production ceiling (`statement_timeout=8s` + `service_role` claims): 27 keys, 1,146/995/974 ms, and the gate's own baseline comparison returns 0 regressions / 0 missing. The body is `70000101100000` VERBATIM (`prosrc` md5 `a7ed49bf570ac4e02b43dbf2d5936bde`, checked before and after), so the only change is the SET clause and it cannot alter a result. Guarded on **`pg_proc.proconfig`** rather than on source text, plus `src/lib/__tests__/tagHygieneStats.test.ts` -- `create or replace` drops a SET clause exactly as silently as it drops a counter.

**`[skip ci]` written INSIDE PROSE is still `[skip ci]`, and the commit asserting the branch was clean is what dirtied it (2026-09-14).** A commit message on the glossary branch ended *"Also verified for the squash merge: no commit on this branch carries [skip ci]/[ci skip], which on a squash would silently suppress CI…"* — writing the assertion falsified it. **The repo's `squash_merge_commit_message` is `COMMIT_MESSAGES`** (read from the API, not assumed), so the default squash body is the concatenation of every commit message and would have carried the marker onto `main`, suppressing CI, `deploy-supabase-functions` and the Pages build while the PR reported merged — the #1952 incident again. **The fix is at MERGE TIME, not in the history**: the merge API takes an explicit `commit_message`, and a human can edit the body in the merge dialog. **A `filter-branch` rewrite was built and then ABANDONED, which is the more useful half of this entry**: every published commit here is SSH-signed (`gpgsig -----BEGIN SSH SIGNATURE-----` in the raw object), `filter-branch` drops signatures, and `user.signingkey` points at a **0-byte** file with no private key, so the rewrite could not be re-signed and would have turned ten commits Unverified to fix one sentence. **Auto-merge is the one path that cannot take a custom body** — it composes the default when it fires — so it must stay off while such a commit is in the history.

**Substance interactions — attribution is a COLUMN, and the grid forgot (2026-09-02/03):** `public.substance_interactions` backs the per-tag "Mixing X" band and the full grid at `/tags/interactions`. One row per **unordered** pair, pinned by a `tag_a_id < tag_b_id` CHECK plus a unique index — both halves load-bearing, since the inequality picks the canonical direction and without it the index happily accepts the mirror. `status` is a normalised 7-value key (never TripSit's display strings, which carry ampersands and would leak source formatting into every URL, filter and i18n key) and `substance_interaction_rank()` is what "worst first" sorts by, so the safety ordering is a database fact rather than a hand-maintained TS array that can drift. **The table is MULTI-SOURCE and that is the whole trap: 421 `tripsit` + 48 `eve&rave Substanzhandbuch` + 7 `FDA label` = 476.** `substance_interaction_matrix()` nevertheless built its envelope with two literals — `'source','tripsit'` and `'source_url','https://combo.tripsit.me/'` — so the footer read *"Interaction data researched and published by TripSit"* over a grid where **55 of 476 cells are not TripSit's**, denying two sources their credit and attributing 55 safety claims to an organisation that never made them. The per-tag band was already correct (it reads `source` per row, which is why `/tags/methamphetamine` reads "eve&rave Substanzhandbuch" — all ten of its interactions are eve&rave and TripSit's chart does not cover methamphetamine at all), so the fix generalised it: `20261202100000` added a `sources` array of the distinct `(source, source_url)` pairs present, and `20261207100000` **deleted both scalars** and gave every cell its own `source`/`source_url`. Envelope is now exactly `{axis, cells, sources}`. **Three rules survive a refactor.** (1) The credit is computed over the **CELLS**, sharing one CTE with them, so a source whose only rows sit behind a deprecated tag cannot be credited for a grid that does not show them — credit and grid cannot drift apart. (2) **Dedup by source NAME, never by URL**: the 7 FDA rows cite **four** different DailyMed documents (sildenafil/Viagra, tadalafil/Cialis and vardenafil/Levitra share a label; avanafil has its own), so a URL-keyed dedup prints "FDA label, FDA label, FDA label, FDA label" — a defect the per-tag band shipped once already. The rule now lives in exactly one place, `creditSources()` in `src/lib/substanceRisk.ts`, used by both surfaces. (3) **The e2e derives its expectation from the page's own RPC response**, never a list written in the spec — a hardcoded `['TripSit','eve&rave…','FDA label']` goes green the day a fourth source lands and is silently left out of the credit, which is this defect again one source later. **Refresh + sentinel:** weekly cron `source_tripsit` (`40 4 * * 2`) → `sync_tripsit_interactions`, breaker `tripsit`, feed-shrink floor so a truncated fetch is never read as a retraction; the sync restamps **every** row it confirms, which is why `max(fetched_at)` per source is a true "last successful sync". Staleness lives in `check-pipeline-health.mjs` §9 at **14 days = two missed weekly runs**, and the watched set is **derived from `ingestion_sources.target_table`**, not listed — eve&rave and FDA have no automated refresher at all, so their `fetched_at` is frozen by design and they can only WARN; they have nothing to be late for. The gate **arms itself** (it fails on staleness only once a write proves the path works, else after 14 days it fails for never having written) and carries a **positive control**: PostgREST answers a no-match with an empty SET rather than an error, so flipping the single `ingestion_sources.is_enabled` flag would make the loop iterate zero times and the gate pass having checked nothing while 476 rows kept serving — that flag is NOT the one the loop already handles (`admin_automations.enabled`, different column, different table), and zero watched sources is now a hard fail. **CI trap worth knowing beyond this feature:** `Critical paths` is a REQUIRED check that builds YOUR branch but calls the **LIVE** backend, so an assertion requiring a key that a migration in the same PR adds can only go green *after* the merge it blocks — a deadlock, not a guard. Assert the **property** ("the credit names exactly the sources the response exposes, one each") rather than the shape, and delete the compatibility branch once the thing it tolerated is gone (`#3332` — a fallback to a deleted scalar would silently reconstruct the single-source provenance the migration removed, at exactly the moment it should fail loudly). Guards: `e2e/tags-substance-interactions.spec.ts` (mutation-tested — 30-days-stale exits 1, disarmed exits 1, `sources` stripped exits 1) + unit tests on `creditSources`. Detail: `docs/architecture/open-data-integration.md` §1.4.

**Address Completeness (state / postal_code / country, 2026-08-07):** Entities carried a city or street address but left `state`, `postal_code` and `country_id` empty — 22,006 venues with no state, 21,874 with no postal code, **26,840 events with a NULL `country_id` despite `events.country` already holding the ISO-2 code**. Three layers. **(1) Schema:** `organizations` gained `address`/`state`/`postal_code`, `hotels` gained `state`/`postal_code`, `events` gained `postal_code` (migration `20260807100000`). Deliberately NOT added to `marketplace_merchants`/`marketplace_brands`/`affiliate_partners` — all three carry `organization_id`, have zero address source data, and the Business Spine plan rules that address/geo "stay per-location"; they inherit through the spine. `personalities.country_id` is a BIRTHPLACE and is excluded entirely. **(2) Instant derive:** one BEFORE trigger fn `derive_entity_geo_address()` on venues/events/hotels/organizations (`trg_*_geo_derive`, sorts before `trg_*_safety_gated` — same-timing triggers fire in NAME order) fills `country_id`/`state`/`city`/`country` from the linked city and the country text, with **zero network calls**. Three rules: explicit caller input wins; fill what is empty; **re-derive on relocation** — a pure NULL-fill rule left `country_id` pointing at the US while the text said `'YE'`. **It recomputes `safety_gated` itself (load-bearing):** `trg_*_safety_gated` is scoped `BEFORE UPDATE OF country_id, city_id`, and a column-scoped trigger fires on the columns named in the UPDATE *statement*, not on what a BEFORE trigger mutated — so `UPDATE venues SET country='YE'` never fired it and left a venue in a criminalizing country publicly visible. **(3) Ambiguity guard (`20260807100200`):** country text is resolved ONLY through `resolve_country_from_text(country, city)`, never a bare `upper(code)=upper(country)`. Two dozen US-state/CA-province abbreviations are also valid ISO codes, and the first backfill pass produced Agawam `MA`→Morocco, Sturgis `SD`→Sudan, Tuscaloosa `AL`→Albania, Petaluma `CA`→Canada — two of them criminalizing, so the safety layer hid real US events. The resolver demands city corroboration for ambiguous codes and returns NULL rather than guessing (`'DE'`+Berlin still → Germany). **`state` is a relational derivation, not a geocode:** it comes from `cities.region_name`, so backfilling ~1,900 cities (Photon reverse, `&lang=en` REQUIRED or you get "Bayern"/"Bavaria" mixed) fills state for tens of thousands of venues/events/hotels and keeps working for every future row. `postal_code` is the only field needing a geocoder: rows are enqueued into `geo_address_queue` (a TABLE, not per-row `net.http_post` — a session-GUC guard would be fail-open across ~12 bulk write paths) and drained by cron `geo_address_drain` (`*/5`, `mode:'postal'` Photon branch in `backfill-venue-cities`, registered in `admin_automations`); the historical ~21k backlog runs from `scripts/data-quality/backfill-venue-postal.mjs`. **Prevention at the source:** `commit_venue_staging_item` DECLAREd `v_country_id`, resolved it, and never put it in the INSERT list (and its country lookup was nested inside `IF city IS NOT NULL`); `commit_event_staging_item` contained the string `country_id` nowhere at all; `pipeline-normalize` dropped the `postcode` that `source-osm-venue` emits and threw away the state/postcode Photon returns in the same response as the coordinates. All fixed (`20260807100100`). Batch discipline: a 300-row events UPDATE costs 14.6s, **13.8s of it `trg_search_documents_event`** — never raise the batch size. Admin: "Addresses" section on `/admin/quality` (`GeoAddressQualityPanel` + `geo_address_gap_counts()`).

**Business Spine (org-as-party unification, 2026-07-26):** `organizations` is the universal business-party spine — every hotel / venue / merchant / affiliate partner / (queer-owned) brand / support org links UP via nullable `organization_id`; `roles[]` says what a business is (vocab CHECK `organizations_roles_known`, NOT VALID: venue/publisher/seller/support/hotel/affiliate_partner/brand/organizer/community — merchants use `seller`). Typed tables stay the detail records; org is link-only (no field canonicalization yet). Hotels joined the safety-gating layer (`hotels.safety_gated` + RLS + `recompute_safety_gated_for_country`). Nightly backfill cron `org_spine_backfill` (05:10 UTC, 200/batch) runs the **adopt-before-create ladder** (`find_org_adoption_candidates`): domain proof or despaced-name+city ≥0.90 auto-links via `link_organization_entity`, ambiguous matches queue in `org_link_suggestions` (own table — deliberately NOT `dedup_review_queue`, whose approve path runs merge cores; decisions via `decide_org_adoption`), rest mints orgs via `org_mint` (slug-collision loop). **bnb/apartment hotels never mint orgs** (private host listings ≠ businesses); **brands are queue-only** (only `ownership_tags`-tagged brands get suggestions; global brands stay unlinked). Progress: `SELECT org_spine_drift_counts()`.
