Admin: **Business console `/admin/business`** (Cockpit nav, directory tab, detail `/admin/business/:id` with per-role tabs + link/unlink picker; data hooks in `src/hooks/useBusinessSpine.ts`), "Promote to business" row action on `/admin/hotels`, `promote_entity_to_organization` RPC; seller-orgs tab absorbed from `/admin/vendors` (redirects). **Link review lives on `/admin/quality`** (2026-07-29) — `OrgLinkReviewQueue` is a card + inline accordion section there, `?tab=review` redirects, and it stays out of the inbox action bar because approving picks a target org (`triage_sources.capabilities.external_console`, honored by `EXTERNAL_CONSOLE` in `TriageDetailPanel.tsx`; `triage_action` has no `org-link-review` branch). Counts via `triage_sources` row `org-link-review` → `review_org_links`. Migrations `20260726182150`–`20260726183016`. Later phases (cockpit absorption v2, claim portal v3): `docs/plans/2026-07-26-business-spine-unification.md`.

**Dedup Truth Engine (2026-07-25):** `/admin/duplicates` is folded into the Truth-Engine architecture. Nightly `run_dedup_truth_sweep_all()` (cron `dedup_truth_sweep` `50 5 * * *`, before the 06:00 marketplace sweep, which keeps running; the 06:15 **event** sweep was RETIRED in `20270822093614` — see the event-dedup entry below) scores duplicate pairs across all 12 merge-capable types using the shared `dedup_despace`/`dedup_core_tokens` keys: exact-identity pairs (per-type gates — venue/hotel key+city+<150 m, city key+country+<10 km, event key+venue+±48 h **plus key+identical instant+same city, see below**, marketplace key+merchant_domain, **news — auto-only, see below**, milestone key+year, org key+domain, village/group key+city, country key; **personality key + equal non-null wikidata_qid or birth_date — name-only person pairs NEVER auto-merge, namesake/outing risk**) auto-merge through the merge cores — this line read "the **reversible** merge cores" from 2026-07-25 until 2026-09-12, and it was true of two of the twelve; see the reversibility entry below — ambiguous pairs land in `dedup_review_queue` (open-pair unique index = idempotent re-sweeps; rejected pairs are never re-suggested). Rollout lever: `admin_automations.conditions.mode` = `dry_run` | `queue_only` (launch state) | `full` — flip with a plain UPDATE, no migration. Caps: 300 merges + 200 queue inserts per type per run (search_documents trigger-storm discipline). Merged duplicates write a `corroboration` signal into the entity's `*_quality_signals` ledger where one exists (village CHECK extended); queued pairs stamp `needs_attention` on the 7 tables that have it. Decision RPCs `approve_dedup_review(p_id, p_keep_id)` (canonical flip supported) / `reject_dedup_review` / `approve_dedup_review_batch` (excludes personalities). Counts: `quality_duplicates` in `get_admin_counts` static block + `triage_sources` row `dedup-review` (view `triage_src_dedup_review`, `risk_flags.namesake` on person rows, inbox-visible; decisions deep-link to the console). Admin: Duplicates card on `/admin/quality`, `DedupPendingLink` cross-links on all six entity quality pages. The nightly-sweep queue lives at **`/admin/inbox?queue=dedup-review`** (`triage_action`'s `dedup-review` branch); the old `DedupReviewQueue` component and the `/admin/duplicates?view=suggested` view were deleted in `fae7a674f` / `20260801050000`, and that page is now only the exact/fuzzy merge power tool. Migrations `20260725200000`–`20260725202000`, plus `20260806130000` (merge cores were anon-callable — revoked to `service_role`) and `20260806140000` (news URL guard + marketplace token-arm deletion, applied to the LIVE function by string surgery, so `20260725201000` in the repo was wrong until `20260809100000` re-committed the whole function).

**Venue dedup was blind in the same way, and the fix is corroboration instead of coordinates (2026-09-06):** `dedup_truth_sweep` ran nightly at 05:50 in `mode='full'`, `last_run_status='success'`, `consecutive_failures=0` — and `run_dedup_truth_sweep('venue','dry_run')` returned **`{would_merge: 0, would_queue: 480}`**. 483 candidate pairs, **zero** auto-eligible, while 530 pairs aged in `dedup_review_queue` (oldest 43 days) at confidences 0.75/0.85 — below every auto-approve threshold, so only a human could ever clear them, and none had since July. **The auto gate was `both coordinates present AND haversine < 150 m`, and coordinates are the least trustworthy column on `venues`.** 2,665 live venues sit on **908 SHARED coordinate points** (largest pile 32) because a missing geocode falls back to a city centroid; hand-read pairs with byte-identical street addresses measure **507 km** apart (Spartakus, Kraków), 466 km (Sauna Le 9), 423 km (Ganimedes), 42 km (Peninsula Sauna). So distance is now **display only** — it gates nothing and, critically, **vetoes nothing** either: a same-address pair 466 km apart is a real duplicate, so coordinates may not overrule an address. `dm = 0` is the *placeholder signature*, not proximity — the one venue pair a human ever rejected as `auto_eligible` is **"Jessheim Pride" ⇄ "Fredrikstad Pride"**, two different Norwegian towns, at exactly 0 m. **The reason string lied on 82% of the queue**: `case when both coords and <150m then 'geo' else 'no_geo'` labels a pair 8.5 km apart identically to a pair with no coordinates at all — 398 of 483 had coords that *disagreed*, only 85 were genuinely missing them, and absence of evidence and evidence-against were shown to the reviewer as one word. **What replaced it came from reading 106 pairs by hand** — `same_street_address` 20/20 same-city and **29/29 cross-city**, `shell_absorbed` 24/24, `same_phone` 8/8, `same_domain` 23-24/25 — and from the reviewers' own record: `one side is an empty shell` fires on **35/65 approvals and 0/4 rejections**, and all 4 rejections are pairs where BOTH sides carry a real, DIFFERENT street address (Rapa Nui's two Buenos Aires branches, two GMHC offices). **Both observed false-positive classes are vetoed by construction**: a multi-branch chain (*Circa Soho* vs *Circa Embankment* share `circasoho.com` but sit on different streets; Silverado before and after its move) dies on the identity-conflict rung, which sits ABOVE every agreement rung; and a single generic core token (`dedup_core_tokens` strips the city name, so both Prides reduce to `{pride}`) dies on a `cardinality(core) >= 2` rung placed above every corroborator, which also took the shell arm from 18/20 to 24/24 by removing *Le Concorde* ⇄ *La Concorde*. **ONE LADDER** (`arm` chosen once, `is_auto`/`conf`/`reason` read off it; the old branch computed reason and confidence as two parallel CASEs re-evaluating the same haversine four times and coalesced nothing). **A third candidate generator K3** — same `country_id`, same despaced name, NOT the same `city_id` — adds 912 pairs the old key could never see, and hand-reading shows they are `city_id` MISLINKS ("Bournemouth Saunabar" filed under Brighton, "Kremlin"/96 Donegall Street Belfast filed under Brighton, "Gothic Sauna" as both Lugano and Como). It uses `not coalesce(a.city_id = b.city_id, false)` rather than `is distinct from`, because the latter silently drops the 3,225 venues with no `city_id` and is not the exact complement of K1; and cross-city pairs can auto-merge **only** on an address or a Google place id — the cross-city rung sits above domain/phone/shell precisely because crossing a city boundary is where chains live. Result **0 → 238 auto-eligible** (145 address, 62 shell, 23 domain, 8 phone), with a deploy-time assertion that fails on `would_merge = 0` *and* on `would_merge > 600`, plus non-zero positive controls for each veto. **A merge that throws is no longer indistinguishable from a skipped candidate** — but note that not every throw is a fault: "Boys Sauna" exists in THREE copies at C/. Luis Braille 1, so the run generates all three pairs and the first merge collapses the chain, making the rest hit `_venue_merge_core`'s own guards. Those are counted as `chain_skipped`, classified **by state** (is either row now a duplicate?) and never by matching the guard's message text, which lives in another function. **The backlog warning keys on the MEDIAN open-pair age, not the oldest** — measured post-deploy the queue reads 202 open / oldest 424h / **median 0h**, because 200 rows were just re-queued and the 2 stragglers are hand-annotated rows left for a human, so a `min()`-based rule warns on a correct deploy on every CI run, and a warning that is always on is one people scroll past. (`percentile_disc`, not `_cont` — the latter only interpolates numerics and fails 42883 on a `timestamptz`.) **`run_venue_fuzzy_automerge` is RETIRED** (`20330101100300`): same 150 m predicate, NULL actor, and **no memory of `dedup_review_queue`**, so the `/admin/duplicates` button could re-merge a pair a human had explicitly rejected — and all four venue rejections on record are reachable by it. It had **no cron and no registry row**, so the retirement is a REVOKE plus deleting the button, and the migration *asserts* that premise rather than performing the disable-registry-row ritual on nothing. **The ingest blocker was starved, not missing**: `pipeline-deduplicate` hardcoded `p_city_id: null` and never passed `p_address`, so **three of `find_venue_duplicate_candidates`'s seven branches** — `despaced_exact` 0.96, `core_token` 0.92, `address_name_proximity` — were structurally unreachable at ingest along with the functional indexes built for them. Staging genuinely has no `city_id` (measured: 0 of 8,161 rows over 60 days, against 8,081 with `location.city` and 8,016 with `location.address`), so the new path is text-keyed and scored **0.88 — deliberately below the 0.90 auto bar**, because `cities` cannot represent Portland ME vs Portland OR; only `address_exact` (0.97) auto-merges. The same migration closes the **country hole** that let a Berlin *Village* merge into an Osaka *Village* at score 1.000 on the name alone (`guards: [geoGuard(250)]` plus `haversine_m` returning NULL for a coordinate-less pair = no veto at all); the veto lives in the RPC's WHERE clauses rather than as a TS guard, resolves through `resolve_country_from_text` (never `upper(code)=upper(country)`, which produced Agawam MA → Morocco) and **fails open** when the country is unresolvable — a missed veto is a duplicate to clean up, a wrong one is a duplicate we can never see again. **Reversibility was a known gap at that commit and is now CLOSED** (`20350101100000`). It shipped without it: `venue_merge_audit` had no `details` column, `_venue_merge_core` recorded reparenting as COUNTS, and `unmerge_venues` only un-hid the dropped row — measured by merging a real venue on prod and unmerging it, **514 events → 0 → 0**, i.e. every child stayed on the survivor while unmerge reported success. The same round trip now returns 514. So the one-shot drain ran at `p_merge_cap = 40` and all 40 were hand-read before merge (40/40 defensible) — but note **the NIGHTLY sweep was never capped at 40**: `run_dedup_truth_sweep_all` calls the 2-arg form, so it has always used the default 300, which is exactly why the reversibility fix could not wait. Two venue-specific differences make copying the event version (`20270822093311`/`093412`) wrong: **`venue_personal_visits` has no `id` column** (PK is `(user_id, venue_id)`, so `returning id` does not compile and the recorded values are USER ids, restored by `venue_id = keep AND user_id IN (…)`), and **`guide_picks` moves `entity_id`** and is shared across entity types. The **1,544 pre-fix audit rows are never backfilled** — the information was never recorded and inventing it would reparent rows that legitimately belonged to the survivor; `unmerge_venues` REFUSES them unless `p_force`, which reports `reparenting_restored:false`. The old 1-arg signature is DROPped (PostgREST resolves BY ARGUMENT NAME; a leftover overload makes a named call ambiguous, 42725). Runtime guard `venue_dup_signals().merges_unreversible_since_fix` is anchored to the FIRST stamped merge, **not to a rolling window** — 52 merges in the trailing week predate the fix, so a `last 7 days` rule fires on a correct deploy, the same cry-wolf shape the median-vs-oldest backlog rule already removed. Sentinel `venue_dup_signals()` is standalone and **service_role only from the start** (its event twin shipped granted to `authenticated` and had to be narrowed afterwards by `20280301104412`); `_dedup_venue_cluster_side` is **SECURITY INVOKER**, because its event twin was DEFINER by reflex and leaked safety-gated EVENTS in the UAE and Malaysia to anon (`20290601120731`); `venues` carries the same `USING (NOT safety_gated OR auth.uid() IS NOT NULL)` policy, so the same reflex here would expose venues in criminalizing countries. Migrations `20330101100000`–`20330101100600` plus `20350101100000`/`20350101100100` (reversibility + its runtime guard); guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/dedupVenueArms.test.ts` and `venueMergeReversibility.test.ts`, mutation-tested (20 mutations, all caught) and by `e2e/venue-merge-redirect.spec.ts`. **Verify a mutation actually APPLIED before believing it was missed** — two of those 20 first read as "not caught" because the perl pattern silently did not match (one used `[^;]*` across a message that itself contains a semicolon), which is indistinguishable from a guard that does not fire.

**Event dedup was running green and merging nothing, for eleven days (2027-05-02):** `dedup_truth_sweep` fired nightly at 05:50 in `mode='full'`, reported success, `consecutive_failures=0` — and `entity_merge_audit` held 645 event merges dated 2026-08-23/24/25 and **zero since**. Not paused: **both auto arms matched 0 candidate pairs.** Both need a venue, and of 47,868 live events only **2,604 (5.4%) carry `venue_id` and 8,385 (17.5%) `venue_name`; 39,383 (82%) carry neither**; `arm_venue_name` additionally demands a byte-identical `start_date`, and while 8 pairs shared a despaced venue name, **0** also shared the instant. So everything fell through to the queue-only arms and 84 pairs aged there, oldest 2026-08-09. **`would_merge: 0, would_queue: 84` is the shape of this failure** — and note that "0 bad merges" passes in exactly this state, which is why the sentinel had to be built around a different quantity. **The fix came from the reviewers, not from a hunch:** all **50** event pairs a human has ever rejected carry the same note — *"separate showtimes of one production"* — and **every one has a DIFFERENT `start_date`**, while 66 pairs sat open with an *identical* one. Hence `exact_instant_same_city` (0.95, auto): same despaced title + identical instant + same city. **Two vetoes, both found by reading rows.** (1) **Different city is absolute** — *Dining out for Life* runs the same day in Asheville, Seattle and Minneapolis, *Transgender Day of Remembrance* in Charlotte and New York; and **`is not distinct from` is NOT the test**, since two NULL `city_id`s satisfy it and 2 live pairs are exactly that. (2) **A midnight stamp is a placeholder, not a measurement** — two rows at 00:00 agree only on the date, which is already the blocking key, so they drop to review (14 pairs). A venue-name *disagreement* drops to review too (2). **Containment is restored but never auto.** `title_containment` was added by `20260510110000` and silently reverted by `20260522000000`, which called itself a no-op; it comes back as a candidate generator only (77 pairs, +92% recall), because hand-reading shows its failure mode is **sub-events, not duplicates** ("Muscle Classic V Pre-Party" vs "Muscle Classic V") — programme relationships, which `_event_merge_core`'s umbrella guard already refuses. **Widening the blocking key silently widened the two PRE-EXISTING venue arms**, written for an exact-title key and merging at 0.97/0.96: measured, 10 containment pairs reached them, and `arm_venue_id` accepts ±48h at one venue, so a pre-party two hours from its parent would have auto-merged. `not same_title` is therefore the **FIRST** rung of the ladder, making every later rung implicitly same-title. **ONE LADDER**: `arm` is chosen once and `is_auto`/`conf`/`reason` are read off it — an earlier draft used two parallel CASEs and they disagreed, emitting `exact_instant_midnight` at both 0.85 and 0.80 where the 0.80 rows shared no instant at all. Every arm predicate is `coalesce(..., false)`: `a.vn = b.vn` is NULL when one side has no venue name, which made `is_auto` NULL on live rows. Result **0 → 37 auto-merges, all five vetoes verified with non-zero positive controls** (14 diff-city, 2 both-null-city, 41 midnight, 21 venue-conflict, 77 containment — 0 auto in every class). **Reversibility had to land FIRST, because the arms auto-merge without a human.** `_event_merge_core` recorded reparenting as COUNTS, which cannot be reversed, so `unmerge_entities` flipped `duplicate_of_id` and left every child on the keep row — the same asymmetry that keeps the city geo arm non-auto. It now stamps `details.moved` (row ids per child relation) + `schema:1`, and unmerge replays them; a pre-fix audit is **refused** rather than half-undone (`p_force` for the old behaviour, returning `reparenting_restored:false`) — all 645 existing rows are in that class. The 1-arg overload is DROPped, since PostgREST resolves BY ARGUMENT NAME and a mismatch is a silent PGRST202 404. Verified on prod in a rolled-back txn: an 86-source event merged and unmerged returned all 86, and the pre-fix behaviour mutation-tested to 86 → 0. **`events.venue_name` never arrived from the highest-volume feeds** — `source-ticketmaster` writes it to `metadata.venue_name` while `commit_event_staging_item` read the TOP level; **417 of 417** missing Ticketmaster rows carried one in metadata, outsavvy 221 of 221, and the UPDATE branch never mentioned `venue_name` at all, so an existing row could never acquire one. Both closed, `NormalizedItem` gained the field (same contract-gap class as accessibility), backfill 659 rows batched at 300 → Ticketmaster **17 → 434**. **The legacy 06:15 `run_event_dedup_sweep` is RETIRED** (`20270822093614`): no queue, no confidence, no showtime suppression and **no memory of human rejections**, so a pair rejected at 06:00 could be merged at 06:15. Registry row disabled FIRST then guarded unschedule — never DELETE, which would make it "unregistered" and un-killable; the reconciler's dry run confirms `recreated: []`. **The queue payload now carries `start_date`/`city`/`venue_name`/`source`** — every one of the 50 rejections turned on the start time, which the old two-titles-and-a-match_type payload did not show. Sentinel `event_dup_signals()` is a **standalone** function (restating `pipeline_hygiene_stats` is a merge-collision surface) and **its own first draft would have missed this incident** — it failed on `would_merge > 0 AND merges_last_7d = 0`, and in the broken state both halves read zero. It hard-fails on a *blocked* engine, a stuck auto-eligible pair, the retired cron returning, or a broken probe (`would_merge` is **NULL, never 0**, when the dry run failed); the rotting-backlog *warning* is what would actually have surfaced this. **No sentinel here can assert the arms are well-specified** — that the 66 same-instant pairs were real took a human reading rows. Migrations `20270822093311`–`20270822093816`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/dedupEventArms.test.ts` + `eventMergeReversibility.test.ts`, both mutation-tested (11 mutations, all caught).

**Ten of twelve types auto-merged irreversibly, and Undo said otherwise (2026-09-12):** `grep "'schema', 1"` over the migration tree returned exactly TWO hits — `_venue_merge_core` and `_event_merge_core`. The other nine cores plus `merge_cities` recorded reparenting as COUNTS, which cannot be reversed, so nine of `unmerge_entities`' ten branches only cleared `duplicate_of_id` and left every child on the survivor — while returning `{"undone": true, "reparenting_restored": null}`, on which `AdminDuplicates.tsx` printed *"Merge undone"* under a header promising **every merge is reversible**. Measured on prod: marketplace 2,965 merges / 0 reversible, news 139/0, organization 86/0, personality 5/0, `city_merge_audit` 335 with **no `details` column at all**. **What those merges actually moved is NOT what it looks like, and a first draft of this entry asserted the wrong thing**: `marketplace_favorites` holds 1 row in the whole database, `marketplace_reviews` 0, `wishlist_items` 1 — **no user row has ever been moved by a merge.** The loss is PROVENANCE and TOPICAL LINKS (1,845 `marketplace_listing_sources`, 32 `news_article_cities`, 14 `news_stories_hero`, 29 `personality_relationships`); the user-data exposure is real the moment those tables fill, but it is a future risk, not a past harm, and conflating them overstates the incident. All ten cores now stamp `details.moved` + `schema:1` and `unmerge_entities`/`unmerge_cities` replay it; a pre-schema audit is REFUSED rather than half-undone (`p_force` gives the old flag-flip and reports `reparenting_restored:false`), and the 3,885 + 1,544 + 335 existing rows are all in that class and are never backfilled. `reparenting_restored` stopped being `case when r.entity_type='event' then v_restored else null end` — a null there is what let the UI treat "not applicable" and "did not happen" as one thing. **SIX child tables have no `id` column**, so `returning id` does not compile for them: `news_story_articles` (PK story_id,article_id), `user_news_reads` (user_id,article_id), `trip_group_links` (trip_id,group_id), `personality_internal_notes` (personality_id), `user_presence_location` and `user_travel_preferences` (user_id). Each records the half of the composite key the merge does NOT rewrite, and the restore is scoped `where <recorded> in (…) and <fk> = keep_id` so it cannot drag back a row that was always on the survivor — the `venue_personal_visits` trap from `20350101100000`, six more times, found by reading `pg_index` rather than the cores. **City is the largest and the only one that DESTROYS**: `merge_cities` overwrites `venues.city`/`events.city` TEXT before the ids move (restore writes `drop_name` back), rewrites `news_articles.city_ids` through `array_agg(distinct)` which COLLAPSES an article carrying both cities (so the ORIGINAL ARRAY is stored, not row ids — the one place reversing needs the old value rather than the old address), and `delete from news_article_cities where city_id = drop` destroys the losers outright (so whole rows are captured as jsonb and re-inserted). **`marketplace_listings_merchant_id_fkey` referenced the WRONG TABLE and that is why no marketplace merge could be undone**: it said `REFERENCES affiliate_partners` and was flagged `convalidated`, yet **69,737 of 69,737** rows resolve in `marketplace_merchants` and **zero** in `affiliate_partners` — merge succeeded, unmerge threw 23503, on **2,925 of the 2,965** merges on record, reproduced against the CURRENT unmodified functions so it was pre-existing. `affiliate_partners` is the TRAVEL registry (Booking.com, FlixBus, GetYourGuide) and 0 listings can match one, which is what made the repoint safe; fixed with the commit path in `20800301100000` (#3644), not here. **THE VERIFY BLOCK THAT ABORTED ITS OWN MIGRATION:** `20810101100100`'s guard searched for the literal `'v_pre_schema and not p_force'` after the condition had been restructured to the nested `if v_pre_schema then / if not p_force then` that `20270822093412` established — the function was correct, the guard was not, `db push` aborted on main and left the change HALF-APPLIED (cores recording, unmerge not replaying). **A check that encodes one PHRASING of a condition fails on a rewrite that preserves the CONDITION**; sweep every `position('X' in v_src)` needle against its own file's statements before merging. Migrations `29000101100000`–`29000101100200`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/mergeCoreReversibility.test.ts`, mutation-tested 14/14 — and **three of its assertions were VACUOUS on the first pass** because these migrations' own `do $verify$` blocks echo the strings being asserted, so `toContain` over the whole file passed with the real statement deleted (`statementsOf()` now cuts at `do $verify$`); two more read as "not caught" only because `perl` without `/g` mutated one of several occurrences.

**The dedup queue was a treadmill nobody could drain, and the pair was not decidable (2026-09-12):** 1,455 pairs open (venue 1,159 · event 128 · city 108 · personality 46 · milestone 12 · org 2), the venue arm pinned at its **200/night cap on 5 of the last 6 nights**, and **zero human decisions since 2026-08-17** — the 152 "approved" of 2026-08-03 and the 591 "rejected" of 2026-07-27 both carry `reviewer_id IS NULL` and were the autoapprove cron and a scripted bulk rejection. Both instrumented sentinels read `would_merge: 0`: the arms are SATURATED, not broken. **The reviewer could not decide the pair as presented.** `triage_src_dedup_review` emitted `q.entity_type` as `entity_table` — the SINGULAR `'venue'` — while `useTriageDetail.ts` allowlists PLURAL table names, so `validTables.includes('venue')` was false and the entity preview **never rendered once**; and the view exposes only `keep_id`, so even a fixed allowlist could load one side. Only venue and event ever had a cluster-side builder, so `city` and `personality` payloads were `{id,title}` and nothing else — `Ulm ⇄ Neu-Ulm` (two real towns 1.8 km apart) and `Søren Dahl ⇄ Soren Dahl` (the namesake/outing class) arrived as two names. The cluster was then dumped twice as `JSON.stringify`, by `FallbackPreview` and again by the Context section. `dedup_pair_side(type,id)` (SECURITY INVOKER — its event predecessor was DEFINER and leaked safety-gated events in the UAE and Malaysia, `20290601120731`) now resolves BOTH sides **in the view, not into the row**: writing them at sweep time would only help pairs queued from tomorrow, and the 1,455 already there are the backlog. City gets country/region/population/venue+event counts, personality gets `wikidata_qid`/`birth_date`/`death_date`/profession. The canonical flip was plumbed through `triage_action`'s `p_payload.keep_id` since `20260801050000` and through `useUnifiedTriageQueue` all along, but `TriageView.handleAction` never passed a payload — reachable from SQL and from the hook and from **no button**; `risk_flags.namesake` was likewise emitted for personalities and read by nothing. `src/hooks/useDedupReview.ts`'s `useDedupReviewQueue` — list, approve, reject, the flip, AND `approve_dedup_review_batch` — had **zero consumers** and was deleted; the batch RPC is still unexposed and recorded as such. Pairs render through `AdminCompareFrame`, which already existed, was tested, and was used by **nothing including the one route its own docblock names**. **A blank side is a GAP, not a conflict** — 6,049 of 26,688 live venues carry a blank-string address, and marking those rows conflicting makes the marker meaningless. **THE LADDER GAINED A THIRD EXIT.** It had merge-or-park, so a pair it had ALREADY judged distinct waited for a human forever; `run_dedup_close_distinct` (cron `dedup_close_distinct` `20 6 * * *`) closes them. It is narrow because hand-reading forced it to be, and each narrowing came from rows: diff city + diff street key + contradicting domain/phone was 143 pairs and **18/20** by hand; requiring a house number on BOTH sides (the junk address `Bars` turns into a bogus "different street") gave 132 and **20/22**; requiring the two cities **>25 km apart** gave 122 and cleared the last two, which were both a DISTRICT vs ITS PARENT CITY with the same address spelled differently (*DJ Station*, Bang Rak/Bangkok, "8/6-8 Si Lom 2" vs "8/6-8 Silom Road, Soi 2"; *Cabaretito Neón*, Cuauhtémoc/Mexico City). That veto removes exactly ten live pairs and **every one is that class**, including five the sample never reached (King's Arms London/Soho 1.2 km, Scottsdale/Phoenix 14.8 km); missing city coordinates FAIL SAFE. **A blanket close of the `identity_conflict` bucket would have been wrong** — 12 read at random gave ~7 distinct, ~3 real duplicates blocked by address-format noise, 2 ambiguous. It writes `status='rejected'` and NOT a new `'distinct'` status, deliberately: the sweep's memory is `status='rejected'` and the open-pair unique index only covers `WHERE status='open'`, so any other value is re-inserted as open by the next nightly run and the work repeats forever; machine closes stay distinguishable by `reviewer_id IS NULL` plus an `auto-distinct:` prefix carrying the evidence. First run closed **122** (1,455 → 1,333). **`dedup_signals(p_type)` + `dedup_signals_all()` cover all twelve types** — the other ten had no sentinel, so a mis-specified arm yielding `would_merge = 0` was undetectable there. The sweep probe is **opt-in**: twelve dry runs measured **34.6 s** (venue 10.6, event 9.7, marketplace 6.3) and no PostgREST call survives that, so `dedup_signals_all()` passes `p_probe => false` and reports `dry_run_error: 'not probed'` rather than a reassuring zero. **It adds the gate the existing two would have MISSED**: they fail on `would_merge > 0 AND merges_7d = 0`, and in this incident BOTH halves read zero — the drain rate does not (`opened_7d` 1163, `decisions_7d` 532 all machine, `human_decisions_7d` **0**). `triage_sources.capabilities.external_console` still pointed at `/admin/duplicates?view=suggested`, deleted in `20260801050000`, and was removed — dedup is decided in the inbox. **`triage_src_dedup_review.title` read the STORED cluster and the sweep is not its only writer**: hand-queued repairs store their own shapes with no `keep`/`drop` objects, so 9 rows rendered **`? ⇄ ?`** — precisely the pairs someone had already investigated far enough to write a paragraph into `reason`, and the list row is how a reviewer chooses what to open. Found by reading the DEPLOYED result on prod, not in review. Migrations `29000101100300`–`29000101100500` + `35000101100000`.

**News dedup is fully automatic (2026-08-09):** news auto-merges or drops — it can **never** enter `dedup_review_queue` or the inbox, in any mode (two guards in `run_dedup_truth_sweep`; `triage_src_dedup_review` excludes `entity_type='news'`). Reason: the old `key+source+day` gate queued 406 news pairs in one week and **not one was a duplicate** — `source_id` is the FEED, not the publisher, so aggregator feeds made cross-outlet pairs look same-source and they were queued at 0.97 `auto_eligible`; all 406 were bulk-rejected 2026-08-01. `20260806140000`'s fix (require identical URL) over-corrected into unreachable, because `news_articles.fingerprint` is UNIQUE. The gate is now same source + same day + same despaced title **AND a byte-identical body** (≥32 chars) or identical `canonical_url`, with **no 90-day window** — all 17 real duplicates were Feb–Apr 2026. **`image_hash` and `excerpt` were measured and rejected as identity arms** (5,120 rows share an image_hash, one image spans 104 articles). First run: 12 merged, 0 queued, 0 cross-source. **Multi-outlet coverage is NOT a duplicate** — it belongs to `news_stories`; merging it deletes journalism (one live story legitimately has 84 outlets). Migration `20260809100000`.

**News story clustering v2 (2026-08-09):** `cluster_news_article` matched within ±7 days of `s.last_updated_at`, which is bumped to each new member — so a story **walked forward a week at a time forever** while its averaged centroid went generic and became an attractor ("Results", 40 articles over 54 days, containing `i570`/`SD 518`/Charli XCX). Every runaway sat at avg similarity 0.847–0.856, barely over the 0.82 bar; correct stories sit at 0.87–0.95 — **drift, not the threshold, builds junk drawers**. Now: window anchored to `first_seen_at`, hard **14-day span cap**, and a candidate must also clear the bar against the story's **hero** embedding, not just the centroid. Threshold **0.82 → 0.78**, but the 0.78–0.82 band additionally requires a shared `dedup_core_tokens` term (which strips gay/lgbt/lgbtq/queer, so the guard isn't satisfied by this corpus's universal vocabulary). `cluster_news_backfill(p_limit, p_days, p_oldest_first)` — **`p_days => NULL` means no lower bound**; the old 2-arg signature had to be DROPped or the existing cron call would 42725. New crons `news_story_backfill` (`*/10`, oldest-first drain of the 20,937 never-clustered articles) and `news_story_resplit` (`40 3 * * *`, dissolves any over-span story and re-clusters its members). First resplit: 15 dissolved, 396 re-clustered, over-span 15 → 0, biggest story 94 → 84. Migration `20260809110000`.

**`news_articles.content_hash` was 93% stale (2026-08-09):** written once at INSERT by `news_commit_staging_batch` and never recomputed, while `news_resanitize` (*/5) and `news_fulltext_backfill` (*/10) rewrite `content` continuously — 35,781 of 38,299 rows disagreed with their own body. It is now derived by BEFORE trigger `news_articles_zz_content_hash` (the `zz_` prefix is load-bearing: BEFORE triggers fire in NAME order and `news_articles_decode_entities` rewrites both inputs). **Deliberately not backfilled** — `trg_search_documents_news` fires on every UPDATE and the column has zero readers; rows self-heal as the re-sanitize crons touch them. Never use it as a merge key. Migration `20260809120000`.

**Podcast AUDIO was stored as article artwork — 5,607 rows, and the repair inverts for half of them (2026-09-08):** `extractMediaUrl` in `source-rss-news/rss-parse.ts` fell back to the first `<enclosure url="...">` with **no type check**, and on a podcast item the enclosure IS the audio. Both call sites reached it: the news branch unconditionally, and the podcast branch whenever an item carried no `itunes:image`/`media:thumbnail` — which is *most* episodes, because shows overwhelmingly declare artwork at CHANNEL level and the parser never read it. **The harm is not only the browser's torn-page glyph:** `functions/_lib/detail.ts` feeds news `image_url` into the crawler `<head>`, and **2,405 of the 5,607 were `seo_indexable`**, i.e. publishing an MP3 as `og:image`. **The detector already existed and nothing acted on it** — `probeImage` (`_shared/news-quality/image-check.ts`) returns `reason:'not_image'` on content-type and `news-quality-backfill` feeds it to the publish gate, so the platform had been correctly identifying these for months and only ever *blocked auto-publish*, never cleared the column. **Measuring before writing the repair is what saved it:** the rows split three ways, and the briefed one-line `SET image_url = NULL` would have destroyed data. **Cohort A — 2,865 rows** (`media_type='article'`, **`audio_url IS NULL`**, created 2026-03-06 → 2026-07-29, 2,857 on `feed_type='podcast'` sources) straddle `20260623063103` (podcast support): they were ingested through the news branch before `audio_url`/`media_type` existed, so **`image_url` is the only surviving copy of the episode's audio** — they are MIGRATED (`audio_url := image_url`, `media_type := 'podcast'`), not cleared. `media_type` is flipped too because the player and every podcast-filtered surface key off it, so recovering `audio_url` alone stores the URL somewhere nothing reads. **Cohort B — 2,742** already have `audio_url` (2,741 byte-equal to `image_url`): plain NULL. **Cohort C — 6** `.mp4` on *news* sites: plain NULL, `news_articles` has no video column. Dry run on prod in a rolled-back txn: 5,607 → 0, podcasts 5,461 → 8,318, and `podcasts_with_audio` == `podcasts_after`. **The widened check came back clean on `.pdf`/`.zip`** (mp3 5,548 · m4a 52 · wav 1 · mp4 6) — but it also found `aspx` 20, `axd` 7, `php` 4, `cms` 3, which are **legitimate dynamic image endpoints**, so `public.is_non_image_url()` is a **DENYLIST**; an image-extension allowlist would have deleted 34 working images (plus 92 `.svg` and 83 `.avif`). It tests the **URL PATH ONLY** (`split_part` on `#` then `?`) because matching the whole URL false-positives on `…/photo.jpg?meta=x.json`. **`image_asset_links` is a SECOND surface** — `tg_news_articles_sync_image_assets` fires on every `image_url` write, so the registry held **5,708 audio/video assets over 5,715 links** (more than the article count, because an article whose image later changed left its old asset behind) and renders independently of the column; links deleted, assets flagged not dropped. Sealed in three places because `image_url` has several writers: the parser, and a **BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger** `news_articles_zz_reject_non_image_url` (`zz_` is load-bearing — BEFORE triggers fire in NAME order and `news_articles_decode_entities` rewrites `image_url`, so it must test the final value; sorts after `zz_content_hash`, `'c' < 'r'`). Podcast episodes now inherit show artwork via `extractChannelImage`, **scoped to the text before the first `<item>`** — unscoped it finds the FIRST episode's art and stamps it on every other episode, wrong for exactly the feeds that publish per-episode art; `itunes:image` only, never RSS `<image><url>` (the site logo, historically 88×31). Cached on `news_sources.artwork_url` (refresh-on-success, **never clobber a stored value with null**) and drained by cron `news_podcast_artwork_fill` — **a cron, not a one-shot, because `artwork_url` is empty until each source next runs**, so a one-shot in the migration would update zero rows and report success. Sentinel `news_image_signals()` is **standalone, not a new `pipeline_hygiene_stats()` key** (restating that body is a merge-collision surface) and reports `trigger_attached` separately from the count, because an absent seal and a clean corpus give the same zero. Migrations `20361118143700`/`20361118143800`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/newsNonImageUrlSeal.test.ts` (mutation-tested — and the first draft's artwork-guard assertion was **vacuous**: the guard appears twice and a bare `toMatch` found the reporting copy while the load-bearing one in the UPDATE's CTE was deleted).

**Content versioning + the live↔CMS switcher (2026-09-13):** every registry content type now records every write, and an admin can cross between a public page and the CMS in both directions.

**Two versioning tables existed before this and NEITHER worked.** `admin_edit_log`: **0 rows in its entire life** — RLS enabled, one policy (`Admin read edit log`, SELECT only), **no INSERT policy**, so every insert from `useInlineSave` was denied and swallowed by a bare `catch {}`; the alt-click inline edit, the path an admin actually uses on a public page, recorded nothing and nothing said so. `cms_revisions`: **28 rows over seven months across 4 of 26 content types, `max(revision_number) = 1` on every one** — written client-side, fire-and-forget in a `void (async …)` whose catch only console.errors, read-then-increment against a UNIQUE index (a race is a swallowed 23505), snapshotting the CLIENT's form state rather than the stored row. `useCMSRevisions.restoreRevision`/`.diffRevisions` had **zero callers**. The gap was already named in `20270301100400_external_correction_audit.sql`. Both client paths and `useCMSRevisions` are deleted — two writers is how rows get silently lost.

**The replacement is a trigger** (`log_content_revision`, modelled on `log_unified_tag_change`, the one working audit in this schema) writing `content_revisions`, attached to all 25 tables in `content_versioned_tables`. **It records machine writes too** — a deliberate choice, and affordable ONLY because of two things. (1) **Changed-columns delta, never a whole-row snapshot**: measured, ~5,400 content rows are touched per day and the wide tables average 4.3–4.6 KB/row (venues has 90 columns), so whole-row before+after would be ~45 MB/day ≈ **16 GB/yr against a 12 GB database**; the delta is ~573 bytes/revision all-in ≈ 1.2 GB/yr. (2) **A retention horizon on machine revisions only** — `run_content_revision_prune` (cron `content_revision_prune`, `20 2 * * *`) drops `actor_kind='system'` rows older than 90 days and NEVER touches `human`/`declared`, giving a steady state near 290 MB. That horizon is the one place full coverage is traded away.

**Traps, each found by measuring rather than reasoning:** (a) **`now()` is TRANSACTION time**, so every revision written by one statement shares a `created_at` and "newest first" over a tie is whatever the planner chose — order by **`seq`** (a monotonic identity), never the timestamp. (b) **Generated columns are real on the hot tables** — `marketplace_listings` has FIVE (`title_normalized`, `subcategory_slug`, `boutique_score`, `brand_key`, `content_rating`) and is the highest-volume table in the registry; `cities.canonical_key` and `personalities.name_initial` one each. They are derived, so recording them stores the same thing twice and no revert can write one back; they are excluded at capture time, read from the catalog rather than hand-listed. (c) **`community_submissions` (the `feedback` type) has NO `updated_at` column at all** — nothing here may assume one. (d) Measured cost: **+0.34 ms/row** (a 300-row `events` UPDATE went 243 → 345 ms with caches warmed). pg_cron runs at a 2 min statement timeout and the largest registered batch is `event_series_recompute(4000)`, so the margin is wide; PostgREST is 8s for single-row writes.

**The kill switch is `content_versioned_tables.enabled`, a COLUMN, not `ALTER TABLE … DISABLE TRIGGER`** — one UPDATE, no DDL, no lock, effective on the next statement. Disabling a trigger takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE, and taking that on a 370 MB table during an incident is its own outage. (`CREATE TRIGGER` needs the same lock, which is why the attach migration sets `lock_timeout = '5s'` and fails rather than queueing.)

**Revert is PER FIELD** (`content_revision_revert_fields(p_revision_id, p_fields)`), never whole-row: content rows are continuously enriched by crons, so restoring a whole snapshot would undo every enrichment since. It refuses a table absent from the allowlist, a column that is generated/identity, and — the load-bearing one — **a field whose live value has moved since the revision**, reporting which and why (`rollback_external_correction_batch`'s skip discipline). It RAISEs rather than swallowing into `RETURN FALSE`, which is `revert_content_change`'s mistake. All accepted fields are put back in ONE statement, so an undo is one entry in the trail, attributed `human` / `admin:revert`.

**Sentinel `content_revision_signals()`** (in `check-pipeline-health.mjs`) reports trigger ATTACHMENT separately from revision VOLUME, because an unattached trigger and a quiet week both produce zero and conflating them is how a dead trail reads as a clean one — the `accessibility_contradictions` lesson. Hard-fails on an enabled table with no trigger, a trigger disabled behind the registry's back, a generated column that escaped an ignore list, and a live trail with no prune cron.

**The switcher.** The blocker was structural: **there is no `/admin/content/:type/:id` route** — the record editor is a modal owned by `AdminShell` (`openEditor`) — so a URL that opens a record has to be a query param. `?edit=` existed for ONE type, hand-rolled in `PersonalitiesAdmin`; it moved into `ContentListPanel`, so every registry type is deep-linkable. `src/lib/cmsLinks.ts` is the one module that knows both directions and **returns `null` rather than guessing** (a fabricated URL is a 404 the caller cannot tell from a real page). Measured, not assumed: **tags are SLUG-keyed** (the route param is merely NAMED `tagName`; `fetchTagWithCategories` resolves against `slug`, lowercased, `status='active'`), **groups are ID-keyed**. Public→CMS is an "Open in CMS" item in the `AdminEditButton` dropdown on **14 surfaces**; `marketplace_brands` is absent because `get_marketplace_brand` does not return `id`, and `cms_pages` because it has 0 rows and its hero tests render without providers — both stay editable from the CMS list, which gained a **View live** row action (and the editor header a **View live** button). Inline editing used to be reachable ONLY by holding Alt, which nothing advertised: `useAdminEditMode` now exposes `editMode = altHeld || pinned` with a visible toggle, and **the `isAdmin &&` guard on the exposed value is load-bearing** — it covers a role revoked mid-session, which the mount-time restore cannot see (`useAdminRoles` re-runs on Supabase `TOKEN_REFRESHED` churn). **An admin control belongs on the COMPOSITION layer, not inside a presentational hero** — `MilestoneHero`/`OrgHero` tests render with no providers, and pulling `useAuth` into one turns a design-invariant test into an auth-context test; the button lives in the descriptor adapters instead. Migrations `50010101100000`–`50010101100300`.

**Profile settings (redesigned, 2026-06-11):** `/profile/settings` is a hub-and-sheets page (no tabs): live `IdentityPreviewCard` (tap-to-edit + public/community/private lens) + ONE gap-driven prompt card (username claim > default avatar > pronouns) + summary cards opening bottom `Sheet` editors + review-only `PreferencesMirrorCard` (prefs are CAPTURED in context — `TravelPrefsPrompt` sheet on /trips, vibes in `/onboarding/search`; settings only clears). **Username v2** (migration `20260612160000`): mandatory rollout, 3-20 lowercase `a-z 0-9 . _`, `username_key` (separators stripped) blocks lookalike-handle impersonation, `reserved_usernames` (routes/brand/impersonation only — deliberately NO reclaimed-identity terms), `change_username` RPC (claim free; once/12mo + 90-day `username_redirects`; auto-assigned handles get one free change), `admin_change_username(.., p_with_redirect=false)` safety fast-track gives NO redirect (deadname linkability is the threat), `auto_assign_usernames` registered DISABLED in `admin_automations` — enable at T+60. **Avatar mandatory-without-walls**: deterministic neutral builder-config backfill (`20260612160200`, `avatar_auto_assigned` flag → settings nudge), 3-path `AvatarChooser`: upload (react-easy-crop → 512px webp client-side), unavatar import via `workers/image-cdn` `POST /avatar/resolve` (JWT-gated proxy → R2 `avatars/unavatar/`, client NEVER talks to unavatar.io; gravatar/email source behind explicit consent checkbox), simplified 4-choice builder (full 16-control `AvatarBuilder` UI no longer reachable from settings; `AvatarSettings` deleted). **Pronouns**: ordered `pronoun_tags text[]` is the source of truth; `pronouns` text stays the derived display string so render sites are untouched (display rule: join first segments → "she/they"); per-field visibility `privacy_settings.pronouns_visibility`. **Occupation**: `profession-autocomplete` against the shared `professions` vocab; free text stored AS-IS, never auto-normalized (`occupation_freetext_candidates` view, service_role-only, manual promotion only). **Personal documents deprecated**: settings shows removal notice (T+30 = 2026-07-11), `DocumentsList readOnly` blocks new personal uploads; final deletion `scripts/data-quality/delete-personal-documents.mjs` (storage objects FIRST, then rows, verified, supports --dry-run); trip-attached docs unaffected. **Accessibility payoff**: `AmenityDisplay` shows "✓ matches your needs" badges (`travel_preferences.accessibility_needs` ∩ venue `accessibility_attributes`, mapping in `src/lib/accessibilityNeeds.ts`); unlisted needs render as honest absence-of-data; needs are never public.

**Safety layer (high-risk gating, 2026-06-23):** Venues, events and organizations in **high-risk countries** (criminalizing — `lgbti_criminalization.legal=false` — or death-penalty) are visible **only to logged-in users**; country/city pages stay public. The *geographic* threshold lives in one pure-SQL predicate `location_is_high_risk(country_id, city_id)` (resolves country via city when `country_id` is null), mirroring `src/hooks/useTripSafety.ts` / `src/lib/lgbtLegality.ts`. Each entity carries a denormalized `safety_gated boolean` (BEFORE-trigger on `country_id`/`city_id` change; `recompute_safety_gated_for_country()` fires from a trigger on `countries.lgbti_criminalization` — re-run by the nightly ILGA cron). **`safety_gated` is NOT `location_is_high_risk()` — on venues it is TWO terms, and this sentence said otherwise until 2026-09-10:**

```
venues.safety_gated = location_is_high_risk(country_id, city_id) OR category = 'cruising'
```

`20261112100000` added the second term: a cruising venue is gated in **every** country, regardless of local law, because publishing a cruising location to anonymous visitors is an outing/safety exposure on its own. 122 of the ~1,346 gated venues are gated **only** by that term, and they cluster in the safest countries (Spain 36, Germany 21, Italy 10, UK 7) — so a country-only reading of the column looks exactly like drift in liberal Europe and is not. **Never reconstruct `safety_gated` from a predicate you inferred; read the BEFORE trigger, or `release_gate_checks().city_safety_gate_drift`, which encodes the real rule and is `critical`.** A 2026-09-10 "repair" that recomputed the column from `location_is_high_risk()` alone ungated 106 cruising venues into anon search for ~40 minutes; its own fail-open guard passed because it was built from the same wrong predicate (`20260910143903`, reverted by `20260910145524`). **Two enforcement surfaces:** (1) **RLS** on venues/events/organizations — `USING (NOT safety_gated OR (select auth.uid()) IS NOT NULL)` — covers all direct PostgREST reads + the SECURITY-INVOKER `search_events`; the SECURITY-DEFINER `rpc_venues_ranked` gates internally on `auth.uid()`. (2) **Search-proxy worker** (service key + SECURITY DEFINER bypass RLS, and its body `user_id` is spoofable) — so `search_documents` gets its own `safety_gated` (kept in sync by a BEFORE trigger mirroring the entity, indexers untouched) and the discovery RPCs exclude gated rows unless told otherwise: `search_hybrid`/`search_facets` via `p_filters.include_gated`, `search_autocomplete`/`get_recommendations`/`related_entities` via a new `p_include_gated` param, `events_in_window`/`get_trending_entities`/`v_popular_entities` always exclude gated. The worker (`workers/search-proxy/src/jwt.ts`) verifies the caller's Supabase JWT (**fail-closed**) and only then passes `include_gated=true`; `src/lib/searchFetch.ts` attaches the access token. Verification needs **no new secret**: offline HS256 when `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET` is set (zero-latency, preferred), else a GoTrue `/auth/v1/user` check using the worker's existing `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` (positive results KV-cached 60s). **Anon UX:** `GatedContentNotice` ("Sign in to view N places") on city/country pages via anon-safe count RPC `gated_count_for_location` (counts only); direct links to a gated detail page show `GatedDetailFallback` (sign-in gate vs 404 via `gated_entity_exists`). Gated entities become non-indexable to anon crawlers — intended. Migrations `20260623160000`–`20260623160002`.

**Design & Branding Control Center (live runtime theming, 2026-07-23):** `/admin/design` manages the entire design system + brand identity with **live apply, no deploy**. Single-row `site_branding` table (migration `20260723174925`) holds a **sparse jsonb override doc** (`draft` + `published` + `site_branding_versions` history capped 50; empty doc = stock site byte-for-byte). Sections: `tokens` (light/dark HSL channel triples for the 44 color vars + `global` radius trio / `--text-*` scale / tracking / transition), `meta` (site_name, default title/description, twitter handle, OG image, theme-colors, JSON-LD org logo+sameAs), `manifest` (name/short_name/colors), `email` (from + wrapper). Writes ONLY via admin-gated SECURITY DEFINER RPCs `branding_save_draft`/`branding_publish`/`branding_revert`/`branding_set_enabled`, all validated by `branding_validate` (hard key whitelist + strict value regexes, ≤150 keys ≤32 KB). **Delivery is edge-injected:** `functions/_lib/branding.ts` (60s in-isolate memo, fail-open to stock, kill-switch `overrides_enabled`) → `functions/_middleware.ts` appends `<style id="brand-overrides">` at the END of `<head>` (wins the custom-property cascade after the Vite CSS link) + DB-driven og:site_name / twitter:site / default OG / theme-color / JSON-LD; `functions/manifest.json.ts` overlays the static manifest. Compose-time re-filter = defense vs rows edited outside RPCs. Email: `supabase/functions/_shared/branding.ts` (`fromHeader`/`wrapHtml`) consumed by `send-templated-email`. Frontend catalog `src/components/admin/design/tokenCatalog.ts` mirrors `src/index.css` — guarded by a drift unit test that parses the stylesheet; WCAG math in `src/lib/wcagContrast.ts`. Brand assets upload to public storage bucket `brand` (admin-write, 2 MB, image mimes — sanctioned exception to the images-on-R2 convention). Audit artifact `public/design-audit.json` regenerated by `node scripts/design-audit.mjs` (token usage counts, unused tokens, lint suppressions, doc drift). ESLint: `src/components/admin/design/**` has its own no-restricted-syntax block (hex literals allowed — it edits colors; the rest of the admin design rules re-stated). Staleness after publish ≤60s (+5 min on edge-cached detail pages).

**Content Graph explorer (admin ontology viz, 2026-07-24):** `/admin/graph` (Cockpit → Content Graph) visualizes how the ~17 content types + attribute layers link. **Macro** = a React Flow ontology map (`src/components/admin/content-graph/OntologyMap.tsx` + custom `TypeNode`): one node per type sized by row count, badged with quality counts (orphans, dup clusters); each edge is a relationship *class* (geo/structural FK, tag assignment, tag ontology, cross-entity, media) whose thickness = real link count and whose **stroke dash — never hue** — encodes the class (monochrome; `edgeDash`/`GRAPH_STROKE` from the allowlisted `chartPalette`). **Micro** = click a type → `GraphDetailPanel` (counts + orphan/dup badges + deep links to the admin list / `/admin/duplicates`) with a record search → structural instance ego graph (`EntityEgoExplorer` reuses the public `EgoGraph`/`EntityNode`, fed by `useAdminEgoNetwork`). **Data is a nightly snapshot, not live scans** (disk-constrained DB): pure-SQL `run_content_graph_recompute()` writes a jsonb `{nodes,edges}` doc into singleton `content_graph_stats` (registered in `admin_automations` + pg_cron `20 4 * * *`); admin-gated read RPCs `admin_content_graph()` (returns the snapshot) + `admin_entity_neighbors(p_type,p_id)` (structural neighbours — distinct from the semantic `related_entities`). `content_graph_norm_type()` normalizes dirty polymorphic `entity_type` strings. Migration `20260724101247_content_graph.sql`; e2e `e2e/content-graph.spec.ts`.

**Geo Hierarchy Unification (staged, P0+P1 live 2026-07-25):** the geographic taxonomy (continents → regions → countries → cities → queer_villages → landmarks) is unified on a **`geo_places` spine** (class-table inheritance: narrow spine row per node with `place_type` + `parent_id`/`parent_type` composite FK + per-type hierarchy CHECKs + trigger-derived `country_id`/`city_id`; type-specific columns in `geo_country_profiles`/`geo_city_profiles`/`geo_village_profiles`/`geo_landmark_profiles`). **UUIDs are preserved** from the typed tables, which remain the system of record during the strangler phases — dual-write triggers (`trg_sync_geo_spine`, SECURITY DEFINER) mirror every write; nightly `geo_spine_drift_check()` (cron `40 4 * * *`, `geo_spine_drift_log`) must show a clean week before P2 (FK flips) → P3 (engine/RPC rewrites, golden-set `safety_gated` parity gate) → P4 (table→view swaps) → P5 (client migration) proceed — full plan in `docs/plans/2026-07-25-geo-hierarchy-unification.md`. **Landmarks (parks/beaches/monuments/…) are a first-class type living natively on the spine only**: `landmark_kind` on the profile, parent = city or village, `safety_gated` computed in the derive trigger via `location_is_high_risk` (RLS + search mirror like venues), search indexer `search_documents_index_landmarks` (rows with `needs_review=true` — e.g. seeds from the legacy `notable_landmarks text[]` — are excluded from search + public pages until an admin approves). RPCs: `get_geo_breadcrumbs` (anon; powers breadcrumb JSON-LD), `get_geo_children` (admin lazy tree + entity counts), `geo_move_node` (re-parents via the typed tables and repairs denormalized country FKs on descendants/venues/events/hotels), `geo_integrity_violations` view (56 legacy FK mismatches repaired 2026-07-25, 0 remain). Surfaces: `/admin/geography` (tree + move/merge + landmark CRUD + integrity tab; `src/pages/admin/AdminGeography.tsx`), `/place/:slug` (`PlaceDetail.tsx`), `CityLandmarksRail` on city pages, `sitemap-landmarks.xml`; data hooks live in `src/hooks/useGeoPlaces.ts` (lint rule: `supabase.from()` only in `src/hooks/`).

**Unified Guides family (editorial consolidation, 2026-07-25):** ONE content family replaced five parallel editorial systems (marketplace_guides / venue_guides / event_guides quartets, quests, /places editorial_rails): `guides` (`format IN ('guide','list','quest')`) + polymorphic `guide_picks` (**search_documents entity vocab** — venue/event/marketplace/city/country/queer_village/…; `is_orphaned` tombstones; no FKs) + `guide_sections` / `guide_reads` (merged reading streak) / quest module `guide_participations`+`guide_contributions` / `guide_slug_redirects`. **Quest lifecycle is DERIVED from the publish window** (published ∧ now vs starts_at/ends_at → scheduled/active/completed), not a status machine. Pick integrity = nightly `guide_picks_maintain()` janitor (repoints merged targets via `duplicate_of_id`, tombstones deleted ones; `admin_automations` + pg_cron `40 4 * * *`) — the 12 dedup merge cores were deliberately NOT patched. Public: `/guides` hub + `/guides/:slug` (`src/pages/Guides.tsx` / `GuideDetail.tsx`; picks hydrated per-type via `src/lib/guidePickAdapters.ts` under entity RLS so safety-gated picks self-filter for anon); legacy routes (`/venues/guides*`, `/events/guides*`, `/marketplace/guides*`, `/quests*`) redirect; embedded rails = `<GuidesRail>` + `<FeaturedInGuides>` (`src/components/guides/`); `/places` EditorRail reads `format='list'`. Header/mobile nav "Guides". Guides are search-indexed (`search_documents_index_guides`, sync trigger **excludes pick_count/updated_at** to avoid the statement-level pick_count trigger looping a reindex) and safety-gated (city→country criminalization; satellite RLS embeds the FULL parent predicate). RPCs: `guides_recommend` (new name — old `recommend_guides` return type differs), `guide_reading_streak`, `active_quest_guide()` (**no-match serializes as an all-null composite row** — clients must check `row.id`), ported `quest_progress`/`quest_public_contributors`/`quest_create_recap_stub`, `resolve_guide_slug`. Admin: registry type `guides` → `/admin/content/guides` (CMS editor + Picks/Sections/Quest `extraPanels`, `src/components/admin/guides/`); edge fn `guide-draft` (replaced marketplace-/venue-guide-draft + both recommend fns — hooks call the RPC directly). `editorial_covers` + `editorial_drafts` (+ `editorial_hook`/`editorial_long` columns) stayed — entity enrichment, not documents. Migrations `20260726150000`–`151000` (schema/RPCs/backfill-UUIDs-preserved/triggers+search/freeze-legacy/grants — **new tables need explicit anon GRANTs in this project**). Legacy tables were dropped after soak by `20260801000000_unified_guides_drop_legacy` and the 4 orphaned deployed fns are gone (verified 2026-08-01: only `guide-draft` + `recommendation-engine` remain).

**LLM provider chain — NVIDIA first, Cloudflare underneath (2026-08-29):** every edge-function text-chat call now tries NVIDIA NIM (`https://integrate.api.nvidia.com/v1`, OpenAI-compatible, free tier) before Cloudflare Workers AI. `_shared/llm-router.ts`'s `tryNvidia()` sits under BOTH clients — `chatCompletion` (openai-client.ts, native `/ai/run`) and `llmChatCompletion` (llm-client.ts, compat `/ai/v1`, and therefore the whole `anthropicMessages` shim) — so ~40 edge functions were covered with no change to their LLM calls (the only call-site edits are the eleven `callerFn:` additions described below). The router owns only the NVIDIA attempt: each client keeps its own Cloudflare transport untouched, because the two are not interchangeable (the compat endpoint hangs 25s on some models, which is why the native one exists) and because that is what makes the rollback free — **with `NVIDIA_API_KEY` unset the router returns before doing any work and the outgoing request is byte-identical to before**. `NVIDIA_DISABLED=1` is the instant off switch; `NVIDIA_EXCLUDE_CALLERS` is a per-`callerFn` opt-out (**`translate-i18n-batch` ONLY as of 2026-09-03, so every residency-sensitive caller stays in scope**; it had silently held all 13 interactive callers, narrowing the documented decision to batch pipelines only, and **a secret's value cannot be read back** — `secrets list` shows only `sha256(value)`, so verify by hashing the value you intended and comparing, never by assuming and never by inferring from traffic. **One badly-fitted caller can take the whole provider down, and that is why the one exclusion exists**: `translate-i18n-batch` failed **3 of 7 calls on 45s timeouts (43%)** against **1.6% for the other eight callers**, and because it fires as an on-the-hour burst it spent all three breaker failures inside two minutes — NVIDIA then served **zero** calls for the full 900s reset, **15 minutes in every hour**, for the callers that were fine. Its 4,000-token batches are simply too large for this tier's latency; excluding it costs ~8 calls/hour and recovers ~61. See `docs/dependency-audit/data-flow-map.md`); `AI_DISABLED=1` remains the global hard stop above all of it. **This is the repo's FIRST real provider chain** — `USE_OPENAI`/`USE_ANTHROPIC` are binary switches where a failed backend simply throws — so the failure classification is the load-bearing part and has no precedent to inherit: **a 429 is NOT a breaker failure** (it means "too fast", and filing it as "broken" would trip the circuit on any burst and disable the provider for the whole reset window — same lesson as the pg_net reaper's `timed_out`), 401/403 and any other non-429 4xx trip `llm.nvidia`, 5xx gets exactly one retry. NVIDIA does not document its credit-exhaustion status, so that arm is deliberately wide and the response body is **printed by the router** — `[llm-router] nvidia <kind> for <caller> model=<id> status=<n>: <body>` — so **read the edge-function logs before narrowing the classifier**. It is NOT on the breaker row: **`api_circuit_breakers` has no `last_error` column**, so recording a failure keeps the COUNT and destroys the REASON. That cost two separate blind stretches, both from a name that was accepted and dropped: `breakerFailure` passed `p_error_msg` to an RPC whose argument is named otherwise (**PostgREST resolves overloads BY ARGUMENT NAME and answers a mismatch with a silent PGRST202 404**, so for three days *no* failure was recorded and the breaker could not trip), and `check-pipeline-health.mjs` asked for `select=…,last_error` on that same table, drew `400 42703` on every run, and so reported the chain healthy while the circuit was open. **Verify a column exists before selecting it, and make a failed probe SAY so** instead of falling through to null. **Rate limiting is in Postgres, not in-process**: the ~40 RPM ceiling is an ACCOUNT limit while edge functions are stateless and horizontally scaled, so `llm_provider_rate` + `llm_rate_acquire()` implement a leaky bucket with the availability test inside the `UPDATE ... WHERE` (the EvalPlanQual trick from `llm_budget_consume`). Seeded at **32, not 40** — a 429 reaching the router at all means `rpm_cap` is too high. A bucket, never a per-minute window: a fixed window allows 40 in the last second of one minute and 40 in the first of the next. **Pacing is opt-in via a `BATCH_CALLERS` ALLOWLIST, and the inversion is load-bearing** — anything unrecognised waits **0 ms** and falls back instantly, because pacing a request a human is sitting in front of is just a queue in front of the user; mutation-testing pacing onto an interactive caller made a single `trip-concierge` call take **61 s**. **It was first written as an `INTERACTIVE_CALLERS` denylist and that was wrong in a way its own test could not see**: the eleven shim callers reach the router through `anthropicMessages` → `llmAnthropicStyle`, which **silently dropped `callerFn`**, so every trip flow arrived as the fallback string `'llmChatCompletion'`, matched no entry, and would have paced. The test passed because it called `tryNvidia` with `callerFn: 'trip-concierge'` — a string that existed only in the test. `callerFn` is threaded through the shim now (which also closes a pre-existing hole: the whole trip surface, `generate-usernames` and `translate-i18n-batch` had been logging spend anonymously for as long as `llm_call_log` has existed, invisible to `llm-caller-attribution.test.ts` because its regex looks for `chatCompletion(`/`llmChatCompletion(` and these files call `anthropicMessages(`), but the allowlist stays inverted regardless: getting an entry wrong costs money in one direction and user experience in the other, and those are not equal. Batch callers wait `NVIDIA_MAX_WAIT_MS` (2000) and `enrichment-driver` publishes its `wallClockMs` via `setInvocationDeadline()` so pacing can never sleep past the 546s edge wall — the between-waves check alone is not enough, since it bounds when a wave STARTS while pacing adds latency inside a running one. **NVIDIA cannot go through AI Gateway** (24 providers, NVIDIA is not one, no universal passthrough), so `llm_call_log.provider` is the only record the path ran; `cost_usd` stays **NULL** for it per llm-cost.ts's null contract — an invented zero is indistinguishable from "this model is free". `_shared/pg-rpc.ts` exists so the router reaches Postgres without supabase-js: llm-client.ts takes no client and is imported by nearly every edge function, so pulling the SDK down that path would add it to every cold start (same reasoning as llm-usage-log.ts). **If the rate RPC is missing the router falls back rather than running unmetered** — so a function deployed ahead of its migration silently uses no NVIDIA at all until the migration lands. **Model ids: `meta/llama-3.1-8b-instruct` and `meta/llama-3.3-70b-instruct` are NOT in the catalogue** despite every blog post naming them — re-read `/v1/models` (unauthenticated) rather than trusting memory. **Being IN the catalogue is not enough either**: `/v1/models` lists 82 ids but only the **39 badged "Free Endpoint"** in the build.nvidia.com UI are callable on this account, and the rest 404 with a body naming an internal function UUID and never the model — which is how `nemotron-nano-3-30b-a3b` shipped as the default tier and 404'd (correlation verified against the UI, 2026-09-02; signing in changes nothing). Defaults are now `nvidia/nemotron-3.5-lightning-30b-a3b` / `nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b`, both env-overridable. **EVERY reachable model here is a reasoning model, and left alone they narrate instead of answering** — `ai-enrichment.ts` prompts demand bare JSON, the model replies *"Here's a thinking process: 1. **Analyze User Request**…"*, and `parseAIResponse`'s three-stage extraction finds no JSON at all. **The fix is one request field, `chat_template_kwargs: {thinking: false}`**, sent on every call. Measured on the real 1.8k-token prompt: lightning went **900 tokens / 29s / no JSON → 169 tokens / 3s / valid JSON**; super went 540/5s → 183/3s. It is sent unconditionally rather than to nemotron ids only, because the two failure directions are not symmetric — omitting it yields an HTTP 200 that costs full price, returns prose and is recorded as a **success**, while a model that rejects the field returns 4xx, trips the breaker, gets logged and falls back. `openai/gpt-oss-{20b,120b}` accept it as a no-op but are **rejected on latency, not quality** (15–35s against the 45s ceiling). **Do NOT reach for `response_format: json_object` instead** — measured, `gpt-oss-20b` TIMED OUT with it on a prompt it answered in 256 tokens without it, the same failure Cloudflare has. **The dangerous part is that none of this fails loudly**: a 200 with prose is `served: true`, so Cloudflare is never reached, the breaker counts a success and the stage just records "no result" — the live run that proved it read `enriched: 0, skipped: 5` while three calls returned **exactly 900 output tokens each**, the `max_tokens` ceiling. That is now the sentinel: **a completion pinned at `max_tokens` is truncated, cannot be complete JSON, and is classified as a failure** rather than served (`reason: 'truncated'`). **Verify a candidate against a REAL prompt, not a short one** — every model that failed the workload passed a toy prompt first, and presence in `/v1/models` proves only that the id resolves. `mapToNvidiaModel` returns **null** for vision/embedding/reranker ids so a stray caller gets Cloudflare instead of chat text where a 1024-dim vector was expected. **Out of scope and still Cloudflare-only:** embeddings (`bge-m3`, the fixed vector space the entire HNSW search index is built on), vision, and everything on the Workers `env.AI.run` binding. **Residency:** this sends user trip prompts and `intimate-moderation` text to a free US tier — a deliberate exception to the rule stated in `docs/dependency-audit/data-flow-map.md`, documented there in full. Migration `20261101100000`.

That cost two separate blind stretches, both from a name that was accepted and dropped: `breakerFailure` passed `p_error_msg` to an RPC whose argument is named otherwise (**PostgREST resolves overloads BY ARGUMENT NAME and answers a mismatch with a silent PGRST202 404**, so for three days *no* failure was recorded and the breaker could not trip), and `check-pipeline-health.mjs` asked for `select=…,last_error` on that same table, drew `400 42703` on every run, and so reported the chain healthy while the circuit was open. **Verify a column exists before selecting it, and make a failed probe SAY so** instead of falling through to null. **Rate limiting is in Postgres, not in-process**: the ~40 RPM ceiling is an ACCOUNT limit while edge functions are stateless and horizontally scaled, so `llm_provider_rate` + `llm_rate_acquire()` implement a leaky bucket with the availability test inside the `UPDATE ... WHERE` (the EvalPlanQual trick from `llm_budget_consume`). Seeded at **32, not 40** — a 429 reaching the router at all means `rpm_cap` is too high. A bucket, never a per-minute window: a fixed window allows 40 in the last second of one minute and 40 in the first of the next. **Pacing is opt-in via a `BATCH_CALLERS` ALLOWLIST, and the inversion is load-bearing** — anything unrecognised waits **0 ms** and falls back instantly, because pacing a request a human is sitting in front of is just a queue in front of the user; mutation-testing pacing onto an interactive caller made a single `trip-concierge` call take **61 s**. **It was first written as an `INTERACTIVE_CALLERS` denylist and that was wrong in a way its own test could not see**: the eleven shim callers reach the router through `anthropicMessages` → `llmAnthropicStyle`, which **silently dropped `callerFn`**, so every trip flow arrived as the fallback string `'llmChatCompletion'`, matched no entry, and would have paced. The test passed because it called `tryNvidia` with `callerFn: 'trip-concierge'` — a string that existed only in the test. `callerFn` is threaded through the shim now (which also closes a pre-existing hole: the whole trip surface, `generate-usernames` and `translate-i18n-batch` had been logging spend anonymously for as long as `llm_call_log` has existed, invisible to `llm-caller-attribution.test.ts` because its regex looks for `chatCompletion(`/`llmChatCompletion(` and these files call `anthropicMessages(`), but the allowlist stays inverted regardless: getting an entry wrong costs money in one direction and user experience in the other, and those are not equal. Batch callers wait `NVIDIA_MAX_WAIT_MS` (2000) and `enrichment-driver` publishes its `wallClockMs` via `setInvocationDeadline()` so pacing can never sleep past the 546s edge wall — the between-waves check alone is not enough, since it bounds when a wave STARTS while pacing adds latency inside a running one. **NVIDIA cannot go through AI Gateway** (24 providers, NVIDIA is not one, no universal passthrough), so `llm_call_log.provider` is the only record the path ran; `cost_usd` stays **NULL** for it per llm-cost.ts's null contract — an invented zero is indistinguishable from "this model is free". `_shared/pg-rpc.ts` exists so the router reaches Postgres without supabase-js: llm-client.ts takes no client and is imported by nearly every edge function, so pulling the SDK down that path would add it to every cold start (same reasoning as llm-usage-log.ts). **If the rate RPC is missing the router falls back rather than running unmetered** — so a function deployed ahead of its migration silently uses no NVIDIA at all until the migration lands. **Model ids: `meta/llama-3.1-8b-instruct` and `meta/llama-3.3-70b-instruct` are NOT in the catalogue** despite every blog post naming them — re-read `/v1/models` (unauthenticated) rather than trusting memory. **Being IN the catalogue is not enough either**: `/v1/models` lists 82 ids but only the **39 badged "Free Endpoint"** in the build.nvidia.com UI are callable on this account, and the rest 404 with a body naming an internal function UUID and never the model — which is how `nemotron-nano-3-30b-a3b` shipped as the default tier and 404'd (correlation verified against the UI, 2026-09-02; signing in changes nothing). Defaults are now `nvidia/nemotron-3.5-lightning-30b-a3b` / `nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b`, both env-overridable. **EVERY reachable model here is a reasoning model, and left alone they narrate instead of answering** — `ai-enrichment.ts` prompts demand bare JSON, the model replies *"Here's a thinking process: 1. **Analyze User Request**…"*, and `parseAIResponse`'s three-stage extraction finds no JSON at all. **The fix is one request field, `chat_template_kwargs: {thinking: false}`**, sent on every call. Measured on the real 1.8k-token prompt: lightning went **900 tokens / 29s / no JSON → 169 tokens / 3s / valid JSON**; super went 540/5s → 183/3s. It is sent unconditionally rather than to nemotron ids only, because the two failure directions are not symmetric — omitting it yields an HTTP 200 that costs full price, returns prose and is recorded as a **success**, while a model that rejects the field returns 4xx, trips the breaker, gets logged and falls back. `openai/gpt-oss-{20b,120b}` accept it as a no-op but are **rejected on latency, not quality** (15–35s against the 45s ceiling). **Do NOT reach for `response_format: json_object` instead** — measured, `gpt-oss-20b` TIMED OUT with it on a prompt it answered in 256 tokens without it, the same failure Cloudflare has. **The dangerous part is that none of this fails loudly**: a 200 with prose is `served: true`, so Cloudflare is never reached, the breaker counts a success and the stage just records "no result" — the live run that proved it read `enriched: 0, skipped: 5` while three calls returned **exactly 900 output tokens each**, the `max_tokens` ceiling. That is now the sentinel: **a completion pinned at `max_tokens` is truncated, cannot be complete JSON, and is classified as a failure** rather than served (`reason: 'truncated'`). **Verify a candidate against a REAL prompt, not a short one** — every model that failed the workload passed a toy prompt first, and presence in `/v1/models` proves only that the id resolves. `mapToNvidiaModel` returns **null** for vision/embedding/reranker ids so a stray caller gets Cloudflare instead of chat text where a 1024-dim vector was expected. **Out of scope and still Cloudflare-only:** embeddings (`bge-m3`, the fixed vector space the entire HNSW search index is built on), vision, and everything on the Workers `env.AI.run` binding. **Residency:** this sends user trip prompts and `intimate-moderation` text to a free US tier — a deliberate exception to the rule stated in `docs/dependency-audit/data-flow-map.md`, documented there in full. Migration `20261101100000`.

**Competition spine — Drag Race and the title-contest circuit (2026-09-06):** `/competitions` holds competitive queer performance on ONE schema: `competitions` → `competition_editions` → `competition_entrants` → `competition_episodes` → `competition_episode_results`. 33 competitions / 348 editions / 1,814 entries / 7,418 grid cells, 1973–2026: the 22 Drag Race franchises plus 11 independent contests (International Mr. Leather, Mister International Rubber, Mr Gay World/Europe/India, Miss International Queen, Miss T World, Miss Star International, Miss Fabulous Thailand, Miss Gay America, Miss Continental). One schema because they are one shape, and because **the title contests are OLDER than the television** (IML 1979, Miss Gay America 1973) — modelling them as a lesser sibling of a TV show would invert the history. **`kind` is STRUCTURE ONLY (`series` = has episodes and a grid, `title` = decided at a single event); identity lives in `competitions.format`, one honest label per competition taken from its own source's lead sentence.** It shipped as `drag_race|pageant` and that was a category error corrected the same day (`20360101100500`): defining eleven independent competitions by what they are NOT and then naming the residue is wrong for at least three of them — IML is "a multi-day **convention and competition**", MIR "a convention and contest", Mr Gay Europe "a male **competition** ... about important LGBTQIA+ themes". None is a pageant. The word survives where the source uses it (Miss Gay America is "a national pageant for female impersonators"), because deleting it wholesale is the mirror-image error; the migration asserts BOTH directions. Filter chips are derived from `format` at runtime, so a new format needs no code change. This fills the gap `docs/architecture/open-data-integration.md` §1.8 records as *"there is no performer entity"*. **The grain is an APPEARANCE, not a person** — a returning queen has several rows pointing at one `personalities.id`, which is what reconciles Wikipedia's own two numbers for the US main series (242 season *slots* vs ~238 unique *people*). **Winner / runner-up / Miss Congeniality are FLAGS on the entrant, never scalar FKs on the edition**: seven US seasons have two runners-up, season 16 has a Miss Congeniality *tie*, and one of those two is simultaneously a runner-up — a scalar column cannot express any of it. **The data was already being parsed and thrown away**: `import-dragrace-contestants.mjs` (#1698) built `{franchise, season, outcome}` per queen and flattened it into a prose bio via `buildBio()`; the parser now lives in `scripts/data-quality/lib/dragrace-wiki.mjs` and both importers share it. **Four parser traps, each found by measuring, not reasoning:** (1) `rowspan` must be EXPANDED — Wikipedia gives joint runners-up a single spanned cell, so without expansion all 7 two-runner-up seasons reported one and Phi Phi O'Hara had no placement at all; (2) `Untucked!` companion pages parse as seasons and produced 25 duplicates, every one colliding with its parent on `(competition_id, edition_number)`; (3) a finale is given TWO columns with the SAME episode number (challenge result, then final placement) — keep the LAST or the finale renders "safe" for a queen who was eliminated in it;
