(4) the episode list must be the UNION of the episode table and the grid, because `parseEpisodeTable` requires a parsed air date and a season whose grid outruns its dated episodes silently loses whole columns — measured at 23% of the corpus before the union. **Outcome codes are read off the source page's OWN LEGEND, never inferred, and inference was wrong every time it was tried**: `BDT` means *saved from* elimination (not bottom), `SDADHH` is awarded for *winning* a lip sync, `IN` means re-entry and NOT a win (eleven pages define it; three involve no win at all, so mapping it to `win` inflates `challenge_wins`), and `BGT`/`CT`/`HRT`/`GB`/`SAVE`/`RSU`/`BVR`/`BLK` are all rescue tokens that read like danger. **An unrecognised code returns null and is counted and named — never bucketed as `safe`** (44 of 7,462 cells, 0.59%), because a silent default makes a new twist indistinguishable from "nothing happened to her that week". `src/lib/dragOutcome.ts` owns the vocabulary and is drift-tested against the SQL CHECK; `src/lib/dragPlacement.ts` is the ESLint-allowlisted palette (border-gated tints, ≥25° off `--destructive`, glyph + code + `sr-only` on every cell). **The personality link is filtered to public non-duplicate rows INSIDE the RPCs** so no surface can leak a draft by forgetting; 743 of 1,814 resolve. **Matching is by `wikidata_qid` ONLY** — `personalities.wikipedia_url` is null on all 776 drag rows, and a name fallback was measured and REJECTED: of 776 unlinked title-contest entrants only 10 match by name, none has a drag profession, all 10 are `draft` (so invisible anyway), and three are `Dempsey`/`Rush`/`Ben Brown`, i.e. the namesake-chimera pattern. **There is deliberately NO admin CRUD**: the seed's `on conflict do update` would clobber hand edits on the next regeneration, which is the "human decisions silently discarded" failure this file documents elsewhere. Migrations `20360101100000`–`20360101100600`; guarded by `dragOutcome.test.ts` / `dragPlacement.test.ts` / `e2e/competitions.spec.ts`.

**Styleguide & Tone of Voice — the voice is rows now, not prose in three source files (2026-09-11):** the editorial standard existed in THREE places, all of them prose only a committer could change and none of them reconciled with the others — the `Copy: direct factual voice` bullet in this file, `TAG_STYLE_SYSTEM` in `_shared/tag-style.ts`, and a third idea of it inside `MOAT_SYSTEM_PROMPT` + `CITY_MOAT_SYSTEM_PROMPT` in `_shared/ai-enrichment.ts`. `styleguide_rules` / `_terms` / `_examples` are now the single source, edited at `/admin/styleguide`, rendered at `/styleguide`, and compiled by `styleguide_compile()` into an immutable semver row served at `GET /api/v1/styleguide/prompt` and read by edge functions through `_shared/voice-style.ts`. **Row-per-rule, NOT the `site_branding` jsonb document** — the deliberate divergence from that precedent: the editing unit is one term a community editor adds on its own, needing its own audit row, active flag and rationale. **THE FRAME IS CODE, THE CONTENT IS DATA** — the preamble, the fence and the six non-negotiables are hardcoded in the compiler; editors own rules/terms/examples and nothing else, because the compiled text is sent verbatim to third-party models and a row that can rewrite the frame can rewrite the model's instructions. Three layers enforce it: a BEFORE-trigger control-character/length gate (`[:print:]`-based, so accented and non-Latin text passes), `styleguide_strip_fence` on EVERY editor-authored column at compile time (verified: a row containing the literal END marker yields a prompt with exactly ONE end fence), and the non-negotiables emitted AFTER the fence closes. **`styleguide_strip_fence` must stay STRICT.** The first draft coalesced NULL to `''` inside it, and since every optional field compiles as `COALESCE(' (' || strip_fence(x) || ')', '')` — which only collapses on NULL — every wrapper always fired: each term without a context note printed a bare `()`, and a term with no replacement rendered `-> ""`, i.e. "replace it with nothing", the exact opposite of "rewrite the sentence". **Found by READING the compiled output, not by any test** — all 20 structural tests were green across it. **PROFILES exist because the full prompt is ~6,500 tokens and that is a bill, not a style decision:** measured on v1.0.0, full 25,979 chars / core 21,173 / compact 13,125 — **both of the latter two were PRE-`STRICT`-fix numbers and were wrong in the shipped doc; see the hardening entry below for the current measured values**; `getVoicePrompt()` defaults to `core`, and a classifier on a five-minute cron should take `compact`. **Every profile is frozen into the version row AT PUBLISH TIME** — compiling on read would make `?profile=compact&v=1.0.0` silently follow live edits, which is the one thing a pin promises not to do. Two postconditions: a publish with zero active rules is REFUSED (it is a valid-looking 200 that switches the voice off everywhere while every call reports success), and exactly one version is active via a unique index on `((TRUE)) WHERE is_active` — **and a first draft of the test for that asserted the index name and `WHERE is_active` with `[\s\S]*?` between them, which still passes when the expression is changed to a real column and the index constrains nothing**; assert the `((TRUE))` expression itself. The API **fails loudly** (5xx), unlike branding's fail-open, and `getVoicePrompt()` treats a body under 500 chars as a failure: "the styleguide was unreachable" must not be indistinguishable from "the styleguide says nothing". **NO LIVE PIPELINE IS SWITCHED OVER YET, deliberately** — changing a running enrichment function's system prompt changes its output and wants its own before/after measurement, not a side effect of shipping the machinery; `TAG_STYLE_SYSTEM` and the MOAT prompts stay until their callers move (deleting one before its consumer reads the other is how you get zero). **Hardened 2026-09-11 (`20460318142700`-`20460318142900`).** Five things the first cut got wrong, each now measured. (1) **`applies_to` was FREE TEXT** behind only a cardinality bound and a slug regex, so `venue` and `venues` both validated and silently meant different things — a typo made a rule invisible to the surface it was written for with nothing reporting it. Now a CHECK (`applies_to <@ vocabulary`) + `styleguide_scope_values()` + `src/lib/styleguideScopes.ts`, drift-tested; the test finds the LATEST migration defining each rather than pinning a filename, which is the `venueCategories.ts` trap. `glossary` retired in favour of `tag` (both were in use — two spellings of one surface on day one). (2) **14 of 30 seeded MUST/NEVER rules had no rationale** while the editor micro-guide told contributors a rule without a reason gets argued about forever; filled, and a CHECK now requires one on binding severities (`should` stays exempt). (3) **`core` saved only 19%** and was not worth choosing. The rationale lever is now split from the severity lever: full 28,956 / core 14,547 (-50%) / compact 13,293 (-54%). Honest residue: core and compact are only 9% apart — dropping rationales+examples is the whole saving, severity adds ~4% — so three tiers survive on an editorial distinction, not a numeric one. (4) **No sentinel.** `styleguide_signals()` in `check-pipeline-health.mjs` hard-fails on empty-wrapper artifacts, a broken fence, lost non-negotiables, an unknown scope, a reasonless binding rule, or nothing published. It checks the PUBLISHED TEXT, not row counts — the `()` / `-> ""` defect was invisible to all twenty structural tests because every one parsed migration SOURCE. It also counts **adoption from the source tree and fails at zero**: nothing in the DB can see whether any pipeline reads the voice, and the Village Truth Engine precedent is a relink batch that shipped with no cron and sat dead. **Audit retention was flagged and deliberately NOT built** — 69 rows after a full seed, growing at human rate; the versions cap exists because each version row stores ~60 KB of prompts, which audit rows do not. (5) **`styleguide_compile` was granted to anon** for no reason — the API serves the frozen version, the admin preview is definer-gated, the page reads rows — so it was an uncapped aggregation open to anonymous traffic. Revoked. **The fence is STRUCTURAL only**, and the doc now says so: it stops a row closing its own block, not a row that reads like a voice rule and alters behaviour anyway; the real controls are admin-only RLS, the audit trail, and a human reading the preview before publishing. First adopter is `tag-enrichment-sweep` (`withVoice(TAG_STYLE_SYSTEM,'core')`), chosen because its cron is disabled and both auto-apply paths are retired, so the wiring is proven at zero behavioural risk. Output-level guard `e2e/styleguide-voice.spec.ts` runs against prod. Adoption order and the whole design: `docs/architecture/styleguide-voice-system.md`. Migrations `20450210090000`/`20450210090100` + `20460318142700`-`20460318142900`; guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/styleguideVoiceSystem.test.ts` + `styleguideFallbackDrift.test.ts` (mutation-tested, 13 mutations all caught) + `_shared/voice-style.test.ts`. The fallback's RULE LIST is a snapshot and may lag; its FRAME may not — a stale rule list is fine to serve during an outage, a stale non-negotiable means the fleet runs on a superseded safety rule every time Supabase blips, and both copies pass every other test because each is individually valid.

**Running the standard against the corpus corrected the STANDARD, not the corpus (2026-09-11, v1.2.0, `20470922084600`/`20470922084700`).** The first audit scanned our own voice only (cities, countries, `unified_tags`, `queer_villages` — 14,335 rows; venues/events/news/marketplace are third-party text republished under our name and are a separate decision about whose voice binds). **Six of the 36 terms were wrong, and the largest single source of noise was the vocabulary's own imprecision.** (1) **`gay friendly` was stored SPACE-ONLY and missed every real use** — measured 64 "gay-friendly", **0** "gay friendly"; a `legal_safety` term with a 100% miss rate, and "queer-friendly" (50 more) was not in the vocabulary at all. (2) **`a transgender` matched the CORRECT adjective** — all 9 hits are "a transgender woman/man/person", which is exactly what that term's own `preferred` recommends; it now carries the `(as a noun)` annotation three entries already use. (3) bare **`ethnic`** was ~100% false positive ("ethnic group/minority/majority", Wikipedia-derived city prose) → narrowed to the exoticising collocations. (4) bare **`lifestyle`** fought two of our own rules — 34 hits, overwhelmingly the kink and swinging communities' own word ("the lifestyle", "Naturism is a lifestyle") which `community-words-in-their-real-sense` and `reclaimed-words` exist to protect, plus "lifestyle changes" in health copy — and the harmful sense was already covered twice by "gay lifestyle"/"chosen lifestyle". (5) bare **`minorities`** is standard rights vocabulary ("sexual minorities", "racial and ethnic minorities"). (6) bare **`urban area`** is a unit of geography ("the Sonoma urban area had a population of 31,479") — **158 hits, 22% of the whole baseline, all noise**; the coded sense is "urban" applied to PEOPLE, which is what it names now. **The rule that falls out: an avoid entry must be wrong in essentially every context, or it teaches the model to avoid correct English.** `clean` and `accessible` are deliberately NOT narrowed — both are unusable to a matcher ("clean towels", "accessible toilet") but both are real guidance for a model reading the prompt, and `step-free` carries severity `context` precisely because it needs a human; a term's job is to instruct an LLM, not to be greppable. **`styleguide_content_drift()` makes the backlog a number that moves** — per-surface counts of own-voice rows matching an active avoid phrase, **571 of 14,335 at baseline** (city.description 356, city.editorial_hook **48 of 105 — 46%, which is the LLM composer's register, not scattered slips**, tag.long_description 80; most common: vibrant 374, explore 174, gay-friendly 73, queer-friendly 50). It is **advisory on the counts and hard on a broken probe**: `rows_scanned` and `phrases_active` are reported separately because an empty corpus, an unreadable vocabulary and a clean corpus all return the same reassuring zero, and the scanner's exclusion list is itself guarded — the migration asserts `it`/`clean`/`accessible` still exist in the vocabulary, so the exclusion can never become a back door for deleting a term. **It is a counter and NOT a rewrite, deliberately**: bulk-LLM-rewriting the ~400 offending city descriptions is precisely the experiment already run and retired here (the tag prose judge retracted 16 of its first 18 rows, 13 of them WRONG, and both auto-apply paths are disabled), and this would repeat it at six times the scale on pages that rank. **The other half — a `withVoice()` adoption on `city-agentic-enrich` so new copy stops arriving in this register — is now WIRED and ships `off` (2026-09-11).** Baseline measured BEFORE writing any code, free, from `cities.enrichment_status.agentic` where all 328 real runs since 2026-06-07 had already recorded their own output: **16.8% of generated descriptions (54/321) and 21.8% of hooks (70/321) carry an avoid phrase**, avg confidence 0.609, and the failure is concentrated — **`vibrant` alone is 95 of ~124 hits**, then explore 17, discover 13. **Two measurements changed the design.** (1) `styleguide_compile` takes a `p_scope` and `getVoicePrompt()` has never passed one; that looked like the way to shrink the prompt until it was measured — **`city` scope is only 3.7% smaller than `all`** (14,169 vs 14,711) because 21 of 30 rules are `applies_to:['all']`, so scope-aware freezing would have bought nothing. (2) The rule+term banning vibrant/explore/discover are ~634 chars of the 13,457-char `compact` profile, i.e. **77% of the measured hits are fixable with 5% of the prompt** — and that cheap fix is deliberately REJECTED, because hand-copying the standard into a prompt recreates the three-prose-copies problem this system exists to end, and because **16.8% is a floor on the problem, not the problem**: a regex sees `vibrant` but cannot see tokenism, a city note written as though every reader is a gay man, or an unsourced legal claim about a criminalising country, which is exactly what `not-only-gay-men` / `anti-racist-specificity` / `legal-claims-are-sourced` / `respect-the-gate` address. **It ships `off` because the risk is SILENCE, not bad content** — `parseAIResponse` returns null on unparseable output and the run records `no_ai`, so a JSON regression degrades to *nothing enriched*, and prepending ~13.5k chars to a 1.8k bare-JSON prompt on models that already need `chat_template_kwargs:{thinking:false}` is precisely what causes that. Precedence is `body.voice` (the A/B) > `admin_automations.conditions.voice` (the production lever, flipped with a plain UPDATE — no migration, no deploy, the dedup `conditions.mode` shape) > `off`; an unrecognised value falls back to `off` rather than throwing, since a typo in a hand-edited row must not take an hourly cron down. **The dry run is now usable as a measurement**: it already guarded every write, but returned only counts, and a dry run that hides the model's output cannot evaluate a prompt change — it now carries the generated proposal, so both arms run against the same cities on prod writing nothing (note it still SPENDS `llm_budget`, one unit per city against a 120/day cap, so keep the sample small). Every produced row is stamped `voice_profile` + `voice_system_chars` in `enrichment_status.agentic`, stamped AFTER parsing and deliberately absent from `CITY_MOAT_KEYS` — asking the model to report its own system prompt would be meaningless and would let it misreport which arm ran. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/cityAgenticVoiceAdoption.test.ts` (mutation-tested 13/13; one first read as caught because `[\s\S]*?voice,` reached forward to a later `voice,` in the results push — the same vacuous-assertion class as the styleguide term tests, scoped and re-run). Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/styleguideTermPrecision.test.ts` (mutation-tested, 15/15 across both migrations and the health script).

**The A/B ran, the voice won on every number that was asked for, and the thing that stopped the flip was a field nobody was watching (2026-09-12, `20490210090000`).** Five drift-bearing cities (Bisbee, Busan, Manila, Chillán, Warren — chosen because their *stored* output demonstrably drifts; **the first sample was wrong and must not be revived**: selecting by the cron's own lowest-completeness ordering surfaces tiny factual towns whose control output scores ZERO avoid phrases, so the A/B could not have shown an improvement), both arms fired at the same cities through `pg_net` with `dry_run`, read back **by request id**. `compact` beat `off` on everything the measurement was designed around: JSON parsed **5/5** (the silent-failure risk the flag exists for did not materialise), drift **2 hits → 1** (`vibrant` gone from Manila's hook; `iconic` survives in both arms on Chillán's market), confidence **0.82 → 0.86**, `voice_system_chars` 15,531, both arms inside 90s for five cities — and the control arm, not the treatment, is the one that returned a description truncated mid-sentence ("Busan is general."). On Busan the qualitative half went the right way too: the compact hook states an event and a port where the stored control hook asserted an unverified welcome. **And it was still wrong to flip, for a reason no number in that list can carry.** `compact` filled `best_time_to_visit` on **2 of 5** cities where `off` filled **0 of 5** — a field this composer had returned null on **209/209** times, conservatism this file already records as CORRECT rather than as a gap. One of the two fills dated the **Busan Queer Culture Festival to "June or July"**; it is a September/October event, and June/July is *Seoul's*. At the ≥0.8 bar that sentence auto-publishes into an empty column. **`styleguide_content_drift()` could never see it — the sentence carries no avoid phrase at all.** It is not in the wrong register; it is wrong, about a queer event, on a queer travel platform. **The lesson is that a voice prompt does not only change HOW a model writes, it changes WHAT it is willing to assert, and a vocabulary scanner measures only the first.** `best_time_to_visit` is the one narrative field that is a fabrication surface **by construction**: the grounding sources say what a city IS and almost never say when to go, so `description` and `local_customs` paraphrase their sources while this one extrapolates past them — which is why the fix gates that field alone and leaves the other two auto-publishing. **The edge-function change is the smaller half and shipping it alone would be the worse half-fix**: `approve_entity_review()` resolves the field through `review_field_registry` and RAISEs `unsupported review field` when no row exists, so queuing without registering rebuilds the queue-that-discards-human-decisions failure of the 40-day `ai_validation_status` incident. **`batchable=false` is the load-bearing column**, not a detail — `approve_entity_review_batch()` approves every open row whose registry entry says `batchable` with nobody reading it, so a batchable row would hand the fabricated sentence straight back and make the whole change decorative; `apply_mode='text_required'` so an empty proposal RAISEs instead of blanking a curated column, and `risk_gate` stays NULL because travel timing is not an outing risk. Validated on prod in a rolled-back transaction before merge. Guarded by the `best_time_to_visit is review-gated` and `the review registry row` blocks of `src/lib/__tests__/cityAgenticVoiceAdoption.test.ts` (mutation-tested, 10/10 — including one that empties the auto-apply block, because every "does not auto-apply" assertion is vacuous against a slice that does not exist).

**`profiles` column allowlists — THREE grants, and RLS is not one of them (2026-09-12):** `public.profiles` has 174 columns and is FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY, but **RLS filters ROWS ONLY** — it never stopped `GET /rest/v1/profiles?select=email` from returning every other member's address. The protection is column-level GRANTs, now three of them: **anon SELECT 21** (`20260816120000`), **authenticated SELECT 38** (`20510101100000`), **authenticated INSERT/UPDATE 52** (`20540101100000`). Each is asserted by exact set equality in `supabase/migrations/__tests__/profiles_column_grants.sql` (tests 9 and 10), because a superset is the leak and a subset is a broken editor path. **A column GRANT is per-ROLE and `authenticated` is ONE role** shared by the profile owner, every other member and every admin — `has_role_jwt('admin')` cannot grant a column privilege — so the naive allowlist is the UNION of all three needs and would have to contain `email`, `date_of_birth` and `phone` anyway. That is why `20440101100000` had to come FIRST: it moves the self-read onto `get_my_profile()` and the admin reads onto `admin_users_list` / `admin_user_detail` / `admin_user_stats` / `admin_resolve_user_by_email`, all SECURITY DEFINER and revoked from PUBLIC/anon. Those depend on owner `postgres` holding `rolbypassrls`; without it every one of them returns zero rows. **Read your own row through `get_my_profile()`, never `select('*').eq('user_id', …)`** — eight hooks were converted and the two remaining direct selects (`useUserDirectoryQuery`, `useGroupMemberManagement`) read OTHER people, which is what the 38 cover. **A bare `.select()` after an UPDATE re-opens the hole**: PostgREST turns it into `select=*` with `Prefer: return=representation`, which needs SELECT on all 174; `useProfile` deliberately has no trailing `.select()` and refetches through the definer function instead.

**The SECURITY INVOKER views are the trap in that change, not the frontend.** Five views over `profiles` carry `security_invoker` — `profile_status_v` (needs 13 columns), `public_profiles` (10), `intimate_discovery_v` (8, the dating discovery surface), `safe_profiles` (6), `contributor_recognitions_public` (4). An invoker view reads the base table with the CALLER's privileges, so a column missing from the allowlist breaks **the view**, not the query that named it — and the failure surfaces in a feature nobody was editing. Derive the allowlist from the views as well as from the hooks. The mirror hazard is arm 3 of `profiles_column_exposure()`: a view WITHOUT `security_invoker` runs as `postgres` and bypasses both RLS and the allowlist entirely, so `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` — which silently resets `reloptions` — is a re-exposure waiting to happen (test 8 guards the five by name).

**Until 2026-09-12 any signed-in user could self-verify and self-approve.** Measured on prod as `authenticated` before the fix: `update profiles set verified_identity = true`, `set moderation_status = 'approved'` and `set verified_email = true` were all ALLOWED. RLS confined each to the caller's OWN row, which is exactly what made it exploitable rather than harmless. `20540101100000` withdraws those plus `verified_phone`, `is_business`, `profile_completion_percentage`, `id`, `created_at`, `welcome_email_sent_at` and every `*_encrypted` twin. **Enumerating the 52 writable columns needs brace-matching, not grep** — `Settings.tsx`'s editor object spans lines 384-413 and carries 26 keys; a line-range sweep found six of them and produced a list that would have broken profile editing in silence. Sources are the 13 `updateProfile` call sites (including `useStatus` and `PreferencesMirrorCard`, which forward a caller-supplied patch), `useProfile`'s upsert plus two avatar updates, and the six direct `.update()` hooks. `user_id` is granted because the upsert supplies it as the conflict key; RLS still decides which row may be touched. No edge function is affected — all nine that write `profiles` use `service_role` (two also read `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, but only to verify the caller's JWT).

**Diagnosing a `42501` on `profiles` needs the MESSAGE, not the SQLSTATE.** An RLS violation and a missing column privilege both raise `insufficient_privilege`: `new row violates row-level security policy for table "profiles"` is RLS, `permission denied for table profiles` is the grant. A handler that switches on `insufficient_privilege` alone reports an intact path as broken — which is what an `auth.uid()`-less test role does to every INSERT, since no INSERT policy can match. **And `npm test` / `npm run typecheck` are structurally blind to all of this**: `supabase gen types` introspects `pg_attribute` regardless of grantee, so `types.ts` keeps all 174 columns, and every vitest spec mocks the Supabase client wholesale. The contract test and `e2e/members-directory-anon.spec.ts` are the only coverage.
**Flipping the lever proved the gate and then found the gate fed nothing (2026-09-12, `20540301100000`).** The flip to `compact` landed cleanly — 16 runs, `voice_system_chars` 15,531 — and production produced the before/after pair the A/B could not: **Vadnais Heights** at 01:20Z on the old build proposed *"June to August for warm weather and outdoor activities around the city's lakes and parks"* and **auto-published it**; **Haapsalu linn** at 03:21Z on the new build proposed *"Summer, for festivals and mud spa treatments"* and `best_time_to_visit` **stayed NULL**. Same field, ninety minutes apart, live data. **Then the proposal vanished** — nothing in `entity_review_queue`. The hourly cron posts `{"batch_limit": 5, "skip_gated": true}`, and `if (skipGated) gatedProposals.length = 0` runs AFTER the array is built, so every gated proposal was **produced and then discarded before the insert**. Measured since 2026-09-03: **52 runs produced 48 `editorial_hook`, 42 `lgbt_friendly_rating` and 1 `best_time_to_visit` proposals, and the queue received none**; the newest open review row for either long-standing gated field was 2026-09-02 20:36 while the cron ran hourly throughout. **This is the `ai_validation_status` failure one step earlier — the loss happens before any row exists, so no queue-depth sentinel and no counter could ever have shown it.** The only symptom was a queue that quietly stopped growing, which reads exactly like a quiet week. **What made it visible was a NEW field entering the gated set and someone reading one row**: Haapsalu's claim fact-checks CORRECT (Estonia's oldest resort, curative sea-mud clinic since 1825, an event resort through the summer season), so the flag was not protecting anyone — it discarded good content along with bad, which is the argument for removing it rather than tuning it. **The fix edits `action.command` ONLY and never `cron.job`** — the registry is canonical, `admin_automation_effective_command()` derives the wrapped form, and `sync_automations_to_cron()`'s drift branch applies it; a `cron.schedule()` in a migration is the `detect_stale_venues` mistake. The migration deliberately does **not** call `sync_automations_to_cron(true)`: that reconciler is global and can recreate, re-wrap and kill other jobs in the same pass, so whatever drift exists at CI time is not this change's to apply — propagation is the nightly `automation_cron_sync`. Applied live first, where the dry run reported exactly `command_rewrapped: [{"jobname":"city_agentic_enrich"}]` and nothing else, and the resulting command was verified to have lost `skip_gated` while keeping `admin_automation_run_begin` + `automation_http_post` (a re-wrap that falls back to a raw `net.http_post` yields a job that works and records no runs — a failing cron that reads healthy). **A correction worth keeping: the first alarm from this rollout was wrong.** At n=4 the compact arm looked like it had a tenfold refusal-prose regression (3/4 descriptions saying "the provided sources do not contain…" against 2/27 for `off`); at n=16 vs n=21 it is 3/16 against 2/21, and compact is BETTER where it counts — zero-confidence `no_ai` runs **0/16 vs 3/21**, average confidence **0.794 vs 0.733**. Reverting on the n=4 reading would have discarded a working improvement. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/cityAgenticSkipGated.test.ts` (mutation-tested, 11/11 — one of them the same vacuous-assertion class again: a bare `skip_gated … RAISE NOTICE` span was satisfied by the sibling "already clean" notice and stayed green when that branch was turned into an EXCEPTION, so it is scoped to the branch).
**Every crawler detail page served its prose as ONE paragraph, for as long as `paragraphsHtml` has existed (fixed 2026-09-12, #3625 / `6d5e8a21f`).** `paragraphsHtml` in `functions/_lib/detail.ts` renders the prose body of **13 call sites** — venue, event, news, personality (description + bio), city, country, hotel (description + safety notes), village (description + history), tag, milestone, guide — and it called `collapseWs`, a bare `\s+ -> ' '`, **BEFORE** splitting. Every `\n` was already a single space by the time `.split(/\n{2,}|(?<=[.!?])\s{2,}/)` ran, so **neither arm could ever match** and the function could not emit more than one `<p>`. Verified on prod with a Googlebot UA, not inferred: `/villages/chueca` returned its whole History section as a single paragraph, same on `/city/hatboro`, `/tags/stealthing`, `/venue/tramp-s`. The fix is order — split first, collapse INSIDE each paragraph. **The second failure is CRLF and it is invisible to reasoning about the regex: `[ \t]*` does not match `\r`**, so on a `\r\n` row every lookbehind arm sees `\r` instead of the character it tests for and never fires — **61 of the 189** newline-bearing `venues.description` rows and 19 `events.description` rows, i.e. a third of the venue corpus would have kept the exact bug the fix was written to remove. Line endings are normalised first; `\n\s*\n` was always fine because `\s` matches `\r`. **Three arms, and each condition removes a MEASURED defect rather than a guessed one.** (1) A blank line — 4,558 events, 240 `unified_tags.long_description`, 150 cities, 7/7 `guides.intro_md`. (2) A single newline after sentence-terminal punctuation — **entire content types separate paragraphs this way and never use a blank line**: 106 of 175 `queer_villages.history` rows contain `\n` and **ZERO** contain `\n\n`, so a blank-line-only rule leaves every village history page a single block; same shape on 858 cities rows. It also still catches **272** breaks arm 3 cannot — a colon or `?` followed by a URL, an email or a `-`/`$`/`*` line — and 8+ of those carry a trailing space before the newline (`"Fragen? "` ⏎ `spiele@haz.ch`), which is why the `[ \t]*` tolerance is load-bearing. (3) A single newline whose next line opens with a capital or digit, where the current line does **not** end in a comma or semicolon — **this is the events corpus, which writes headings and timetables with no terminal punctuation at all** (`Zugänglichkeit`, `Code of Conduct`, `Türöffnung: 21 Uhr`, `23.15h - Milky Diamond` ⏎ `23.30h - Sado Opera`): 1,906 such breaks rendered as one run-on paragraph. **Three alternatives were measured and REJECTED, and the numbers are the reason.** A bare `\n+` split cuts real sentences in half — **22 broken sentences in a 1,182-row sample** (German event copy, verse, Wikipedia list runs) against **0** for the shipped rule. Reviving the old dead `(?<=[.!?])\s{2,}` sentence-gap arm fragments running text at every typewriter double space — 110 venues, 117 cities, 35 events (*"…was 60 at the 2000 census.  The area is named for…"*). And the comma exclusion must stay **NARROW**: a draft that also excluded `+ / -` re-glued `Must be 19+`, bare URLs ending in `/`, and `-----` separators. **One known defect is left in DELIBERATELY**: the digit half of the lookahead is worth 294 splits, 293 of them timetables, and its one bad split is `…Gramercy Theater (127 East` ⏎ `23rd Street)`. An "unclosed `(` means continuation" rejoin was written for it and **measured net-negative** — only 3 fragments in 24,498 carry an unclosed paren, so it fixes that one and wrongly glues the other two. Do not re-add it without re-measuring. **Blast radius was measured, not assumed**: every detail slug referenced by `e2e/*.spec.ts` was enumerated (128 bodies, 14 change), and every spec string literal ≥18 chars was tested against the before and after HTML — 2 match an affected body, **0 break**. Post-deploy verification was a comparison against the database, not a smoke test: 33 live crawler pages across six cohorts (including 6 CRLF venue rows on purpose) checked that every paragraph the new function should produce is actually present in what prod serves — **33/33, zero still-one-blob, zero mismatch**. Guarded by `functions/_lib/detailParagraphs.test.ts` (15 cases, every string a real prod row; mutation-tested 13/13, including drop-CRLF-normalisation, drop-each-arm, drop-the-comma-exclusion, drop-digits-from-the-lookahead and widen-the-exclusion-to-the-rejected-draft). **Two mutations SURVIVED the first round and the right response was to test the arms, not delete them** — measurement showed both still do work no other arm covers. **`functions/**` is not covered by `npm test`'s intuition but IS by its config** (`vite.config.ts` includes `functions/**/*.{test,spec}.ts`) and by the zero-tolerance `npm run typecheck:functions`, which `tsconfig.app.json` never sees. **`prettier --write` on `detail.ts` also reformats 4 unrelated hunks that were already non-conforming on `main`** — formatting is not gated for `functions/**` (lint-staged covers `src/**` only, no CI format step), so revert those rather than bundling them into a behavioural diff.

**The queue filled, and the first thing a human read in it was a Greek myth (2026-09-12, `21050101100000`).** The un-gated cron queued 9 rows at 07:20Z — 4 `editorial_hook`, 4 `lgbt_friendly_rating`, 1 `best_time_to_visit` — the first gated proposals in ten days, which is the fix above working. Reading them found something bigger: **`/city/daphne` had AUTO-PUBLISHED a description of the Greek nymph pursued by Apollo.** The row is Daphne, ALABAMA (`region_name='Alabama'`, 30.65/-87.91, Mobile Bay, `seo_indexable`). `city-agentic-enrich` fetches its grounding extract with `action=query&prop=extracts&redirects=1&titles=<cities.name>`, and **`redirects=1` follows a bare name wherever it leads** while a disambiguation page carries an extract long enough to pass the >120-char check. `cities.description` is NOT review-gated, so this publishes at confidence >=0.8 — the gated `editorial_hook` waits for a human while the wrong-subject `description` ships. **This is the namesake chimera `tag-wiki-guard.ts` documents for glossary tags (`golden-shower` -> Cassia fistula), one entity type over and with a worse blast radius.** Measured across ALL 79 agentic-enriched cities with a NULL `wikidata_qid`: **14 wrong, 12 of them indexable** — 7 wrong subject (Daphne->the myth, Delphi, Indiana->the Greek sanctuary, Parma, Ohio->Parma ITALY, Brisbane, California->Brisbane AUSTRALIA, Highland, California->the common noun, West, Texas->the compass direction, Englewood, Colorado->the ETYMOLOGY of the name), 4 unresolved disambiguation, 3 refusal prose. **The refusal prose was a SYMPTOM, not a third defect**: Milton, Morton and Spooner were handed a disambiguation page, correctly found no city in it, and told the reader "the sources do not provide any information about LGBTQ+..." — sealing the source removes the cause, and no output filter is needed. Morton additionally invented a fact off the page's own list ("known for being the location of Morton Arboretum in nearby Lisle" — the arboretum is Lisle's, 130 miles away). **NOT caused by the voice flip**: the 14 split 8 `compact` / 3 `off` / 3 predating the stamp, and 13 of 13 `off` runs in the window hit no-QID rows. The hole is arm-independent and as old as the composer. **THE FIX IS RESOLUTION, NOT REFUSAL, and that is the part worth copying.** `_shared/city-wiki-guard.ts` gates the extract — disambiguation / lead-describes-a-place / lead-corroborates-our-own-region-or-country — and on a refusal the caller RETRIES under Wikipedia's own convention for a shared name, `"<Name>, <Region>"`. Measured: **14 of 14 corrected — 13 rescued onto the right article** (`Daphne, Alabama` -> "a city in Baldwin County, Alabama"), 1 correctly refused (Esmeralda: no `Esmeralda, Camagüey` article exists). **Collateral is ZERO BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by measurement** — the retry is reachable only where the existing path already failed a gate, so the 64 cities that resolve correctly today never enter the new code. **Title agreement is deliberately NOT a gate**: asking for "Daphne" and being served the article *titled* "Daphne" is what this failure looks like, so name agreement is the bug's signature, not evidence against it. **Three traps, each found by measuring rather than reading.** (1) A bare `seat of` in the place vocabulary let Delphi's "the seat of Pythia, the major oracle" pass as a settlement — it is `county seat` / `seat of government` now. (2) **Corroboration must read the LEAD only**: the Brisbane, Australia article mentions the United States far below the fold, which corroborated it for Brisbane, California when the whole body was searched. (3) `island country` and `local board` are in the vocabulary because a city-state is a city and Auckland's Henderson-Massey opens "is one of the 21 local boards" — both were measured false blocks. **Two things the retraction itself teaches.** **`jsonb_set(fp,'{description,retracted}',…,true)` SILENTLY WROTE NOTHING**: `create_missing` creates only the LAST path element, and none of these rows had a `description` key in `field_provenance`, so the retraction would have destroyed the text with no record while every other check passed. Caught by dry-running on prod, not by reading the call; build the nested object with `||` instead, and assert the snapshot count. **And `brisbane` BY SLUG is Brisbane, AUSTRALIA**, whose byte-identical description is CORRECT for that row — a slug-keyed or description-keyed repair would have retracted it, which is why this one is keyed by **id** and asserts the Australian row survived. Retraction only ever REMOVES; the 14 refill through the normal grounded path once the seal makes it resolve correctly. Known and accepted: a city left with no description may be reclassified `ghost` and deindexed by the nightly recompute if it also has no venues or events — honest, and self-reversing when content returns. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/cityWrongSubjectRetraction.test.ts` + `_shared/city-wiki-guard.test.ts` (whose fixtures are the real extracts Wikipedia served that day), mutation-tested **15/15**.

**The correct description was already in the row, unpublished — and the previous pass could not have seen it (2026-09-13, `40000101100000`).** Verifying the retraction above found two more wrong-subject descriptions through a DIFFERENT producer. `/city/cambria` — `seo_indexable`, `shell_status='real'` — published en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambria, **the Latin name for WALES**, on a California city page; `/city/timon` published the given-name disambiguation page for Timon, Maranhão, Brazil. **`21050101100000`'s audit was structurally blind to both**: it selected `city-agentic-enrich`-touched cities with a NULL `wikidata_qid`, and these carry a CORRECT qid (Q1028196 / Q1017101, both resolved live), a CORRECT cached `wikipedia_title`, and were never agentic-enriched at all — they came from the `city-factual-backfill` / `city-corroboration` path. **A selection criterion tuned to one producer's failure cannot find the same defect in another's.** **THE FINDING IS IN THE PROVENANCE, NOT THE COLUMN.** The correct text had ALREADY been fetched by each row's own cached title and was sitting UNPUBLISHED at `field_provenance.description.candidates[0]` — Cambria's correct 396-char San Luis Obispo lead under its wrong 813-char Wales one. `city-factual-backfill` is **fill-if-empty**, so it recorded the right answer as a candidate and never overwrote the non-empty wrong column. **These rows could therefore NEVER self-heal**: the filler skips them for being non-empty, and nothing revalidates a derived field against the input it was derived from — the same class as the 86 wrong-country `safety_notes` (`20260816112824`), and corrected the same way (in place, guarded on the defect's own signature, prior text preserved under `.corrected.from`). **So the repair is a CORRECTION, not a retraction**: nulling would strip an indexable `real` page of its description and then depend on a refill that only fires once the column is empty — two steps where one suffices, and a thinner page in between. **Before repairing a wrong derived value, check whether the right one is already in the row.** The published text is a **VALUES literal, not the row's jsonb read at apply time**, so what lands is deterministic and is exactly what was verified against live Wikipedia, not whatever a later enrichment pass leaves in `candidates` by the time CI applies it. Both texts also PASS `cityWikiVerdict`, asserted in BOTH directions (the text removed is refused, the text published is adopted) — a correction that published prose the seal would itself reject would be a defect wearing a fix's clothes. **The Cambria trap is worth keeping**: its CORRECT article contains the sentence "The name Cambria, chosen in 1869, is the Latin name for Wales" — the defect's own explanation sits inside the right article — so a guard keyed on the WORD rather than on the lead's SUBJECT would have refused the fix. **The seal from `21050101100000` is now validated in production, not just in tests**: overnight, 11 of the 14 retraction-set rows were re-visited and every one resolved correctly (Daphne -> "a city in Baldwin County, Alabama", Parma -> Cuyahoga County Ohio, West -> McLennan County Texas, Englewood -> Arapahoe County Colorado), and **Spooner exercised the RETRY path specifically** — no qid, previously re-publishing refusal prose every hour, now "a small city in Washburn County, Wisconsin". **Refusal prose is 0 corpus-wide**, confirming it was a symptom of the bad source rather than a defect needing its own output filter. Brisbane, California was reclassified `ghost` and deindexed for having no description and no content — the outcome the previous entry records as accepted and self-reversing. **Migration versioning: FIVE mandatory renumbers across the two migrations of this effort in about 24 hours** — the retraction moved 20700101100000 -> 20710601100000 -> 20800101100000 -> 21050101100000, and this correction then moved 21050101100100 -> 22500101100000 -> 40000101100000, chasing a ceiling that went 2070 -> 2075 -> 2080 -> 2099 -> 2200 -> 2900 -> 3000 -> 3500. A one-year gap was overtaken, then five years, then fifty, then five hundred. Each renumber is MANDATORY (`db push` aborts on the first file sorting below remote `max(version)` and takes every later migration in the same push with it, which blocks the repo rather than one PR), and each is free (`VERSION_RE` is any 14 digits, no date validation), **which is exactly why nothing stops the escalation**. Picking a bigger number is not a fix; the answer is a merge queue or a shared convention, and it is a coordination problem rather than a numbering one. On every renumber the load-bearing pair is the `migration:<version>` stamp written into `field_provenance.description.corrected.by` AND the postcondition counting by that exact string — assert `grep -c` = **2**, because a disagreement reports 0 corrections AFTER both descriptions have already been rewritten. **Re-read the live applied ceiling immediately before merging; the constant in the test is a floor recorded at authoring time, not proof the file is still above it.** Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/cityCachedTitleCorrection.test.ts` (mutation-tested 10/10) plus two paired cases in `_shared/city-wiki-guard.test.ts`; dry-run in a rolled-back transaction on prod before each of the three versions.

**Payments:** Stripe via `create-checkout-session` + `stripe-webhook` edge functions.

**User submissions (Chrome extension):** `extension/` (MV3, React 19) extracts venues/events/hotels/marketplace/news from any webpage via JSON-LD/microdata/OpenGraph/DOM heuristics. `workers/submit/` (CF Worker) verifies user Supabase JWTs and stages into the same `ingestion_staging` table the scraper uses, with `source_type='user_submission'` — submissions flow through the existing normalize → dedupe → quality-score → review-gate → commit pipeline. Submitter columns + RLS added via migration `002_user_submissions`.

**Note:** `supabase/functions/` and `supabase/migrations/` at the repo root are the canonical locations.

## Repo stats

- **Edge functions:** 226
- **Migrations:** 1611

## Infrastructure

- **Supabase:** project `xqeacpakadqfxjxjcewc` (eu-central-2)
- **Cloudflare Pages:** project `queer-guide` at `queer-guide.pages.dev`
- **CF Account:** `7aa3765cc5f50f2b681b782eb4a8d296`
- **Search:** **Postgres + Cloudflare** (migration plan, historical: `docs/search-intelligence/meili-to-postgres-migration-plan.md`). The Postgres engine serves production; Meilisearch is decommissioned code-side (only the Infomaniak node shutdown remains, external infra).
  - **Postgres engine (live):** denormalized `search_documents` table (weighted tsvector + PostGIS `geog` + facets/trust/liveness/price/temporal). **`search_documents` has NO embedding column** — this line claimed a `vector(1024)` HNSW embedding on it until 2026-09-10 and that was never true of the live schema. The vectors live in the sidecar `search_embeddings (doc_id text, embedding vector(1024))`, keyed `'<entity_type>:<entity_id>'`, with the HNSW index on it (`search_embeddings_hnsw`, 902 MB, the one `search_hybrid`'s vector arm actually scans). RPCs: `search_hybrid` (RRF keyword+vector fusion in SQL, with target_groups filter + news-recency decay + vnn top-200 admission), `search_facets`, `search_autocomplete` (prefix + trigram), plus discovery RPCs (`get_recommendations`, `related_entities`, `find_duplicate_clusters`, `events_in_window`, `personalities_on_this_day`). Excludes `duplicate_of_id IS NOT NULL`.
  - **CF Worker:** `search-proxy` serves `/search` + `/autocomplete` from the Postgres RPCs (the former `SEARCH_BACKEND` meili/pg/shadow selector and the shadow-compare tooling were removed after cutover). Rollout runbook: `docs/deploy/search-rollout.md`.
  - **Sync:** Postgres `search_documents` stays fresh via entity + `content_embeddings` triggers (the `meilisearch-sync` edge function is gone).
  - **The vectors themselves come from ONE cron, and it was starving (fixed 2026-08-23):** `workers/ingest` is the only writer of the 1024-dim `content_embeddings` rows that `search_embeddings` — the vector arm of `search_hybrid` — reads. Its `wrangler.toml` called the `*/10` cron a "backstop" for a Supabase DB webhook and said *"the webhook remains the fast path"*. **That webhook does not exist and there is no evidence it ever did**: zero triggers in the whole database reach `net.http_request` (`pg_get_triggerdef(…) ilike '%http_request%'` → 0 rows). So the backstop was the only path for all eleven entity types while sized as a trickle — 15 rows × 144 runs = **2,160 rows/day for the entire platform** — and the 15 was itself pinned by the drain spending **3 subrequests PER ROW** (fetch, embed, upsert) against the Workers Free 50-per-invocation cap. **The ordering is what made it starvation rather than slowness:** `get_stale_embeddings` ended `ORDER BY cand.updated_at DESC`, newest-dirty-first, so under continuous churn (the `*/5` news re-sanitize, the nightly 300-1500-row backfills) the tail is never reached at all. Measured: **6,209 active `marketplace_listings` missing or stale**, fresh imports landing as deep as position ~2,900, keyword-searchable and vector-invisible — `"DanceSafe"` returned the rows while `"xylazine test strip"` returned apparel. `20260927123000` makes the work list FIFO (never-embedded first, then oldest-dirty-first; a row that keeps being rewritten keeps moving to the BACK, which is what bounds the backlog) and the worker batches all three legs — PostgREST takes `id=in.(…)`, **bge-m3 already accepted an array of texts** (the old code sent `{text:[t]}` and read `data[0]`), `content_embeddings` takes an array upsert — so 200 rows cost ~24 subrequests instead of 600 and throughput no longer depends on which Workers plan the account is on. 200 rows every 5 min = 57,600/day. **The webhook is deliberately NOT restored**: a per-row `http_post` trigger re-couples every writer to an external POST and the batch-capped backfills would each enqueue thousands of `pg_net` requests whose responses are retained ~6h — the same reasoning that produced `search_reindex_queue` in the P1 overhaul. `POST /drain {limit}` (token-gated, cap 500) runs the same work list on demand. **There was no health check on this path at all**, which is why an eleven-entity-type index gap sat unnoticed; `get_stale_embedding_backlog()` now reports per-table depth, head-of-queue age and a liveness timestamp, and `check-pipeline-health.mjs` **fails on liveness, warns on depth** (a healthy drain working through an import is legitimately deep; a dead one on a quiet day never is — the lesson `search_reindex_drain` learned the hard way). Liveness needs no run log: `workers/ingest` is the sole writer of `content_embeddings`, so `max(updated_at)` there IS the last time the drain worked. Drain and sentinel read one shared `embedding_candidates` view so the sentinel cannot measure a different set than the worker drains. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/staleEmbeddingsOrder.test.ts`. **Still open:** the candidate set embeds every row regardless of status (only `milestones`/`guides` filter to published) while `search_documents` itself excludes `duplicate_of_id IS NOT NULL` — if archived/duplicate rows are a real share of the corpus the drain is paying for vectors search will never return.
  - **Decommission (code-side complete, 2026-06-07):** the worker serves Postgres directly; the DB sync triggers/crons and the `meilisearch-sync` edge function are gone; `workers/search-proxy/src/meili.ts`, the `meilisearch/` ops dir, and `configure-meili.sh`/`meili-direct-resync.sh` are removed; the admin search-intelligence UI is Meili-free (dead `Index*`/`Consistency*` types dropped). `INDEX_MAP`/`ALL_INDEXES` live in `workers/search-proxy/src/entityIndex.ts` and are **active Postgres-side** entity-type→pg-type normalization (not Meili code, despite the name) — keep. Verified no live Meili cron/trigger/function/env ref remains; residual `meili` strings are only a shared `x-webhook-secret` default (`meilisearch-sync-webhook-2026`) in geocode/backfill functions — cosmetic, leave. **Only remaining task: the Infomaniak Meili node shutdown (external infra).**
  - **Legacy:** PostgreSQL FTS `universal_search()` and `algolia-sync` are REMOVED (dropped via migration `20260618150000`; `venue_amenities`/`attributes` fell with it)
- **Search:** **Postgres + Cloudflare** (Meilisearch fully decommissioned code-side 2026-06; the flag-based cutover scaffolding — `SEARCH_BACKEND`, shadow mode, shadow-analyze — was removed 2026-07).
  - **Postgres engine:** denormalized `search_documents` table (weighted tsvector + PostGIS `geog` + facets/trust/liveness/price/temporal). **`search_documents` has NO embedding column** — this line claimed a `vector(1024)` HNSW embedding on it until 2026-09-10 and that was never true of the live schema. The vectors live in the sidecar `search_embeddings (doc_id text, embedding vector(1024))`, keyed `'<entity_type>:<entity_id>'`, with the HNSW index on it (`search_embeddings_hnsw`, 902 MB, the one `search_hybrid`'s vector arm actually scans). RPCs: `search_hybrid` (RRF keyword+vector fusion in SQL, with target_groups filter + news-recency decay + vnn top-200 admission), `search_facets`, `search_autocomplete` (prefix + trigram), plus discovery RPCs (`get_recommendations`, `related_entities`, `find_duplicate_clusters`, `events_in_window`, `personalities_on_this_day`). Excludes `duplicate_of_id IS NOT NULL`.
  - **CF Worker:** `search-proxy` serves `/search` + `/autocomplete` from the Postgres RPCs and layers embedding (bge-m3 via AI Gateway), personalization, optional reranker and safety gating on top. See `workers/search-proxy/README.md`.
  - **Sync:** Postgres `search_documents` stays fresh via entity + `content_embeddings` triggers.
  - **Meili residue (intentional):** `INDEX_MAP`/`ALL_INDEXES` in `workers/search-proxy/src/entityIndex.ts` are **active Postgres-side** entity-type→pg-type normalization (not Meili code, despite the name) — keep. Residual `meili` strings are only a shared `x-webhook-secret` default (`meilisearch-sync-webhook-2026`) in geocode/backfill functions — cosmetic, leave. **Only remaining task: the Infomaniak Meili node shutdown (external infra).**
  - **Legacy:** PostgreSQL FTS `universal_search()` and `algolia-sync` are deprecated
- **Dedup:** `find_duplicate_clusters(content_type)` groups near-duplicate live entities (date-aware for events/festivals). Admins review + merge venues at `/admin/duplicates` — a soft, reversible merge via `merge_venues`/`unmerge_venues` (sets `duplicate_of_id`, reparents children, slug redirect via `venue_slug_redirects`, audited in `venue_merge_audit`).

## Environment

- Frontend (root): see `.env.example` — Supabase URL + anon key, Mapbox token, feature flags
- Scraper (`scraper/`): `DATABASE_URL`, source-specific API keys (see `scraper/.env.example`)
- Workers: each has `.dev.vars` for local dev

## Deployment

- **Frontend:** push to `main` → Cloudflare Pages auto-deploys
- **Edge functions:** `supabase functions deploy <function-name>`
- **Workers:** `wrangler deploy` from each worker directory
- **Scraper:** GitHub Actions — daily full refresh (03:15 UTC) + hourly events
- **DB migrations:** merged to `main` auto-apply via CI `db push` (history drift repaired 2026-06-10; remote `schema_migrations` == repo files). If applying early via MCP `apply_migration` (records history), commit the file with the SAME version — CI then skips it. Raw Management-API SQL does NOT record history → drift returns.

### SPA routing on Cloudflare Pages (2026-08-01)

**Three things here are load-bearing and `scripts/check-pages-routing.mjs` fails the build if any of them changes: no SPA catch-all in `public/_redirects`, no `public/404.html`, and `public/_routes.json` present.** With no `404.html` in the output, Pages' built-in fallback serves `index.html` (200) for any unmatched path and leaves static assets alone. That is the only shape that works; every alternative was measured against the real rule engine (`wrangler pages dev`) and fails:

- `/*  /index.html  200` — **silently dropped.** Pages strips `.html`/`/index` from rewrite targets, so the rule re-enters itself and the parser discards it as an infinite loop. Everything then fell through to `404.html`: `/help`, `/city/:slug` and every sitemap 404'd site-wide. Wrangler printed the warning on *every* deploy for months and nobody read it.
- `/*  /  200` — parses, but **200-rewrites outrank static-asset serving**, so `/robots.txt`, `/build-id.txt` and every hashed `/assets/**.{js,css}` are served the SPA HTML. The homepage rendered completely unstyled. (#2469, reverted in #2473.)
- `/section/*  /index.html  200` — same infinite-loop drop. `/section  /index.html  200` — 308-redirects instead of rewriting. **No rewrite whose target is `/index.html` can ever work here.**
- Status `404` is not expressible: Pages accepts only 200/301/302/303/307/308. Four `/assets/* /404.html 404` guards lived in the file for months and were rejected by the parser on every deploy.

`public/_routes.json` is shipped explicitly rather than left to wrangler. Without it wrangler generates the routing config and reads it back inside a bare `catch {}` (the `routesOutputPath` branch of `pages deploy`), which can upload the Functions bundle with no routes at all and no warning. The explicit file takes the validated branch and logs `✨ Uploading _routes.json` on every deploy, so the config is provably applied. **This was tried as a fix for the dead-Functions fault below and did NOT fix it** — keep the file (it removes a real silent-failure path), but do not mistake it for the cause.

### Pages Functions and the Workers quota — RESOLVED 2026-08-10, keep the diagnostic

**Current state (re-measured 2026-08-10): Functions execute, Workers respond, no `1027` anywhere.** Verified against prod, not inferred: `/brand/tokens.css` → 200 with 4,199 bytes of generated CSS (a Function-only route with no static file behind it), `/sitemap.xml` → 200 `application/xml` with 13 `<loc>` entries and **no `public/sitemap.xml` in the repo at all**, `/sitemap-landmarks.xml` → 200, `/manifest.json` → 200 (2,890 B, the Function overlay), and `/` carries a per-request `nonce-…` CSP from `functions/_middleware.ts`. Worker subdomains: `search.` answers `405` to a GET and `400` to a malformed POST (executing and validating), `img.` and `extract.` both respond. A `405`/`400`/`404` from a Worker means it RAN; only `1027` means the quota.

Everything below is the diagnostic from the outage, kept because it cost a full session to establish and the failure can return the moment billing lapses. **Do not read it as the present state** — the paragraphs are past tense on purpose. If Functions 404 again, start at "Check the Worker subdomains for 1027".

<details>
<summary>The 2026-08-01 outage: every Pages Function 404'd, and the cause was the account quota</summary>

Every Pages Function 404'd in production: `/sitemap*.xml`, `/api/*`, `/brand/tokens.*`, all crawler `<head>` injection and the nonce CSP. **The repo was correctly eliminated as the cause** — bundle compiles and uploads (158 KB), `_routes.json` uploaded and validated, the identical bundle serves every Function under `wrangler pages dev`, it fails on the raw `*.pages.dev` URL too, no `_worker.js`, no Node builtins, no top-level throws, and the responses are clean static 404s rather than `1101` Worker errors (never invoked, not invoked-and-crashing).

**It is the account request quota, not routing.** One command settles it — curl any Worker subdomain:

```
curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' "https://search.queer.guide/search?q=test"   # 429
curl -s https://img.queer.guide/ | head -1                                            # error code: 1027
```

`search.`, `img.` and `extract.queer.guide` all returned `429 error code: 1027` simultaneously on 2026-08-01. **Three unrelated Workers failing at once is account-level, never a code bug.** Invoice IN-72568830 is unpaid, the account is off Workers Paid, and the free **100k requests/day** cap is exhausted daily — and **Pages Functions ARE Workers billing against that same cap**, so they stop executing with it. It self-heals at UTC midnight and breaks again each afternoon. That also explains the two observations that looked most like a routing bug: it fails identically on `*.pages.dev` (same account), and it works under `wrangler pages dev` (local runtime, no quota).

**Check the Worker subdomains for 1027 BEFORE investigating Pages routing.** The repo-side elimination above cost a full session and could not have succeeded, because nothing in the repo was wrong.

Only a billing action fixed it. `public/_routes.json`'s exclude list (below) was the code-side mitigation and still earns its place — it cuts invocations ~50× so ordinary traffic cannot exhaust the cap again. `scripts/smoke-pages.sh` reports the degradation on every deploy without failing it (a known, tracked fault should not mask a new regression).

</details>

The built-in fallback answers *any* unmatched path with `index.html`, hashed-asset URLs included, so a stale bundle asking for a deleted chunk gets `200 text/html`. `functions/_middleware.ts` turns that into a real 404 — but only while Functions run, so `public/sw.js` is the layer that has to hold when they don't.

### Poisoned BROWSER cache for /assets/* — per-user, no purge or deploy can fix it (2026-08-08)

`_headers` stamps `/assets/*` `immutable, max-age=31536000` **on whatever Pages serves under that path** — verified live: a nonexistent hashed URL returns the SPA shell `200 text/html` with the immutable header; mid-deploy it can be a 404. A browser that fetches a chunk during a deploy window caches that failure **for a year, in the user's own HTTP cache** — a third variant after the CDN-PoP and CORS-variant poisonings, and the only one no purge, redeploy or hash rotation can reach. A document reload (including `?__fresh=`) cannot fix it: the fresh document re-imports the same hashed URL through the poisoned cache (measured on a real user's Chrome after the #2621 deploy — 8 chunks pinned at 404, guard fired every reload, page blank forever). The ONLY escape is a per-URL `fetch(url, {cache:'reload'})`, which bypasses **and rewrites** the entry. Three layers now do this: (1) `public/sw.js` retries any suspect `/assets/*`/`/fonts/*` response with `cache:'reload'` and serves the fresh copy transparently — controlled clients never even see the failure; (2) the inline boot guard (`index.html` + the middleware-injected copy in `functions/_lib/boot-guard.ts`, kept byte-identical by `src/test/__tests__/bootGuardSync.test.ts`) heals every known asset URL **before** its `?__fresh=` reload and allows **two** gated attempts (attempt 1 can land while the window is still propagating); (3) `src/main.tsx` heals failed URLs quietly on post-boot `vite:preloadError` so a poisoned lazy-route chunk can't brick that route on every future visit (`src/utils/assetHeal.ts`). A `_headers` per-status override is not expressible, and `/assets/*` must stay excluded from Functions (`_routes.json`, quota) — client-side healing is the only available layer.

**Read wrangler's parser output.** It reports dropped `_redirects` rules — and says nothing at all about unrouted Functions — on a deploy that otherwise exits 0 and prints "Deployment complete".

**A bad `_redirects` rule outlives its revert — it poisons the CDN.** The `/  200` window above lasted ~13 minutes, but Pages answered `/assets/css/index-*.css` with `index.html` during it and `public/_headers` stamps `/assets/*` `immutable, max-age=31536000`, so Cloudflare cached the SPA shell **under the stylesheet URL for a year**. The site rendered completely unstyled for hours after the revert deployed, across three clean deploys, because reverting config does not evict a cached object and the content hash never moved. Two things to know: **(1) it is per-colo** — `smoke-pages.sh` passed from the CI runner while the same script failed from Zurich, so a green deploy proves one colo is clean and nothing more; **(2) the escape is a purge or a new URL, never a redeploy.** `expect_asset_type` in `scripts/smoke-pages.sh` now tells the two causes apart (plain URL wrong + `?cb=` right ⇒ poisoned cache; both wrong ⇒ origin broken). Hashed chunks can be rotated by changing their bytes; `/fonts`, `/icons` and `/images` cannot be rotated at all — only a purge clears those.

### Stale SPA documents on the APEX — SOLVED 2026-08-04/05, and it was OUR code

**The conclusion this section carried for a day ("Cloudflare-side; needs support") was wrong, and so was its instruction not to look in `functions/_middleware.ts` — that is exactly where the bug was.** Fixed in #2591 + #2593; re-verified on prod 2026-08-10: `/events`, `/venues`, `/news` and `/cities` all return **no `age` and no `x-robots-tag`**, which is the signature's absence.

Two defects, both ours:

1. **`env.ASSETS.fetch('/')` used a key that never changes across deploys**, so a stale shell answered `/` for days and every deep route served a document referencing a chunk later deploys had deleted. Now keyed by `env.CF_PAGES_COMMIT_SHA` (`functions/_middleware.ts:256`), falling back to the bare path — a 404 there would kill EVERY deep route.
2. **`headers: indexResponse.headers` copied the subrequest's headers wholesale**, publishing its `age`, `accept-ranges` and an `x-robots-tag: noindex` that nothing in this repo sets — to Googlebot, on real indexable pages. Now only `content-type` plus an explicit `cache-control`.

**The leaked `age` fabricated the evidence for the wrong theory.** `age: 130273` beside `cf-cache-status: DYNAMIC` is a contradiction, and that contradiction became the central clue about a CDN cache that never held the object — which is what bought purge-by-URL, `purge_everything`, a dashboard purge, and **Always Online switched off on the zone**. Rules earned: **never copy a subrequest's headers onto a public response**, and **any internal fetch with a constant key is a staleness bomb** — if the body is build-specific, the key must be. Also: a CSP nonce compared across two requests always mismatches (it is per-request), so compare within ONE request (`curl -sD h.txt URL -o b.html`) — the cross-request comparison is what produced the false "the guard is CSP-blocked" finding below.

Round 2 (#2593) found two more of ours: the stale shell arrives as **`200`, not `404`**, so the SPA-fallback branch gated on 404 never ran — the discriminator is that our Functions build HTML per request and never emit `age`, so `status === 200 && has('age')` means a cached static object (`functions/_middleware.ts:244`). And **Cloudflare Rocket Loader re-executes inline scripts without their nonce**, so the nonce-CSP blocked the injected boot guard — the one script whose only job is recovering a blank page was itself disabled. `bootGuardTag()` now emits `data-cfasync="false"`; `vite.config.ts` had it at *build* time, but the guard is injected at *request* time. **`wrangler pages dev` has no Rocket Loader, so this class is invisible locally.**

<details>
<summary>The eliminations that cost several sessions before the cause was found — still useful, but read them knowing where this landed</summary>

Extends the stale-document fault above.

**`purge_everything` fails too, not just purge-by-URL.** Measured 2026-08-03: a targeted purge of all 55 shell URLs left `/venues` and `/events` untouched, and the escalation to `{"purge_everything":true}` was accepted by the API and *also* left them untouched. Together with `cf-cache-status: DYNAMIC` alongside `age: 130273`, that definitively excludes the zone cache — there is nothing there to evict. `scripts/smoke-pages.sh` now automates the whole ladder (targeted purge → escalate → re-verify) and prints "STILL stale after purge_everything". **That message means this fault. Stop purging.**

**Diagnose in this order.** The apex-vs-pages.dev comparison comes first, because it rules out the deployment in one command:
```
curl -s https://queer.guide/venues           | grep -o 'index-[^"]*\.js'   # stale
curl -s https://queer-guide.pages.dev/venues | grep -o 'index-[^"]*\.js'   # CURRENT
```
Pages serves the current build; only the apex is stale, so no redeploy can help. `?cb=` is second and **falsely reports healthy** — it is a different cache key, so it reaches origin and tells you nothing about what users get. Third, curl a worker subdomain for `error code: 1027` (the Workers quota, which independently kills every Function daily until ~00:00Z).

`functions/_middleware.ts` DOES run on the affected routes — canonical and CSP nonce are both injected. **That was read as "so the middleware is not the cause", which was the fatal inference**: the middleware ran, and dutifully rewrote a stale body it should have replaced. A previous session also inspected the entire zone in the dashboard (Always Online, URL purge, Cache Rules, Workers Routes, Page Rules, DNS, the Pages custom domain) and ruled all of it out — correctly, but the conclusion drawn from that ("Cloudflare-side; needs support") was wrong.

**The inline self-heal guard can never fire on an already-stale document.** `index.html` and `main.tsx` reload through `?__fresh=` for exactly this case (a bare `location.reload()` re-fetches the same key, and the one-retry gate then pins the page blank forever). But the guard is an inline script, and a stale document carries the nonce from its original render while the CSP header is regenerated per request — mismatch, blocked. When Functions are quota-dead there is no nonce at all and `script-src 'self'` has no `unsafe-inline` — blocked again. The guard protects documents cached from now on; it cannot repair the ones already stuck. (The CSP half of this was a measurement artifact — see the per-request nonce note above. The real reason the injected guard did not run was Rocket Loader.)

</details>

**The old advice here was "do not fix this in `_middleware.ts` — sourcing the shell from `env.ASSETS.fetch('/')` is the wrong layer."** It was exactly backwards: that call *was* the bug (see the top of this section). Only its caveat survived and is still live — replacing a 200 body must not clobber the Function-generated HTML that `functions/news/[slug].ts` and friends legitimately return from `next()`, which is why the 200-branch is gated on the `age` header and was verified against `/news/<slug>` keeping its own `<title>`.

**Why `queer-guide.pages.dev` looked healthy and made this look CDN-shaped:** pages.dev deep routes hit the same branch, its shell key was simply warm. And a direct hit on `/` never enters the branch at all, which is why the homepage was always fine — an asymmetry that reads as per-route caching.

### `public/_routes.json` — assets must not invoke Pages Functions (2026-08-01)

**Pages runs Functions AHEAD of static assets by default, and Pages Functions ARE Workers billing against the same request quota.** With no `_routes.json`, every hashed chunk, font and icon invoked `functions/_middleware.ts` just to reach its pass-through — putting one cold page load at **~51 billed invocations** (1 HTML + 1 CSS + 47 JS + 2 fonts). Off Workers Paid the account has a free **100k requests/day** cap, so the whole site died after roughly **1,800 page views/day** with `429 error code: 1027` — every afternoon, self-healing at UTC midnight. `public/_routes.json` excludes the static paths, cutting that ~50×.

**Do not add `/manifest.json`, `/brand/*`, `/sitemap*.xml` or `/api/*` to `exclude`** — those are real Function routes (`functions/manifest.json.ts`, `functions/brand/`, the 15 sitemap generators, `functions/api/`) and excluding them serves the static file or a 404 instead of the generated body. `/manifest.json` is the trap: a static `public/manifest.json` also exists, so excluding it fails silently with stale content rather than an error. `exclude` wins over `include`; the two lists together cap at 100 rules.

This does not replace paying the Cloudflare bill — Workers AI, `search.`, `img.` and `extract.queer.guide` still need Workers Paid. It only stops **the site itself** from being collateral.

## Testing

- **Always verify on production** (https://queer.guide) after deploy, not just localhost. The deploy target is Cloudflare Pages, not Vercel — Vercel is preview-only.
- **Frontend unit (root):** `npm test` — vitest + jsdom, `src/**/*.{test,spec}.{ts,tsx}`. That glob covers **only `src/`** — it does NOT reach `supabase/functions/`, `functions/`, `workers/` or `scraper/`, each of which has its own runner. Putting a test outside `src/` and assuming `npm test` runs it is the trap that hid the edge-function suite (next bullet).
- **Edge functions (Deno):** `npm run test:functions` — `deno test --allow-net --allow-env --allow-read` from `supabase/functions`, **by discovery, no file list**. These are Deno tests (`Deno.test` + `https://deno.land/std` imports), not vitest — vitest cannot run them at all, and vite's `src/**` include never saw them. Until 2026-08-02 CI named just 7 files under `_tests/`, so 38 of the 45 test files — every one under `_shared/`, including the `venue-consensus` and `amenity-normalize` suites this file calls "pure + unit-tested" — had never executed anywhere. They were not rotten: all 395 tests passed once run; only 4 type errors needed fixing. Now gated on every PR (`ci.yml` `edge-fn-tests`) and again before deploy (`deploy-supabase-functions.yml`) with the identical invocation, so a new `*.test.ts` anywhere in the tree is picked up automatically. Deps pinned in the root `deno.lock`. The 7 `pipeline-nodes` smoke tests self-ignore unless `SUPABASE_URL` + `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` are set (they call *deployed* functions); CI sets neither, so they stay ignored there — `pipeline-health.yml` is the cron that exercises that path.
- **Scraper:** `cd scraper && npm test` — vitest, `tests/**/*.test.ts`, 30s timeout, v8 coverage
- **E2E:** Playwright config at `playwright.config.ts`; specs in `e2e/`. Run via `npm run test:e2e` (or `test:e2e:ui` for the Playwright UI). Full suite runs nightly at 03:00 UTC via `.github/workflows/e2e-nightly.yml`; an `e2e-i18n.yml` smoke job runs on PRs touching i18n / trip-planner code.

## Gotchas

### iCloud & Git
The repo lives in an iCloud-synced folder. `.git` objects get evicted. If git commands hang or fail, run `brctl download .git` first.

### DB Column Names (common traps)
- All entity tables use `is_featured` (boolean). The legacy `featured` column on `venues` and `events` was dropped in PR #312; codebase migrated to `is_featured` end-to-end.
- `personalities.birth_date` / `death_date` (date type, NOT `birth_year` / `death_year` int)
- `news_sources.source_type` (NOT `type`), `.last_fetched_at` (NOT `last_fetch_at`)
- `countries.code` (NOT `iso_code`)
- `events.title` (NOT `name`)
- Table is `unified_tags` (NOT `tags`), has NO `is_active` column
- `personalities` has NO `known_for` column — use `profession` + `lgbti_connection`

### Squash merges & `[skip ci]`
GitHub squash merges concatenate every branch commit message into the merge commit. If ANY squashed commit contains `[skip ci]` (e.g. the CLAUDE.md stats-sync bot commit), the resulting push to `main` skips ALL push-triggered workflows (CI, `deploy-supabase-functions`) AND the Cloudflare Pages build — nothing deploys, silently (bit us on PR #1952). If a bot commit with `[skip ci]` lands on your branch, drop/reword it before merge, or after merging recover with `workflow_dispatch` on `deploy-supabase-functions.yml` plus a follow-up commit to `main` for the Pages build.

### Migrations
- Cannot use `CONCURRENTLY` (migrations run inside transactions)
- `supabase/migrations/` is large — check for conflicts before adding new ones (see Repo stats for current count)
- NEVER reuse a 14-digit version across two files — duplicate versions break `db push` file↔history matching (34 dups renamed 2026-06-10, PR #1553)
- **Two OPEN PRs can each pass `migration-versions` and still collide — the check is per-branch, the collision is a property of the MERGE (2026-09-02, PRs #3275/#3276).** #3274 and #3275 both chose `20261119110000`; #3274 merged first, and #3275's own check had passed honestly because its branch predated #3274's file. **The failure is NOT a silent skip, which is the intuition to correct**: `db push` tries to apply the loser and dies on `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "schema_migrations_pkey"` at `INSERT INTO supabase_migrations.schema_migrations`, and the workflow ends `##[error] MERGED MIGRATIONS DID NOT APPLY`. It is loud — but only in the Actions log, and the resulting DB state reads *exactly like success*: the version IS in `schema_migrations`, so any check of the form `select count(*) ... where version='…'` returns 1. **`version` applied is not evidence YOUR migration ran — compare `name`.** Measured: version present, `name` = the other PR's, gate unchanged at 4, target rows still carrying their pre-migration stamps. Two consequences worth knowing: the deploy continues to `functions deploy` after a failed push by design, so prod can run new code against the old schema (here it printed "No edge function changes detected", so nothing shipped); and renaming the loser to a fresh version is safe **precisely because it never applied** — no history row is bound to it, so no file↔history match breaks. (Renaming an APPLIED migration is the thing that must never happen.) **CLOSED STRUCTURALLY 2026-09-02:** branch protection on `main` now sets `required_status_checks.strict=true` ("require branches to be up to date before merging"), and `migration-versions` is one of the 12 required contexts. A PR whose base has moved can no longer merge until it is updated, and that update recomputes the merge commit and re-runs the check against a tree that now contains the other PR's file — the step that did not exist before, which is why nothing re-ran when the base moved. Replayed against this incident it blocks at step 3. The cost is that every branch update re-runs all 12, including `test` (~16 min); auto-merge absorbs most of it by updating and waiting, and a merge queue is the answer if it ever thrashes. **Still worth doing by hand** when you are not relying on auto-merge: re-read remote `max(version)` immediately BEFORE merging whenever another migration PR is open — the number you picked when you branched is stale. And independently of any of this, end a data-repair migration with a `DO $$ … RAISE` that re-asserts the condition it exists to fix, which is what made this recoverable in one round instead of shipping a second false green.
- **NEVER PICK A VERSION BY HAND AGAIN — run `node scripts/next-migration-version.mjs` (2026-09-19).** It prints a version; `--name <slug>` prints the filename, `--create <slug>` writes the file, `--renumber <path>` moves an existing migration. **The format is `9997` + 10-digit Unix epoch seconds** (`99971789798078` = 2026-09-19T14:47:58Z), and the reason it is a clock rather than `max + 1` is the whole point: deriving from the ceiling is the round-number heuristic wearing a script's clothes, and two sessions reading the same max get the same answer. Measured 2026-09-18/19 — #3778 and #3779 independently claimed `99800101100000`, then #3783 independently picked `99920101100000`, the version #3779 had renumbered TO inside the same hour; #3779 and #3783 each renumbered **four times**.
- **NEVER PICK A VERSION BY HAND AGAIN — run `node scripts/next-migration-version.mjs` (2026-09-19).** It prints a version; `--name <slug>` prints the filename, `--create <slug>` writes the file, `--renumber <path>` moves an existing migration. **The format is `9999` + 10-digit Unix epoch seconds** (`99991789807658` = 2026-09-19T17:27:38Z), and the reason it is a clock rather than `max + 1` is the whole point: deriving from the ceiling is the round-number heuristic wearing a script's clothes, and two sessions reading the same max get the same answer. Measured 2026-09-18/19 — #3778 and #3779 independently claimed `99800101100000`, then #3783 independently picked `99920101100000`, the version #3779 had renumbered TO inside the same hour; #3779 and #3783 each renumbered **four times**.
- **THE SPACE WAS NEVER THE PROBLEM, and "six increments left" is what made it look like one.** Against the 14-digit ceiling `99999999999999` and a max of `99960101100000` there are **39,898,899,999** increments remaining.
