The six is the number of steps left in the `999X0101100000` PATTERN, because every session only ever moved the fourth digit. **Re-anchoring the corpus back to real `YYYYMMDDHHMMSS` timestamps was measured and REJECTED**: it renames 478 files and rewrites 478 prod `schema_migrations` rows to buy headroom that already exists, while breaking things a rename cannot repair — **52 distinct `migration:<version>` strings are written into PROD DATA** (`field_provenance.*.by`) and several migrations carry postconditions that COUNT rows by that exact string, plus 167 migrations citing their own version in-body, 139 test files and 215 versions cited in this file. Do not re-propose it without re-measuring those five numbers.
- **RENUMBER-ON-MERGE IS INHERENT AND CANNOT BE REMOVED — only made cheap.** `db push` aborts on a pending file sorting below APPLIED history, so of two concurrent PRs the later merge must hold the higher version, which is unknowable when the file is written. `--renumber` is the answer: it moves the file AND rewrites the version in the migration's own body and in this branch's other files, which is the half that was going wrong by hand. **The version lives in more places than the filename** — `20260919130000_tag_uncategorized_disposition.sql` calls `set_config('app.actor', 'migration:20260919110000_…')` **three times**, carrying into production data the version it was renumbered AWAY from. **The rewrite is BOUND-ONLY outside the migration itself**: `<version>_<slug>` (a test's `MIGRATION` constant) or `migration:<version>` move; a bare version in prose is a CITATION and is left alone and reported. That distinction is not cosmetic — the tool's own first dry run offered to rewrite a comment in its own source that merely mentioned the version being moved. **Two refusals guard the one unrecoverable mistake** (renaming a migration already applied to prod): remote history says applied, or the file exists on the base ref. Both need `--force`, which prints what it is overriding.
- **Cross-PR collisions are now a CI gate: `scripts/check-open-pr-migrations.mjs`** (step `Check for version collisions with other open PRs` in `ci.yml`). It lists every OTHER open PR's changed files and fails on a version this branch also adds — the hole `check-migration-versions.mjs`'s own header has always named as still open ("a branch that exists only on another machine or only on the remote"; its check 5 reads sibling worktrees and is a no-op in CI). **A SEPARATE script deliberately**, because that file is pure-local by design (it runs in `.husky/pre-push` with no credentials) and a GitHub dependency there would make the local hook slow or quietly degraded. **Fails closed**: no token, an API error, a rate limit or a truncated listing all exit **2** and surface as a warning, never as a pass. Verified with a positive control against live open PRs — a synthetic file at a version PR #3790 held exited 1 and named the `--renumber` fix; without the control a green run is equally consistent with a gate that does nothing.
- **A MERGE QUEUE IS PRE-WIRED AND DELIBERATELY NOT ENABLED (decided 2026-09-19).** `migration-guard-main.yml`'s header names it as the real fix, and the groundwork is done: **all 12 required contexts already trigger on `merge_group`** (ci.yml, i18n-check, e2e-pr, trust-safety-gates, data-quality-gates, a11y — verified, not assumed), so a queued PR would not hang on a check that never reports. Three measured reasons it is on the shelf instead. **(1) It is now largely redundant** — the queue's value was "serialise so the per-branch check becomes sufficient", and `check-open-pr-migrations.mjs` reaches that outcome without serialising anything. **(2) It costs the setting that closed this bug class.** Classic branch protection exposes NO merge-queue field (confirmed against the live `/branches/main/protection` response; `/rulesets` is empty), so it needs a new ruleset — and GitHub disallows "require branches to be up to date" alongside a queue, i.e. `required_status_checks.strict` must go **false**, which is exactly the flag the 2026-09-02 entry above records as the structural fix for two PRs colliding. **(3) It amplifies the prod coupling this file already records.** 4 of the 12 required checks read LIVE PROD; on 2026-09-05 a Supabase 522 turned all 21 open PRs red at once. Today that reds every PR in parallel; behind a queue it stalls and evicts entries serially, which is worse. **Revisit if the every-update-reruns-12-checks thrash starts costing more than the prod-stall risk** — and if you do, flip it while the repo is quiet and prod is healthy, because a misconfiguration blocks ALL merges repo-wide and cannot be tested except live.
- **Escape hatch, if `9997` is ever exhausted (year 2286): `MIGRATION_VERSION_PREFIX=9998`, then `9999`.** Each is a further FULL epoch range — 260 years, not another six commits — and `allocate()` refuses a prefix that would sort below a version already claimed, which is the one change that would invalidate every allocation at once.
- **THE PREFIX IS `9999`, THE LAST ONE, AND A LOWER ONE WAS TRIED AND MEASURED AWAY THE SAME HOUR.** The allocator was first written with `9997`, against a repo max of `99960101100000`, deliberately leaving `9998`/`9999` as spare epoch ranges. **Within that same hour three sibling sessions hand-picked `99970101100000`, `99970101100100` and `99980101100000`** — straight through the chosen prefix and out the other side — and the first run against fresh `main` degenerated to `floor+1` and said so. That is the round-number heuristic demonstrating itself while its own fix was being written, and it settles the design question: **a hand-picker reaches for the next round number ABOVE whatever they see, so any prefix low enough to leave a spare is a prefix they will overshoot. A spare range is not an escape hatch if the thing it protects against can reach it first.** What is given up is small and stated rather than hidden: `9999` + epoch exhausts exactly at the 14-digit ceiling in **2286**, with 8.2×10⁹ increments in between, and there is no prefix after it. The real protection was never the spare range — it is that nobody picks by hand any more. `MIGRATION_VERSION_PREFIX` still overrides, for a future format change rather than as a ladder rung.
- **If the allocator ever warns `clock-derived … is not above the highest claimed version`, do NOT raise the prefix — find the hand-picked version and `--renumber` it.** That warning means someone picked a round number above the clock, and every allocation is back to `max+1` (i.e. collision-prone) until it is moved. It is a pointer to one bad row, not a signal that the space is exhausted.
- History repair: `supabase migration repair --status applied|reverted <versions>` from a checkout with all files + `supabase/.temp` link state. Pre-repair backup: `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations_backup_20260610`
- **MCP `apply_migration`'s `name` param is NOT the version.** It stamps the version from its own call timestamp and stores whatever you pass as `name` verbatim — it does NOT make the remote history match a repo filename you intended. Applying live via this tool WILL create version drift unless you immediately follow up: call `list_migrations` right after to read back the real stamped version, then `git mv` your committed file to that exact version before opening the PR (see `20260705215233_20260709100200_get_my_agenda.sql` / `20260706042931_20260709100600_fix_marketplace_brands_anon_401.sql` for the established repair-shim naming). Caused two same-day incidents on 2026-07-06 (PRs #2019/#2020, #2021/#2023) that broke `deploy-supabase-functions` with "Remote migration versions not found in local migrations directory." Also: this Supabase project has concurrent agent sessions applying migrations directly — before repairing a drift-fail, grep the target version across `supabase/migrations/` for a file someone else already added, don't assume it's yours to fix alone.
- **Drift monitor (safety net, 2026-07-26):** `.github/workflows/migration-drift-monitor.yml` runs `scripts/check-migration-drift.mjs` every 6h (+ `workflow_dispatch`) — it queries remote `schema_migrations` via the Management API and FAILS on two states: a remote version with no repo file (the exact state that makes `db push` skip silently), and — added 2026-09-02 — a version whose remote `name` disagrees with the single repo file at that version, i.e. **applied under another name**, which is what a merge-time version collision leaves behind and what no count-by-version check can see. A leading 14-digit prefix is stripped from both sides first, because the MCP repair-shim convention puts the intended version in the name; measured, that takes the disagreements from 23 to 6, so no allowlist is doing the work. The 6 survivors sit in `KNOWN_NAME_MISMATCHES`, which may only SHRINK — an entry that starts agreeing fails the check and tells you to delete it, so the list cannot rot into an allowlist nobody re-reads. This catches drift within hours regardless of push activity, closing the gap where the push-triggered check in `deploy-supabase-functions.yml` only fires when a migration is actually pushed to main (that gap let 30 versions drift undetected on 2026-07-25/26). **Remediation is automated too since 2026-09-02 — the "detection only" sentence that stood here is retired, not softened.** `.github/workflows/migration-drift-recovery.yml` runs `scripts/recover-migration-drift.mjs --write` on the same 6h cadence offset by 30 min and **opens a PR**; it never pushes to `main`, because a migration that arrived via MCP skipped review once already and committing it unreviewed skips it twice. It enumerates every orphan in ONE pass, which matters because `db push` reports only that *some* version is missing, never which or how many — so hand-recovery is iterative and each orphan hides the next, which is how four were found one at a time across hours on 2026-08-29. The manual flow above still applies to anything it skips, and the detector staying red is the backstop. Complements the pure-local `check-migration-versions.mjs` (dup/malformed versions, no remote view).

  Four properties of that script are load-bearing, each measured rather than assumed. **(1) `schema_migrations.statements` strips every trailing semicolon** — statement 1 of `20261007163200` ends `= '120s'`, its last ends `end $verify$` — so joining on newlines alone emits invalid SQL that fails only on a rebuild from zero, the one occasion an applied migration ever runs again; `STATEMENT_SEPARATOR` puts them back. **(2) Migrations are routinely multi-statement** (that same file is 4), so reading `statements[1]` silently truncates to the first — exactly what all five hand-recoveries of 2026-08-29 did, surviving only because every one of them happened to be single-statement. **(3) It never invents content**: a digest mismatch (local md5 vs a server-side digest over the same join), an empty `statements`, or an unreadable version is skipped and reported, leaving CI red. A plausible file at the right version is worse than a missing one — the missing one keeps failing until someone fixes it, the corrupted one passes every check and quietly misrepresents what ran; an empty `statements` is likewise not evidence the migration was a no-op. **(4) With no token it exits 2 rather than reporting a clean tree** — "could not look" is not "nothing found". Where the migration exists on some branch the authoring commit is preferred over reconstruction, because `statements` does not record comment headers and this repo keeps its reasoning there. The rebuild logic is exported pure and unit-tested (`src/lib/__tests__/recoverMigrationDrift.test.ts`, both guards mutation-tested) because the script cannot run without a token — otherwise CI would be the first place it ever executed, on real drift, with someone already blocked. **Still unproven: the reconstruct-and-write path has never run for real** — the first live run (2026-09-02, dispatched) found no drift, which exercises token, API and the clean exit but not the rebuild.
- **A live-applied migration left in an UNMERGED branch blocks every PR in the repo, and the two guards then deadlock (2026-08-10).** The drift check runs on the *merge commit* of every PR, so a version prod has applied whose file sits only on an unmerged branch fails `migration-versions` on **unrelated** PRs — measured: `20260810075202` was applied via MCP and stranded in draft PR #2680, which failed #2681 and #2672 as collateral for hours. `apply_migration` therefore is not "done" until the file is **merged to main**, not merely committed to a branch. The deadlock: drift demands the file at that exact version, while check 3 of `check-migration-versions.mjs` rejects a new file sorting below remote max — and renaming it upward re-breaks the file↔history match. Fixed by making the ordering rule ask remote history: a version already in `schema_migrations` is exempt, because `db push` matches by version and **skips** an applied migration instead of aborting on it. Both scripts now share one fetch (`scripts/lib/remote-migrations.mjs`) so they cannot disagree about what "applied" means, and the `migration-versions` CI step needs `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` for it. It **fails closed** — no token or an unreachable API means no version is treated as applied and the check stays strict, saying it could not verify rather than reporting clean. So a legitimate recovery can still fail here during an API outage; that is the intended trade.

- **Nothing parsed a migration before `db push` met it, and that cost main four and a half hours (2026-09-14).** `db push` was broken on `main` from 10:57 to 15:33 across four deploy failures on four different files. The first was `50500101100300`, whose `COMMENT ON ... IS` ended `'…' || 'Complements tag_wikidata_repair_regressions(), …'` — **`COMMENT ON` takes a string LITERAL, so that is a `42601`**. A parse error is unreachable at runtime, so no test that executes logic can find it, and because `db push` stops at the FIRST failing file and takes every migration queued behind it, **the blast radius is the repository, not the PR**. Nothing in CI could see it: `check-migration-versions.mjs` and `check-migration-drift.mjs` only ever look at 14-digit VERSIONS, no linter here parses SQL, and no unit test executes migration SQL. `scripts/check-migration-sql.mjs` closes it with **libpg-query, Postgres's own parser pinned to 17.x** (the server version this project runs, so the CI grammar cannot drift from the grammar `db push` will meet); it is a step in ci.yml's fast `migration-versions` job — **1.6 s for 1,719 files, no database, no Docker**. **State the limit, because a gate believed to cover more than it does is worse than none:** it catches top-level syntax only. It does **NOT** see inside a plpgsql body — the outer grammar treats `$$…$$` as an opaque literal, measured: a body of `begin if 1 then end` parses clean — and it catches **nothing semantic**, so `50500101100400`'s `hard-point` postcondition failure the same afternoon would still get through. Closing that needs a scratch database with the full schema replayed, which is a separate and much larger change. **The first sweep found SIX already-applied migrations that did not parse, and ALL SIX ARE NOW REPAIRED (2026-09-14); the allowlist is empty and the whole 1,721-file corpus parses with no exemptions.** This entry first said they were *corrupted mid-file* and could not be fixed without inventing content, and **that was wrong — the diagnosis was made from a line-based dollar-tag counter that mis-pairs nested quoting.** Five of the six were ONE mechanical corruption repeated: a block spliced in twice, the duplicate carrying a header split across lines (` RETURNS jsonb` / ` LANGUAGE plpgsql`) where the survivor has it joined onto one line — sometimes at the head of the file, sometimes mid-file, sometimes as an orphaned copy of a function's body tail left sitting after its own `END; $function$;`. **The repair is pure DELETION — +0/-25 lines across the four `2026062317*` files, with 22 of the 25 removed lines verified to still have an identical twin in the file**, so nothing was reconstructed. The other two needed a decision: `20260511000000` ended with two `CREATE POLICY IF NOT EXISTS` statements — **a clause PostgreSQL has never had in any version**, verified on this project's own server (42601 `syntax error at or near "not"`, against 42P01 for the same statement without it) — so they never executed and prod carries **0** such policies; they are removed rather than repaired, because restoring them would make a rebuild diverge from production, and the bucket is public with **0 objects** on a platform that mirrors images to R2. `20261028120000` had been deliberately emptied to a no-op and **the emptying was left half-done**, stranding ~70 lines of plpgsql after the block that replaced their opener. **Three things the RECORD settled that reading could not.** (1) `schema_migrations.statements` is a partial record for four of these rows — the CLI's splitter gives up on unbalanced dollar-quoting and stores one truncated statement — so it cannot rebuild a file, **but it is still authoritative about WHICH of two candidate forms ran**: in `20260623170823` the file carried both a `DO $$`/`END $$;` pair and a `DO $outer$`/`END $outer$;` pair around one block, and the applied statement contains the `$outer$` pair and **does not contain `DO $$` at all** — the opposite of what the surrounding comment placement suggested. Ask the record, not the layout. (2) A short `statements` record is **not** evidence of partial application: every function and cron those four files create exists on prod. (3) `20261028120000`'s own header claimed it was *never applied*; **both** it and `20261027120000` are in `schema_migrations` today with full five-statement bodies, so that line was true when written and is not now — corrected in the file rather than deleted. `KNOWN_UNPARSEABLE` survives as an empty mechanism and may only **SHRINK**: a listed file that starts parsing is a hard FAILURE telling you to delete its entry, so the list cannot rot into a place a new defect hides — the same discipline as `KNOWN_NAME_MISMATCHES`. Guarded by `src/lib/__tests__/migrationSqlParse.test.ts`, which runs the REAL parser over the REAL corpus rather than asserting on the script's source (a source-string assertion passes against a gate that has stopped matching anything), mutation-tested 4/4 at first and 2/2 again on the repair (re-introducing a removed splice fails the sweep; re-adding an exemption fails two tests) — and **one mutation first read as a survivor only because a `sed` delimiter clashed with the `||` it was meant to introduce, so the edit silently never happened**, which is the fifth time this file has recorded that same check.

### Frontend
- Path alias: `@/*` → `src/*`
- Vite manual chunks configured for: vendor, router, utils, graph, exceljs, maplibre, tiptap, PDF, mammoth, sentry, i18n

## Design

LGBTQ+ travelers, locals, activists, researchers, allies. Safety-first, inclusive by default, content is the hero.

- **Color — SUBWAY MAP track set (2026-08-09; surfaces re-valued 2026-08-17).** Frame-grey page (`--background: 60 16.3% 91.6%` #EDEDE6) over a paper card (`--card: 60 33% 97%` #FAFAF5) + ink (`--foreground: 0 0% 6.7%` #111) plus **four SEMANTIC track colors** modelled on subway lines: `--track-pink` `330 100% 56%` (#FF1F8F), `--track-blue` `193 100% 45%` (#00B4E6), `--track-green` `136 75% 52%` (#2BE05A), `--track-yellow` `50 100% 50%` (#FFD500). Track colors are wayfinding, not decoration — **one accent per context**; the four blend only in `.intersection-gradient` (master-symbol moments, never a background wash). They are **fill-only** (never body text) and **ring-gated**: blue/green/yellow measure under 3:1 vs any light surface, so every track-coloured *mark* carries a 1px `border-track-ring` and WCAG 1.4.11 is satisfied fill-vs-ring (`tokenContrast.test.ts` gates this, anchored to `--track-ring` so it stays mode-independent). This is why a badge and a track-filled button keep an edge when cards lost theirs. A track-coloured *line* on a diagram is exempt — it is far past the size 1.4.11 applies to and reads as illustration, which is why route lines carry no casing. Separately WCAG 1.4.1: **colour is never the only cue** — a mark encoding a state also carries a glyph or a label; no token guard can see this, it lives in the components. Text ON a fill is **ink on all four** (paper-on-pink is 3.43:1 and paper-on-blue 2.3:1 — deliberate deviation from the source mock; the README table said "paper" for pink until 2026-08-17 while the code and test both said ink, and the code was right). Track colors never encode risk and never reach `/help`, `/safety`, `/report-*`, the trip-safety briefing, the equality scale or any risk badge; every hue sits >25° from `--destructive` (hue-gated). Adding/renaming a color token touches four layers in lockstep — `src/index.css` → `tokenCatalog.ts` → `functions/_lib/branding.ts` `COLOR_KEYS` → a migration extending `branding_validate`'s `v_color_keys` (precedent: `20260825100000_branding_track_tokens.sql`). `--spot`/`--ink-blue`/`--ink-over` are **deprecated aliases** of pink/blue/green (old PASTE-UP names) kept until the Public/Admin phases retire their call sites. `--destructive` remains the only non-track semantic hue, alongside the locked functional palettes (trip-safety traffic-light, equality-score scale, content warnings). ESLint still errors on hex/rgb/hsl literals outside allowlisted files. Button variants: `accent` = pink track, `brand` = blue track, `secondary` still a deprecated alias of `default`.
- **Typography:** **Anton** for display + **Space Grotesk** for everything else (2026-08-09 subway-map rebrand; Inter removed). Both self-hosted woff2 (`public/fonts/anton/`, `public/fonts/space-grotesk/`). `--font-display` = Anton, applied to `h1`/`h2` + `.text-hero(-xl)` + `.font-display`; body/UI is Space Grotesk (400 reading, 700 "station names" — nav, card titles). Editorial ladder re-tuned to 96/76/52/32/20 px: `--text-hero-xl` (6rem), `--text-hero` (4.75rem), `--text-display` (3.25rem), `--text-headline` (2rem), `--text-title` (1.25rem), `--text-body-lg` (1.0625rem, prose bump not a rank); adjacent ranks step 1.26×/1.46×/1.63×/1.60× — **all ≥1.25×**. Micro-scale (`--text-15/13/xs2/2xs/3xs`) unchanged. Always use a token; ESLint blocks arbitrary `text-[NNrem]` / `font-extrabold`. Anton is never letterspaced apart (tight tracking); the **eyebrow convention** (`text-2xs uppercase tracking-wide(r)`) stays the one wide-tracking exception. The **rank table** is in `docs/design-system/README.md`. **Changing a size token is still a six-layer change**: `index.css` `@theme` + `@source` safelist, `tokenCatalog.ts`, `branding.ts` `SIZE_KEYS`, `src/lib/utils.ts` `customTextSizes`, and a `branding_validate` migration (RAISEs on unknown keys — check `site_branding` + `site_branding_versions` before removing one).
- **Color — PASTE-UP ink set (2026-08-08): RETIRED 2026-08-19, kept only as history.** The risograph-press system put three brand "inks" over a monochrome base: `--spot` fluorescent pink, `--ink-blue`, `--ink-over` violet, plus the `--color-ink-pink` alias. The subway-map rebrand made them aliases of `--track-pink` / `--track-blue` / `--track-green` (same values), and they are now **deleted** from all four layers — `src/index.css`, `tokenCatalog.ts`, `functions/_lib/branding.ts` `COLOR_KEYS`, and `branding_validate` (migration `20260911160000`). Use the track tokens. Two rules from that era survive because they are what makes chromatic brand colour survivable at all, and they now attach to the tracks: **colour is non-semantic by construction** (red means danger, a track means nothing — a track may never encode a state or risk level, never be body text, and never appear on `/help`, `/safety`, `/report-*`, the trip-safety briefing, the equality scale or any risk badge), and **enrolment in the guards is one flag** — `ink: true` on a `COLOR_TOKENS` entry, which is what puts a colour into `tokenContrast.test.ts`'s fill-only ban and its >25° distance check against `--destructive`'s ACTUAL hue per mode. That flag replaced three hardcoded `['spot','ink-blue','ink-over']` arrays, one of which failed OPEN. **Never hand-copy a channel value into this file** — read `tokenCatalog.ts`, which is drift-tested against `src/index.css`. This bullet once carried six literals and all six were wrong, including a 30° error on `--ink-blue` in the one dimension that matters. **Ceiling:** `branding_validate` rejects override docs over 150 keys; removing these six bought headroom back. **Historical:** the berry `--accent-brand` token went in the 2026-06-25 monochrome strip, which left `accent`/`brand`/`secondary` as Button variants collapsed onto `default`; `secondary` is still a deprecated alias of `default`.
- **Typography:** Inter for body/UI + **Space Grotesk** display face for large headings (2026-06-17). Both self-hosted woff2 (`public/fonts/inter/`, `public/fonts/space-grotesk/`); Plus Jakarta Sans removed. `--font-display` = Space Grotesk, applied to `h1`/`h2` + `.text-hero(-xl)` + the `.font-display` utility; `h3`/card titles and all body text stay Inter. Editorial size scale in `@theme` — `--text-hero-xl` (7rem/112px), `--text-hero` (4.75rem/76px), `--text-display` (2.75rem/44px), `--text-headline` (1.75rem/28px), `--text-title` (1.25rem/20px), `--text-body-lg` (1.0625rem/17px), *(the hero/display/title figures here read 88/64/40/22px until 2026-08-08 — stale since the PHOTOCOPY re-tune; `tokenCatalog.ts` is the source of truth and is drift-tested against `index.css`)*, `--text-15` (15px), `--text-13` (13px), `--text-xs2` (11px), `--text-2xs` (10px), `--text-3xs` (9px). Always use a token; ESLint guards block arbitrary `text-[NNrem]` / `font-extrabold` (error in BOTH trees since 2026-07-11). **`--text-headline-lg` (2rem) was deleted 2026-08-04** — at 1.14× from `--text-headline` it sat under the ~1.2× a reader resolves as a different level, so the two were used interchangeably across its 34 call sites. Heading ranks now step 1.47× / 1.73× / 1.57× / 1.40×, and **adjacent ranks must stay ≥1.25× apart**; `--text-body-lg` is the deliberate exception (1.06× from base — a reading-comfort bump for prose, not a rank). The **rank table** — which token belongs at which nesting level, plus the rule that a card title may never use the same token as the section heading above it — is in `docs/design-system/README.md`; its absence is why four different section-header grammars grew independently. **Deleting a size token is a six-layer change**: `index.css` `@theme` **and** the `@source inline(...)` safelist, `tokenCatalog.ts` `GLOBAL_TOKENS`, `functions/_lib/branding.ts` `SIZE_KEYS`, `src/lib/utils.ts` `customTextSizes` (without it tailwind-merge collapses `text-13 text-background` into one class), and a migration narrowing `branding_validate` — which **RAISEs** on an unknown key, so check `site_branding.published`/`draft` **and** `site_branding_versions` first (`branding_revert` replays a stored doc back through the same validator). **Sanctioned eyebrow convention:** small uppercase metadata labels use `text-2xs uppercase tracking-wide(r)` — this is the one place wide tracking is allowed (233 sites); display headings keep tight tracking (≥ -0.02em) and body text is never tracked.
- **Page layout — ONE container (2026-08-10).** Every page frames its content with `<PageContainer>` (`src/components/layout/PageContainer.tsx`): gutter `px-4 sm:px-6 md:px-8`, vertical `py-8 md:py-12`, cap from `--container-page` (1600) / `--container-reading` (768, prose) / `--container-form` (512, forms). `size` picks the measure, `flush` drops the vertical for pages that own their own bands, `as` swaps the element. **Nothing owned page layout before this** — `LayoutShell` and the `<main>` in `routes.tsx` contribute zero horizontal spacing by design (routes must be able to go full-bleed), so ~118 page files each declared their own frame: 12 distinct max-widths, **62 pages pinned at a flat `px-4` while the header/footer/home sections grew to `md:px-8`**, so on any viewport wider than `md` the chrome breathed and the content did not; vertically it was `py-6/8/12/16/20` picked at random. Admin had the mirror-image bug — `AdminShell`'s `main.p-4 sm:p-6` PLUS 17 of 31 pages padding again on top (48px on some pages, 16px on others, six content widths). **The cap is 1600, not the de-facto `max-w-7xl` (1280) it replaced**: the cap exists to stop grids spreading, and 1280 left about a third of a common desktop viewport as dead margin. Full-bleed bars (header rows, breadcrumb bar, tinted home bands) stay full-bleed — the rule or tint IS the band's edge — and take the cap on their *content row* only; `AdminShell`'s `<main>` is the single owner of admin spacing and admin pages now render bare content. **Traps:** (1) adding a container token is a **three-layer** change — `index.css` `@theme`, `customContainerSizes` in `src/lib/utils.ts`, and the docs table; tailwind-merge's `max-w` group only knows t-shirt sizes, so without the registration two caps apply at once and stylesheet order decides. It does NOT touch `tokenCatalog`/`branding_validate` (colour + text-size keys only — the drift test iterates catalog→CSS, so an unknown `@theme` key is safe). (2) **You cannot opt out of the gutter with `px-0`** — tailwind-merge drops the base `px-4` but leaves `sm:px-6 md:px-8` standing (different modifiers, different merge keys), so the gutter silently returns at ≥640px; `PageHero`'s `bare` swaps the container for a plain div instead. (3) One bespoke width survives on purpose: `LegalPageLayout` at 1100px (prose + a 224px sticky TOC — the page cap would stretch legal text unreadably, `reading` would leave the prose ~430px). Guards: `queerguide/no-hand-rolled-page-wrapper` (a NAMED ESLint rule scoped to `src/pages/**`, deliberately not another `no-restricted-syntax` selector — that rule is replaced WHOLESALE per file in flat config, so a new selector would have to be re-stated in all four blocks and one miss silently disables load-bearing ones, precedent #2049) + `e2e/page-layout.spec.ts` (asserts the page's outermost container's content edge equals the header's across 12 routes × 390/768/1440/1920, i.e. the *relationship*, so it survives a change to the ladder itself). Full spec: `docs/design-system/README.md` §Page layout.
- **Spacing rhythm:** strict 8 pt grid. Use even-step Tailwind utilities only — `p-{0,2,4,6,8,10,12,16,20,24}`, same for `m-`, `gap-`, `space-{x,y}-`. Odd-step utilities (`p-3`, `gap-3`, `p-5`, `space-y-7`, …) were removed 2026-05-21 (UI audit P8); ESLint-warn them in new code. `.5` increments (e.g. `p-1.5`, `gap-2.5`) remain allowed as the only sub-8pt micro-spacing — useful for icon-level offsets. Arbitrary `[NNpx]` values are only acceptable for genuine element-sizing constraints (icon dimensions, dropdown widths, card max-widths); never for spacing that could use the scale.
- **Shape — SOFT RE-SKIN (2026-08-17), reversing "squared corners everywhere".** The design project chose option **1b** of `Softer Drafts.dc.html` and took it through every template; Brand Guidelines §02b now reads *"26 for page-level panels, 18 for cards and fields, 12 for chips and small controls. **Nothing square.**"* The radius tokens are re-valued, not renamed: `--radius-container` 18px, `--radius-element` 12px, `--radius-badge` 9px, plus a NEW `--radius-panel` 26px for dialogs/sheets/page shells. Because 1,164 call sites already used the semantic tokens this was a **value flip, not a sweep**. `--radius-panel` and `--hairline-alpha` are declared in `@theme` **only** — deliberately absent from `tokenCatalog.ts` / `functions/_lib/branding.ts` / `branding_validate`, following the `--radius-full` precedent, which is what kept the whole re-skin free of a Supabase migration. That matters: a full `/admin/design` override already sits within a handful of keys of `branding_validate`'s **150-key ceiling**, so cataloguing either one means raising the cap in the same migration.
- **The ground layer (2026-08-18, brand update).** `body` carries a **1600×900 repeating tile of the four track colours drawn as beziers at 13% opacity**, `background-attachment: fixed` (scroll below 768px — iOS Safari repaints a fixed background every scroll frame). It is **load-bearing, not decoration**: this update returns `--background` to paper #FAFAF5, so **page and card are now the SAME colour** and a card reads because it *covers* the pattern, not because it is a lighter tone. Delete the ground and every card goes invisible — the same failure the frame-grey page was introduced to prevent on 2026-08-17, solved the other way round. `tokenContrast.test.ts` therefore no longer asserts a card/page tonal step (that would now fail on correct code); it asserts the ground exists, carries all four tracks, and that card === background by design. Text over the tile is safe — worst case is muted-on-magenta at 6.03:1, ink at 15:1. The `sanctioned ink only` e2e sweep does NOT see it: that sweep reads element `background-color` under `#root, header, main, footer`, and `body` matches none of those. Wash re-valued #F1F1E9→**#EAEADE**, well #E8E8E1→**#DDDDD1**.
- **Surfaces without cages (2026-08-17).** A container **never carries a frame**. What replaced the 3px ink cage is a pair: `--card` (paper #FAFAF5) sitting one tonal rung above `--background`, which **is no longer paper** — the page is frame-grey #EDEDE6, with wash #F1F1E9 for insets and #E8E8E1 for image wells. **`--background` moving off paper is the load-bearing fact of the whole system**: restore it to paper without also restoring the cage and every card becomes invisible, not subtler. `--border` dropped from ink to a 12%-ink divider and is now for **row rules only**; the one line allowed between surfaces is `border-border-hairline` (channels in the token, alpha in `--hairline-alpha`, because `branding_validate`'s `HSL_RE` forbids an alpha component in a value). **Three borders survive on purpose, all WCAG 1.4.11 obligations:** form controls (`border-input`, gated at 3:1 against *both* page and card), the ink ring on a track-coloured mark (`border-track-ring` — its own token, not `--foreground`, so it cannot invert when dark mode lands), and the outline of anything whose edge IS the component (outline buttons, the `DetailMasthead` status chip, alert callouts, reversed CTAs on ink bands). The de-caging codemod that deleted ~390 frames could not tell those apart and flattened 113 of them; they were restored by hand. **Check for a replacement boundary before deleting a border on an ink band**, where neither tint nor shadow registers.
- **Shadows — ONE soft elevation (2026-08-17), reversing the hard poster shadow.** `--shadow-soft` (`0 16px 40px` @6% ink) at rest, baked into `Card` itself rather than opt-in; `--shadow-soft-hover` (`0 12px 30px` @13%) + `translate(-3px,-3px)` on `.card-lift`; `--shadow-soft-lg` for dialogs/sheets/the search plate, which float over a scrim. `:active` seats back to `--shadow-soft`, **not** `none` — collapsing it drops the card flat into the page mid-tap. Shadows are authored as literal `rgba(17,17,17,α)`, never `hsl(var(--foreground)/α)`, which would flip them to a white glow in dark mode. `.card-lift-invert` was **deleted**: a card on an ink band can use no shared elevation at all (a black blur is invisible on ink, a paper blur reads as a halo), so such a tile separates by `bg-background/10` and deepens that tint on hover — see `src/pages/About.tsx`. `--shadow-hard*` are gone. Tailwind's `shadow-(md|lg|xl|2xl)` ramp **stays ESLint-banned** — soft depth being legal here does not make a second, competing ladder legal. A card **fills ink on hover or lifts — never both**. `e2e/design-system.spec.ts` now asserts the *inverse* of what it used to: a shadow at rest and **no** border. The `.plate-offset` misregistration layer and the whole PASTE-UP print layer (halftones, deckle, duotone, grain) are deleted, and as of 2026-08-19 so are the leftover class names that used to sit inert in components.
- **Gradients:** still not allowed **in JSX**. ESLint errors on `bg-gradient-to-*` in components (exception: black readability scrims over images, `from-black/NN`, and `from-background` scroll fades). `.intersection-gradient` (the four tracks blending) lives in `src/index.css` as a utility class — ESLint is scoped to `**/*.{ts,tsx}` and never parses CSS, so the one sanctioned gradient exists without weakening any JSX rule.

  **Print layer: DELETED 2026-08-19.** The last survivors of the PASTE-UP print system are gone from `src/index.css` — `.duotone-riso`, every `.halftone-*`, and `.ink-bleed` (which had zero call sites). The band-level screens, deckle, grain, misregistration and `.plate-*` classes went earlier. **The reason is the brand rules, not tidiness:** Brand Guidelines §08 says photos "run full color and unretouched. No duotones, no color washes, no gradient overlays." The `<Image treatment="riso" | "halftone">` prop, `src/lib/imageTreatment.ts` and its one call site went with them; the `image_treatment` columns on `venues` / `events` / `news_articles` stay but were non-null on **zero rows**, so nothing stored was discarded. Do not rebuild any of it. §08 Photography still contains one stale line of its own — a photo "sits inside the same 3px ink rule as every other block" — which §02b ("Ink keylines are gone from containers") supersedes; follow §02b.
- **Icons:** two tiers (2026-08-09). **`TransitIcon`** (`src/components/transit/`) is the 42-icon custom wayfinding set — stroke-only, currentColor, ROUND terminals, one station ring per icon, stroke weight bumps below 32px, never takes track colors; use it for navigation/content-type concepts, and never mix it with lucide in the same surface. **lucide-react** remains the default for UI chrome (edit/trash/chevron/…) across the ~780 importing files; `<LucideProvider strokeWidth={2.5}>` still wraps the non-admin branch of `LayoutShell` (the old butt/miter cap override was removed — round caps are the subway rule and lucide's default). Admin keeps the softer default.
- **Motion:** functional only (skeleton pulse, dialog/sheet transitions, accordion). No decorative animation (Aurora removed, ScrollReveal on hero removed). **Exception — a sanctioned queer-joy motion zone, extended 2026-07 beyond `/messages` to Groups, Dating's mutual-match reveal, and the public homepage (`/` — `src/pages/Index.tsx` + `src/components/home/*`):** these surfaces may use celebratory motion (`ReactionBurst`/`ParticleBurst` (`src/components/joy/`) on adding a reaction or saving from a home rail, `JoyBurst` confetti on a new match's first message, a group join, the dating match reveal, and the homepage born-this-week birthday tap). Homepage joy is **interaction-earned only** — never on page load, scroll, or hover. The bursts are **monochrome** (`hsl(var(--foreground))`/`--muted-foreground`/`--border` confetti pieces — the former rainbow was removed in the 2026-06-25 monochrome-strip refactor; this bullet previously described that removed rainbow treatment, which was stale) plus the bundled sticker pack in `/messages`. Every such effect is hard-gated behind `useMotionTokens().reduced || isLowEndDevice()`, `aria-hidden`, and auto-unmounts; none of it is imported by `/help`,`/safety`,`/report-*`, or Plans/Calendar (travel content stays motion-free — safety-adjacent). Do not extend this exception to other routes without updating this line. **"Motion-free" here scopes the JOY ZONE, not the functional subway keyframes** — `.track-loader`, `.station-pop`, `.station-arrive` and `.route-draw` are functional and already run across travel: `RouteFade` wraps *every* route in `.station-arrive`, `ui/loading.tsx` uses `.station-pop` app-wide, and `Button loading` renders a `TrackLoader` inside `src/components/trips/**`. `/trips/discover`'s line-draw is the same category — result-arrival feedback on an explicit user action, bounded, one-shot, never on mount, final state rendered instantly under `prefers-reduced-motion` (`src/components/trips/discover/useRouteReveal.ts`). What stays banned on travel is `src/components/joy/**`, any confetti/burst, `.ink-bleed`, and any scroll- or hover-triggered reveal.
- **Copy:** direct factual voice. No "discover/explore/unlock/curated/journey/amazing/tailored/personalized for you". Empty states: "No X yet." not metaphors.
- **Dark mode is LIVE — this line used to say the opposite and was wrong (corrected 2026-08-19).** It said dark mode was removed with the 2026-08-09 rebrand, the `.dark` block gone, `ThemeToggle` deleted and every `dark:` utility inert. None of that holds: `src/index.css` carries a full `.dark {}` token block, `ThemeToggle.tsx` exists and renders from `Footer.tsx` and `MobileNavSheet.tsx`, `ThemeProvider` applies the class and swaps the `theme-color` meta, and ~45 `dark:` utilities are load-bearing. The design agrees — `Brand Guidelines.dc.html` carries `body[data-theme="dark"]` with the same ink/paper swap and the same `--frame` / `--wash` values the repo uses. Treat dark as a supported mode: a token needs a value in BOTH blocks, and `tokenContrast.test.ts` gates each pair in both. The one rule that does NOT flip is a track colour: those are identity, which is why type on a track fill takes `--track-ring` (ink in both modes) and never `--foreground`.
- Components: shadcn/ui primitives in `src/components/ui/`.

### Documented exceptions
- **`--destructive`** token for hard-error semantics (payment declined, pipeline failed, irreversible confirms). Reserved muted red — the ONLY chromatic color in the entire product. User-locked 2026-05-19.
- **Trip safety briefing traffic-light.** `src/components/trips/TripSafetyBriefing.tsx` retains low/moderate/high/critical risk colors. Safety > consistency for LGBTQ+ travelers in high-risk destinations. User-locked 2026-05-19.
- **Functional categorical scales** still allowlisted in `eslint.config.js`: map vector tiles, equality scores, news taxonomy, avatar gradients, submission scan flyers, trip cover gradients, content warnings, password strength meter, OAuth brand SVGs. Each is functional, not decorative.
- **City network diagrams carry all four track colors and run straight (2026-08-14).** The homepage city cards render an octilinear abstraction of each city's REAL rapid-transit network (`src/components/home/subway/CityNetwork.tsx` + generated `cityNetworkGeometry.ts`, produced by `scripts/generate-city-transit-lines.mjs` from OpenStreetMap route relations and committed — no runtime fetch, no DB column, no cron). Two rules bend, both deliberately: **(1)** all four track colors appear in one component, assigned by line LENGTH RANK (flagship = pink), because on a network map the four colors *are* the wayfinding vocabulary — "one accent per context" would make the artifact unreadable, not calmer; this is the only sanctioned four-track surface outside `.intersection-gradient`. **(2)** Segments run straight between 45° bends, so hard rule #1 ("illustrative transit lines are never straight") is now scoped to the SINGLE-line case — the fallback template line, used for cities with no rail, still obeys it. Coordinates sit on an **integer lattice**, so a diagonal step of *k* is exactly (±k, ±k) and "every bearing is a multiple of 45°" is exact arithmetic in `cityNetworkGeometry.test.ts` rather than a tolerance a rounding change could silently widen. Lines are **bare strokes, no ink casing** (user decision, 2026-08-14 — a black outline reads as a border around a shape, not as a route; border-gating still governs filled shapes, and a route line is not one). Trunk-sharing lines are separated by small INTEGER nudges whose pairwise differences are neither axis-aligned nor diagonal, so two lines can never re-converge along a heading they are allowed to run in — without them New York's R/N/F/M drew on identical pixels and only the last color was visible. Lines are ranked by length **inside a 12 km core**, not total length, or a suburban orbital leads (Madrid opened with L12/MetroSur drawn as a hook detached from the rest); relations tagged proposed/under-construction are dropped, or Paris leads with the unbuilt Grand Paris Express and Tel Aviv publishes two light-rail lines that do not exist. **Two Overpass traps, same shape — an empty 200 is never an answer.** (1) It returns **HTTP 200 with an empty element list and a `remark`** when a query times out, so a bare `res.ok` check cached "Madrid has no metro"; Madrid, Paris, Mexico City and San Francisco all lost their metros and the empties were indistinguishable from genuine absences. (2) Worse, **`overpass.osm.ch` is a SWITZERLAND-ONLY extract that returns a clean `200 {"elements":[]}` for the rest of the world with no `remark` at all** — pinned to a third of a 487-city run it cached "no network" for Essen, Dortmund, Gothenburg, Leipzig and ~450 others, and the run reported 34/487 as if that were data. Nothing in a response says "I am a regional extract", so `probeEndpoints()` now asks every endpoint a **control query the whole planet agrees on** (Berlin's U-Bahn, ≥5 relations) at startup and drops any that fails. Only planet mirrors belong in `OVERPASS_ENDPOINTS`, and the probe distinguishes BUSY (a 504 — retried with backoff) from REGIONAL (an empty 200 — condemned at once), because after a few thousand requests both mirrors 504 for a while and treating that as unhealthy aborts a run that would be fine ten minutes later. **Coverage is a population-ordered PREFIX, not a threshold** (307 cities, everything listable down to ~180k): Overpass throttles a long sweep from 2/min to 0.39/min, so the run is built to be stopped and shipped at any point. Responses are cached on disk, so extending it only costs the new tail, and `--cached-only` re-derives the committed file without fetching. A city already fetched under the old per-mode split keeps those files; a fresh one asks for all three modes in ONE request (~85% of small towns have no rail and used to pay a rail query AND a tram query to learn it — combining them was a 2.4x speedup with zero cache invalidation). Line identity is `ref || colour || name`: systems with no `ref` are named for their endpoints, so Stockport drew Manchester Metrolink's ONE line as four colours ("Rochdale - East Didsbury", "Rochdale Town Centre - East Didsbury") — no string surgery recovers identity from that, the colour does. The write guard gates on whether a run would SHRINK the committed file, never on yield rate: yield is 46% above 500k and a few percent below 100k, so a rate gate would refuse every legitimate expansion. Geometry is ODbL; the footer carries the credit.
- **Inline links underlined.** `p a, li a, td a, span a, label a` get `text-decoration: underline` in `src/index.css`. Without color difference from body text, the underline is the only cue that distinguishes a link (WCAG 1.4.1, axe `link-in-text-block`). Standalone links — nav, buttons, cards — stay un-underlined.
- **Card links are overlay siblings, never wrappers (2026-07-27, #2370).** A whole-card click target is an absolutely-positioned `LocalizedLink` rendered as the LAST child of the card's `relative` wrapper (`CardHoverEffect`), a **sibling** of `<Card>` — never an `<a>` wrapping the card. Wrapping puts the card's tag chips and its favorite / add-to-trip / check-in / share buttons inside an anchor: invalid HTML and axe `nested-interactive` (serious, WCAG 4.1.2). Canonical examples: `EventCard.tsx`, `VenueCard.tsx`, `GeoCard.tsx`, `NewsCard.tsx`. Rules when using it: the overlay needs `no-underline` (the unlayered `li a:not(.no-underline)` rule in `index.css` would otherwise force `position: relative` and collapse it) and an `aria-label` (it has no text); action buttons need `z-10` to sit above it — `FavoriteButton` and `VenueCheckInButton` take no `className`, so put it on their wrapper. Tag chips inside a card pass `linkless` to `TagChipRow` (renders `<span>`, not `<a>`); on detail pages they stay links. `<Link>` wrapping `<Button>` is the same violation — use `Button asChild` with the link inside. Guarded by `e2e/nested-interactive.spec.ts` and `expectNoNestedInteractive()` in `src/test/test-utils.tsx`.
- **Crisis & safety pages are animation-free.** `src/pages/HelpHotlines.tsx` and any future route under `/help`, `/safety`, `/report-*` must not consume Aceternity components, scroll-reveal effects, or decorative motion. Functional motion only (focus rings, dialog transitions, accordions). Protects users in crisis from cognitive overload and respects `prefers-reduced-motion` (WCAG 2.3.3). The Pattern Library (`/pattern-library` → §A11y exemption) documents the canonical static pattern.
- **Semantic radius tokens.** Always pick from the trio `rounded-container` (16px — cards, sheets, dialogs, hero blocks), `rounded-element` (8px — buttons, inputs, list rows, nested cards, image frames), `rounded-badge` (4px — chips, pills, status tags) over raw `rounded-(sm|md|lg|xl|2xl|3xl)` literals. The trio is a single point of change for the entire visual rhythm. `rounded-full` permitted for avatars/dots only; `rounded-none` for explicit flat overrides.
- **Pride map canvas — REMOVED (2026-06-25).** The former pride-spectrum overlay (`PRIDE_LAYER_COLORS` + the rainbow density-heat ramp, gated by the `pridePalette` prop) was stripped in the monochrome refactor. The `/map` canvas now uses only the **functional** categorical `LAYER_COLORS` (`src/hooks/useExploreMapData.ts`) — distinguishing layer types (venue/event/city…), the same functional map-color exception allowlisted for vector tiles — plus a monochrome black-alpha density ramp in `src/components/map/ExploreMap.tsx`. The `pridePalette` prop was renamed `mapShellMode` (it now only gates MapShell-specific UX like the empty state, not color). Chrome stays strictly monochrome.

### Design System Files
- Tokens: `src/index.css` (Tailwind v4 `@theme` block — CSS variables; no `tailwind.config.ts`)
- Animation: `src/lib/animation.ts` (durations, easings, distances)
- Charts: `src/lib/chartPalette.ts` (monochrome recharts palette + stroke patterns; added Phase 3a)
- Components: shadcn/ui primitives in `src/components/ui/`. For monochrome status semantics use the `Badge` variants (the unused `StatusBadge` primitive was removed in the 2026-05-31 declutter; see git history).
- Enforcement: `eslint.config.js` (hex/rgb/hsl + chromatic classes + shadows + gradients: ERROR in both trees since 2026-07-11 — the public block had silently dropped the hex selector via flat-config wholesale replace, repaired in #2049; semantic radius + spacing error in the same blocks; admin motion ban error). Flat-config gotcha: the LAST matching `no-restricted-syntax` block replaces ALL earlier selectors for a file — every public selector must live in the final public block.
